## Launch 2026-10-26 (Monday morning)

Every App launches publicly on Monday morning 2026-10-26 (Ben, 2026-10-24: "definitely the 26th, in the morning"), so everything ships Sunday 2026-10-25. The review file keeps its original name. The review of the whole is [docs/launch-2026-10-27.md](docs/launch-2026-10-27.md): the launch-blocking items (the public repository with a license, the public site, the Access command, everyapp.team on the launch commit, the `npx` trap, a fresh-account run), the decisions from this file Ben should settle before Monday with a recommendation each, what not to do before launch, the Sunday-evening checklist, and the launch messaging. Postiz is ported but not part of the launch, and was deleted from everyapp.team on 2026-10-24; nothing below should read otherwise. Items this review found done or superseded are under "Done or superseded (2026-10-23)" at the end, each with its commit or live check.

- [ ] **Ben: review and merge every-app/private-open-seo PR #1067 (the Every App mode; open-seo #474 was opened by mistake and is closed) before announcing OpenSEO on Every App; then the release workflow on every-app/open-seo must run once.** The workflow attaches `everyapp-release.tar.gz` to the release `pnpm release:publish` creates, so the next OpenSEO release after the merge (v0.1.18) is the first `pnpm everyapp install https://github.com/every-app/open-seo` can fetch.

Open decisions and follow-ups from the October 2026 platform work (#267–#274).
Check an item off, or delete it, once it's decided or done.

## Decide

Each of these shipped with a default. Confirm it or change it.

### Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/openmail-rename

Ben, 2026-10-26: the agentic-inbox port launches as OpenMail. Apache-2.0 §6 grants no trademark rights, so the fork doesn't ship under upstream's name; §4 keeps the LICENSE (upstream has no NOTICE) and a statement of changes (the app README's Origin line, with docs/EVERY_APP.md listing them). Defaults taken:

- [ ] **Product name OpenMail**, matching OpenCRM, OpenAnalytics and OpenSEO. Alternative: a name without the Open prefix.
- [ ] **Public repository `every-app/open-mail`**, matching open-crm, open-analytics and open-seo; the private one was renamed to `every-app/private-open-mail` (GitHub redirects the old URL). Alternative: `every-app/openmail`.
- [ ] **App id `openmail`**, not `mail`: `mail.<domain>` is commonly a mail host. A Gateway that ran `agentic-inbox` sees `openmail` as a new app; everyapp.team's live inbox stays on the old id until Ben moves it (accepted on 2026-10-26).
- [ ] **Test fixtures and history left as they were.** `packages/cli/src/test/fixtures/manifests/agentic-inbox.ts`, the pinned deploy snapshot `agentic-inbox-durable-objects.md`, the ports test, the perimeter/toolbus/ai-proxy tests' sample ids and dated design, briefing and rollout records describe the upstream port as it was; renaming them changes no behaviour. The docs that cite the fixture still name its path. Alternative: rename them in a follow-up.
- [ ] **No redirect from `/docs/apps/agentic-inbox`** to `/docs/apps/openmail`: the site has no path redirects and the page shipped only on 2026-10-26. Alternative: add one if the old page was ever deployed.

- [ ] **Password accounts and Cloudflare Access.** An existing password account has to connect Access once before it can sign in through Access.
- [x] **Mobile app behind Access.** Superseded on 2026-10-24: the mobile app (`apps/mobile-native`) was deleted in the simplify stage 1; the gateway's mobile-only sign-in code went in #442 (f6207144).
- [ ] **MCP defaults.** OAuth is on, and dynamic client registration is on with limits. `MCP_OAUTH_ENABLED=false` and `MCP_DCR_ENABLED=false` turn them off.
- [x] **One scope rule: app routes take `*` only** (#757, HELD, 2026-10-26). Ben confirmed 2026-10-26: full scope or nothing. A personal access token opens an app's own routes only when it holds `*`; any narrower token (`mcp:read`, an app-domain scope the person typed) is `403 token_scope` everywhere but a served `/mcp`, which also takes a token with an `mcp:` scope. Before, a token with app-domain scopes reached app routes and `patterns.md` told apps to check `hasScope(user, "*")` while `identity.md` said they needn't. On a route the manifest declares public, a narrow token is served as an anonymous visitor (never less than no token). The manifest's top-level `scopes` field is deleted in the same PR (no app declared it; the `scopes` in app manifests are OAuth capability scope sets, a different thing). `hasScope` stays exported because CRM calls `hasScope(user, "*")`; phase 2 removes that call, then the export (lanes 1/2). The Admin token dialog offers only `access` (`*`) or `deploy`, so tokens made there keep working; a token created through the API with app-domain scopes alone (`schemas/user-token.ts` accepts any) now opens no app route.
- [ ] **Destructive tools.** Tools marked destructive (e.g. delete todo) stay hidden from AI clients until approvals exist.
- [x] **WebSockets live 15 minutes** (#759, HELD, 2026-10-26). Ben confirmed 2026-10-26: 15 minutes is enough for launch. The Gateway relays every proxied WebSocket through its own `WebSocketPair` and closes both ends 15 minutes after the handshake with code 1000 and the reason `Reconnecting: sessions renew their sign-in every 15 minutes.` (`WEBSOCKET_MAX_LIFETIME_MS`, `packages/perimeter/src/webSocketLifetime.ts`). 15 minutes is what OpenSEO, CRM and the inbox each enforced themselves, so phase 2 deletes their timers without a behaviour change; 1000 rather than 4xxx because the Agents SDK client treats 4xxx as terminal. Not configurable: a manifest field would need the schema, docs and a reason an app wants a longer window. The relay also strips `Domain=` from a first-party handshake's `Set-Cookie` (a known gap, now closed). `everyapp dev` raw-pipes upgrades and caps nothing.
- [ ] **Close a removed member's sockets at once** (after launch; design). Ben 2026-10-26: not before launch; the relay stays, no SDK-side close. The Gateway has no Durable Object today, and a plain Worker holding a relay can't be reached from the request that removes the member, so removal, a role drop below the app's `access`, or suspension leaves an open socket for up to 15 minutes. Design: a `SocketRelay` Durable Object per organization (`idFromName(orgId)`) takes the upgrade from the perimeter, opens the app side over the same service binding, accepts the client with the Hibernation API tagged `user:<id>` and `app:<rowId>`, keeps `expiresAt` in the attachment and sweeps it from an alarm each minute; `MembershipService.remove/changeRole/deactivate` and the app-access writes call `stub.revoke({ userId, appRowId? })`, which closes `getWebSockets(tag)` with 1000 and a reason. Cost: Durable Object duration for every open socket (the app-side outgoing socket keeps it awake), plus a DO migration in the Gateway's `cloudflare.config.ts` and its deploy path. Ben: worth the DO, or is 15 minutes enough for launch?
- [ ] **Setup link expiring mid-wait.** If the owner setup link stops working while `everyapp setup` waits, setup doesn't make a new one.
- [ ] **RepoLens badges.** Badges are public even for private repos. Keep that, or return 404 for private ones?
- [ ] **RepoLens access.** Set `access: "everyone"` in its manifest before its first deploy if the whole org should see it (`--access` went with the flat command tree).
- [ ] **RepoLens retry backfill.** Left out of its MCP tools, because it deletes fetched history.
- [ ] **TanStack Start 1.169.** The platform's apps are pinned to Start 1.168.61, because 1.169 needs Vite 8. RepoLens already runs 1.169 on Vite 8, so the upgrade looks safe.
- [ ] **Gateway UI scope.** Only the gateway moved to OpenSEO's shadcn design system. The starter (`starter/`) uses plain Tailwind utilities since #544; the landing page still uses daisyUI or its own styles (the example apps were deleted on 2026-10-24).
- [ ] **Gateway look.** Every App's metallic primary buttons and neutral accents, on OpenSEO's surfaces. Dark mode uses OpenSEO's dark greys, with a lighter metallic so the buttons stand out on them. Check the dark-mode buttons.
- [x] **Gateway layout.** A sidebar (OpenSEO's app shell) replaces the top header and the admin tabs. Admin pages now open on phones too, where the old UI showed "not supported on mobile".
- [ ] **When the schema check runs.** `deploy` checks an app's drizzle migrations against its schema after the build, so under the app's lease: the build needs resources created under the lease, and the check needs the dependencies the deploy installs. The redesign's step 7 asked for it before the lease as well.
- [ ] **A schema check drizzle-kit can't finish.** When `drizzle-kit generate` fails (most often a renamed column, which it asks about only at a terminal), the deploy stops with `SCHEMA_CHECK_FAILED`. Only a missing drizzle-kit or drizzle config is a warning.
- [ ] **`dev --host` takes an address.** `everyapp dev` listens on 127.0.0.1; `--host 0.0.0.0` opens it to other machines. Vite's bare `--host` (no value) isn't supported.
- [ ] **`create -y`.** With nothing left to confirm, `-y` means "never ask": an app id is then required, as under `--json`. A failed `cf workers types` or local migration in `create` is a warning, since `dev` redoes both.
- [ ] **The organization before an owner.** A deploy to a Gateway with no organization makes one named after the Cloudflare account (slug from that name, else "default"). Its org key, and so every app's Worker and resource names (`every-acme-todo`), comes from that slug for good, even after the claim renames the organization to the owner's name.
- [ ] **Deployer access needs a verified email.** A new app is granted to the `users` row whose verified email is the cf login's. Password accounts are never verified (only Access sign-in verifies), so without Access "private to you" means owners and admins only.
- [ ] **API-token deploys.** With `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` set the actor is `api-token:<account id>`, and nobody gets the new app as its deployer, even if cf reports the token's user's email.
- [ ] **`--no-wait` skips the whole live wait.** The app then goes live on its first visit. (Named after the certificate wait until the flat command tree.)
- [ ] **The live wait's outcomes.** No certificate after 10 minutes is a warning and exit 0 (as the certificate wait was); the Gateway answering for 10 minutes without activating the app is `APP_NOT_LIVE` (exit 1, retryable).
- [ ] **No SDK or CLI version check when deploying an app.** `minSdk` and `minCli` came from `/api/deploy/whoami`. The redesign's "a checkout older than the live Gateway refuses" (step 3) may be enough; else read the checkout's build constants.
- [ ] **Status reads certificates from Cloudflare's certificate packs** (active, exact host or one-label wildcard). A hostname covered only by Universal SSL could read as "no certificate yet".
- [ ] **The release-identity probe went with `/api/deploy/status`.** An upgrade that stopped before recording itself is no longer recognized as newer: `setup` and the rebinds stop unattended and ask at a terminal. Step 3's install row written before the upload closes that window. Since step 3 the install row is the only truth: a reconcile with the live release no longer checks which version the Worker serves (only cf's strict mode stops it over a dashboard edit).
- [ ] **Setup's reachability check only warns.** A Gateway that doesn't answer over HTTPS yet, or answers with Cloudflare Access's sign-in, is a warning before the owner link, and setup waits for the claim all the same.
- [ ] **App commands find the account without `--account-id`.** Since step 3: Gateway discovery through every account the login sees; one Gateway is the one, several need `--account-id`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, the named host or a prompt.
- [ ] **`--gateway` picks among several Gateways.** When the login sees several, the host `--gateway` (or `setup --domain`) names picks the one install with that host before anything asks; it is still `GATEWAY_URL_MISMATCH` when it names none.
- [ ] **Several Gateways, unattended: `choose_account`.** A new `action_required` action (exit 2) with `data.choices` (account, name, Gateway URL, and each one's rerun with `--account-id`); its `data.rerun` is a template (`… --account-id <account-id>`).
- [ ] **`CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` counts like `--account-id`.** Naming an account without a Gateway is `GATEWAY_NOT_FOUND` for app commands (saying where the Gateway is), not ignored; for `setup` (and `deploy`'s offer to install) it is where to install a new one.
- [ ] **Tokens that list no accounts.** An `--account-id` or `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` that `cf auth whoami` doesn't list is probed anyway, for API tokens without account read access.
- [ ] **Errors while probing an account.** `auth` and `not_found` (also when reading the install row) mean "no Gateway here"; any other failure (network, timeout) stops the command rather than calling the account empty. No probe timeout beyond the runner's 2 minutes yet (live check below).
- [ ] **Device login under `--json` everywhere the connect path runs.** `setup`, `status`, `deploy` (and `secret`), `delete` and `db query` with `--json` wait for cf's device login.
- [ ] **What the build cache hashes.** Every file `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` lists under `apps/every-app-gateway`, `packages/sdk`, `packages/perimeter`, `packages/toolbus` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, as on disk (ignored files such as `.env*` never count). Without git there is no cache: every deploy builds.
- [ ] **The build cache keeps three builds**, the most recently used, under `node_modules/.cache/everyapp/<tree>.tar.gz`.
- [ ] **Releases ordered by commit time.** The downgrade guard orders by newest migration, then the last commit touching the Gateway's sources (`git log -1` over the hashed paths), not build time, which an old checkout or a cache hit gets wrong. The install row keeps it as `release_committed_at`, with `source_tree_sha256`. A dirty tree orders as its base commit; without git, as now.
- [ ] **Same version, different commits, in the refusal.** `DOWNGRADE_REFUSED` names versions, and adds each side's source commit date when the versions are equal (`0.2.0 of 2026-10-12`).
- [ ] **App deploys and deletes refuse an older checkout even without a rebind.** Checked before the registry is touched, with no override; only `setup --allow-downgrade` rolls the Gateway back.
- [ ] **A rebind with the live release skips migrations and the replace check**; one with a newer release (the checkout moved on) migrates first, as an upgrade.
- [ ] **The tree hash is the Gateway's identity, not the release SHA-256.** Builds aren't reproducible (checked locally: two builds of one tree name their client chunks differently), so another machine at the same commit builds another SHA-256. "The live Gateway" means the same SHA-256 or the same source tree everywhere: `status`'s `upToDate`, `setup`'s nothing-to-do, the downgrade guard, and a rebind that skips migrations.
- [ ] **Status's checkout fixes.** `GATEWAY_OUTDATED` (sources changed since the live build) is informational since #400 and runs `everyapp gateway deploy`; `CHECKOUT_BEHIND` runs `git pull`. `upToDate` is `null` when either tree hash is unknown. (stage 4, 2026-10-24: `gateway deploy` is now `everyapp update`)
- [ ] **The install row before the first upload** names the release being installed, with `live_version_id`, `live_domains` and `jwt_public_key` empty; app commands refuse it (`GATEWAY_NOT_INSTALLED`, run `setup`) and `status` says `GATEWAY_NOT_UPLOADED`.
- [ ] **`deploy --yes` installs the Gateway without asking.** At a terminal, `-y` takes `apps.<zone>` (still asking which zone when the account has several); unattended (`--json`, CI, piped output) `deploy` never installs one, `-y` or not: it stops with `install_gateway`.
- [ ] **The install offer proposes `apps.<zone>` only.** Another hostname means declining and running `setup --domain`; the offer doesn't ask for a hostname the way `setup` does.
- [ ] **The inline install waits for the owner.** `deploy`'s install runs setup's owner step (up to 15 minutes) before the app goes up; if nobody opens the link, the deploy ends with `claim_owner`, and the next `deploy` puts the app up.
- [ ] **`install_gateway`'s rerun names a host.** `pnpm everyapp setup --domain apps.<first active zone>` of the account setup would pick (with `--account-id` when one was given), or `<hostname>` when it can't tell (several accounts, no zone). `secret set|unset` don't offer to install (`GATEWAY_NOT_FOUND`).
- [ ] **`db migrate` is local only.** `--local` is accepted, documented as the default; there is no production migrate outside `deploy`. (The `--local` flag itself went with #360, 182c34d9; the default stands.)
- [ ] **The skill is copied, not linked**, into `.claude/skills/everyapp` and `.agents/skills/everyapp` of each new app (a standalone app is its own repository; git on Windows checks symlinks out as files). A missing `AGENTS.md` is written; an existing one that doesn't mention the skill gets a line only after a person agrees. The skill's "Ship" line has no `--yes` (nothing to skip under `--json`). (Since #295 an existing AGENTS.md that doesn't mention the skill gets the pointer line without asking: `packages/cli/src/lib/app/skill.ts`.)
- [ ] **OpenSEO: a mode, not a fork.** OpenSEO gets `AUTH_MODE=everyapp` upstream. Since 2026-10-17 the mode is prototyped in `bensenescu/open-seo-everyapp` (PRs #1 mode, #2 deploy-stage, #3 follow-ups; merge there when CI is green) and comes home to `bensenescu/open-seo` as one PR when Ben says it is settled; nothing is opened or merged on open-seo before then, and app.openseo.so is never deployed from the scratch repo. The Docker edition stays a fork, because it replaces the runtime.
- [ ] **Integrations before GitHub.** DataForSEO and Context.dev came first, as API-key integrations in gateway Worker secrets ([docs/capabilities.md](docs/capabilities.md)). The manifest's request is the grant, as for AI providers; admin approval and keys encrypted in D1 come with the Google phase. The redesign review ([docs/design/2026-10-redesign-review.md](https://github.com/every-app/private-every-app/blob/5ef30882/docs/design/2026-10-redesign-review.md)) keeps the Worker secret as the operator default behind a `resolveCredential` seam, with per-organization keys later.
- [ ] **AI providers fold into `integrations`.** Reverses the phase-0 default (OpenRouter beside OpenAI in `providers` and `/api/ai`): one registry, one `/api/integrations/<id>/<host>/<path>` route, one `gatewayFetch`, with the model and route allowlists kept as the AI packs' own checks. `providers` and `ai` leave the manifest (redesign review #1).
- [ ] **Apps may frame their own pages.** The gateway keeps an app's `frame-ancestors 'self'` (and a stricter `base-uri 'none'`) instead of forcing `'none'`, for OpenSEO's report viewer.
- [ ] **App Workflows.** Allowed when the app's own Worker defines them and they're named `<worker>--<binding>`.
- [ ] **Helper Workers.** An app may ship Workers of its own (`workers` in its manifest), deployed as `<worker>--<name>` before the app's Worker. Only the app's Worker can reach one; it shares the app's D1, KV and R2 and calls the gateway as the app. OpenSEO's site-audit engine runs on one. Redesign review #3: a helper presents its own Worker name in the binding's props, so a background call is attributed to the Worker that made it.
- [x] **Manifest integration entries lose `as` and `background`.** Landed with the key renamed to `capabilities` (entries `{ access, why }`). An approval is of the app, attribution comes from the identity header, and an organization's connection always allows background use (Ben, 2026-10-15). Background writes on write-capable providers get an admin checkbox later (redesign review #2).
- [ ] **Google: the operator holds the OAuth client, each organization connects its own accounts.** Decided by Ben on 2026-10-15; designed in [docs/design/2026-10-google-integration.md](https://github.com/every-app/private-every-app/blob/5ef30882/docs/design/2026-10-google-integration.md). The remaining defaults, each with a recommendation in that doc's Appendix B:
  - [x] Hosted has two kinds of apps (settled by Ben, 2026-10-16): first-party catalog apps such as OpenSEO run as one shared Worker per app over multi-tenant Postgres (the hosted research's platform tier), and third-party apps run one Worker per organization on Workers for Platforms (dispatch tier). Google accounts and grants are per organization on both. A platform-tier Worker learns the organization from the identity token, or for background work asserts it with `x-everyapp-org` and the gateway checks that organization's install row (research H17a, [docs/research/2026-10-hosted-platform](https://github.com/every-app/private-every-app/blob/5ef30882/docs/research/2026-10-hosted-platform/README.md) on `research/hosted-platform`, PR #299). The earlier "about 200 organizations under the 500-Workers limit" framing is withdrawn. Built without install rows (#310): the gateway accepts `x-everyapp-org` from a shared Worker for an active organization with a live member. Settled and built (#310, 6b251463); the checkbox was the only thing open.
  - [ ] No multi-organization gate: every uploader we ship is trusted (the operator's CLI on self-host, the deploy service on the dispatch tier, our own code on the platform tier). A gateway-issued per-app caller credential stays a later hardening for self-host gateways where several people deploy for several organizations.
  - [ ] Names follow the hosted research so both designs read the same: manifest key `capabilities`, table `app_capability_grants` keyed by the app row, per-organization keys on `organization_data_keys`; one route name, settled in the contracts PR.
  - [ ] The gateway uses its own Google Cloud project and OAuth client, not OpenSEO's verified one, because adding redirect URIs to a verified client may trigger re-verification (research R39, unverified). Organizations reconnect Google once rather than having tokens migrated; the OpenSEO cutover is dropped and app.openseo.so stays as it is.
  - [ ] `INTEGRATIONS_KEKS` is a keyring (`active` plus keys by id) so it can be rotated; the rewrap cron and CLI runbook can follow the first release.
  - [ ] Removing a Google account revokes at Google only when no other organization holds the same identity, since Google revokes per person and client, not per token.
  - [ ] Owners and admins connect Google; members get an ask-an-admin link; agents can't create connect links.
  - [ ] `needs_reauth` emails the organization's owners and admins and banners the gateway home, because background work otherwise stops silently.
  - [ ] Rate limiting: start with Google's own 429 passed through; add per-organization and gateway-wide counters only if a shared client's quota becomes a problem.
  - [ ] Local dev: the SDK's direct-to-provider fallback goes away; the gateway-lite proxies `/api/integrations` from `.dev.vars`, and `everyapp dev google connect` stores a refresh token from one team Desktop-type client.
- [ ] **OpenSEO in Every App: how the two threads divide the work (reviewed 2026-10-16; Ben ruled the same day).** Settled by Ben: `app.openseo.so` keeps running as it is; on everyapp.com OpenSEO is a first-party app (one shared Worker over one multi-tenant Postgres database through Hyperdrive, identity from the gateway); organizations may also deploy forks of OpenSEO as third-party apps on Workers for Platforms with D1; the Google OAuth client and secret live in the gateway for both. The hosted thread ("Design a Multi-Tenant Hosted App Platform", PR #299; its research is numbered H0 to H42 and its chat plan of 27 PRs is written down nowhere else) had marked OpenSEO as not forkable onto the dispatch tier, so forks are new work for both threads. Defaults proposed, each to confirm:
  - [ ] One `AUTH_MODE=everyapp`, not one or two new modes. Identity is the same in both hosted shapes; what differs is three switches: `EVERY_APP_ORG_ID` set pins a fork to one organization, unset means the organization row comes from the token (first-party); `DATABASE_PROVIDER` picks D1 or Postgres as it does today; a job-runner flag with a derived capability list hides the job tools where no runner exists. The client reads `AUTH_MODE` at build time, so each extra mode would be a separate client build; per-tier differences reach the client as runtime data.
  - [ ] First-party OpenSEO deploys through `everyapp deploy` from its manifest (helper audit Worker, three Workflows, the five-minute cron), not through an OpenSEO Alchemy stage as the research's H18 assumed. The CLI gains a `hyperdrive` resource bound to the app and the helper, a Postgres migration step (drizzle-kit against a direct database URL the operator holds), `DATABASE_PROVIDER=postgres` as a text binding, and a placement hint near the database. The dispatch tier refuses `hyperdrive` (25 configs per account). Rendering stays Context.dev through the gateway rather than a Browser Rendering binding, unless audit quality suffers. As of 2026-10-16 the CLI side is #309 (merged), and the first deploy is open-seo-everyapp #2's Alchemy stage, registered with `everyapp register` (#307). (CLI surface removed 2026-10-24; see docs/hosted-vision.md)
  - [ ] The first-party database is a new PlanetScale Postgres, not app.openseo.so's, in whichever Cloudflare account runs the hosted gateway, since service bindings are same-account (research D7, still open; default a new dedicated account for everyapp.com).
  - [ ] Forks ship in tiers. Tier 0 when the dispatch tier opens: research tools, SAM chat and Google through the gateway, with `run_site_audit`, `run_rank_tracker`, `run_ai_visibility_check` and `complete_ai_research_setup` hidden by a capability flag and a manifest without Workflows, crons, KV or the audit helper (about 1 to 1.5 agent-days). Rank tracking once the gateway scheduler (research H15) and a job runner exist; AI visibility next (its research-setup result moves from Workflow output to the database); audits last, after a spike on a Durable Object binding between two user Workers decides single Worker or helper. Full parity is not a launch requirement.
  - [ ] A fork is code the organization deploys itself with the CLI through the hosted deploy API (research H12), under the research's rules for custom code: zero inherited grants with the admin's "approve as upstream" shortcut; the Paid plan once audits add a second Durable Object class and audit-length CPU. No one-click "my own OpenSEO instance": anyone who just wants OpenSEO uses the first-party app, so H17b's lazy installs and warm D1 pool are not needed.
  - [ ] Third-party background work: the gateway scheduler (research H15) first; then a gateway-hosted replay step runner (the gateway posts `{instanceId, params, steps}` to `/__everyapp/workflow/<name>/step`; the Worker replays and runs one new step or returns `sleepUntil`), built by the hosted thread after H15 with the protocol in the contracts PR. OpenSEO's side is the same for any runner: a replay-based step shim, a jobs facade over its 10 Workflow call sites, three rethrow edits, about 4 agent-days. No Hatchet or Temporal; Inngest only if we buy instead of build; Dynamic Workflows (open beta) watched, not planned on. First-party OpenSEO keeps its Workflows, cron and helper Worker as they are.
  - [ ] Fork egress: OpenSEO fetches arbitrary customer sites, which default-deny egress for third-party apps (research H16) would block even at Tier 0 (website research). Default: a `crawl` capability the organization's admin approves at deploy, allowing open http(s) egress except the metered provider hosts.
  - [ ] Launch gating for first-party: an invite-only preview as soon as identity, Postgres and background organization attribution work, with Google shown as not available yet and provider calls attributed and logged at the gateway. Public sign-up waits for verified Google and the org wallet (research H13, H18). OpenSEO's own Autumn credits stay off on everyapp.com in both shapes.
  - [ ] Google consent screen reads "Every App" for every app and fork (branding is per Google Cloud project and Google enforces one brand per project); scopes are Search Console and Analytics read-only only (OpenSEO reads Business Profile data through DataForSEO and submits URLs through IndexNow); the project is owned by Ben's Google account with everyapp.com DNS-verified in Search Console. Verification is submitted this week; it is the external critical path.
  - [ ] This branch lands first: rebase phase 0 onto `main` and merge it, then the OpenSEO `t3/every-app-mode` PR (51 uncommitted files), so the hosted thread builds on committed code. Contract names come from the research (`capabilities`, `app_capability_grants`, `x-everyapp-org`, `aud = workerName`); the step-runner protocol joins them. The hosted thread owns the dispatch tier, deployer, scheduler, wallet, grants and the step runner; runs the live checks this week (service-binding props from a namespaced script, cross-script Workflow binding, Durable Object alarm limits on Workers for Platforms, a Durable Object binding between two user Workers, the 32-invocation cap); and amends its README (first-party OpenSEO on Postgres through Hyperdrive and the CLI, the Google relay through app.openseo.so dropped, `forkable` per tier for OpenSEO, H25 multi-Worker apps if forks ship audits). Done 2026-10-16: phase 0 landed as #303 and #306 and the OpenSEO mode is open-seo-everyapp #1 (was open-seo #983, closed unmerged on 2026-10-17 with the move to the scratch repo); `aud = workerName` is #304 and `x-everyapp-org` #310; the grants table was deferred.
  - [ ] Todo, chef and workout become first-party multi-tenant apps too (the hosted thread's default; the three example apps were deleted from this repository on 2026-10-24, so the day-one catalog is decided when the hosted build resumes). The dispatch tier is needed for OpenSEO forks, not for anything first-party.
- [ ] **OpenSEO roles.** Gateway members are OpenSEO members: they research, but can't create or delete projects or connect Google. Cloudflare Access mode made everyone an owner.
- [ ] **OpenSEO's destructive tools.** Only its deletions (`delete_*`, `remove_*`) are destructive on the gateway, so saving keywords or reports stays usable. Its own MCP marks 11 tools destructive.

### Hosted build (2026-10-16)

Defaults the overnight build of the hosted platform shipped without Ben. Each item says what shipped; yes keeps it. [docs/hosted-platform-build.md](https://github.com/every-app/private-every-app/blob/5ef30882/docs/hosted-platform-build.md) §3 has the reason and the walk-back for each.

**Hosting shape**

- [ ] **First-party hosts share the control plane's cookie.** First-party apps live at `<app>.app.everyapp.com` on one session cookie for `app.everyapp.com`; third-party apps live on `everyapp.dev` with a session per host (#314).
- [ ] **The host never picks the organization.** One hostname per first-party app; a person works in their session's organization (their personal one, else the last active, else the first joined; the switcher changes it).
- [ ] **The install organization is the platform organization**, always named Every App whatever the claimant types; it owns every shared app, and first-party Workers' binding props name it.
- [ ] **The hosted gateway activates first-party apps on their first request**, as self-host does.
- [ ] **A missing or mistyped `GATEWAY_DEPLOYMENT_MODE` fails closed in both modes**: a self-hosted gateway that loses it refuses the setup claim, Access sign-in, dynamic registration, app activation and account actions until someone sets it.
- [ ] **The hosted gateway ignores and logs `CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_*` and `EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_IN`** rather than refusing to start.
- [ ] **The identity audience is the Worker name for every app**, first-party and self-hosted included, with no compatibility window: an app not built with `everyAppWorker()` answers 500 until it sets `EVERYAPP_AUDIENCE`, and apps refuse identities between a gateway upgrade and their own redeploy.
- [ ] **A browser carrying two session cookies is locked out until it clears cookies** (a 401 with a hint), rather than treated as signed out.
- [ ] **`APP_BASE_DOMAIN` stays the gateway's host on hosted**, so the mobile shell's copy of the session cookie matches the server's and it never holds two.
- [ ] **Redirect URIs on the platform's domains are refused at authorize, consent and the token endpoint**, not at dynamic client registration.
- [ ] **Deferred**: publishing the SDK and CLI, the agent, organization deletion and export, legal pages, and email bounce handling.

**Sign-up and organizations**

- [ ] **Open sign-up with verified email and daily mail budgets**, no Turnstile or social sign-in yet; invited sign-ups verify too, and verification links last 24 hours.
- [ ] **The preview launches invite-only** (`HOSTED_OPEN_SIGNUP=false`, as the hosted-install runbook says).
- [ ] **Exactly one personal organization per verified account**; growth by invitation; no self-serve creation or deletion.
- [ ] **A personal organization is named after the person** ("Ada Lovelace", else the email's local part), not "Ada's workspace".
- [ ] **An invitee whose invitation expired before they joined has an account and no organization**, and gets no personal one at the next sign-in.
- [ ] **Mail budgets**: hosted 5 per address per kind per day and 2,000 verification, 1,000 reset and 2,000 invitation emails per gateway; self-hosted 10 and 1,000.
- [ ] **Self-hosted people in several organizations start in their last active one**, not the organization picker.
- [ ] **On hosted, Delete removes a person from the organization and keeps their account**; admins can't deactivate accounts or reset passwords.
- [ ] **A member of two organizations keeps one MCP connection per organization** for the same AI client, rather than the newest replacing all.
- [ ] **A suspension applies to the organization acted under**: a member of a suspended organization still opens their other organizations' apps.
- [ ] **A suspension takes up to 30 seconds** to stop a shared app's background work (the caches' window).

**Shared apps and attribution**

- [ ] **No install rows**: every organization gets every shared app, and an organization can't hide or cap one.
- [ ] **Other organizations see shared apps read-only in Admin → Apps** rather than not at all.
- [ ] **An API token may deploy, register and delete shared apps** as the account's own authority (CI), alongside the platform organization's owners and admins. (CLI surface removed 2026-10-24; see docs/hosted-vision.md)
- [ ] **Externally deployed first-party Workers take the registry's names** (`every-everyapp-<app>`), not names of their own.
- [x] **Chef is in the day-one catalog.** Superseded on 2026-10-24: Chef was deleted in the simplify stage 1; the catalog is decided when the hosted build resumes.
- [ ] **Background work counts only for an active organization with at least one member who isn't banned.**
- [ ] **Pending and unverified members count as live** for background attribution.
- [ ] **A capability's status is answered to a shared Worker without naming an organization.**

**Capabilities and keys**

- [ ] **Keys at the gateway, metering later.** The DataForSEO, Context.dev, OpenRouter and OpenAI keys live only at the gateway; OpenSEO's Autumn is off in both Every App shapes; nothing is metered until the wallet; public sign-up waits for verified Google and the wallet (also the other thread's default 2).
- [ ] **DataForSEO's account-wide endpoints are refused for every app**, first-party OpenSEO included.
- [ ] **An organization's own app may call without an identity**: the call is served and attributed to the app's organization, with no `background` opt-in in the manifest.
- [ ] **`integrationFetch` and `integrationStatus` keep their names** (not `capabilityFetch`).
- [ ] **`read` is the only access level DataForSEO and Context.dev offer.**
- [x] **The AI proxy's own refusals carry no `x-everyapp-error`** until the AI providers fold into the capability registry. Reversed on 2026-10-17 (#322): they carry it, with OpenAI's error body ("Ports of Postiz and agentic-inbox" below).
- [ ] **Helper Workers share all of the app's D1 and KV and call the gateway as the app**, with no narrower grant.
- [ ] **Helper Workers get the app's secrets by default.**

**OpenSEO**

- [ ] **OpenSEO's own MCP, API keys, team UI, invitations, ownership transfer and Google connect are off in `everyapp` mode** until the gateway provides them.
- [ ] **Every organization gets paid-size audits (10,000 pages) but two running at a time**, and the plan gates the gateway can meter stay open during the preview.
- [ ] **Scheduled rank checks and AI visibility run by default in the first-party shape** (open-seo #990, reversing #983); `FIRST_PARTY_SCHEDULED_CHECKS=off` holds them.
- [ ] **A `[research-spend]` log line is the per-organization record of research cost** until the wallet.
- [ ] **Gateway organizations are stored as `Organization <first 8 characters of the id>`** (OpenSEO shows no organization names in `everyapp` mode).
- [ ] **Forks keep their data in the single shared-workspace organization**, not under the organization's own id.
- [ ] **"Sign out on Every App" opens the gateway's home**, where you sign out, rather than signing out in one click.
- [ ] **A SAM chat closes 15 minutes after it connects** (open-seo #990) and reconnects through the gateway, which re-checks the member; there is no per-turn membership check.
- [ ] **OpenSEO attributes gateway calls through two async-context scopes** (the request being served, the organization named) rather than a `for` argument on its ~40 fetchers; a path that names nobody throws before the call leaves (open-seo #990).
- [ ] **Request paths forward the person's identity** rather than always sending `x-everyapp-org`.
- [ ] **Inside a request, a call naming another organization is refused.**
- [ ] **A request still working within 15 seconds of its identity's 120-second expiry continues as background work** for its own organization.
- [ ] **SAM resolves its organization on each model call**, so a missing binding fails the turn when the model calls out rather than when it is built.
- [ ] **The platform organization "Every App" has the slug `every-app` and the key `everyapp`**, so OpenSEO's Worker is `every-everyapp-openseo`; `EVERYAPP_WORKER_NAME` overrides the name otherwise, rather than editing a constant.
- [ ] **The audit Worker's gateway binding names the app Worker** as its `workerName`.
- [ ] **The `everyapp` stage keeps its KV, R2, Workers and Hyperdrive on `alchemy destroy`**, like production.
- [ ] **A separate `.env.everyapp` and `pnpm deploy:everyapp:first-party`** rather than a flag on `deploy:postgres`.
- [ ] **Hyperdrive keeps production's 30-connection origin limit** for the new PlanetScale database.

**Third-party tier**

- [ ] **Third-party deploys stay off until the launch gate in docs/hosted-platform-build.md §1 passes, and opening them is a hand edit**: a hosted gateway with `THIRD_PARTY_DEPLOYS` exactly `on`, which `secret set` refuses to set (#311).
- [ ] **A deploy key is a personal access token whose only scope is `deploy`**, owners and admins only, 30 days by default (#311).
- [ ] **A deploy key opens no app and no `/mcp`**, even its owner's.
- [ ] **`GET /api/v1/deploy-key` tells the CLI the key's organization** (a route beyond the spec's three).
- [ ] **Registering a third-party app is a separate call before its build**; deploying an unregistered app answers 404.
- [ ] **A deploy runs inside its POST** (no queue or Durable Object): the finished job under 20 seconds, else `running` for the CLI to poll for up to 6 minutes; one running job per app; a job past its 5-minute deadline is abandoned.
- [ ] **Third-party apps may use up to 2 Durable Object classes and static assets**, while KV, Workflows, helper Workers and crons are refused and compatibility flags are limited to `nodejs_compat`, `nodejs_compat_v2`, `nodejs_als` and `global_fetch_strictly_public`.
- [ ] **A third-party Worker gets only `GATEWAY_URL` and `EVERY_APP_ORG_ID`, as plain text**; any other secret or variable is refused until a secrets API exists.
- [ ] **A third-party app's D1 is recorded in the CLI's resource ledger**, not a new `apps` column.
- [ ] **Without `THIRD_PARTY_DOMAIN` a deploy is accepted with no hostname**; a later deploy names it.
- [ ] **Third-party bundles are built with the sentinel Worker name `every-hosted--sentinel`**, and the deployer writes the real names.
- [ ] **An app's tier never changes** (`APP_TIER_MISMATCH`), shared and dispatch included.
- [ ] **`EVERYAPP_DEPLOY_TOKEN` switches `everyapp deploy` to the hosted deploy API** (renamed from `EVERYAPP_TOKEN` in #311's fix round, which the docs use for the MCP bearer). (CLI surface removed 2026-10-24; see docs/hosted-vision.md)
- [ ] **`CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN` lives in the public gateway Worker for the preview**, not in a separate deployer Worker as the research preferred.
- [ ] **`APP_BASE_DOMAIN` is always set on a hosted gateway**, so #313's domain check runs (it is skipped when unset).
- [ ] **A registration that fails after its deploy job started leaves a failed `deploy_jobs` row** as the record of the attempt (#313).
- [ ] **The hosted pipeline runs every static check, creates the D1, uploads assets and builds the upload's metadata before migrating**, as the self-host deploy does (#313).
- [ ] **Host sessions last 24 hours, with a silent re-handoff** when one ends (#314); revocation is immediate because every request re-reads the gateway's session, membership and access.
- [ ] **Personal access tokens work on third-party hosts** (`*.everyapp.dev`) under the first-party rules.
- [ ] **`everyapp.dev` goes on the Public Suffix List.**
- [ ] **The handoff page posts itself** rather than asking "Continue to <app>?".
- [ ] **The host-session key is derived from `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`** (HKDF), so rotating that secret ends every host session; no separate secret.
- [ ] **Third-party hosts are served by one hand-made Workers route and a wildcard DNS record**, not per-app Custom Domains, until the CLI's hosted install manages them.
- [ ] **A dispatched app has no internet egress** until the manifest gains an `egress` list.
- [ ] **Third-party apps start at 5 seconds of CPU and 200 subrequests per invocation**, OpenSEO forks included.
- [ ] **The hosted deployer authenticates with an API token from our own Cloudflare account** (no OAuth).
- [ ] **Namespaced scripts are tagged `org=<id>` and `app=<id>`**, so deleting an organization's Workers is one delete-by-tag.
- [ ] **A failed create or SQL call is reported, not retried** (it may already have been applied).

**Deploy toolchain**

- [ ] **A helper Worker the manifest dropped keeps running**, with a warning, until `delete --purge`.
- [ ] **The deploy reads the PlanetScale URL from the app's `.everyapp/secrets.env`.**
- [ ] **One URL serves migrations and Hyperdrive** for the first-party OpenSEO, split later with `HYPERDRIVE_ORIGIN_URL` if needed.
- [ ] **OpenSEO's Workers use targeted placement in the database's region**, not smart placement (#309; open-seo #984 uses smart placement until OpenSEO moves to `everyapp deploy`).
- [ ] **A Hyperdrive config binds to every Worker of the app by default.**
- [ ] **A password-only rotation needs a manual `cf hyperdrive update`** until the deploy can detect it.
- [ ] **The CLI's tests import the gateway's cf-rest client by path** (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/cf-rest`, from `packages/cli/src/test/fakeCloudflareApi.test.ts`) until the deployer lands.

**The other thread's unconfirmed defaults** (handoff §5; the fuller text is in "OpenSEO in Every App" above; defaults 2 and 7 are under "Capabilities and keys" and "Deploy toolchain")

- [ ] **One `AUTH_MODE=everyapp` with switches**, not one or two new modes (built that way in open-seo #983).
- [ ] **Forks ship in tiers**: research tools, SAM and Google first; rank tracking with the scheduler and step runner; AI visibility next; audits last.
- [ ] **A fork is code the organization deploys itself** through the hosted deploy API, with zero inherited grants; no one-click "my own OpenSEO".
- [ ] **Google's consent screen says "Every App"** for every app and fork; the project is owned by Ben's Google account; verification is submitted this week.
- [ ] **A new dedicated Cloudflare account for everyapp.com.** The build assumed the existing account; decide before the first live provisioning. The gateway, the first-party Workers and OpenSEO's Hyperdrive config must share one account.

### Ports of Postiz and agentic-inbox (2026-10-17)

Postiz is ported but not part of the launch (Ben, 2026-10-23); its entries stay as the record of what the platform does for a two-Worker app. Platform defaults the ports shipped with, in #322 to #327: Postiz as two Workers (its frontend and a NestJS helper) and agentic-inbox as one. Postiz's frontend, a vinext build when these shipped, is a TanStack Start host since 2026-10-18 (round 2's lane S1; the verdict and the measurements are in the port's `docs/every-app/TANSTACK-START-POC.md` and `docs/every-app/BOOT.md`); the helper is unchanged. Each entry says what shipped; where a PR changed the ports plan's default, the entry names the plan's choice. The apps' own defaults are in their ports' `TODO.md`.

**Workers AI through the gateway (#322)**

- [ ] **Workers AI is a gateway AI provider over REST** (`workers-ai`, `WORKERS_AI_API_TOKEN` + `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`; the OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/embeddings` and `/v1/responses` only), not an `ai` binding on apps (dead under `everyapp dev`, needs a login per developer, no attribution) and not a gateway-side `AI` binding with an RPC. Alternative: RPC over a gateway `AI` binding for non-chat tasks (vision, speech, images) when a port needs them.
- [ ] **The gateway normalises Workers AI's SSE** (numeric `delta.content` -> string) because vLLM-backed models emit numeric tokens as JSON numbers; re-check and remove after Cloudflare fixes it. One shared function (`normalizeWorkersAiResponse` in `@every-app/sdk/internal`) serves the gateway and the SDK's dev fallback, on `/v1/chat/completions` only; it fixes whole JSON bodies (`message.content`) and numeric `reasoning_content`/`reasoning` too. Alternative: every consumer normalises in its own fetch wrapper.
- [ ] **Provider base paths are joined, never assigned**, in `buildUpstreamUrl` and the SDK's direct fallback, through `joinProviderUrl`, which refuses a path that would leave the base; a test pins the full account-scoped URL.
- [ ] **The AI proxy's refusals carry `x-everyapp-error` and an OpenAI-shaped error body**, `{ error: { message, code, type: "gateway_refusal" } }`, with `code` and `message` at the top level too. The AppGateway entrypoint's refusals on `/api/ai/*` take the same shape, so a missing `providers` entry (`provider_not_allowed`) reaches ai-sdk with a message; both 400s are `ai_request_invalid`; capability routes keep `{ error, code, message }`. Reverses the hosted build's "no `x-everyapp-error` until the fold". Alternative: one refusal body shape for every gateway route (redesign #10).
- [ ] **`api.cloudflare.com` is a reserved provider host** for dispatched apps (the whole host). Alternative: path-level egress rules if an app ever needs another Cloudflare API.
- [ ] **The Workers AI token is made from the "Workers AI" template (Read and Edit)**, which Cloudflare's REST docs require for a custom token (the plan said Read). Its account is the gateway's install `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, not settable separately.

**Outbound email (#325)**

- [ ] **Outbound mail is a gateway `service` capability (`email`, access `write`)**: route `POST /api/integrations/email/send`, SDK `sendEmail()` that throws `GatewayRefusalError`. Apps get no `send-email` binding (the policy keeps refusing it). Alternative: allow `send-email` on apps only with `allowedSenderAddresses` matching the manifest's senders (no budgets, no attribution).
- [x] **The default From is the gateway's sender; app senders were `capabilities.email.senders` until 2026-10-19** (`*@<domain>` for exactly that domain, or one address; lowercase, at most 20), each domain onboarded to Email Sending in the gateway's account. `GET /api/integrations/email` echoes the effective senders and the gateway's sender. A hosted gateway refuses every app sender in v1. Superseded: senders are the installation's, set with `everyapp email set --sender` ("Email configuration on the gateway" below).
- [ ] **`app` mail budgets are per address (100 a day self-hosted, 20 hosted) and per gateway (5,000), counted once per distinct recipient; there is no per-organization budget** (#316). A message any of whose recipients is spent is refused whole, with `Retry-After` to UTC midnight. Alternative: per-organization settings when the wallet exists.
- [ ] **Mail under an agent's identity needs `mcp:write`** (`integration_read_only` otherwise).
- [ ] **Attachments go through both transports as base64.** The plan sent them on the binding only (REST answering `email_attachments_unsupported`), but Email Sending's REST `send` takes `attachments` (base64 `content`, `disposition`, `content_id`) and the binding takes base64 strings too (the local simulator can't serialize `ArrayBuffer`s). Alternative: refuse them on REST until a live send proves it.
- [ ] **Headers that set the sender, recipients, subject, date, MIME structure or DKIM are refused** before sending (`From`, `Sender`, `To`, `Cc`, `Bcc`, `Reply-To`, `Subject`, `Return-Path`, `Date`, `MIME-Version`, `Content-Type`, `Content-Transfer-Encoding`, `DKIM-Signature`); the rest pass through to Email Sending, at most 20, one line each. Unknown message fields are refused too.
- [ ] **Email Sending's validation codes (`E_VALIDATION_ERROR`, `E_HEADER_*`, `E_TOO_MANY_*`, `E_CONTENT_TOO_LARGE`, REST `10001`/`10200`/`10202`) answer 400 `email_invalid_message`**, beside the plan's `email_sender_not_verified`, `email_provider_limited` (also REST `10004`) and `email_send_failed`. A budget the gateway can't read answers 503 `email_budget_unavailable`.
- [ ] **A binding send reports `delivered`; a REST send reports `queued` when any recipient is queued.** A REST send fails only when every recipient bounced.
- [ ] **Dev email is checked like production, then printed** (`{ status: "logged" }`), unless `.dev.vars` sets `EMAIL_REST_API_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` and `EMAIL_FROM`, which send for real from the developer's account. In dev `.dev.vars`' `EVERYAPP_EMAIL_SENDERS` stands in for the installation's senders (since 2026-10-19; `everyAppWorker()` passed the manifest's before); no dev-only `send-email` binding is emitted.

**Pluggable email providers (#329)**

- [ ] **The email provider is operator-level: one per gateway**, Cloudflare Email Sending or Resend, serving the gateway's own mail and every app's (`src/server/email/providers`; `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_PROVIDER` are gateway secrets `everyapp secret set` sets). Alternative: a per-app or per-message choice (a manifest naming a provider, or per-organization domains on different providers).
- [ ] **Selection: `EMAIL_PROVIDER` (`cloudflare` or `resend`, trimmed, any case) wins and must be set up; without it the first provider, Cloudflare then Resend, whose settings are set (the REST pair, `RESEND_API_KEY`), else Cloudflare's `EMAIL` binding.** The brief had "the first configured provider", the binding counting as configured; every gateway Worker declares `EMAIL`, so that rule would never pick Resend without `EMAIL_PROVIDER`. An `EMAIL_PROVIDER` that isn't a provider, or isn't set up, leaves the gateway unable to send (invitations hand out links, verified sign-up refuses, apps get 503 `integration_not_configured`) rather than falling back. One rule (`chooseEmailProvider` in `@every-app/sdk/internal`) serves the gateway and the SDK's dev status. Alternative: the brief's literal order (operators set `EMAIL_PROVIDER=resend`), or fall back to the automatic choice when `EMAIL_PROVIDER` is unusable.
- [ ] **Resend's errors map onto the capability's existing codes**: a 403 or `validation_error` naming a domain -> `E_SENDER_NOT_VERIFIED` (422 `email_sender_not_verified`), 429 or `rate_limit_exceeded`/`daily_quota_exceeded`/`monthly_quota_exceeded` -> `E_RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (429 `email_provider_limited`), other 400s and 422s -> `E_VALIDATION_ERROR` (400 `email_invalid_message`); a refused key (401, the `*_api_key` names, an "API key" message) and anything else stay `EMAIL_SEND_FAILED` with Resend's error name as `providerCode` (502 `email_send_failed`). `emailProviderRefusal()` takes the provider for its wording only (Resend's names https://resend.com/domains); no new refusal codes. The logged message carries Resend's text with addresses redacted. Alternative: Resend-specific refusal codes.
- [ ] **No per-provider budgets**: the daily mail budgets are counted before the provider is called, whichever sends. Alternative: budgets per provider (Resend's own daily quota mirrored).
- [ ] **A Resend send reports `delivered` and sends no `Idempotency-Key`**: Resend answers an id, not per-recipient queues, and `sendEmail()` has no stable message key yet. Alternative: derive a key from the message (a retried send within 24 hours would then not go twice).
- [ ] **"No provider" keeps the error code `EMAIL_BINDING_UNAVAILABLE`** (the auth tests and the capability's mapping read it); its message names both providers. Alternative: rename it `EMAIL_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`.
- [ ] **In local dev the status names the provider `.dev.vars` selects, but only Cloudflare's REST API sends from dev**; with `RESEND_API_KEY` alone the SDK's `sendEmail()` still prints. Alternative: send through Resend in dev too (follow-up below).

**Inbound email (#327)**

- [ ] **Inbound mail: the `email` trigger stays refused; `everyapp deploy` creates Email Routing rules from the installation's inbound addresses (`everyapp email set --inbound`, since 2026-10-19; the manifest's `email.inbound` list before)** (`*@<apex>` the zone's catch-all, any other address a literal rule, both `actions: [{ type: "worker", value: [<worker>] }]` named `everyapp:<worker>`), recorded in the ownership ledger as `email-route` rows, deleted when the list drops them and by `delete --purge`. The manual catch-all stays documented for apps whose addresses aren't set. Apps on the dispatch tier take no inbound addresses (hosted organizations get no inbound domains in this series). Alternative: allow the trigger and let cf's bundled wrangler reconcile the Worker's `addresses`.
- [ ] **Email Routing is turned on for a domain only with a person's yes at a terminal or `--enable-email-routing`**; unattended, the deploy stops with `EMAIL_ROUTING_DISABLED` (its rerun carries the flag, marked as a takeover) and names the manual `npx cf email-routing enable` commands. It turns routing on only for the domains the inbound addresses name (`--name <domain>`), never a zone's apex for a manifest that lists only subdomain addresses. A domain counts as routed when the zone's setting is on (apex) or Cloudflare's `*.mx.cloudflare.net` MX records are at the subdomain. Alternative: always turn the apex on first.
- [ ] **The checks run before anything is created or uploaded, the writes after the upload.** The plan put the whole step after the upload; finding zones, refusing taken addresses and catch-alls and asking about routing now happen first, so a refused address leaves the live version alone. Rules are still made after the upload, since a rule may only name a Worker that exists, and before the registry records the version. Alternative: the whole step after the upload, as planned.
- [ ] **Catch-alls on apex zones only is checked by the deploy against the account's zones** (`EMAIL_CATCH_ALL_NOT_APEX`), not by the manifest schema, which can't tell a zone apex from a subdomain (`example.co.uk`, subdomain zones). `everyapp email set` checks the address pattern, lowercase, uniqueness, at most 50 addresses and Email Routing's 90 characters (the manifest schema did, before 2026-10-19). Alternative: a public-suffix heuristic in the schema.
- [ ] **A rule is adopted when it is named `everyapp:<worker>`, recorded as the app's, or already sends the address to the app's Worker** (the documented manual step); any other rule on the address, enabled or not, refuses with `EMAIL_ADDRESS_TAKEN`, and so does an address another app recorded. A catch-all that is on and does anything but drop, and isn't the app's, refuses with `EMAIL_CATCH_ALL_TAKEN`. Nothing is taken over. Alternative: a takeover flag.
- [ ] **An `email-route` row records its zone**: `<zone id>/<rule id>` for a rule, `catch_all:<zone id>` for a catch-all (the plan had the rule id alone), so deleting a route or checking it in `status` needs no zone lookup, and a zone added later for a subdomain can't redirect it. A row claimed before its create (`resource_id` null) is found again by name and address.
- [ ] **A dropped catch-all goes back to `{ enabled: false, actions: [{ type: "drop" }] }` only while it is still the app's**; one someone changed since is left as it is and forgotten.
- [ ] **`everyapp status` reads each recorded route live** and says `EMAIL_ROUTE_MISSING` (run `deploy`) when its rule is gone, off or sends mail elsewhere, or routing is off for its domain; when cf can't read them it warns `EMAIL_ROUTES_UNKNOWN` and the rest of the report stands. `delete` without `--purge` keeps the routes, like the Worker's crons and Workflows.

**Social networks' OAuth clients (#326)**

- [ ] **`oauth_client` is a capability kind with 16 entries** (`linkedin`, `facebook`, `instagram`, `threads`, `youtube`, `tiktok`, `tiktok-business`, `reddit`, `pinterest`, `discord`, `slack`, `mastodon`, `kick`, `twitch`, `tumblr`, `dribbble`; 33 gateway names with `MASTODON_URL`): the gateway completes token endpoints only, with four inject rules (form, basic, query, json), `access: "write"`, agent identities refused even with `mcp:write`, `clientId` in the status, redirect URIs on the app's host. `youtube` serves YouTube and Google Business Profile apart from the planned Google client. No `everyapp capability setup` command: the docs table is generated from the registry. Alternative: per-organization clients with the admin pages.
- [ ] **Every rule removes the client credentials an app sends, wherever it puts them**: `Authorization`, and `client_id`/`client_secret` (and the rule's names for them, in any case) in the query string and the body, for `basic` and `query` endpoints too, whose bodies the plan streamed untouched. Token request bodies are therefore buffered (64 KiB) and must be form-encoded or JSON (`integration_body_invalid` otherwise). The query string keeps the app's other fields byte for byte; bodies are re-serialised. Alternative: stream `basic`/`query` bodies untouched.
- [ ] **The outbound Worker reserves the 16 clients' token endpoints, not their hosts** (`reserved_endpoint`, matched however the path is spelled): ten of the hosts (`graph.facebook.com`, `slack.com`, `discord.com`, `api.pinterest.com`, `api.tumblr.com`, `graph.instagram.com`, `graph.threads.net`, `open.tiktokapis.com`, `business-api.tiktok.com`, and `dribbble.com` over `api.dribbble.com`) also serve the APIs a dispatched app calls with its users' tokens. Alternative: reserve the hosts and proxy those APIs too.
- [ ] **`MASTODON_URL` defaults to `https://mastodon.social`** and must be an https origin (a bare host is taken as https); a set but invalid value answers `integration_not_configured`. The status carries the instance as `origin`, since the app needs it for the authorize page and the API. Alternative: require `MASTODON_URL`.
- [ ] **The registry entries carry the operator's checklist**: `console`, `authorize.url` and `scopes` (Postiz's), `api` hosts, and `redirectPaths` (a list: LinkedIn, Meta and Google each take two callbacks on one client). Since the scope-sets work ("OAuth scopes (2026-10-24)" above) a client with a scope policy derives `authorize.scopes` from its sets, and the gateway enforces them at the token endpoint.
- [ ] **Logs name a token call's grant type from a known list** (`authorization_code`, `refresh_token`, `client_credentials`, Meta's three exchanges, token exchange; `other` for anything else), never the query, body or answer; an unreachable network logs the error's name only, since a message may quote a URL carrying the query rule's secret.
- [ ] **X stays an app secret** (OAuth 1.0a needs the user's token secret); Discord bot, Telegram, MeWe, Neynar, short links and media vendors stay app secrets until write-capable `api_key` capabilities exist (follow-up).

**The SDK for ported apps and Node frameworks (#323)**

- [ ] **`compatibilityFlags` is an SDK option; `global_fetch_strictly_public` is not yet the default.** `everyAppWorker()` and `everyAppAuxiliaryWorker()` append the app's flags to `nodejs_compat`, each once; both ports pass `global_fetch_strictly_public`. No per-app compatibility date change (the manifest's `compatibilityDate` stays the only one). Alternative: turn it on for every app (redesign #15).
- [ ] **A Durable Object the app's Worker defines names that Worker by a reference**, `{ name, compatibilityDate, entrypoint }`, when the entrypoint is a `cf-worker` module import, so `cf workers types` gives `DurableObjectNamespace<Class>` (todo-app: `USER_SYNC: DurableObjectNamespace<UserSyncDO>`); cf resolves it to the name, so the Build Output is unchanged. A small reference object rather than the Worker definition itself, so the config has no cycle. Classes a helper Worker defines, and any class behind a path entrypoint, stay untyped, with an `Omit<Env, …>` retype in the README (a plain cast is refused). Alternative: reference the helper's definition too, once the Vite plugin resolves references in `auxiliaryWorkers`.
- [ ] **`everyApp()` passes the runtime's `ExecutionContext`** (`EveryAppHandler<TEnv, TCtx extends ExecutionContext = ExecutionContext>`), so Hono and React Router take it without casts; tests that call `fetch` themselves cast a `{ waitUntil }` stub. Alternative: keep accepting any `{ waitUntil }` and give the handler the narrow type.
- [ ] **`nodeServerHandler()` adds no identity of its own.** The Node app gets the request with `x-everyapp-identity` (already verified) and re-verifies it with `getEveryAppUser(new Request(url, { headers }), process.env)` when it needs the user. Alternative: the handler sets a decoded-user header, or runs the bridge inside an `AsyncLocalStorage` the SDK exposes.
- [ ] **`nodeServerHandler()` loads `cloudflare:node` on the first request**, not at import, so `@every-app/sdk/server` still loads under Node test runners and on older compatibility dates. It drops `Set-Cookie` and passes a body Node already encoded through with `encodeBody: "manual"` (the bridge alone double-encodes it).
- [ ] **Node-framework ports must minify the Worker and keep module scope free of work.** The README recipe says so and names the heap gate (at most about 64 MB used locally, through miniflare's inspector) as the budget that binds; `wrangler check startup` is a smoke test only.
- [ ] **A Durable Object export backed by a `container` is refused** by the deploy policy. Queues, the Images binding and the rate-limit binding stay refused.

**Resources and migrations (#324)**

- [ ] **Prisma on Postgres runs `prisma migrate deploy` through `migrations.engine: "prisma"`** with the drift check skipped (`SCHEMA_CHECK_SKIPPED`: Prisma needs a shadow database); D1 stays `d1-sql` with Prisma's generated SQL plus `--> statement-breakpoint` markers. The URL reaches Prisma only as `POSTGRES_DATABASE_URL`, which the app's `prisma.config.ts` reads. Alternative: a shadow-database drift check when a second URL is configured.
- [ ] **On the prisma engine `migrations.dir` is optional.** Prisma finds its migrations through its config; the deploy skips the migration only when a `dir` the manifest names is missing. Alternative: require `dir`.
- [ ] **Hyperdrive configs the CLI creates have caching disabled** (`caching: "off"` by default; `"default"` opts back in). The deploy turns caching back to the manifest's setting on recorded and adopted configs, and resends `disabled: true` with every update to an "off" config. Alternative: Cloudflare's default, with read-after-write documented.
- [ ] **`resources.r2` lands as redesign #6 PR A only**: names assigned at registration, bindings emitted on the app and every helper, policy membership. Provisioning, ledger rows and `--purge` stay PR B (cf's prebuilt deploy creates the bucket; unverified live). A hand-written `bindings.r2({ name: worker.name })` keeps working while the manifest declares no buckets. Alternative: membership-only policy for every app.
- [ ] **`R2` is the bucket named after the Worker; other bindings are `<worker>--<binding>`**, refused at registration when Cloudflare would refuse the name. `DB`/`KV` keep their special meaning for D1 and KV only.
- [ ] **The dispatch tier refuses `resources.r2`** (`manifestTierProblems`) until the hosted deployer accepts R2 bindings.

**Across the ports**

- [ ] **No platform support for OpenNext/non-Vite Build Outputs**; OpenNext emits no Build Output, and Postiz's OpenNext build was over the 64 MiB limit. Postiz's frontend built with vinext under Vite 8 until 2026-10-18; since then it is a TanStack Start host, a Vite build like the gateway's and OpenSEO's.
- [x] **Postiz's backend stays NestJS + Prisma and keeps merging upstream (Ben, 2026-10-18); step 2 of the TanStack verdict is off.** The Postiz port's `TODO.md` has the decision and what would reopen it ("Frontend: the TanStack Start host", the step 2 entry, added in a9c018c6 on its `everyapp` branch).
- [ ] **Helper Workers verify no identities**; apps hand them ids as headers/RPC arguments/Workflow params; the policy's "only the app's Worker may bind a helper" rule is what makes that safe. Alternative: identity keys on helpers (Postiz's helper reads the app's `x-postiz-identity` today).
- [ ] **Cookieless POSTs without `Origin`/`Sec-Fetch-Site` stay anonymous** (documented for curl in [docs/TESTING.md](docs/TESTING.md)): with no cookie there is nothing to forge, so a private route answers 401 `unauthenticated`, the challenge a programmatic client needs, and one carrying a cookie answers 403 `csrf_denied`. An app without `defineTools()` keeps having its own `/mcp` proxied.
- [ ] **The 171-statement Prisma init migration goes through the gateway's single `{ batch }` REST request** on the first live deploy (precedent: 35 statements; `everyapp deploy` applies it with `cf d1 migrations apply --remote`; `deployApp.ports.test.ts` applies a nine-statement stand-in against the fake account's SQLite, so the 171-statement size is unproven offline); fallback: split the init into several files. (The self-host half is proven: the init and two later migrations applied on Postiz's first `everyapp deploy` on the dogfood gateway on 2026-10-19. The `{ batch }` REST half is hosted-only and unrecorded, and Postiz is not launching.)
- [ ] **`docs/capabilities.md`'s overview and operator tables are written by hand; only the OAuth clients' tables are generated** from the registry (`capabilitiesDoc.test.ts`). Alternative: generate the operator table too, from `CAPABILITIES` and the AI proxy's providers.

**Tool snapshots (2026-10-19)**

- [ ] **The consent page and `/mcp` fetch the tool snapshots they are missing within the request**, all missing apps at once, waiting 3 s for up to 20 apps (logged as `mcp.catalog.sync` with the triggers `consent` and `first_use`); what finishes in time is in the answer, the rest finishes after it. On the consent page an app whose tools aren't known yet is selectable, marked "(tools load on first use)"; one that reported no tools stays greyed out; the decision accepts an unknown app. A failed snapshot is retried in the background every five minutes, never within a request; a serving snapshot whose newer version can't be fetched keeps serving and is retried after the answer. Activation and hosted deploys snapshot at once. A self-host redeploy is followed from the app host's next request in each isolate (within the 30 s hostname cache), since the CLI tells the gateway nothing. Not built: a deploy-time trigger from the CLI, an hourly reconcile in the cron.

### Email configuration on the gateway (2026-10-19)

The two installation facts the manifest carried, `capabilities.email.senders` and `email.inbound`'s addresses, moved to the app's registry row (#352, #353). Each entry says what shipped.

**App email configuration (#352, #353)**

- [ ] **The manifest declares, the installation configures.** `capabilities: { email: { access: "write" } }` says the app sends; `email: { inbound: true }` says its Worker exports `email()`. `everyapp email set --sender … --inbound …`, in the app's directory (or `--manifest <path>`) after its first deploy, writes `apps.email_senders` and `apps.email_inbound` (JSON `string[]`, null for none; migration `0002_app_email_config`) and routes the addresses at once. A release carries only the declarations, so an installer inherits no one's domain. The strict validator refuses an old `senders` list or `inbound` array by name (`senders moved out of the manifest`, `inbound is true … or left out`); registration reads a non-boolean `inbound` as undeclared (`.catch(undefined)`) so the live agentic-inbox row kept routing until its redeploy. Alternative: a satellite table keyed by (app row, organization); a strictly boolean lenient schema.
- [ ] **One list per installed app: a shared app has one sender list for every organization it serves.** `GET /api/integrations/email` answers before `admit()` attributes the call, so per-organization lists can't be echoed without reordering admission. Alternative: key by (app row, attributed organization) and attribute the status route.
- [ ] **A hosted gateway keeps refusing every app sender in v1** (`effectiveSenders` is empty when hosted; `email set --sender` on a hosted install refuses with `EMAIL_SENDERS_HOSTED`). Alternative: per-organization verified domains with the admin pages.
- [ ] **The gateway reads senders from `RegisteredApp.emailSenders`, cached with the row for up to 30 s**; a change made with `everyapp email set` reaches each isolate within that, and the CLI says "on the Gateway", never "live". Alternative: a D1 read per send.
- [ ] **`everyapp email set` replaces each list it names and keeps the other; `email unset senders|inbound` clears; `email show` prints; a bare `set` or `unset` refuses.** No `--yes` (nothing asks but Email Routing's takeover, `--enable-email-routing`), no add/remove verbs (20 and 50 entries at most). It runs in the app's directory like `secret set` (the manifest says what the app declares), with `--manifest <path>` for an app registered from a file or deployed from a release. Alternative: `email senders add|remove`; `--no-senders`/`--no-inbound` on `set`.
- [ ] **The stored list is the intent, the ledger what exists.** `email set` runs every check, then `lease.check()`, writes the row, `lease.check()`, then makes the rules under the app's lease (`app:<worker>`, purpose `app.email`); a reconcile that stops after the write (a rule that can't be made, the lease lost) warns `EMAIL_ROUTES_PARTIAL`, `status` reports `EMAIL_ROUTE_PENDING` (or `EMAIL_ROUTE_MISSING` for an address claimed before its rule), `email show` counts a claim with no rule as not routed, and `everyapp deploy` (or the command again) finishes; a rerun that makes or mends a rule says "goes to", not "already goes to". Addresses set for a `pending` app (Worker not uploaded), or for one whose deployed manifest doesn't declare inbound yet (`RegisteredAppInfo.declaresInbound`, from the stored manifest; the pre-move address list counts), are stored and checked and routed by its next deploy, never made for a Worker that isn't there or has no `email()`; `email show` says so for a pending app too (`deployed: false`). Alternative: rules first, row after; refuse `--inbound` until the Worker exists; run the plan's checks for a pending app at set time (its Email Routing confirm would ask for an enable the command then doesn't do).
- [ ] **`everyapp deploy` reconciles Email Routing from the stored inbound list**, removes the routes and keeps the list when the manifest no longer declares `inbound: true` (warning `EMAIL_INBOUND_UNDECLARED`), prints one line on the first deploy of an app that declares inbound with no addresses (`app.email` step, `skip`), and refuses `--enable-email-routing` on the hosted path (`FLAG_NOT_FOR_HOSTED_DEPLOY`) instead of ignoring it. Afterwards `status` reports the kept list as `EMAIL_INBOUND_UNDECLARED` (run `everyapp email unset inbound`, or deploy once the manifest declares it again; the Email line reads `receives nothing (… set, not declared)`) and `email show` names the clearing command, rather than a deploy that changes nothing. The deploy's hints (`email set --inbound <address>` (in its directory), `email unset inbound`) assume the app's directory on a release deploy too, where the command needs `--manifest <path>` to a checkout's everyapp.config.ts; the refusal texts say "in the app's directory" likewise. Alternative: clear a list the manifest no longer declares; keep ignoring the flag; release-aware hints.
- [ ] **The hosted deploy API, the deploy upload and the GitHub install refuse `email: { inbound: true }`** (`manifestProblems`, `BUNDLE_REFUSED`, before any row is written), so a dispatch app's declaration is refused rather than stored and ignored.
- [ ] **In dev, `.dev.vars` `EVERYAPP_EMAIL_SENDERS` (a comma list or a JSON array) stands in for the installation's senders; unset, dev refuses every `from`**, as a gateway with none set does, and the dev status reports that list. `everyAppWorker()` declares the binding from `.dev.vars` like the other dev keys and no longer reads the manifest. Alternative: no sender rule in dev.
- [ ] **One validator for both lists, `emailListProblems()` in `@every-app/sdk/internal`, with the manifest's two patterns kept as they were** (`EMAIL_SENDER_PATTERN`, `EMAIL_INBOUND_PATTERN`; limits 20, 50 and 90 characters). The perimeter validates no list any more; the CLI command is the only writer. Alternative: one pattern for both (the inbound regex takes hyphen-edged labels and letters-only TLDs).
- [ ] **A shared app's lists are the platform organization's owners' and admins' to change** (`writeAppEmail` checks as `deleteApp` does, `SHARED_APP_FORBIDDEN`).
- [ ] **`email set --sender` doesn't check that the domain is onboarded** to Email Sending (or verified in Resend); the provider refuses at send time (`email_sender_not_verified`). Alternative: `cf email-sending subdomains list` at set time, once a live account has shown its output.
- [ ] **The "to reply from …" hint stays in the `app.email` step message and in `email set`'s done message**, naming `everyapp email set --sender`, and is left out where that command refuses: a hosted Gateway, or an app that doesn't declare `capabilities.email` (`describeMailRoutes`' `sendersSettable`). Alternative: `data.next` only (#333).
- [ ] **A shared app's admin check runs at the write, after the plan's Email Routing confirm** (as `delete` confirms before its check): a non-admin at a terminal may be asked "Turn on Email Routing for <domain>?" and then refused `SHARED_APP_FORBIDDEN`, nothing changed. `deploy` refuses the same login before its plan. Alternative: check right after `readApp`, which needs the row's scope on `RegisteredAppInfo`.
- [ ] **A literal address under another app's catch-all isn't refused**: the ledger check matches names exactly and the foreign-rule check sees rules only, so `email set --inbound ceo@example.com` in one app's directory carves the address out of another app's `*@example.com` with no word to that app (Email Routing delivers the literal rule first; unchanged from #327, reachable without a deploy since #353). Alternative: warn or refuse naming the catch-all's app.
- [ ] **A sender or inbound column that isn't a JSON array is read as a comma or whitespace list** by the gateway, the CLI and the perimeter (one `parseEmailList` for the row and `.dev.vars`' `EVERYAPP_EMAIL_SENDERS`), while Admin → Apps reads such a column as none; only `everyapp email set` writes the columns, always JSON, so the two disagree only on a hand-edited row. Alternative: a strict row parser with the fallback for the dev variable only, or the admin page parsing the same way.
- [ ] **Admin → Apps shows an installation's senders and addresses read-only** (the app cell and the Edit dialog) and names `everyapp email set`. Alternative: an editing dialog for senders, once the gateway holds a Cloudflare token to reconcile Email Routing itself (inbound would stay CLI-only until then).
- [ ] **Tool list changes reach connected MCP clients without a reconnect (2026-10-19).** `/mcp` still keeps nothing between requests. 2026-07-28 clients (Claude Code 2.1.304 negotiates this era with the gateway) get `notifications/tools/list_changed` on the `subscriptions/listen` stream the ToolBus holds open: the caller is rechecked and the catalogs re-read every 30 s (about four D1 statements per open stream) and the stream ends gracefully after the surface's hold (30 minutes on the gateway, 10 on an app host since 2026-10-24; 10 on both before), which the client answers by opening a new one and refetching `tools/list`. 2025-era clients get an `Mcp-Session-Id` of `<uuid>.<tool list hash>` at `initialize`; a request on a session whose hash is no longer the list's is answered as an SSE stream, the notification before the result, and a `tools/list` on such a session gets HTTP 404 so the client initializes again (the spec's "server MAY terminate the session"; Claude Code's client keeps its initialize-time session id and ignores one sent later, the 2025 SDK adopts it). Each poll first rechecks the caller (the OAuth connection or the personal token, as a request would), so a revoked connection's stream ends at the next poll; a credential may hold 8 streams per isolate, more are refused in-band; since 2026-10-24 the stream also ends at the access token's expiry and survives up to three polls whose D1 read fails ("MCP surface (2026-10-24)" below). The app-host surface rechecks nothing (the perimeter authenticates per request; a held stream there only re-reads the snapshot). Decide: the 30 s poll and the 10-minute cap (`listenPollMs`, `listenMaxMs` on `ToolBusDeps`); the per-isolate cap of 8 (a global cap needs a counter outside the isolate); whether the app-host surface should hold streams and recheck too; and whether a stale session's `tools/list` should answer 404 rather than keep notifying on every call.

### MCP through Cloudflare Access (2026-10-20)
