- [ ] **MCP through Access (Managed OAuth).** With the outer ring configured, the gateway's `/mcp` takes a verified `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` (the header only, never the cookie; the gateway application's AUD only; a browser's `Origin` checked before anything is read for the person, since behind Managed OAuth Access vouches for a cookie-carrying browser request too, so `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` then extends to cookie-authenticated callers) as the caller, so an operator can turn on Access's Managed OAuth and bypass nothing on the gateway's host (`docs/runbooks/cloudflare-access.md`, "MCP through Access too"). Defaults: the Access identity must already be linked to an account by a sign-in in the browser, else `403 not_connected`, and `/mcp` never creates an account or membership, whatever the auto-join settings say; the caller gets write access to every app the member can use, like a browser session; the organization is the member's only one, else the active organization of their most recent live session, else `403 organization_unresolved`; no connection row is written, so Connected AI apps shows nothing and revocation happens at Access (or by deactivating); the audit row's credential kind is `access` with the email as id (the `mcp.tool_call` log line omits the email and keeps the user id), and apps see the actor `access:<email>`; a held `subscriptions/listen` stream re-resolves the person every poll; a personal token behind Managed OAuth should keep working with a service token from a Service Auth policy, whose assertion the gateway ignores and logs (`mcp.access_assertion_ignored`, reason `invalid`): not yet checked against a live Managed OAuth application. Alternative: auto-join from `/mcp` for the domains and `everyone` the browser path admits (rejected for now: linking happens where the person can see it); read-only by default with a scope the AI app asks for (Access has no per-application scopes to carry it).

### Cloudflare Access from the CLI (2026-10-23)

everyapp.team went behind Access by hand on 2026-10-23 (gateway application `66f98c9e…`, apps application `8f4956ad…`, policy `a19108d7…`, one-time PIN only, 720h sessions, the team's global session raised to 720h, HttpOnly and binding cookies on, MCP and each app's public routes bypassed), then `everyapp access on|off|show` was built and found it all unchanged. Defaults the command shipped with; yes keeps each.

- [ ] **Verbs.** `access on` (idempotent, like `email set`), `access off`, `access show`, in the CONFIGURE group. Alternative: `access setup`, or the `access enable` the redesign doc named.
- [ ] **Session.** New applications get `--session 720h` (the most Access allows: a PIN a month). The runbook's hand-made bodies said 24h; "Taking someone off" relied on it as a backstop, but deactivation in Every App is the real one. An existing application keeps its session unless `--session` is given.
- [ ] **The team's global session is never touched** unless `--team-session` is given (it applies to every Access application in the account); a new team gets `--team-session`, else `--session`. everyapp.team's was raised to 720h by hand, so its PIN isn't daily.
- [ ] **Sign-in methods** (changed 2026-10-25, Ben's ask: "make sure Cloudflare Access gets set up with sign in with Cloudflare if possible … but support both"). `access on` keeps both Sign in with Cloudflare (type `cloudflare`, created with `restrict_to_account_members: true` when the team has none; a team made through the API has none, checked live on the loop account) and the one-time PIN (created when none), and allows only those two on both applications (the review of #622: the team is account-wide and the Gateway trusts the asserted address for account linking, so the first build's "every provider of the team" was dropped), adding to an existing application's `allowed_idps` and never taking one off (everyapp.team's applications, made with the PIN alone, gain the Cloudflare provider on Ben's next `access on`); `auto_redirect_to_identity` only with one provider, so Access shows its chooser first. A generic `oidc` or `saml` provider and a `cloudflare` provider with Restrict to account members off are warned about (`ACCESS_IDP_UNVERIFIED_EMAILS`, `ACCESS_IDP_ANY_CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT`) and left off, their settings never changed; any other provider (Okta) is listed as the team's, `allowed: false`, for the operator to allow in the dashboard; a Cloudflare login whose email isn't a member gets `ACCESS_LOGIN_NOT_MEMBER`. Alternatives: the PIN alone (no chooser); every provider of the team. Follow-up: the PIN is created again whenever the team lacks it, so a team meant to sign in with an MFA provider alone gets it back on every run; a `--no-pin` flag (or an MFA-capable provider counting as the PIN's substitute) would be the fix.
- [x] **Setup offers Access** (2026-10-25; superseded 2026-10-26: Access is the default unattended too, and `update` keeps it in step; see "Access default + reconcile (2026-10-26)" below). Interactive `setup` (and a `deploy` that installs a Gateway) asks "Put Cloudflare Access in front of your Gateway?" right after the owner exists, Yes by default, on a self-hosted Gateway whose Worker lacks any of the three settings; `--yes`, `--json`, CI and `update` never ask (a new team's name is permanent and never picked unattended), and `next` keeps naming `access on` when it wasn't turned on. Yes runs `access on`'s own code (its done becomes the step `setup.access`); a failure there is the warning `ACCESS_SETUP_SKIPPED`, never a failed setup. What `access on` needs, measured: the Cloudflare login (cf's asks for the Access permissions) and, with no team yet, a team name; the API path creates the team with no plan pick or payment details. Alternatives: on by default with no question; a question on `update` too.
- [ ] **Who is let in.** The policy "Every App gateway members" (found by name) gains every member who isn't deactivated (`banned`, as the gateway reads it), every pending unexpired invitation's email, `--email` addresses (with a warning when they have no account) and `--email-domain` domains; nothing is ever removed from it. `--email-domain` doesn't set `CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_AUTO_JOIN_DOMAINS`; the done output suggests it. Alternative: a `--prune` that drops deactivated members; writing the auto-join setting with the domain.
- [ ] **No members, no run.** With nobody to let in and no policy yet, `access on` refuses (`ACCESS_NOBODY_ALLOWED`) rather than putting the setup link behind an Access nobody passes.
- [ ] **Two applications, found by destination, not by name.** The one whose public destination is the gateway host, and the one whose is `*.<base>`; one covering both is kept as one (warning `ACCESS_ONE_APPLICATION`, both settings get its AUD). Destinations that aren't ours (not the host, the wildcard or a host one label below the base) are kept as found and named (`ACCESS_FOREIGN_DESTINATION`); `access off` then only removes ours instead of deleting the application. An application with policies of its own (not reusable) is left alone with a warning (`ACCESS_INLINE_POLICIES`).
- [ ] **Public routes.** Apps with neither public routes nor `mcp` get no destination (the wildcard covers them, and since 2026-10-25 bypasses nothing: an app host serves no MCP unless its manifest sets `mcp`); each app with some gets one on its host with `/mcp`, `/mcp/*` first only where the manifest sets `mcp`, then its routes as Access path patterns: `**` → `*`, `/:param` → `/*`, methods ignored (the gateway still enforces them; Access's `*` spans segments, so Access is wider than the gateway, never narrower). A segment left with two wildcards, or a route past the 25th override, stays behind Access with a warning. The gateway's host also bypasses `/health` (uptime monitors), under Managed OAuth too. With Managed OAuth on the gateway's application, nothing bypasses `/mcp` on either application (the runbook's rule). A deploy or delete syncs the apps application when the app has public routes or `mcp`.
- [ ] **Settings** (`CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN`, `_APPS_AUD`, `_AUD`) go through the `secret set` machinery in one `cf workers secrets bulk`, so "set AUD last" is moot. What was written is recorded in the Gateway's D1 (`_everyapp_access`, one row beside the install row; `lib/access/record.ts`), since a Worker's secret values can't be read back. They are written when one is missing, when an application was created, when nothing is recorded yet (everyapp.team's first rerun after this), when the record doesn't name the applications found (one recreated by hand has a new AUD tag, which otherwise locks everyone out while `show` says "on"), or with `--write-settings`; otherwise "kept". `access show` says when they are stale. Alternative: write them on every run (one Worker version per rerun, no record).
- [ ] **Deploys and deletes sync the apps application** (`lib/access/sync.ts`) under a lease of their own (`access`, a 60-second wait), as a warning on failure, never a failed deploy: `ACCESS_NOT_CHECKED` when Access couldn't be listed (so whether the Gateway is behind it is unknown, and the warning says "if"), `ACCESS_SYNC_FAILED` when the application couldn't be updated; both carry `data.rerun` (`access on`). A login without the Access permissions is silent unless the app has public routes (a delete reads the app's manifest before the row goes). `access on` itself reads the apps' public routes under the same lease. The policy isn't touched by a deploy: inviting someone still means `access on`.
- [ ] **`access off`** removes the three settings first (one version, the ring stops at once), then deletes the two applications (`--keep-applications` leaves them); the team, providers and policy stay. Asks; `--yes` doesn't.
- [ ] **Not done.** A policy renamed in the dashboard means a second policy gets created; `listAll` pages 100 × 20 and doesn't say when it stops short; Managed OAuth isn't offered (above). Done since: `status` says whether Access is on (#400, ef1de132; `access show` has the detail).
- [ ] **everyapp.team next steps, Ben's call.** Carter signs in once with the password through Access (Ben did on 2026-10-24 01:11 UTC). Then `EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_IN=false`. No native mobile sessions exist, so the outer ring's mobile limitation costs nothing today.

### Access default + reconcile (2026-10-26)

Ben's ruling (2026-10-26): Cloudflare Access is the default, and once it is on it stays correct by itself, Gateway updates included ("default secure always if they enabled that"). Built as #725 (update/setup reconcile), #726 (status drift), #727 (the unattended default) and the register PR; defaults taken, each to confirm or change.

- [ ] **Every Gateway upload reconciles both Access applications** (`keepAccessAfterGatewayDeploy`, `lib/access/applications.ts`): `update`, `setup` on a live Gateway and the `deploy` that installs one, right after the upload or the up-to-date check, under the `access` lease, with the same `reconcileApplication` `access on` uses (destinations, the policy among the reusable ones, HttpOnly and the binding cookie, the sign-in methods `on` vouches for; never the session). Access being on is read from the live applications, never from a flag, so `access off` stays off. Alternative: only after an upgrade, not on an up-to-date run (one list call saved per `setup`).
- [ ] **It creates nothing and never rewrites the three settings.** A missing application is the warning `ACCESS_APPLICATION_MISSING`; an application recreated by hand (new AUD tag) is left for `access on` to fix, and `status` says so (`drift.settings`). Alternative: have the hook write the settings too (one more Worker version per rollout when they changed).
- [ ] **Failures are warnings** (`ACCESS_RECONCILE_FAILED`, `ACCESS_NOT_CHECKED` with the rerun), never a failed update; a login without the Access permissions and an account that never opened Zero Trust are silent (Access can't be on without them).
- [ ] **The plan lives in the CLI** (`lib/access/plan.ts`), which ships with the Gateway it describes from the same checkout; the Gateway release carries no bypass list of its own for the CLI to read. A CLI older than the live Gateway reconciles to its own plan: the downgrade guard refuses such an update before that point.
- [ ] **`status` reads Zero Trust when Access is on** (three cf calls: applications, identity providers, organization) and reports drift on the `Access` row and as the problem `ACCESS_DRIFT` (`run: access on`); a login without the Access permissions reads `in step? this login can't read Access` and raises no problem; any other refusal is `ACCESS_DRIFT_UNKNOWN`. `access show` computes its `inSync` fields from the same `accessDrift`, so the two never disagree. Alternative: keep `status` off Zero Trust and leave drift to `access show`.
- [ ] **Unattended setup turns Access on when the account can**: a Zero Trust team exists (a new team's name is permanent and never picked for someone) and the Cloudflare login's address is an owner or admin of the Gateway (read from `members` and `users`; Sign in with Cloudflare asserts that address, so the operator can get in). Otherwise one warning `ACCESS_SETUP_SKIPPED` says why and names `access on` (`--team <zone's first label>` when there is no team). An API-token login (no email) is a skip, not a stop. Alternatives: turn it on whenever a team exists (the PIN lets the owner in by email anyway); create the team unattended from the zone's first label.
- [ ] **`--no-access` is `setup`'s only opt-out** (`access off` afterwards); the `deploy` that installs a Gateway takes the default and has no flag; `update` never offers Access and has no flag. The interactive question stays (Yes by default).
- [ ] **Not done.** Managed OAuth isn't reconciled or offered; the hook doesn't attach a reusable policy that `access on` never made (no policy, policies kept as found); nothing runs on a schedule (a dashboard change between two CLI runs is caught by the next `status`, `update` or `access on`, not before); everyapp.team is not redeployed (Rollout 3 is Ben's).

### Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/cli-rebind

Lane 4: an app's `deploy` or `delete` never changes the Gateway's code; only `update` and `setup` do. Stacked on the setup-from-release work (#746: the rebind redeploys the live release by the origin the install row records). Defaults taken, each to confirm or change.

- [ ] **The rebind still uploads the Gateway** (the live release, with the new binding list): Cloudflare's script-settings PATCH with `inherit` (what the browser installer does, `cfRestDeployer.ts`) would need no upload, but cf beta.14 has no command for a Worker's settings, no raw API call, and no keep-bindings/keep-assets in Build Output, and a `cf auth login` session gives everyapp no API token. Alternatives, both held for a security review: mint a short-lived scoped token with `cf accounts tokens create` for the one PATCH; or keep a Cloudflare token on the Gateway and have it bind apps itself. Revisit when cf ships a script-settings command.
- [ ] **No fallback: GATEWAY_RELEASE_UNAVAILABLE**, rerun `everyapp update`, when the live release can't be had again (a row from before origins were recorded, a checkout build this machine doesn't have, a checkout-built Gateway from an npm everyapp, a file gone, a tag gone, or an asset whose SHA-256 changed; that last one replaces the warning GATEWAY_RELEASE_DIFFERS). Checked before the registry changes; a redeploy of an app the Gateway already binds needs no release and is never refused. Alternative: #746's fallback (deploy what setup would, said first).
- [ ] **A CLI behind the live Gateway is refused by version** (`refuseGatewayNewerThanCli`: CLI_TOO_OLD when the install row's `releaseVersion` is newer than this everyapp, prerelease suffixes ignored), for every deploy and delete, before the registry changes. It replaces `refuseOlderCheckout`, which compared the checkout with the live Gateway (a no-op once the rebind took the live release). Alternative: record the release's `minCli` on the install row and compare that.
- [ ] **HELD for Ben (security): the settings-PATCH rebind.** The follow-up that makes a rebind take seconds with no Gateway upload: mint a short-lived Workers-Scripts-scoped token with `cf accounts tokens create`, PATCH `/workers/scripts/every-app-gateway/settings` (`inherit` for every binding, plus the app's service binding), attach the Custom Domain, revoke the token. Needs API Tokens permission on the login and a threat-model row; not built.
- [ ] **Untagged and pre-origin Gateways** (`release_origin` null: everyapp.team on 2cfdeb8b and every Gateway installed before #745): a rebind takes this checkout's cached build of the row's tree, or builds it again when the checkout's Gateway sources hash to it; otherwise GATEWAY_RELEASE_UNAVAILABLE until one `everyapp update` records the origin. The next everyapp.team rollout runs `update` first anyway; app deploys from any other checkout or machine refuse until then. Checkout-built Gateways rebind only from a checkout with the live sources (or its cache: three builds kept); another machine runs `update` first.
- [ ] **The `gateway.upgrade` step is gone** (event, renderer, docs); the `gateway.upgrade` lease purpose stays for `update`.

### provider add, one step (2026-10-26)

Ben ran `provider add --oauth google-workspace` and found it wordy and two-step. Now `provider add <id>` adds the provider and asks for its key or client in the same run. Defaults taken, each to confirm or change:

- [ ] **`provider add <id>` takes a built-in id without `--oauth`**: an API-key or AI provider (`openrouter`) writes no row and sets its key; an OAuth preset or built-in client goes the `--oauth` way. A bare id that isn't the catalog's is `PROVIDER_NOT_FOUND`; a Gateway service (`email`) is `PROVIDER_BUILTIN`. `--oauth` stays for OAuth spec files.
- [ ] **Only unset settings are asked for.** A catalog provider whose settings the Gateway has gets no prompt ("Its key is set"; rotate with `secret set`). A setting another provider shares (`CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`) isn't asked again. A registered row is always asked, since a new or replaced row is unbound.
- [ ] **Enter on any prompt sets nothing**, never half a client; exit 0 and `next` is the `secret set`.
- [ ] **Piped `NAME=value` lines are read only from a pipe or a file.** A stdin left open some other way (a socket) is not waited on, so an agent never hangs on `provider add`.
- [ ] **The display name drops a trailing aside** (`Google (Gmail, …)` → `Google`). Lane 5 owns the labels; see below.
- [ ] **The docs link is `https://everyapp.com/docs/providers/<company>`** (`lib/providerDocs.ts`, using the docs generator's own `COMPANY_OF`). A registered spec gets no Docs line; its own `docs` URL is step 1.
- [ ] **`next` carries no note** (the setup lines say what to do; `data` has `redirectUri`, `console`, `docs`). The scope sets and manifest entry are behind `--verbose`, and in `data` and `provider show`.
- [ ] **For lane 5 (capabilities.ts)**: `google-workspace`'s label "Google (Gmail, Calendar, Search Console and Analytics)" should be the name, "Google Workspace", with the products in its description; `microsoft-365`'s likewise ("Microsoft 365"). Scope-set labels such as `identity`'s "Google account (who you are)" read badly inside parentheses.
- [ ] **Not changed**: `signin add` already asks for its client at a hidden prompt (`--client-secret-from-env` for scripts); `mcp add` already offers to set the credential (`Set <NAME> now?`) or takes `--from-env`. Neither has the two-step problem.

### Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/1 sdk-context

Lane 1 of the platform shortcuts swath (HELD: identity; Ben reviews). The SDK keeps the request in context and attributes every Gateway call from it, so the apps delete `gateway-attribution.ts`, their `EVERY_APP_ORG_ID` pin, `ROLES`/`toOrgRole`/`isAdmin` and `withoutCookies()`. Defaults chosen:

- [x] **A Gateway call with no `for` and no context throws** (Ben confirmed strict, 2026-10-26) `RequestContextError` (`request_context_missing`) before it is made, in every Worker (an organization's own included). Before, an own-org app's `scheduled()` could call with nothing and the Gateway attributed it to the app's organization from the props. Now that work wraps itself in `withOrganization(env.EVERY_APP_ORG_ID, fn)`; `for: { background: true }` still says "the app's" explicitly. The alternative (own-org apps keep the silent default) keeps every cron working unchanged but lets a forgotten context pass as the app.
- [ ] **`integrationStatus()` needs a context too**, like every attributed call (its answer carries the person's or the organization's connection). Before, it could be called with nothing and answered without a connection.
- [ ] **The organization pin answers 401** (`IdentityError`, `unauthenticated`) on both the handler path and `/__everyapp/tools/call`, naming both organization ids in the message; the apps answered 401 or `null` (analytics) too. 403 `forbidden` would be the other reading. It is enforced only when the dev opt-in is off: `everyAppWorker()` now sets `EVERY_APP_ORG_ID` to the stub gateway's `dev-org` in development so `withOrganization(env.EVERY_APP_ORG_ID, …)` runs there, and `everyapp dev --mode mirror` signs real organizations in, which the pin would refuse.
- [ ] **Near-expiry fallback is 15 s** before the identity's `exp` (OpenSEO's and CRM's margin), read from the token on every call, so a `fetch` made early in a long request switches to the organization's background work by itself. A call with an explicit `for: request` keeps the identity to the end.
- [ ] **`withOrganization()` inside that organization's own request keeps the person attributed** while their identity is good (CRM's rule) and refuses another organization's (`OrganizationMismatchError`, `attribution_organization_mismatch`). The other reading, "the organization, always", would drop the person from the audit trail.
- [ ] **`everyApp()` drops every `Set-Cookie`** (the perimeter strips inbound `Cookie`, so an app's cookie can never return, and one named like the Gateway's session cookie locks the person out) and warns on each; a `101` passes as it is. The alternative was to document that the perimeter leaves app cookies alone (it strips only `Domain=` and the `__Host-everyapp_*` names) and let apps keep their own `withoutCookies()`.
- [ ] **`orgRole` narrows to `OrgRole`** (`owner | admin | member`); any other claim the Gateway mints reads as `member`, in the SDK rather than in each app. `OrganizationMemberRole` is the same union.
- [ ] **`currentUser()` is `null` under `withOrganization()`** (no person) and throws outside every context; `requireCurrentUser()` throws the 401 `Response` on a public route like `requireEveryAppUser()`.
- [ ] **`getEveryAppUser(headers, env)`** takes `Headers` as well as a `Request`, and answers from the context when the headers carry the token being served, so no app builds `new Request("https://x.invalid/")`.
- [ ] **jose is a dependency** of the SDK (`^6.2.5`, the claim-validation floor) rather than a peer; inbox and analytics bump theirs in lane 10.
- [ ] **Lane crossings, kept minimal:** `packages/sdk/src/cloudflare/worker.ts` (two `EVERY_APP_ORG_ID` lines in the dev env, lane 2's `EveryAppDevEnv` type gets the field), `starter/` (`for: request` dropped in `events.ts`, `home.ts`, `tools.ts`, `todos.ts`; `ensureUser.ts` left for lane 2), `docs/capabilities.md`, `docs/security-model.md`, `docs/manifest-settings.md`, `docs/threat-model.md` (snippets and the SDK-pin sentence).
- [ ] **Phase 2 (after the rc):** OpenSEO #1067, CRM, analytics, inbox, RepoLens and Mission Control delete `gateway-attribution.ts`, `withoutCookies()`, the pin and the role sets, and wrap Workflows, crons and agent turns in `withOrganization()`.

### Agent docs (2026-10-24)

The second world of documentation: `landing-page/content/agent/*.md`, served at `/docs/agent`, in `/llms.txt` (both worlds, one line per page) and `/llms-full.txt` (everything), and pointed at by the everyapp skill. `packages/cli/src/docs/agentDocs.test.ts` fails when a page misses a command, flag, error code, warning, manifest field, Gateway setting, header or SDK export; `cli.md` is generated (`pnpm -C packages/cli docs:update`).

- [ ] **`everyapp access` changes documented contracts.** `t3/access-everyapp-team` adds the `access on|off|show` commands, flags, error codes and events. `access.md` and `setup.md` describe the command as it is on that branch. When it merges: run `pnpm -C packages/cli docs:update` (regenerates `cli.md`), add its new codes to `errors.md`, and re-read `access.md` against the merged code; the agent docs test will fail on main until then.
- [ ] **Sizes.** The agent pages run 86–213 KB each (`errors.md` 213 KB, `troubleshooting.md` 150 KB, `deploy.md` 139 KB; 1.5 MB together), the mirrored repository documents 0.4 MB more. `/llms-full.txt` therefore carries the user guides and the agent pages but not the mirror (the first build served 2 MB); `/llms.txt` tells agents to fetch one page at a time. Decide whether to split the biggest pages (errors by family, troubleshooting by area) or open each with a short "the essentials" section, and whether to put `/llms-full.txt` behind a size budget.
- [ ] **The mirrored repository documents are not committed.** `scripts/sync-agent-docs.js` writes `content/agent/repo/` before every landing page dev and build (gitignored). CI doesn't build the landing page, so a broken link in a mirrored document shows only in Ben's build. Add a landing page build to CI, or commit the mirror with a drift test?
- [ ] **Root `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`.** The checkout now tells an agent to read the skill and where the agent docs are. Every Claude Code session in the repository loads it; keep it that short.
- [ ] **The user docs' agent prompt points at `/llms.txt`** (was `/llms-full.txt`): an index an agent reads before fetching the one page it needs, instead of one large file that fetch tools truncate.
- [ ] **One heading per code in `errors.md`.** A hosted Gateway's deploy API answers codes the CLI also raises itself (`RESOURCE_NAME_TAKEN`, `APP_NOT_FOUND`, `MIGRATION_FAILED`, …); each has one entry that names both sources. Split them if the meanings drift.
- [ ] **The agent docs are indexable** (sitemap, no `noindex`), unlike `/docs/internal`. Fine for search engines and LLM crawlers; revisit if they rank above the user docs for people's searches.
- [ ] **Two repository documents disagree with the code** (found while writing the agent docs; the pages follow the code). `docs/runbooks/browser-app-install.md` tells the operator to run `pnpm everyapp secret set CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID --from-env CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID --json`, which `secret set` refuses (`SECRET_NOT_SETTABLE`: an install-owned setting, written by `setup`). `docs/TESTING.md` names the dev gateway module as `every-app-gateway/v2/dev`; it is `packages/perimeter/src/dev/devGateway.ts`. Both are mirrored onto the site as written.
- [x] **Stale repository documents** (fixed by the docs sweep of 2026-10-24, below; `docs/cli-redesign.md` went with #433). The launch-review thread listed documents that read as current but aren't: `docs/cli-redesign.md` (commands added since; its skill snippet exits 252), `docs/runbooks/hosted-install.md` (`secret set --for gateway`, "redeploys"), `docs/runbooks/mcp-oauth.md:22` and `docs/runbooks/cloudflare-access.md:339,652` (a setting change redeploys; it is live at once since #348), `adr/ADR-0001` §6 and `adr/RFC-0001:33`, `docs/hosted-platform-build.md` §1, `docs/architecture-v2-*.md`, `docs/v2-refactor-recommendations.md`. Fix or archive the sources.

### OAuth scopes (2026-10-24)

Can an app ask the gateway for only the OAuth scopes it needs, enforced at the gateway? Designed and built 2026-10-23/24 in [docs/design/2026-10-oauth-scopes.md](docs/design/2026-10-oauth-scopes.md) (its section 13 lists what was built and where it differs; PRs #398 merged, #414, #411, #412, #408 open, Slack's policy on `t3/oauth-scopes-slack`); its section 1 records that until this work the answer was no: the gateway completed token endpoints without reading scopes, and the app chose the consent's scopes and kept the tokens. Each bullet shipped with a default; confirm or change it.

- [ ] **Named scope sets in the manifest, not provider scope strings.** `capabilities: { "google-workspace": { access: "write", scopes: ["search-console.read", "analytics.read"], why } }`; the registry maps each set to the provider's strings and to the API hosts and paths it unlocks, and the status hands the app the strings for the authorize URL. Google's sets are `identity`, `gmail.read`, `calendar.read`, `search-console.read`, `analytics.read`; Microsoft's `identity`, `mail.read`, `calendar.read`. Alternative: raw provider strings validated against a flat list (more precise, unreadable in Admin → Apps and in `why`).
- [ ] **`scopes` is required for a client with a scope policy and refused for every other capability.** The strict validator refuses a missing or empty list, a duplicate, an unknown name, and `scopes` on Mastodon or DataForSEO; a stored row with a missing, empty or malformed value, or any unknown name, is refused at the token endpoint (`integration_scopes_undeclared`) and shows `ready: false`: an unknown name voids the whole grant rather than narrowing silently. Alternative: `scopes` optional meaning identity only, or unknown names dropped (a grant nobody declared).
- [ ] **The enforced boundary is the token endpoint, not API calls.** The gateway reads the token request and the provider's token response and refuses to forward a token whose granted scopes exceed the app's sets (`integration_scope_exceeded`); the app still holds the tokens and calls the provider itself, and the provider enforces the scopes on its API. The per-set host+path allowlist is built and tested but enforces nothing until the outbound Worker (dispatch tier) or gateway-held tokens (every tier) use it. Alternative: a minimal gateway-held-token mode before launch (3 to 4 more agent-days and Open CRM changes; the design's section 2 says why not now).
- [ ] **No write scope sets this week.** `gmail.send`, `calendar.events`, `mail.send`, `contacts.read` are not defined: no app asks, the deployed manifest is a self-declared grant with no approval step, and the Google design's "read-only by construction" holds; a registry invariant pins it. Alternative: define them now so authors have the vocabulary.
- [ ] **An over-scoped token the gateway refuses is dropped: never forwarded, stored, logged or revoked.** Google's `/revoke` removes every client's grant for that person across the Cloud project, and Slack's `auth.revoke` can kill an installation token another app shares. Alternative: revoke where it is safe (Slack per token; Google only on a client no other app shares), or forward with a warning header (the boundary would be advisory).
- [ ] **A 2xx token response that carries a token but names no scopes fails closed** (`integration_scope_unverified`, 502). All three providers document `scope` on every success response. Alternative: on Google, consult `oauth2.googleapis.com/tokeninfo` with the access token before deciding.
- [ ] **Microsoft is refused strictly, with no advisory flag.** On a shared Entra client a token may carry every scope the person consented to any app on it; the gateway refuses the narrower app's exchange, which is availability loss, not narrowing. One Microsoft consumer today (Open CRM). The fixes are one registered client per Every App app (a registry entry and two secrets, as `youtube` and `google-workspace` are two clients) or gateway-held tokens with the Graph allowlist. Alternative: a registry flag that logs and forwards for Microsoft only (fail open inside a security boundary).
- [ ] **Grant types are per capability.** Google and Microsoft complete `authorization_code` and `refresh_token` only; `client_credentials` and token exchange are refused (`integration_grant_not_allowed`): an app never gets app-level tokens with the operator's client. Slack's policy, when it ships, takes a code exchange without `grant_type` (its `oauth.v2.access`) and `refresh_token`. Clients without a policy keep today's pass-through (Meta's exchanges). Alternative: a grant-type allowlist on every client.
- [ ] **The token request's `scope` is checked, then rewritten per provider.** Anything outside the allowed list is refused (`integration_scope_not_allowed`); then Microsoft's is replaced with the status's scopes on every token leg, Google's is dropped (Google documents none on either leg), Slack's is kept. `grant_type`, `scope` and `user_scope` in the query string are refused (`integration_body_invalid`) so the gateway decides on the body alone. Alternative: check only.
- [ ] **An explicit `identity` set plus a tolerated baseline.** Google: `openid email profile` as a set an app opts into; the two spellings Google echoes (`userinfo.email`, `userinfo.profile`) are tolerated and never refuse. Microsoft: `User.Read` as the set; `openid profile email offline_access User.Read` tolerated; `offline_access` always requested and never echoed. Alternative: always request `openid` and `email` for every Google app so each can key accounts by `sub` (one consent line Open CRM doesn't ask for today).
- [ ] **Search Console and Analytics live on the `google-workspace` client** (Ben's premise: one client reaches all four). The operator allows the scopes on that client's consent screen, and one Google verification covers Gmail's restricted scope and the two read-only data scopes; its label is now "Google (Gmail, Calendar, Search Console and Analytics)" and `oauth2.googleapis.com` left its `api` list (a token host). Alternative: a third Google client (`google`, the Google design's `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`) with `identity`, `search-console.read` and `analytics.read` only, keeping Gmail's verification separate.
- [ ] **Slack's policy merged as #425 (d0c3b73f); a gateway carrying it and #411 must not go live before Open CRM redeploys with `scopes` for slack** (its Slack exchange fails closed otherwise; Postiz is gone from everyapp.team). Bot sets `channels.read`, `users.read`, `users.email`, `messages.write`, `channels.join`, `channels.manage`, `files.write`; user set `user-channels.manage`; the four scopes nobody asks for leave the checklist. Alternative: defer Slack, or give Open CRM its own Slack client so each app's installation is its own.
  - Built as #425 (`t3/oauth-scopes-slack`, 2026-10-24): the registry's `slack` policy, which the gateway enforces only once #411 is in. Redeploy order: merge this registry first (the live gateway enforces nothing, so nothing changes); then Open CRM redeploys with `["channels.read", "users.read", "users.email", "messages.write", "channels.join", "channels.manage", "user-channels.manage"]` (Postiz, which would have taken `["channels.read", "users.read", "messages.write", "channels.join", "files.write"]`, is gone from everyapp.team), through a CLI carrying this registry and #414's `scopes`; only then does a gateway carrying #411 and this registry go to everyapp.team. The other way round, crm's Slack token exchange fails closed (`integration_scopes_undeclared`) until it redeploys. Deltas from the design: `files.write` exists because Postiz uploads images with the external upload flow; the user set also carries `channels:read` and `groups:read` (Open CRM's `user_scope`); Open CRM's consent gains `chat:write.customize` as part of `messages.write`; and `users.email` is declared beside `users.read` (Slack grants it only with `users:read`).
- [ ] **Shared-consent collisions on Microsoft and Slack (two apps, one client, one person or workspace) refuse the narrower app.** The fix is the provider's own model, one registered client per app. Alternative: tolerate scopes any other active app on the gateway declares for the same client (the gateway can't tell that union from an app over-asking).
- [ ] **Granted and excess scope names are logged on a refusal** (`integration.proxy.scope_refused`): scope strings are public vocabulary and what an operator needs; tokens, codes, `id_token`, bodies and the client stay out. Alternative: log the code and set names only.
- [ ] **Local dev enforces the same sets.** `everyAppWorker()` and `everyAppAuxiliaryWorker()` write the manifest's capabilities as `EVERYAPP_DEV_CAPABILITIES` in dev; the SDK applies the same checks and answers the same refusal shape; without the variable a policy capability is `ready: false`. Alternative: dev enforces against the registry's whole catalog (narrowing invisible in dev).
- [ ] **The hosted deploy API refuses a bundle listing a policy client without scope sets, or with an unknown one** (`manifestProblems`, `BUNDLE_REFUSED` 422, no row written); the gateway still fails closed on anything that slips through. Alternative: runtime-only.
- [ ] **Outbound-Worker enforcement of the route allowlist for dispatch-tier apps is the first gateway PR after launch** (held for Ben): `OutboundParameters` in `packages/perimeter/src/getAppFetcher.ts` gains the app's sets and `outbound.ts` runs `scopeRouteAllows`, stripping `X-HTTP-Method-Override`, `access_token` and `token`. Until then the allowlist is tested data. Alternative: wait for gateway-held tokens.
- [ ] **The MCP consent page's per-app "reaches Google (Search Console read-only), Slack" line is held** (it touches `mcp-auth`) and dropped if time runs out; Admin → Apps is the surface that ships.
- [ ] **Follow-ups, in order.** (0) #414, #412, #425 and #411 are merged (2026-10-25; #411 was squash-merged by this thread's watcher while its Test job was red, the red being #412's SDK tests versus #425's Slack policy, fixed in #471, tests only; the watcher's merge-on-fail was the thread's mistake, now chained on green); before `pnpm everyapp update` from a main carrying #411 and #425, crm must redeploy with `scopes` on its `google-workspace`, `microsoft-365` and `slack` entries (Postiz is gone from everyapp.team), or its mail capture and Slack posting fail closed. Done 2026-10-24: the gateway carrying #411 and #425 (main 0410c922) went live at 22:12:11Z and crm 8d58273d with the three scope sets at 22:13:46Z, 95 s later, not before: `setup` uploads the gateway with its migrations, and the old gateway would not have parsed the new row (TODO "Rollout on everyapp.team" under Secrets model, docs/overnight/2026-10-24.md item 27). (1) Open CRM (`private-open-crm`): add `scopes: ["gmail.read", "calendar.read"]` to `google-workspace`, `["identity", "mail.read"]` to `microsoft-365` (and Slack's sets with PR 7); drop `include_granted_scopes=true` (`src/server/features/mailbox/services/ConnectionService.ts:94`) and build the URL from `authorizeUrl()`; derive `REQUESTED_SCOPES` from `status.scopes`. It must redeploy before any gateway carrying #411 serves it (the gateway step is `pnpm everyapp update` from main), and PR 2 must be merged before its deploy passes the strict validator. (2) OpenSEO onto the gateway's client: one entry `["identity", "search-console.read", "analytics.read"]`, `fetchTokens` through `integrationFetch`, the PKCE verifier moved to the state row, its redirect paths registered on the client, `getGoogleAccountId`'s audience from the status. (3) One live check per provider on a staging gateway before any production deploy: the token response's `scope` spelling (Microsoft's URL form, Google's `email`/`profile` echo) is from documentation and observed responses; a third spelling would fail closed on every exchange. (4) The secrets thread's required/optional/one-of fields (#383) share the manifest entry with `scopes`; both trial-merge cleanly; keep them orthogonal. (5) Merge order: #414 with or before #411; #412 after #414 (the strict validator `everyapp dev` runs must accept `scopes` first); #387 before #411 (whose branch carries it).

### Threat model and security scanning (2026-10-24)

The threat model is [docs/threat-model.md](docs/threat-model.md) (the register of 328 invariants with their code and tests: [docs/threat-model-invariants.md](docs/threat-model-invariants.md)). Anthropic's security review for Claude Code ran on every pull request here and in new apps from #388 until Ben chose local skills over CI (2026-10-24: "we'll just have our own skills for doing that locally"; #606): `pnpm security-review` (`scripts/security-review.mjs`, the `security-review` skill) runs the same review locally over the branch's diff, and new apps get the app boundaries and the recipe in the everyapp skill (`security-review.md`). Its first full-tree run and a mapped-and-attacked pass over every boundary produced the findings below. Fixed: a release tarball's symbolic links, hard links and out-of-bundle module paths (#386); the OAuth-client proxy completing the client-credentials grant with the operator's client (#387, merged 2026-10-24).

**Needs Ben**

- [x] **The reporting channel.** Ben's decision (2026-10-24, his briefing answer Q11a): GitHub private vulnerability reporting, no address. `SECURITY.md` and the threat model's "Reporting a problem" say "Security → Report a vulnerability", and the README says to report privately, not in a public issue, linking the latter (the `REPORT_ADDRESS` placeholder is gone). The one step left is the switch itself, Settings → Code security → private vulnerability reporting, when the repository flips public: "Ben's briefing answers (2026-10-24, afternoon)" and "Public copy" ("After the push") carry it; nothing to add here.
- [x] **`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the repository's Actions secrets**: not needed. Ben (2026-10-24) chose local skills over a security review in CI; the workflow is gone (#606), and `pnpm security-review` runs on the maintainer's own Claude Code login. "Require approval for all external contributors" on the public repository is still worth setting: the other workflows run on pull requests, and the local review is not hardened against prompt injection (the diff enters the prompt), so a stranger's changes get a person's read first.
- [ ] **The browser install is a second deploy authority on self-host.** Since #371, once `CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN` is set, an owner or admin can have the Gateway deploy a public release with that account-wide token (`POST /api/admin/apps/install`; docs/threat-model.md B7). The security model says so. Narrowed on 2026-10-24 (the agent-parity swath, `deploy.browser-install-install-org-only`): only the install organization's owners and admins (the oldest organization with an owner, as the CLI names it); any other organization is refused before the download, the install page offers no other workspace, and nobody installs before the Gateway has an owner; `install-api.sqlite.test.ts` now has self-hosted cases for the gate (every clause) and that rule (the install, the poll, and availability and the context by the person: an admin of the install organization whose sidebar is in another workspace still sees Install app), up to the download, and a hosted case for a second organization's owner; each refused cross-organization attempt leaves one `app_install.refused` warn line (the organization ids and the user id, never the token). Still Ben's: owners only (admins are made by the owner, so today they are equal to the owner here), default `BROWSER_APP_INSTALLS` to off, or move the token into a thin deployer Worker (the TODO under Build). The self-hosted upload itself is still untested: the fake Cloudflare serves dispatch namespaces, not account Workers (`/workers/scripts/<name>/settings`, `/deployments`, `/domains`, `/dns_records`, `/subdomain`); adding those is the way to pin `install.self-hosted-default-on` in full.
- [ ] **`everyapp status` says nothing about browser installs.** The honest line it could give: `Browser installs: on` when the Gateway Worker's secret names (`listGatewaySecretNames`, read today only when an app declares settings) include `CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN` and not `BROWSER_APP_INSTALLS`; `off (BROWSER_APP_INSTALLS is set; a secret's value can't be read back)` or `off (no CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN; pnpm everyapp secret set CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN)` otherwise, with `data.browserInstalls: "on" | "off" | "unknown"` and the reason. Not built in the parity swath: #594 rewrote the status page and its drift tests today, and a secret's value is unreadable, so the line is names-based. Until then the setup skill's check stands: `secret list --json` → `data.gateway.names` has `CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN`.
- [x] **The old-era Workers in the Cloudflare account** (every-every-workout-tracker, every-git-init2, every-graham, every-julia, every-llm-chat, every-open-minutes, every-open-seo, every-simple-todo, every-simple-todo-template, every-super-seo, every-tab-bar, every-test-create, every-todo-app, every-winery, every-app-every-todo-app-bensenescu, every-app-state, every-app-state-token: all with workers.dev on; every-chef, every-todo, every-workout-tracker, every-counterscale, every-alchemy-example without) ran pre-#267 code behind nothing.
