Gone since the scan: docs/research with its street address, Gmail address and Autumn slug (#433); landing-page/content/internal (not served since #420; deleted 2026-10-25 by the docs sweep); the Postiz fixture's AGPL schema (replaced with SQL of our own, decision 14); apps/mobile-native (#427); the README's `link:` paths into `../private-every-app` (#453); `app.everyapp.com` in the deploy command's help.

**Decided by default, confirm**

- [x] **Security review on every pull request**: superseded 2026-10-24 (Ben: local skills, not CI; #606). `pnpm security-review` runs Claude Code non-interactively in restricted mode (read-only tools confined to the checkout, the branch's settings and hooks ignored) over the branch's diff, with the upstream audit prompt (pinned at its commit 0c6a49f1 in `scripts/security-review/audit-prompt.md`), the repository's boundaries (`scripts/security-review/scan-instructions.md`) and false-positive rules (`false-positives.md`), excluding `docs`/`adr`/`prompts`/`landing-page/content`/`*.md`/`pnpm-lock.yaml`; exit 1 while a high or medium finding remains; a diff too long for one prompt is retried as the file list. Pinned by `packages/cli/src/lib/securityReview.test.ts` with a fake `claude`. The model is the login's default (`--model` overrides). Each pull request that touches a boundary costs one local Claude Code run instead of an API key in Actions.
- [x] **New apps get the same workflow**: superseded the same day. A new app ships no workflow (`starter/.github/` is gone); the everyapp skill's `security-review.md` carries the app boundaries and the local recipe (`/security-review` in Claude Code against that list, or any agent reading the diff).
- [ ] **The OAuth-client proxy completes only a user's grants** (`authorization_code`, `refresh_token`, Meta's exchanges, RFC 8693 token exchange; #387). A network that needs another grant gets it added to `USER_BOUND_GRANT_TYPES` with a sentence on why it is a person's.
- [ ] **A release with a link, a special file or a module path outside its bundle is refused whole** (#386), for the Gateway's releases too.

**Gaps, ranked** (each is a bullet in docs/threat-model.md with its code pointer; the invariant ids are the register's)

- [x] **#404 closed unmerged: #447 deleted `createMcpHandler`**, the only in-repo way an app served `/mcp` itself; the manifest's `mcp: { by: "app", why }` hatch stays through launch (docs/threat-model.md gap 6; since the public-surface swath an app host serves `/mcp` only with that field, and the string form is refused).
- [x] **Fixes from the pass, all merged 2026-10-24** (the overnight rule became "merge everything green, security boundaries included"): #387 (the OAuth-client proxy signs only a user's grants), #402 (DataForSEO `tasks_fixed` refused), #403 (AI proxy: encoded separators in opened routes refused; one `model` part per multipart body), #405 (a password reset ends every session), #406 (cookie `Domain` stripped under every spelling; `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` and `openid-configuration` reserved on app hosts), #407 (an admin's reset mail follows the member rules). Merged earlier: #386 (release tarballs), #388 (the review in CI), #409 (`everyapp dev` serves this machine's names only).
- [ ] **A stolen admin session can take over any single-organization member** (reset links, admin invitations, promotion to admin) with no fresh-session requirement; Access in front is the launch mitigation. Later: a re-authentication step for those actions (docs/threat-model.md B2).
- [ ] **No owner recovery or transfer**: the `recovery` kind in `owner_setup_tokens` is unused; a compromised owner can't be removed by anyone but the operator in D1 (B2).
- [ ] **Sign-out doesn't reliably end the Access session**: only the sidebar's button redirects to `/cdn-cgi/access/logout`; the consent page's "switch account" doesn't (B6; small, with the Access thread).
- [ ] **Access's email is trusted whatever identity provider signed** (B6): the runbook's rule, nothing in the gateway. Option: pin the identity provider id, or refuse assertions from providers the operator didn't list.
- [ ] **With `GATEWAY_URL` unset, the outer ring stops vouching as well as signing in** while the config reports it on (B6; one-line separation in the plugin).
- [ ] **A held `subscriptions/listen` stream on an app host's `/mcp` outlives its token** for up to ten minutes; the gateway's own `/mcp` re-checks every poll and ends at the token's expiry (B5).
- [ ] **Any member reads the member directory** through Better Auth's open `list-members` and `get-organization` (B2): fine for four people; a decision for teams.
- [x] **`everyapp://` was a wildcard-authority trusted origin and verify-email appended the session cookie to custom-scheme redirects** (B2, hosted only). Closed 2026-10-24 by #442: the Expo plugin, the native origin and the Access mobile handoff went with the mobile shell; the gateway's only trusted origin is its own URL.
- [ ] **A first-party WebSocket 101 handshake passes through with `Set-Cookie` `Domain` intact** (B1; rebuild it as the third-party path does).
- [ ] **`ai.models` doesn't check OpenRouter's `models` fallback list**; `AI_PROXY_EXTRA_ROUTES` opens every method and sub-path to every opted-in app (B4).
- [ ] **Proxy-forwarding headers reach apps as sent** (`X-Forwarded-For` and friends; B1): strip or document `cf-connecting-ip` as the only address an app may trust.
- [ ] **Personal tokens: `*` for 90 days by default, no narrower scope in the UI, revocable by the holder only** (B5; the teams roadmap's per-member scopes).
- [ ] **Refresh-token replay has no reuse detection** (B5; the library offers none; a wrapper that records the last-used token id and revokes the grant on replay).
- [ ] **Script on any first-party app host can overwrite the session cookie and sign the victim's browser in as the attacker** (login CSRF; a property of the shared `Domain=<APP_BASE_DOMAIN>` cookie; Access in front mitigates). The remedy is per-host sessions as the third-party tier has (B1, B2).
- [ ] **An unclaimed setup link stays valid 24 hours after `setup` stops waiting** and the `--json` flow prints it into the agent's output (B2; shorten, or revoke when the CLI gives up).
- [ ] **Invitations and admin-minted reset links outlive the admin who issued them**; in-flight deploy jobs and browser installs run to their deadline after the credential that started them is revoked (B2, B7; small).
- [ ] **Hosted only** (nothing reaches a self-hosted gateway): dispatch-tier Workers send from the platform's sender and spend its keys unbounded; with open sign-up an invite link can plant an account for someone else's email; `connect()` from a dispatched Worker is unverified against egress; the org key in a dispatched Worker's name isn't compared with the row; `secret set` on a shared app skips the platform-admin check; the platform-domain refusal doesn't cover `THIRD_PARTY_DOMAIN`; a slug collision between a shared app and an organization's app routes `/mcp` calls to the first; a handoff code presented in the wrong browser isn't consumed; no sign-out on third-party hosts.

**Later**

- [ ] `everyapp status` reads the live Workers back (workers.dev, routes, `GATEWAY_URL`) and the Access applications (two applications, the bypass list, HttpOnly, the binding cookie), so drift from the deploy-time guarantees and the runbook shows up without a redeploy.
- [ ] Mirror docs/threat-model.md into the agent docs (the agent-docs thread) and cite it from the setup skills' Access page (the setup-skills thread asked for a line when it merges).
- [ ] Regenerate the invariant register when a boundary changes; it was produced by hand from the code on 2026-10-23 and the line numbers will drift.
- [x] **docs/security-model.md after the teams PRs merge** (done 2026-10-24: both edits made; docs/threat-model.md B1 and B4 say merged; the register gained `appaccess.groups-same-rule-everywhere` and `perimeter.needs-setup-names-no-setting`, and `cap.per-organization-limits` is enforced for a capped app) (the teams thread, 2026-10-24): when #384 (groups) lands, the "Organizations and Shared Apps" bullet that names `user_app_access` as the only grant becomes "a `user_app_access` row or membership in a group holding a `group_app_access` row" (one union in `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/repositories/effective-access.ts`, applied at the perimeter authenticator, the launcher, the members capability, the MCP catalogs and the consent page; removing a member deletes both kinds of grant in one batch; a group never holds another organization's user or app). When #416 (caps) lands, the "No per-organization usage limits yet" paragraph becomes: calls are counted per organization and app (`usage_daily`, `usage_monthly`, #381), an owner or admin may set `org_app_settings.monthly_call_cap` (null by default), and the AppGateway entrypoint then refuses the app's AI and capability calls for that organization with `429 app_monthly_cap_exceeded` and a `Retry-After` to the UTC month end, settings cached 30 s per isolate; the wording is in #416's body. docs/threat-model.md B1 and B4 already describe both as in flight.

### Security scan (2026-10-25)

An OpenAI Codex Security scan of this repository at main `3848d329` (the day before launch) found 14 things, every one already a bullet in docs/threat-model.md "Known gaps, ranked"; what it added was a concrete remediation and a suggested test per finding. The findings, by the scan's numbering and the gap they match: 1 browser install (gap 1, the only high), 2 dispatch-tier capability broker (46), 3 first-party WebSocket 101 cookie `Domain` (9), 4 refresh-token replay (20), 5 pending invitations outlive the inviter (12), 6 admin reset links outlive the issuer (12), 7 Access email trusted whatever the identity provider (19), 8 personal tokens default to `*` for 90 days (21), 9 `anthropic-workspace-id` forwarded (new), 10 `X-HF-Bill-To` forwarded (new), 11 remote MCP credential pinned on first use (33), 12 consent grants `mcp:write` unrequested (29), 13 OpenRouter `models` fallback unchecked (28), 14 missing `GATEWAY_URL` stops Access vouching (23). Fixed in order of priority, one pull request each, the register row named with each.

**Needs Ben**

- [x] **Browser installs on self-host** (finding 1, gap 1): Ben ruled 2026-10-26: merge #722 with owners and admins. `BROWSER_APP_INSTALLS` off unless exactly `on` (`everyapp setup` writes it beside `CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN` when the person opts in) and a session signed in within 10 minutes for the install itself (`APP_INSTALL_REAUTH_REQUIRED`). Everyapp.team has no `CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN`, so nothing live changed. The thin deployer Worker stays the follow-up.

**Decided by default, confirm**

- [ ] **Personal token defaults** (finding 8; `pat.ui-least-privilege-defaults`). The New token dialog opens on Read only (`mcp:read`), 30 days, and the one app when the person has exactly one (several apps: "All my apps" stays the default, since picking one at random would surprise). Read & write (`mcp:write`) and Full access (`*`) are choices, the latter with a warning. The server still maps omitted `scopes` to `*` and defaults to 90 days (ten test files create tokens that way; no production caller does): the scan asked for omitted scopes to be refused, left as is for now.
- [ ] **The identity-provider allowlist is opt-in** (finding 7; `access.idp-allowlist`). `CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_IDP_IDS` unset means any provider the Access policy admitted, as before; set, a token from another provider or one naming no provider (`idp.id`) is refused. Cloudflare's published application-token payload doesn't list the `idp` claim, so the runbook tells the operator to pin only after `access show` lists the ids, and the refusal is logged with its reason. Not made mandatory for launch: everyapp.team's ring has two providers (one-time PIN, Cloudflare) and pinning is one `secret set`.
- [ ] **What a demotion cancels** (findings 5 and 6; `issuer.revocation-ends-what-they-issued`). Demoting an owner or admin to member cancels every pending invitation they sent and deletes every reset link they minted in that organization; removal does the same, deactivation does it in every organization. Both are also refused at use (invite link, sign-up through it, Access/provider auto-accept, `/reset-password`) when the issuer is below admin by then, and that refusal cancels the invitation on the spot. The alternative, re-parenting them to the acting owner, was not taken: the owner never read what the admin sent. An admin-minted reset token's issuer is a companion Better Auth verification row (`admin-reset-password:<token>`), not a new table, so no migration.

**Post-launch** (recorded with the scan's remediation, not done)

- [ ] **Finding 3, first-party WebSocket 101 handshakes can set parent-domain session cookies** (gap 9, `perimeter.first-party-101-cookie-domain`). The scan: "Before returning a first-party 101, rebuild or clone the handshake in the runtime-supported way and remove Domain from every Set-Cookie; at minimum remove session-named Set-Cookie and credentialed CORS headers. Longer term, use per-host sessions instead of a shared parent-domain cookie." Tests it asks for: a first-party 101 cannot retain Domain on Set-Cookie; session-named Set-Cookie is removed case-insensitively from handshakes; WebSocket functionality and third-party host-session behaviour retained.
- [ ] **Finding 4, rotated OAuth refresh tokens remain replayable** (gap 20, `oauth.refresh-rotation-no-reuse-detection`). The scan: "Wrap refresh handling with a server-side token-family record and atomic reuse detection: accept only the current token, allow bounded idempotence for the same refresh transaction if needed, and revoke the entire grant on reuse of any rotated token. Upgrade to a provider version with equivalent detection when available." Tests: refresh once, replay the predecessor, assert the whole family is revoked; race two identical refreshes and assert documented idempotent or one-winner behaviour; connection revocation still invalidates access and refresh tokens.

**Done**

- [x] Findings 5 and 6 (#709): `issuer-revocation.ts`; the register row `issuer.revocation-ends-what-they-issued`; gap 12 marked fixed.
- [ ] **Consent preselects read only** (finding 12; `oauth.scopes-are-the-users-choice`). The scan asked for least privilege by default, so the Access choice on the consent page now starts at "Read only" and read & write is a deliberate click (`ConsentForm.tsx`, one `defaultChecked`; the decision parser's default is `read` too). The agents in the launch demos need read & write: a person connecting Claude Code or Codex must pick it on the page. Flip the default back if the extra click is wrong for launch.
- [ ] **The challenge names `mcp:read mcp:write`** (finding 12). The MCP SDK's client requests the scopes the 401 challenge names (then the protected-resource metadata's `scopes_supported`), and a grant is now capped by the request, so with the old `scope="mcp:read"` every Claude Code connection would have become read-only with no way to write. `requiredScopes` on the resource server is advertised, not enforced (the validator takes either scope); the resource metadata lists both. A client that deliberately asks for `mcp:read` alone is read-only whatever the person chooses, and the page says so.
- [x] Finding 1 (#722): default-off browser installs and the fresh-session rule; register row `install.self-hosted-fresh-session`, `install.self-hosted-default-on` restated (the id predates the default); gap 1 marked narrowed.
- [x] Finding 11 (#718): no trust on first use for remote MCP credentials; an unpinned value is sent nowhere and its rows need setup until `secret set` pins it; gap 33 marked fixed; the agent docs, design note and register row say so. Default taken: no separate `mcp pin` verb (re-running `secret set` pins; a pin-only verb would let a stolen agent token move a credential, the same reason `signin` has no MCP twin).
- [x] Finding 7 (#717): `CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_IDP_IDS` read onto both Access sign-ins, enforced in `verifyAccessJwt` with `email_verified` strictness; `everyapp access show` lists provider ids and the pin hint; register row `access.idp-allowlist`; gap 19 marked fixed; runbook section.
- [x] Finding 8 (#716): the token dialog's access level, one-app and 30-day defaults; register row `pat.ui-least-privilege-defaults`; gap 21 marked fixed; user page, skill and agent page updated.
- [x] Finding 2 (#715): the AppGateway entrypoint refuses `dispatch`-tier rows (403 `caller_tier_refused`) before route parsing; register row `tier.dispatch-no-capability-broker`; gap 46 marked fixed; the code is in the agent identity page's table. Default taken: a flat refusal rather than a per-capability hosted grant table (the hosted tier is off; the table is the design when it comes back).
- [x] Finding 13 (#714): OpenRouter's `models` fallback list passes the manifest's `ai.models` entry by entry (403 `model_not_allowed` naming the offender), a malformed `models` is 400; gap 28 marked fixed. Default taken: `provider` routing preferences (`order`, `only`, `ignore`, quantizations) are forwarded, since they pick a provider for the named model rather than another model.
- [x] Findings 9 and 10 (#713): `anthropic-workspace-id` and `X-HF-Bill-To` stripped (case-insensitively, as every header) by the AI proxy and the capability forward; catalog notes updated; register rows `ai.key-never-reaches-app` and `cap.credentials-stripped-no-redirect` extended. Default taken: stripped rather than set from a Gateway-owned binding (there is no per-organization Hugging Face or Anthropic workspace setting; the key decides).
- [x] Finding 12 (#712): requested ∩ supported ∩ approved in `grantedScopes`/`grantedAccess`, the capped access on the connection row, the write choice hidden when not requested; gap 29 marked fixed; agent docs (mcp, troubleshooting), the runbook and the everyapp skill quote the new challenge.
- [x] Finding 14 (#711): the Access plugin enforces the outer ring's assertion whenever the ring is configured and signs people in only with a usable `GATEWAY_URL`; register row `access.outer-ring.enforced-without-gateway-url`; gap 23 marked fixed. Default taken: a missing `GATEWAY_URL` is a logged problem, not a refused startup (a gateway that can't start can't be fixed from `everyapp update` either).

### Setup skills (2026-10-24)

Agent-facing setup files for every integration the launch apps need, under the everyapp skill: `packages/cli/skills/everyapp/setup/README.md` (the index and the rules every setup follows) and one file each for gateway, access, email, dataforseo, context, openrouter, openai, workers-ai, google, microsoft-365 and slack; `everyapp create` copies them into each app's `.claude/skills/everyapp/` and `.agents/skills/everyapp/`. Decisions taken first (yes keeps each), then what the files depend on as of 2026-10-24, then the follow-ups the reviews and the harness runs left.

- [ ] **One skill, supporting files**, not one invocable skill per integration: the everyapp skill's "Set up:" entry routes to `setup/README.md`, which routes to the file. Alternative: sibling skills (`everyapp-setup-google`) that Claude Code lists and triggers on their own descriptions; `linkSkill` would copy `packages/cli/skills/*`.
- [ ] **A value never goes through the chat.** The agent's shell has no terminal, so `secret set` never prompts for it; the files use one of three ways: the person runs `pnpm everyapp secret set NAME` in a second terminal (the default when the value is on a dashboard), a command that produced the value is piped straight in (`az ad app credential reset … | pnpm everyapp secret set …`, `jq -r .web.client_secret client.json | …`, Slack's `apps.manifest.create`), or `--from-env VAR` for a variable the person exported. Follow-up: a `secret set NAME --link` that prints a one-time URL where the person pastes the value into the Gateway's admin UI, so the agent path needs no second terminal.
- [ ] **The `claude -p` harness is manual** (`packages/cli/test/setup-skills/`: a fake everyapp shim with the real NDJSON shapes and a scripted person): not in CI, needs a logged-in `claude`. The static test is what CI runs.
- [ ] **Not covered:** Postiz's social networks (not launching), the hosted Gateway (`--mode hosted`: the runbook), a Search Console and Analytics Google client (the gateway-held Google integration in `docs/design/2026-10-google-integration.md` is not built; OpenSEO shows those as coming), X (an app's own secret).
- [ ] **PR #379 merged: `access on|off|show` is a route the CLI has.** The static test (`packages/cli/src/lib/app/setupSkills.test.ts`) no longer carries an `access` exception: every `pnpm everyapp …` a file names must be in `commandPaths()`.
- [ ] **OAuth scope lists are the registry's for all three clients: the Google and Microsoft sets merged with #398, Slack's with #425 (d0c3b73f)** (`packages/sdk/src/internal/oauthScopes.ts`; `scopePolicy` on the `google-workspace` and `microsoft-365` entries in `capabilities.ts`; [docs/design/2026-10-oauth-scopes.md](docs/design/2026-10-oauth-scopes.md)): google.md and microsoft-365.md describe the sets, not a flat scope list; Slack's sets are pending ("OAuth scopes (2026-10-24)": its policy ships only if its PRs are in by Saturday), so slack.md keeps the `slack` entry's flat `authorize.scopes` and cites this bullet by its first words. The console-side rule stays: the operator's client is allowed the union of the provider scopes behind the sets of every approved app, not the registry's whole catalog.
- [x] **Verify sections read `secret list --json`** (`data.gateway.needed[].set`, `data.gateway.missing`) until the secrets-model PR #383 and its CLI lane land. Done in #493 (f2246b9d): the Verify sections read `status --json` `data.apps[].setup` and `secret list <app> --json` `data.app.setup`; `secret set NAME --app <id>` and `secret list [<app>]` are documented.
- [ ] **The file names are a contract: do not rename them.** #383 adds a registry default `setup: "setup/<name>.md"` beside `docs` (`aiProviders.ts` and `capabilities.ts`; a manifest may carry it for an app's own secret), surfaced on every setting in `status --json` and `secret list --json`, with this mapping: openai (the AI routes too), openrouter, workers-ai, dataforseo, context, email, google-workspace → google, microsoft-365, slack; its CLI lane tests that each named file exists under `packages/cli/skills/everyapp`.
- [ ] **A Gateway missing or holding a wrong `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` has no everyapp repair.** `setup` sends the install-owned secrets with the first upload only (`firstDeploy` in `lib/gateway/apply.ts`; `stagedSecrets` is for tests) and `secret set` refuses the name: `setup` should add a missing install-owned setting on upgrade, and `status` should report one that is missing (workers-ai.md review).
- [ ] **`lib/cf/account.ts` overstates the device login and understates the token.** `DEVICE_LOGIN_TIMEOUT_MS` is 15 minutes, so `data.expiresAt` says 15 while cf 1.0.0-beta.14 gives up after 5 (`Math.min(expires_in, 300)`); `CLOUDFLARE_TOKEN_REJECTED` names Workers, D1, KV and Zone DNS Read but not Zone › Zone › Read, which `cf zones list` needs. gateway.md says both out loud; fix the code, then drop its caveats (gateway.md review).
- [ ] **`landing-page/content/docs/domain.mdx` and `index.mdx` still say a `.dev` or `.app` is $1 the first year with Workers Paid**; no Cloudflare page says so (Registrar sells at cost; price trackers list about $8.20 the first year): correct them (gateway.md review).
- [ ] **docs/capabilities.md's Workers AI token wording was settled on 2026-10-24 against Cloudflare's REST guide**, with workers-ai.md: the Workers AI page's prefilled "Create a Workers AI API Token" grants Workers AI Read, which every `/accounts/<account>/ai/*` call needs; a token made by hand needs Read and Edit. The operator table and the AI providers section say so now.
- [ ] **agentic-inbox's own comments still call Workers AI its default.** The fork's `cloudflare.config.ts` has defaulted `AI_PROVIDER` to `openrouter` since 2026-10-19 and openrouter.md says so now, but its `everyapp.config.ts` ("Workers AI by default", "the agent's default") and `docs/EVERY_APP.md` ("For Workers AI (the default)"; a template token with "Read and Edit") say the opposite: fix them in the inbox repository.
- [x] **Whether `access on --team` creates the Zero Trust organization on an account that never onboarded is unverified.** Verified on the loop account 2026-10-24 (#616: `organization create` works with no dashboard step) and wired 2026-10-25: `getAccessOrganization()` reads `access_not_enabled` as "no team", `access on` creates it, then reads providers and policies. access.md no longer sends anyone through the dashboard.
- [ ] **The agent docs pages at everyapp.com/docs/agent/ publish with the launch.** The README's deep references point at them (`https://everyapp.com/docs/agent/`, each page as `/docs/agent/<page>.md`) and say they go live on 2026-10-27, with this checkout's `docs/` and `--help` as the reference until then; the agent-docs PR adds the pages ([docs/launch-2026-10-27.md](docs/launch-2026-10-27.md)), and the site must be deployed with them or the links lead nowhere.
- [ ] **The dashboard tab for the Gateway's logs is unverified in a browser.** The README and every setup file say **Workers & Pages** → `every-app-gateway` → **Observability** (**Logs** on older accounts) → **Live**, from Cloudflare's docs; nobody clicked it today.

### Onboarding audit (2026-10-24)

Both first-time journeys were walked against main (bc3fe476) for the launch: a fresh Cloudflare account through `setup`, the owner link, the Gateway's settings and `access on` (the setup flow driven through the test fakes, every prompt and transcript captured), and the five launch apps (todo, openseo, agentic-inbox, crm, analytics) from clean checkouts with `pnpm install`, `everyapp dev`, `secret list`, `email show` and `deploy --bundle-only` (since stage 4, `release`), plus an agent-driven `--json` run and a docs-against-code pass. 137 raw findings merged to 77; each fix candidate was checked by two independent reviewers before it was built. What shipped: #389 and the docs follow-up (the first-time pages, README, template README, runbooks), #399 (`create` names the app after itself and says `pnpm run deploy`), #400 (`status`), #401 (`setup`), the app-commands and dev PRs, and the CLI polish PR. Findings that belong to the secrets model, the setup skills, the agent docs and the simplify thread were sent to them with file pointers; the per-app doc fixes in the app repositories follow the secrets thread's manifest PRs. Defaults the fixes shipped with; yes keeps each.

- [ ] **An available Gateway update is not a problem.** `status` lists it after everything to fix, as a dim `i` ("Your every-app folder has a newer Gateway than the one online"), with `pnpm everyapp gateway deploy`; `--json` `fixes[]` carry `severity` and `note`. Before, it counted as "1 problem to fix" with `Run: pnpm everyapp setup`, and an agent following the skill upgraded production after every `git pull`. Alternative: keep `setup` as the one upgrade verb (it still works) and drop `gateway deploy` from the docs. (stage 4, 2026-10-24: `gateway deploy` is now `everyapp update`)
- [ ] **Setup's done screen names Access.** A fresh self-hosted install ends with four next steps: `create my-app`, `access on` ("recommended"), `secret set OPENAI_API_KEY` ("or OPENROUTER_API_KEY, WORKERS_AI_API_TOKEN"), `secret set EMAIL_FROM EMAIL_FROM_NAME`. The hosted branch of that list was left as it was (`secret set EMAIL_FROM` alone, which doesn't configure a sender): give it the same two lines without Access, or delete it.
- [ ] **`setup --yes` answers No to "Configure browser app installs?"** (it was declared and never read). The question is still asked on every interactive run, including a rerun with nothing to do; `deploy --yes` that installs a Gateway on the way forwards `yes` too (the CLI polish PR). Alternative: ask only on the install that creates the owner, and point at `secret set CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN` afterwards.
- [ ] **`DOMAIN_REQUIRED` carries a command.** Unattended `setup` without `--domain` reruns with `--domain apps.<zone>` when the account has one zone (the `fix` prose stays), else `data.choices` like `choose_account`. `install_gateway` from `status`, `secret`, `email` and `db` still reruns a bare `setup`; from `deploy` it may carry a literal `<hostname>` when no zone is known.
- [ ] **`create` names the app from its id** ("my-app" is "My App"; the template's description stays) and retitles the README's first H1; its next step says `pnpm run deploy` like the docs and AGENTS.md, while the created-but-not-deployed error still says `pnpm everyapp deploy` (reruns may carry flags). The skill says `pnpm everyapp deploy --json`; three spellings of one command remain.
- [ ] **`dev` says what `.dev.vars` is missing**, from the manifest's `secrets` and the SDK's dev stand-in table for its `providers` and `capabilities`, in stub and mirror mode alike; for an app with the `email` capability the line also names the optional transport settings (EMAIL_REST_API_TOKEN, RESEND_API_KEY, EMAIL_PROVIDER) because the table has no required/optional mark. Switch it to the secrets model's `appSettings(manifest)` once #383 merges, so it says "(optional)".
- [ ] **`status`'s Access row reads only the Gateway Worker's setting names and the `_everyapp_access` record**, never the Zero Trust API (that is `access show`); a cf failure there is a counted `ACCESS_UNKNOWN` warning. The Gateway's email sender has no row; the secrets thread's SETTINGS table may cover it.
- [ ] **A Free account probably installs anyway.** `setup` detects Workers Paid from `cf accounts subscriptions get`; a paid account's rows read as Paid, but a Free account most likely has no `workers_*` row at all, reads as "unknown", gets a warning and installs a Gateway that stops at D1's daily limit (the CLI's own `plan.ts` says the response shape was never checked against a live account; cf's `free_plan` refusal text has no string to match). Not changed: the one thing to do before launch is run `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID=<a fresh Free account> cf accounts subscriptions get` and paste its Workers rows into `plan.ts`'s test; treating "unknown" as Free would block paid accounts whose rows look different.
- [ ] **Whether a fresh account's first upload needs a workers.dev subdomain is unverified.** The audit's transcript of that stop came from the test fake, not from Cloudflare; cf's own code only demands the subdomain for Workers that define Workflows, which the Gateway doesn't. The CLI's handling (`register_workers_subdomain`, exit 2, a rerun that resumes after the database and KV namespace exist) stays, the troubleshooting entry stays, and the docs don't list it as a prerequisite; the open live check in "Check on the first live deploy" (the real error text when an account has no subdomain) decides it.
- [ ] **The SDK's dist is wiped while `dev` runs.** `deploy`, `release` (until stage 4, `deploy --bundle-only`) and any `pnpm install` from an app inside the checkout run the workspace install, whose root `postinstall` deletes and rebuilds `packages/sdk/dist`, so a running `everyapp dev` answers "Failed to load url …/packages/sdk/dist/server/index.js" for a few seconds (seen with todo and agentic-inbox). Options: drop the `prebuild` `rmSync('dist')` (tsc overwrites; stale files only matter after a rename), build into a temporary directory and swap, or have the CLI's install step pass `--ignore-scripts` for apps inside the checkout (the launcher already rebuilds the SDK when its sources change). Not changed.
- [ ] **`secret set` still hides plain values.** EMAIL_FROM, EMAIL_FROM_NAME, EMAIL_PROVIDER, EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_IN, the CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_* values and the other plain settings are typed into the hidden prompt and never validated (a wrong EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_IN is only reported in Worker logs). #383 adds `isPlainGatewaySetting`; the onboarding thread builds the visible prompt and the switch validation after it merges.
- [ ] **`email set --sender` doesn't check Email Sending onboarding** unless the CLI polish PR found it cheap; otherwise the first failure is at send time (`email_sender_not_verified`), though `email set --help` calls onboarding a requirement.
- [ ] **Help text says `everyapp`, docs say `pnpm everyapp`.** stricli prints the binary name, and the examples in `app.ts` and `setup --help`'s "Then:" line say `everyapp …` while every page and the skill say `pnpm everyapp …` until publishing resumes. Left for the simplify thread's help pass.
- [ ] **Argument errors exit 251 and 252** (unknown command, unknown flag or bad value) with `data.help`, while the contract everywhere says 0, 1 and 2 with `fix`/`rerun`/`authorize`. The agent docs document them; not changed.
- [ ] **`dev`'s log mixes the perimeter's JSON proxy lines into the human output** and discards the app's own startup output (`longRunning.ts` keeps only what came after ready); `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` answers a JSON 404 instead of the launcher.
- [ ] **Dashboard names and prices differ** across the docs ("Register domains", $1 for the first year of a .dev), the README ("Domains → Buy a domain", about $10 a year for a .com) and the CLI's `add_domain` text. Pick one set.
- [ ] **Ported apps link `../private-every-app`** and no README says to clone the platform beside the app: a lone clone of OpenCRM or OpenAnalytics installs with dangling `link:` symlinks and then fails with "everyapp: command not found". Goes with publishing; until then the app READMEs say so (the per-app docs PRs).
- [ ] **Per-app docs PRs** in private-open-seo-everyapp, private-open-crm, private-open-analytics and private-agentic-inbox-everyapp, after the secrets thread's manifest PRs (#10, #4, #7, #3) land: the removed `--for gateway` flag in READMEs, runbooks and agentic-inbox's `check-runbook-secrets.ts` (which also imports a function the CLI no longer exports), AGENTS.md pointers to the everyapp skill, OpenCRM's "needs no settings" and "one optional setting", OpenAnalytics' README env vars nothing can set and its `db:generate` line, the clone-beside-the-app note, OpenCRM's hand-made Access bypass line (`access on` does it from `public`), a `pnpm-workspace.yaml` with `allowBuilds` for esbuild and workerd in open-crm and open-seo-everyapp (open-analytics ships one; a created app gets it from `starter.ts`), and OpenCRM's dev Connections page showing the MCP address as `https://gateway.dev.localhost/mcp` (it reads the dev identity issuer; `everyapp dev` printed `http://crm.localhost:<port>/mcp`).
- [ ] **Not detected, by design or by cost:** Node 18 crashed in the launcher before the friendly version message (fixed in the CLI polish PR if it landed); a `pnpm install` never run shows pnpm's `Command "everyapp" not found` (now a troubleshooting entry); `CHECKOUT_MISSING` only fires for an npx or global copy.

### Teams (2026-10-24)

The teams swath for the launch (design in [docs/design/2026-10-teams.md](docs/design/2026-10-teams.md)): usage counters (#381, merged 2026-10-24) and Admin → Usage (#382, merged 2026-10-24 as `177830b7`), opt-in per-app monthly caps (#416, merged 2026-10-24 as `01dcc5c8`), groups with app grants (#384 merged 2026-10-24 as `94a670a4`, #385 as `878bae6a`), and the judge's swap, Admin → Activity (#434, merged 2026-10-24 as `4451b350`). Six readers read `main`, three rankers scored the gaps, a judge settled the order. Defaults the slice shipped with, as the code does them (checked against the PR diffs; the last nine bullets were taken by the builders and their reviewers after the design); confirm or change each.

- [ ] **Counting rules.** Only provider calls are counted: `api_key` capabilities (DataForSEO, Context) and OAuth-client token calls, where `integration.proxy.call` is logged, plus every AI proxy call where `ai.proxy.usage` is logged. The gateway's own `email` and `members` services log their own events and aren't counted; the `target` column takes them when wanted. Tokens only when the provider's response reports them (the existing tail tap); a call with none still counts as a call; OAuth-client calls count at 0 micros. Refused calls (policy, cap, 4xx before upstream) aren't counted; an upstream 4xx or 5xx counts as a call and a failure. Decide: capture OpenRouter's per-response `usage.cost` into `cost_micros`, force `stream_options.include_usage` on streamed chat (OpenAI then streams one more chunk with `usage` and an empty `choices` array before `[DONE]`, which a client reading `choices[0]` unguarded mishandles, so it changes what apps see, not only what the gateway counts), and put the user id on `ai.proxy.usage`, in the same lane (about half a day); and whether a member sees their own usage under `/settings`, as a member's own Activity view is planned.
- [ ] **`user_id = ''` for the app's own calls.** Background work (crons, Workflows, `for: { background: true }`) and an organization's own app whose forwarded identity expired or failed to verify both land under `''`, shown as one "Background" row in By member. Alternative: refuse an expired identity on an organization's own app as a shared app's is refused, so By member is exact.
- [ ] **No foreign keys on `usage_daily` and `usage_monthly`**, like `mcp_tool_calls`: the trail outlives the user and the app. Names are joined at read time (left joins), so a deleted app or user shows by id.
- [ ] **Retention 13 months daily, 25 months monthly**, counted from the first of the current month, both deletes in the hourly `runMcpMaintenance` with one summary field each. The month picker browses the last 12 UTC months only.
- [ ] **Cap semantics.** `org_app_settings(organization_id, app_row_id)`, the install row the hosted research reserved (removed by #433; `docs/hosted-vision.md` section 5 keeps the row as the walk-back), with `monthly_call_cap` NULL by default (unlimited; Ben's 2026-10-16 ruling that limits are a later feature and never on by default). The unit is calls per UTC calendar month, 1 to 1,000,000,000, AI and capability calls alike, read from `usage_monthly` in one row; a refused call isn't counted. The month is UTC, so the Autumn catalog's usage limits later take `anchor: "utc"` to match. A later `monthly_credit_cap` sits beside it, never replaces it, and enforcement moves to the OrgWallet DO with the money lane. Caps are also what the hosted preview waits on: it stays invite-only (`HOSTED_OPEN_SIGNUP=false`) because nothing bounds an organization's spend (`docs/security-model.md:238-244`). Alternative: a per-organization cap across apps (needs an org settings table the research's H22 left unnamed).
- [ ] **The refusal.** 429 with code `app_monthly_cap_exceeded`, a message naming the cap and "an owner or admin raises it in Admin → Usage", `Retry-After` = seconds until the UTC month ends; logged as `usage.cap_exceeded` (organization, app, cap). Settings cached per isolate 30 s hit, 5 s miss, cleared on set.
- [ ] **Caps are per organization for shared apps.** A hosted organization caps a shared first-party app for itself (the row is per organization; a plain FK on `apps(id)`, since the composite FK to `apps(id, organization_id)` can't hold for a row the platform organization owns). The platform organization's own use attributes to it like any other organization's.
- [ ] **Groups rules.** Names unique per organization, case-insensitively (`GROUP_NAME_TAKEN`); only members of the organization (`USER_NOT_FOUND` rather than a silent drop); a deactivated member may stay in a group (the perimeter refuses them anyway); shared apps can't be granted to a group (`SHARED_APP_ACCESS`); `added_by` and `granted_by` are plain text with no FK, so the ids stay as a trail. groups-2 merged 2026-10-24 with neither: a grant carries no level (viewer, editor) and a group no `idp_group` source; both are additive columns later (roadmap 3.5) that shape `group_app_access` and the Manage apps dialog when they come.
- [ ] **The effective access union.** A member opens an organization's own app with a `user_app_access` row OR through a group holding a `group_app_access` row, one `UNION ALL` subquery (`effective-access.ts`) read at every site: perimeter, launcher (`via: { groupId, name }`, direct wins), admin modal (`viaGroups`), members capability, MCP catalogs, access counts, `findAllWithoutAccess`. Saving the Manage access modal diffs direct grants only and never touches group access.
- [ ] **Member removal cleanup.** `removeMember` deletes the member's `group_members` rows in the same D1 batch as the membership, their grants and their PATs; the gateway's app delete (`AppRepository.tombstone`) and the CLI's `everyapp delete` both drop the app's group grants (the CLI's side is the "CLI delete and the groups table" bullet below).
- [ ] **Which PRs were held, and how they merged.** usage-1 (#381), the additive counters, merged 2026-10-24 as `5ef30882`; usage-2 (#382), the view, as `177830b7` under the same rule, and the design doc with this section as #390 (`5dde75a3`); Admin → Activity (#434, additive admin UI like usage-2) as `4451b350`, to confirm after the fact. caps (#416, a refusal path on provider calls), groups-1 (#384) and groups-2 (#385; both change the app access check at every site) were held as security boundaries until Ben's 2026-10-24 rule, merge everything green with security boundaries included, and merged in that order: #384 as `94a670a4`, #385 as `878bae6a`, #416 as `01dcc5c8`, each rebased on main and green on CI, with an independent audit of each rebase against its pre-rebase diff (nothing dropped). For the morning interview: caps are opt-in and null by default (the 2026-10-16 ruling stands); groups carry no level and no IdP source yet; nothing is deployed, so the live gateway runs neither `0005_groups` nor `0006_org_app_settings` until Ben's "go". Migrations on main: `0004_app_secret_names` (#383), `0005_groups` (#384), `0006_org_app_settings` (#416), each regenerated at its rebase with identical SQL and a chained snapshot; none is in `SHIPPED_MIGRATIONS` until the live gateway has run it. The two `docs/security-model.md` amendments (the cap paragraph, wording in the #416 body; the `group_app_access` line) are now due in the threat-model thread's file (recorded on main as a TODO item by #426).
- [ ] **Admin → Activity (#434, merged 2026-10-24 as `4451b350`).** Read-only over `mcp_tool_calls`, no migration, no access-check change: `listToolCalls` (keyset on the `(organization_id, created_at)` index, always this organization, timestamps bounded to the Date range) and `listToolCallApps`, both owner/admin. Defaults: the range is All time (the trail keeps `MCP_AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS`, 90 by default, and the widest dated range is 30 d, so the "No agent tool calls yet" state is only truthful that way); a removed app stays listed as "Removed app" (a tombstone keeps its name, a row that is gone shows its slug); the App filter lists the apps the organization's calls name, so a shared app can be picked in hosted mode, cached on the client for five minutes; the tab sits after Usage; digest and byte counts only, never arguments; no `(organization_id, app_row_id)` index until D1 timings ask for one. Decide: a member's own view, a member filter in the UI, and a CSV button are roadmap items.
- [ ] **Personal access tokens.** The brief's "30-day default" is the deploy key's (`DEPLOY_KEY_TTL_MS`); an access token defaults to 90 days (`DEFAULT_TTL_MS`), `*`, up to a year, and `createUserToken` is member-level while the only Tokens page is under the owner/admin-gated `/admin` (`tokens.tsx:83`). Decide: move the page to `/settings/tokens` for members with a 30-day default, or gate creation to owners and admins as the UI implies; and which preset is the default, since an `mcp:read` or `mcp:write` token reaches `/mcp` only and is refused with `token_scope` on an app's own routes (`packages/perimeter/src/session.ts:25-37`), so a read-only default must be named as agent tools only (MCP) or Full access stays the default.
- [ ] **Owners.** One owner per install and no path to a second. Decide: may an owner make an admin a co-owner, may owners demote owners, and is a transfer (actor demoted to admin) wanted?
- [ ] **Hosted offboarding.** Hosted admins can't deactivate; removing a member leaves their browser sessions until the next organization-scoped check, and on self-host Reactivate restores every app the person had. Decide: should removal also end the member's sessions in that organization, and should Deactivate offer "Clear app access" (default keep)?
- [ ] **The IdP groups carrier**, decided now so groups-2 leaves room for the column even though the build waits: the OIDC `groups` scope on the SSO-button path, the outer-ring JWT's `custom` claim (trimmed past about 1 KB), or a server-side `/cdn-cgi/access/get-identity` call at sign-in.
- [ ] **Launch docs.** State plainly that AI and research calls are billed by your provider and uncapped by the Gateway unless an admin sets a cap (the cost table in `index.mdx` and the README's "No credits, no limits" say nothing today), that on self-host the Cloudflare account is the deploy role, that Deactivate is the offboarding action, which Cloudflare account role a log reader gets versus a deployer (Workers Logs are read with an account permission, redacted by key name only, and kept 3 days on Workers Free and 7 on Paid), and that SSO means self-host behind Cloudflare Access, whose Zero Trust seats are free to 50 users and $7 per user per month after. Words only; the landing page is never deployed unasked.
- [ ] **OpenSEO's `[research-spend]` line.** Keep it beside the gateway counters (it knows projects; the gateway sees calls), or retire it once the gateway records DataForSEO cost and OpenRouter tokens per organization?
- [ ] **A call is counted when the provider answers.** The moment its headers arrive, with that status, under `waitUntil` before the Response goes out (in workerd the gateway isn't told when a browser or an app Worker stops reading the body, and the provider bills from the answer); tokens and cost are added by a second write (`UsageEvent.calls` 0) only when the body was read to the end (the head of the body, for DataForSEO's `cost`). So an abandoned stream is a call with no tokens, a dropped capability body a call with no cost, a provider failing mid-body a call and not a failure. `ai.proxy.usage` says `ended: cancelled | failed` when a reader in the gateway's own isolate cancelled or the provider failed mid-body, absent on a fully read body; a closed tab on everyapp.team doesn't produce it. Alternative: count only bodies read to the end, which undercounts what the provider bills.
- [ ] **Former members in Admin → Usage.** A person who's no longer a member of the organization shows as "Former member" with their id (By member joins `members` for this organization, then `users`), never with the profile they keep in another organization; their own organization still sees their name. Alternative: join `users` directly, which shows a profile across organizations on the hosted gateway.
- [ ] **Usage prune bounds.** 1,000 rows per statement, at most 10 statements per table per hourly run (10,000 rows of each), so a month of daily rows ages out over the hours after a month boundary; the `mcp.maintenance` line carries `usage_behind` while rows remain.
- [ ] **The cap check is soft.** `calls >= cap` read from the one `usage_monthly` row, counts landing under `waitUntil` after the provider answers, so a burst can run a few calls past the cap; a hard stop would need a counter read in the request path (the `rate_limit_counters` pattern). A cap counts exactly what `usage_monthly` counts (AI and provider capability calls, OAuth-client token calls, error answers included); the gateway's own `email` and `members` services are neither counted nor capped (`parseIntegrationProxyPath` kind `service`, before `admit`), and an app with no registry row is never capped.
- [ ] **The cap's edges.** Cached per isolate 30 s when set, 5 s when none, 500 entries, the one (organization, app) entry cleared by `setAppMonthlyCap`, so a cap binds at once where Admin was served and within seconds elsewhere; clearing deletes the `org_app_settings` row (the table holds nothing else yet); `updated_by` is an FK to users, set null; the app must be the organization's own or a shared app (`AppRepository.findCallableById`), else `APP_NOT_FOUND`; the 429 is OpenAI's error shape on AI routes (`aiRefusal` now takes headers) and the capability refusal shape with "Too Many Requests" on capability routes, `Retry-After` the seconds to the end of the UTC month (up to 31 days); `usage.cap_exceeded` is a `console.warn` with `organizationId`, `appId`, `appRowId`, `cap`, `calls`, `month`, and `usage.cap_set` a `logEvent` with the same plus `userId`.
- [ ] **Cap visibility.** `summarize` gained a sixth batched query over `org_app_settings`: By app lists every app the organization can call today, in every month, zeros included (in hosted mode every shared app for every organization; an app registered after a past month still shows there, no `created_at` filter), so a cap can be set before the first call and is always visible to clear; a capped app with no calls shows with zeros; the current month shows a progress bar and "1,240 of 5,000", a past month the cap alone; a deleted app shows only in a month it spent in, muted, with no cap and no button. When nothing was counted in a month the page shows By app alone with the "counted from now on" note as its hint; the full empty state only when the organization has no apps. Deleting an app (Admin, CLI or the perimeter's `deleteUpdate`) deletes every organization's cap on it, a shared app's included when the platform organization deletes it, like its email configuration, so a revived app starts uncapped; a later hide or disable setting in `org_app_settings` goes the same way.
- [ ] **The groups name index is an expression index**, UNIQUE on (`organization_id`, `lower(name)`) (SQLite 3.9+; drizzle-kit emits `lower("name")`), the service mapping the constraint failure to `GROUP_NAME_TAKEN`, rather than a plain unique index plus a service check. Confirm an expression index on D1 is fine.
- [ ] **CLI delete and the groups table.** `everyapp delete` deletes `group_app_access` in its registry batch now, following #352's precedent: the first delete from a checkout with `0004` against a gateway not yet upgraded fails on the missing table until `setup` or `deploy` migrates it. Alternative: a one-release delay, which leaves a group's grant on the tombstone so a redeploy of the same slug restores it for the group.
- [ ] **Manage access wording.** Unchecking a user who holds a direct grant and is also in a granting group shows a neutral "Direct grant removed" badge and the footer counts them as "kept through groups"; a user with group access only is checked and disabled with "via <group>", and Grant all skips them; Revoke all leaves the acting admin's own grant. Confirm the wording.
The roadmap after the slice is under Build, "Teams roadmap" (the design doc's section 5); it absorbs the earlier Build lines for approvals, the Agent activity page, token access levels and ownership transfer, so none of that is tracked twice.

### Caps and budgets (2026-10-24)

Ben's three questions on the evening of 2026-10-24 (a default cap, so apps spend, say, $20 a month and stop at the limit until someone raises it; an email when a budget is hit, where email sending is set up; what "caps count AI and provider calls alike" means), answered by the caps swath: the gateway side is #621, merged 2026-10-25 as `4936ea42` (migration `0014_caps_defaults`: `org_settings` and `org_app_settings.unlimited`; the effective cap, the nameless refusal, the Paused status, the Usage page's Default monthly cap row and dialogs, `cap.raised` and the Cap alerts row from both cap writes; the alerts themselves are the Events-out thread's, below); the docs are the caps docs PR ([landing-page/content/docs/usage.mdx](landing-page/content/docs/usage.mdx)); the CLI and MCP verbs are the Agent parity thread's (below). Defaults the swath shipped with, as the code does them; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **The default cap is off.** `org_settings.default_monthly_call_cap` is null until an owner or admin sets it under Manage workspace → Usage ("Default monthly cap"), so the 2026-10-16 ruling stands: nothing is capped until someone caps it, and a new organization starts with no default.
- [ ] **Per-app override and explicit no-cap.** An app's own cap replaces the default for that app; "No cap" (`org_app_settings.unlimited`) leaves one app uncapped while the default stands; "Remove cap" deletes the row, so the app inherits the default (with no default, unlimited, as before). The rule: the app's row, then the default, then nothing.
- [ ] **The row invariant.** An `org_app_settings` row holds exactly one of (`monthly_call_cap` set, `unlimited` false) or (`monthly_call_cap` null, `unlimited` true); the Gateway's writer sets both columns in one statement (`OrgAppSettingsRepository.setMonthlyCap`); the CLI's upsert sets the cap alone (the ask on #617 below). The reader fails safe: a row holding a cap keeps the app capped whatever `unlimited` says; a row with neither (from before the column) falls through to the default.
- [x] **Cap alerts are the Events-out thread's.** The launch orchestrator ruled on 2026-10-25 that cap alerts are one path, the events system's ([docs/design/2026-10-events-out.md](docs/design/2026-10-events-out.md)), and #621 merged that way: `checkMonthlyCap` answers `{ exceeded, cap, calls, month, source }` on every capped check, and the AppGateway entrypoint and the provider call hand that standing to `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/events/cap-events.ts` (`noteCapCheck`, under `waitUntil`, after the answer), which emits `cap.warning` at 80% and `cap.reached` with `app.paused_cap` at the cap, once per organization, app, UTC month and cap (raising re-arms); `AppCapService.setMonthlyCap` and `setDefaultMonthlyCap` make the built-in "Cap alerts" subscription with the organization's first cap, the default included (`EventSubscriptionService.ensureCapAlerts`: owners and admins, `cap.warning` + `cap.reached`, mailed once the Gateway can send), and emit `cap.raised` when the cap that binds ends higher or gone (the default's with `app: null` and `source: "default"`; a lowered cap emits nothing; for an app the two caps compared are the ones that bound it, so a cap set under the default is no raise and `unlimited` under a default is a removal); an admin disables the subscription under Manage workspace → Events or with `everyapp events disable`; a webhook subscribed to `cap.*` gets the same events. The notice mail this swath first built (owners and admins, one mail each, at 80% and 100%, a `notice` mail kind with its own budgets, a `usage_cap_notices` table) was deleted before the merge and is kept on branch `t3/caps-notices-reference`.
- [ ] **Refusals name the role only.** The 429 body and the MCP app's tool error say the app, the cap (and that it is the workspace default, when it is), that its AI and provider calls are refused until the UTC month ends, and that an owner or admin raises it under Manage workspace → Usage (`capRefusalMessage` in `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-cap.ts`, quoted in the agent docs); never a name or an address, because the body reaches the app's code and, through an MCP app, a connected agent (`members.agent-refused`). The names are on the Usage page, where the reader is already an admin; the refusal path reads no members.
- [ ] **Paused is a launcher status.** A capped app's card keeps its link (the app opens; only its AI and provider calls are refused) and shows one "Paused" chip in the status slot of both layouts, its description line swapped for "Monthly cap reached. An owner or admin can raise it under Manage workspace → Usage." Setup wins: an app that needs setup is never shown as paused; an MCP app at its cap shows Paused in place of its own chip. `CACHE_BUSTER` was bumped for the new `paused` field, which drops everyone's cached apps list once; after that the list never refetches on reload (the Gateway shell item below), so a card's chip can lag until the collection refetches.
- [ ] **The Usage page's words.** By app carries one hint: "Calls the Gateway makes for apps: AI providers and capabilities such as DataForSEO, counted together. Cost only where the provider reports it." The Default monthly cap row above the table reads "Every app without its own cap" with "Unlimited [Set default]" or "1,000 provider calls [Edit]". The cap cell reads "Unlimited [Set cap]", "240 of 1,000 · default [Edit]", "No cap [Edit]", or "Paused · 1,000 of 1,000" in red with the button "Raise", which opens the app's dialog as "Raise the monthly cap for X" on "Custom cap" prefilled with the effective cap (an app override even when the pause came from the default; the dialog says "The default itself is changed in the Default monthly cap row."). With a default the app's dialog offers "Workspace default (1,000 calls)", "No cap" and "Custom cap"; without one, a number and "Remove cap"; the default's dialog has "Remove default". Both dialogs share one description ("Provider calls per UTC month, AI providers and capabilities together. Once reached, the Gateway refuses the app's calls until the month ends or the cap is raised.") then the alerts sentence ("Owners and admins are emailed at 80% and at the cap once the Gateway can send mail; change that under Manage workspace → Events.") and the link "Usage and caps" (/docs/usage), one component for both (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/client/components/admin/CapDialogDescription.tsx`). "N so far this month." always shows for the current month (the UI review item below).
- [ ] **"Manage workspace → Usage" is the spelling.** Settled by this swath for the refusals, the cards and the docs it wrote; the Docs sweep item below is closed by it. "Admin → Usage" is left in `packages/cli/skills/everyapp/SKILL.md` (the MCP-app paragraph, which also knows no default cap), `landing-page/content/agent/events.md` (two "not counted in Admin → Usage" lines), `landing-page/content/agent/mcp.md` (two lines), `landing-page/content/agent/workspace.md` ("As in Admin → Usage") and `packages/toolbus/src/workspace.ts` (the `workspace_app_cap` tool's description, which every MCP client reads), each another thread's, and in the dated design docs and the launch page on purpose. "Admin →" for the other tabs stays in messages the swath did not touch: `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/auth.ts` (two deploy-key messages, "Admin → Tokens (Deploy key)"), `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.ts` (the no-release message), `packages/cli/src/commands/deploy/steps/waitForLive.ts` ("Admin → Apps"), `packages/cli/src/commands/mcp/index.ts` and `packages/cli/src/commands/mcp/command.ts` ("Admin → Apps → Manage access", echoed in the generated cli.md), `packages/cli/src/commands/workspace/activity.ts` ("Admin → Tokens"), the services' reset-link and invitation messages ("Admin → Users"), the setup skills' "Admin → Users" and "Admin → Apps" (`packages/cli/skills/everyapp/setup/access.md`, `remote-mcp.md`, on purpose), code comments throughout, and docs/ and the agent pages as "Admin → Apps", "Admin → Users", "Admin → Activity", "Admin → Tokens", "Admin → Groups" and "Admin → Events". One sweep, or leave the comments.
- [ ] **The CLI and MCP twins.** #617 merged 2026-10-25 as `a854e12a` with `everyapp workspace usage` and `workspace app cap <app> <n|off>` (`off` deletes the row: the UI's "Remove cap", which under a default means inherit it); in the write's own D1 batch its cap verb makes the Cap alerts row and queues `cap.raised`, as the Gateway does (`setMonthlyCap` in `packages/cli/src/lib/gateway/workspace.ts`). Left for the Agent parity thread now that #621 is on main (its plan in [docs/design/2026-10-agent-parity.md](docs/design/2026-10-agent-parity.md)): `workspace app cap <app> --unlimited` (the no-cap row: `monthly_call_cap` null, `unlimited` 1), `workspace cap default <n|off>` and `everyapp_workspace_cap_default` (the twins of `usage.setDefaultMonthlyCap`, whose matrix row has none today), `unlimited?` on `everyapp_workspace_app_cap`, and `workspace usage` showing each app's effective cap, its source, `unlimited` and `paused`. Three asks on #617's code as merged: its upsert sets `monthly_call_cap` alone, so `workspace app cap <app> 500` on an app set to No cap leaves a row holding both (the Gateway reads the cap, which fails safe, but the row breaks the invariant above): set `unlimited = 0` in the same statement; its `cap.raised` compares the row's own column, not the cap that bound the app, so under a default a first cap below the default is announced as a raise and `off` as a removal, where the Gateway says no raise and a raise to the default (`raised` in `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/AppCapService.ts`); and the verbs that will touch `unlimited` or `org_settings` must refuse a Gateway from before 0014 with a code whose fix is `pnpm everyapp update` (today's verb touches neither, so it needs no refusal). `updated_by` is settled: #617 writes the operator's Gateway account. The human page names `workspace app cap`; the default's verbs get named there when they land.
- [ ] **Migration 0014 into `SHIPPED_MIGRATIONS`** (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/db/release-migrations.test.ts`, which ends at 0008 today) once everyapp.team has run it (`pnpm everyapp update`); until then the live gateway has neither `org_settings` nor `org_app_settings.unlimited`, and the merged CLI's cap verb touches neither.
- [ ] **Left in the docs after the caps docs PR.** `packages/cli/skills/everyapp/SKILL.md` (the MCP-app paragraph: "Admin → Usage", no default cap), `landing-page/content/agent/gateway.md`'s Usage row ("Unlimited by default, and a Set cap dialog": no Default monthly cap row, no Paused) and `landing-page/content/agent/workspace.md`'s cap row ("Once the workspace default exists") predate #621; `landing-page/content/agent/events.md`'s Cap alerts paragraph says `everyapp workspace app cap` makes no Cap alerts row and no `cap.raised`, which since #617 it does. Each is another thread's page; one pass.
- [x] **A cap in dollars (the "$" question): built 2026-10-25** as the dollar cap beside the call cap, counted on reported cost only ("Model providers (2026-10-25)", the Caps in dollars item). The answer as it stood: A dollar cap needs a cost per call, and the Gateway knows cost only where the provider reports it: DataForSEO and OpenRouter exactly (both in `cost_micros`; OpenRouter's `usage.cost` is read off the answer's tail since #656), OpenAI in tokens only, and none on streamed chat unless `stream_options.include_usage` is forced, which changes every app's stream (the Teams "Counting rules" item above, decision 15; a per-model price table would be hand-kept and stale), Workers AI nothing. So "$" this weekend would be exact for two providers, an estimate for OpenAI and blind for Workers AI. Keep calls now; proposed: `monthly_cost_cap_micros` beside the call cap, enforced on reported cost, with the Usage page naming which providers report cost, gated on decision 15. Two truths the Usage page and the docs carry meanwhile: a cap of N calls bounds the count, not the bill (one call costs a fraction of a cent or dollars, by model and endpoint; your provider's invoice is the bill), and the stop is soft (the check reads a counter written after the provider answers, so a burst runs a few calls past the cap; a hard stop needs a counter in the request path).

### Simplify: addition by subtraction (2026-10-24)

Ben's fifteen decisions are in [docs/simplify-2026-10-24.md](docs/simplify-2026-10-24.md) §1; the hosted-tier alignment contract is [docs/hosted-vision.md](docs/hosted-vision.md). Done: #427 (the example apps, `prompts/`, `.opencode/`, `docs/images`), #429 (the plan), the live `todo` and `postiz` apps deleted from everyapp.team with `--purge` (the Gateway upgraded from main 7608ad00 by the rebind). Defaults taken without a ruling, and follow-ups:

- [x] **`everyapp create --deploy` and the `secret ls` alias went in stage 2** (#441) as dead surface (no doc teaches them; every page teaches create, cd, deploy). Say if you use either.
- [ ] **`everyapp dev --mode mirror` stays for now.** It is undocumented outside docs/TESTING.md and no live app's `pnpm dev` uses it, but its gateway half is under `src/auth` (`src/routes/api/dev/identity.ts`, `config.ts`), so deleting it is a held PR of its own. Delete, or keep as the gateway developers' tool?
- [ ] **The perimeter e2e test has no WebSocket case** (`packages/perimeter/src/integration/e2e.test.ts`; `gateway.test.ts` covers upgrades with a fake fetcher). After `apps/todo-app`, nothing in the repo runs a real Durable Object plus WebSocket app. Add an upgrade case to the fixture app.
- [x] **Postiz fixture SQL.** `packages/cli/src/test/fixtures/postiz/migrations/0001_init.sql` was Postiz's AGPL schema verbatim; replaced with nine statements of our own (seven tables, two indexes), keeping the helper-Worker, Workflow, cron and d1-sql coverage (decision 14).
- [ ] **`npm deprecate @every-app/mcp`** (0.1.0 still installs and advertises the deleted example-apps server). Ben, after launch.
- [ ] **`regenerate-secrets.js` and `scripts/{access-local,mcp-oauth-local}.mjs` stay** (they back the key-rotation, Access and MCP runbooks). Delete any you never run.
- [x] **Stage 3 before launch** landed: #448 (one shared lockfile, `packages/cf-rest` into `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/cf-rest/`, `templates/simple-todo` to `starter/`, a root tsconfig base) and #449 (the landing-page leftovers, decision 15); #451 then pinned one `@types/react` and one `csstype` so the gateway's type-check stops depending on pnpm's hoisting. Directory moves (`gateway/`, `cli/`, a merged platform package) wait until after launch.
- [x] **The MCP skill** `packages/cli/skills/everyapp/mcp.md` landed (#436); `createMcpHandler` and `docs/todo-mcp-example.md` went with it (stage 2b).

(Built on `t3/simplify-4-cli-language`, 2026-10-24: the new verbs are `update`, `install <source>` and `release [--out]`; `deploy` lost its positional, `--bundle-only` and `--out`; `BUNDLE_ONLY_WITH_SOURCE` and `OUT_WITHOUT_BUNDLE_ONLY` went with them.)

### Onboarding loop (2026-10-24)

The loop that proves the home-page setup prompt on any coding agent, from nothing, is designed in [docs/design/2026-10-onboarding-loop.md](docs/design/2026-10-onboarding-loop.md) (four candidates, 24 adversarial reviews; local Docker containers on Ben's Mac won, Modal sandboxes are the fallback behind one interface) and built as `packages/onboarding-loop` (`pnpm loop run --harness <claude|codex|opencode|cursor|hermes|script> --account loop-<n>`). The clean-slate wipe is `everyapp teardown` (#444, merged 2026-10-24 as 08871b76). Test accounts: [docs/runbooks/onboarding-loop-test-accounts.md](docs/runbooks/onboarding-loop-test-accounts.md). Live runs on the dedicated test account started 2026-10-24 (#488, #497, #500, #506, #518 fixed what they found in the loop); the control harness passed end to end (run 9: setup 51 s, the owner link claimed by the loop 1 s after it appeared, create 11 s, deploy 78 s, every outside-in probe green), and the runs found four product defects: Linux builds resolving localhost to ::1 (#492), resume after the workers.dev stop refusing its own Custom Domain (#498), teardown edge cases (#497), and `create` inside the checkout rewriting the lockfile so the first `deploy` rebuilds the Gateway and a fresh clone's `status` says outdated (open, with onboarding-DX). Of the onboarding audit's two live checks: a fresh account's first upload does stop on the workers.dev subdomain, and never again once it is set; the Free-plan subscription rows are still unobserved (the test account is on Workers Paid). The ledger is `docs/onboarding-loop/` (`pnpm loop report`). Defaults to confirm:

- [ ] **Docker Desktop is the substrate.** It is installed and running (4.95.0, engine 29.8.2, arm64); the design brief assumed it wasn't. Raise its VM memory to 16 GiB and turn off automatic updates for launch week; `--parallel 2` is refused below 14 GiB. Alternative: swap `src/docker.ts` for the Modal shim (configured; smoke-tested in 36 s).
- [ ] **A dormant `EVERYAPP_EVENT_LOG` seam ships in the CLI** (the launcher and the event sinks append every invocation, event and exit to the named file; nothing happens without the variable). It is how the loop sees the owner link and every error without parsing harness transcripts, and a bug-report aid for people. Alternative: the D1-side claim only (no CLI change; blind to `--json` being dropped).
- [ ] **The owner claim is immediate by default** (the loop claims the link the moment the CLI mints it), so a `pass` says nothing about whether the agent relayed the link to a person; `linkSurfacedToPerson` records it and `--claim-after turn-end` measures it. Default stays immediate; the report shows both when both exist.
- [ ] **Permission prompts are bypassed on every harness** (`--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`, `--auto`, `--force`, `--yolo`), recorded as `permissionBypass: true`; a visitor-like mode is a later variant.
- [ ] **Claude Code runs in two labelled modes**, `defaults` (a command over 2 minutes is backgrounded, killed at 10 in `-p`) and `raised` (one long-lived stream-json process); the Monday report shows `defaults` first as the stock experience.
- [ ] **Unattended teardown confirmation is a 600 file Ben writes once** (`~/.config/everyapp/onboarding-loop/confirm-<accountId>`), forwarded as `--confirm`; attended runs type the id. There is no `--yes`.
- [ ] **Certificate packs are deleted on every wipe** (what `everyapp teardown` does: every planned pack, unconditionally), so every run pays the certificate wait twice (gateway and app host) and is a true first-time run; `fixtures.cert_packs` is always `deleted`. Keeping packs between runs to skip that wait would be a new flag on #444, not proposed this week; it becomes work if Cloudflare's certificate-order limits bite on repeated runs. Confirm the all-delete default.
- [ ] **The committed ledger is public on Monday**: whitelisted fields only (no zone, account id, host, email, raw text), one JSON per run under `docs/onboarding-loop/runs/`, plus the README table and `findings.json`; a test enforces the whitelist. Alternative: README and findings only.
- [ ] **Provider accounts are dedicated and capped**: a separate Cursor account on Premium (a user key reaches every repository the account connects, so never Ben's own), an OpenAI project budget for `CODEX_API_KEY`, a prepaid OpenCode Zen key, an Anthropic workspace limit shared by Claude Code and Hermes (Hermes on the Anthropic key rather than OpenRouter). Cloudflare AI Gateway in front of the harnesses is a later option (Cursor cannot use it).
- [ ] **Test-account tokens live 30 days and are rotated by hand** (the token template deliberately lacks API Tokens Write, so the loop cannot roll its own); both rolled after launch week.
- [ ] **The whole matrix re-runs after the simplify thread's stage-4 CLI PR** (`update`, `install`, `release`); every report row names the CLI commit and prompt sha, and `teardown` moves under RUN A GATEWAY then.
- [ ] **Findings route to the onboarding-DX and agent-docs threads** by `t3_thread_send` with the finding id and a 200-character excerpt marked as untrusted agent output; a plain docs fix goes straight to a small auto-merging PR.
- [ ] **The committed ledger starts at run 9**, the first run whose verdict the loop stands behind (raw event-log tail, gap sleep and whole-body verifier probes all in). The nine earlier local runs were the loop debugging itself (fail-infra, blocked, or a false verdict from a verifier defect) and stay in the local store (`~/.config/everyapp/onboarding-loop/runs-archive/`), listed with their causes in the thread's report. Alternative: publish them all with a "loop defect" column.
- [ ] **`upToDate=false` from `status` is a finding, not a verifier failure** (S1 passes with a note). The cause is product finding 4 (`create` rewrites the lockfile, the next `deploy` rebuilds the Gateway, a fresh clone calls it outdated); failing every harness's run on it would hide what the run proved.
- [ ] **A run sleeps through the 30-minute wipe gap by default** (`--no-wait` refuses as before), so chained runs on one account need no babysitting; the sleep is a `preflight.gap_wait` phase outside `durationS`.
- [ ] **G3 probes `/mcp` without a token** (401 unauthenticated) as "the gateway refuses an unauthenticated API call"; `/api/me/apps` is gone.
- [ ] **Harness rows need keys Ben supplies**: CURSOR_API_KEY and OPENCODE_API_KEY are absent from providers.env; Claude Code and Codex run on the keys given. A second test account would halve the wall time per harness (the gap is per account).
- [x] **A3 stays `perimeter-only` for now**: the marker is the `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` meta in the starter's server-rendered head (run.ts `TEMPLATE_MARKER`), and since the starter rewrite (#544) the signed-in page also server-renders the email and the Allowed card, so the row can assert on text too. It stays `perimeter-only` until the cookie domain covers `*.apps.<zone>`.

### App releases (2026-10-24)

The four app repositories publish `everyapp-release.tar.gz` on every push to their default branch, and a clone of each builds with no Every App checkout beside it: the CLI and the SDK are vendored tarballs (`vendor/`, `file:` dependencies in `package.json`), set up with `pnpm pack-for-app <app> --workflow` (`scripts/pack-for-app.mjs`; the workflow template is `scripts/pack-for-app/release.yml`). [docs/runbooks/app-releases.md](docs/runbooks/app-releases.md) is the runbook, with the alternatives (npm at a pinned version, a git dependency, a sibling checkout) and why each lost; the Sunday steps are in docs/launch-2026-10-27.md (section D, step 0). PRs: private-open-crm #9, private-open-analytics #11, private-agentic-inbox-everyapp #8, private-open-seo-everyapp #14; here, the script, runbook and docs (this section) and the R2 bucket fix (`packages/cli/src/lib/app/buckets.ts`). Verified on 2026-10-24: a fresh clone of OpenCRM with the tarballs installs in 8 s and packs a release in 19 s; OpenAnalytics' release, packed from its vendored worktree, installed on the onboarding loop's test account (`pnpm everyapp install <tarball>` from the platform checkout) and went live; OpenCRM's reached the upload and found no R2 bucket (below).
