### MCP apps (2026-10-24)

Data providers (DataForSEO; Treg and Monid next) offered to members' AI clients as apps of the Gateway's own, with nothing to deploy, through the one `/mcp`: the design is [docs/design/2026-10-mcp-provider-apps.md](docs/design/2026-10-mcp-provider-apps.md), the research behind it (three providers, MCP-proxy feasibility, what the clients do with large tool lists, seventeen open questions) [docs/design/2026-10-mcp-provider-apps-research.md](docs/design/2026-10-mcp-provider-apps-research.md). Ben ruled on 2026-10-24: an `apps` row on tier `provider`; DataForSEO curated first; proxy kind for Treg and Monid next against fakes; the operator's key; everything via the CLI, MCP and the UI where possible; nobody has access until granted; the live check with his key against the free sandbox. Built: PR 1 (#485, the registry rules and `everyapp provider on|off|list`), PR 2 (the gateway serving it in-process, the seventeen DataForSEO tools, usage, audit, the e2e test). Defaults taken and follow-ups; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **The curated DataForSEO set is seventeen Live tools** (`dataforseo_serp_google`, `_serp_google_maps`, `_serp_google_autocomplete`, `_keyword_search_volume`, `_keyword_overview`, `_keyword_suggestions`, `_keyword_ideas`, `_related_keywords`, `_keyword_difficulty`, `_search_intent`, `_ranked_keywords`, `_domain_rank_overview`, `_competitors_domain`, `_domain_intersection`, `_backlinks_summary`, `_backlinks`, `_referring_domains`), every one read-only; keyword lists capped at 100 or 200 (20 seeds for ideas, 80 characters each for Google Ads), `limit` at 100, `depth` at 100 on the SERP tools and 3 on related keywords; `search_intent` takes keywords alone. Not in: On-Page (instant pages, Lighthouse: slow), AI Optimization (LLM mentions, $0.10 a call), Business Data, Merchant, Standard (task_post) endpoints, and a locations lookup (the lists run to tens of thousands of rows; tools take `location_name`). Add what OpenSEO's users ask for.
- [ ] **A tool answers `{ cost, result }`**: DataForSEO's `tasks[0].result` as is (no `.ai` cropping, which drops the `cost` the usage counters read) and the call's cost in USD. Alternative: a trimmed shape per tool (fewer tokens, more code to keep in step with DataForSEO).
- [ ] **Every task carries `tag: everyapp:<organization id>`** for DataForSEO's own usage reports; the member isn't named to the provider. The research's open question 3 (whether `id_list` metadata carries the tag) is unanswered.
- [ ] **The identity's audience is `provider:<row id>`**, verified with `JWT_PUBLIC_KEY` or the public half of `JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` where an install never wrote it (the install writes both).
- [ ] **A provider row's snapshot records the gateway's provider catalog version** (a digest of every provider catalog's hash, computed once per isolate through the tools contract) so a gateway whose tools changed re-syncs on first use. A gateway that can't compute it (no signing key) logs `mcp.catalog.provider_version_unavailable` and syncs the other apps all the same.
- [ ] **A provider app without its key needs setup like any app** (#396's gate reads the generated manifest): its tools are left out of `/mcp`, `everyapp_list_apps` names the command, the launcher and Admin → Apps show "Needs setup" with the setup dialog; with the key set they show "AI tools". The CLI's `provider on` says the same with `PROVIDER_KEY_UNSET`.
- [ ] **Access follows a deployed app's rule**: `provider on` grants the person turning it on, the owners and the admins; an admin grants others (Manage access, groups). `is_default` is off. Alternative Ben declined: everyone by default.
- [ ] **`everyapp delete` and `email` refuse a provider row** (`APP_NOT_FOUND`: no Worker), and `everyapp status` and `secret list` leave it out of their per-app reports (PR 2: a row without a Worker would otherwise be read as an unbound app, and its secrets listed with `--worker ""`); `provider list|off` are its commands. `status` could list provider apps under `data.providerApps` with the key's state.
- [ ] **The MCP way to turn a provider on** (a write built-in on `/mcp`, owner/admin only) and an Admin → Apps "Turn on" button are not built: the built-ins are read-only by invariant (`toolbus.builtins-readonly-reserved`), so the MCP way is a reviewed change to that register; the UI toggle is a server function over the same rules. (2026-10-25: Admin → Apps' Turn on and Turn off are #629, for both kinds of MCP app; the MCP way is the workspace tools in "MCP apps by URL (2026-10-25)".)
- [x] **The launcher tile for a provider app opens nothing** and shows "AI tools" (Admin → Apps: status "AI tools", "tools on /mcp as dataforseo_<tool>"). Done 2026-10-24 (evening; the MCP apps reframe PR, [docs/design/2026-10-mcp-apps-ui.md](docs/design/2026-10-mcp-apps-ui.md)): the kind is called **MCP apps** everywhere a person reads it (Ben: "maybe we just call them MCP apps"; "Tools" collides with every app's tools, "Connectors" with AI connections and claude.ai's word for the Gateway itself; the code keeps `provider`); the launcher lists them in an "MCP apps" section under the grid (a segmented Apps | MCP apps toggle and a distinct card in the one grid were the alternatives; the section keeps one page, one count and one search), the card carries an "MCP" chip where the open arrow would be and opens the "Use it from your agent" dialog (the Gateway's `/mcp` address with a copy button, `dataforseo_<tool>`, the provider's example sentence from `PROVIDER_APPS[id].example`, links to /docs/connect-ai and /docs/agent/mcp#mcp-apps), as do the "MCP" chip on Admin → Apps and a pinned entry in the sidebar (`?mcp=<id>`); "AI tools" is gone (a ready MCP app is Active; the chip beside the name is the kind), the Admin → Apps line reads "used from AI clients as dataforseo_<tool>", the row menu says "Turn off" with a dialog that names `mcp on` as the way back; the CLI group is `everyapp mcp on|off|list` with `provider` an alias through launch week. A workspace whose only app is an MCP app shows the gallery (nothing to open yet) with the MCP apps section under it. Still open: an icon per provider.
- [ ] **Dynamic MCP via worker loaders (parked; Ben, 2026-10-24: "the Executor pattern of dynamic MCPs with dynamic worker loaders, forward-looking, too much for this weekend").** Executor (executor.sh, UsefulSoftwareCo/executor, MIT, YC; not E2B) lets you paste an OpenAPI, GraphQL or MCP URL, indexes the tools and holds the credential, and serves everything through one `/mcp` with six fixed tools (`skills`, `search`, `describe`, `call`, `execute`, `resume`; `execute` is code mode in a sandboxed interpreter; runtimes QuickJS, a Deno subprocess or a dynamic worker). For us, in order: PR 3 (the remote-MCP proxy kind, static registry: Treg first); then `everyapp mcp add <url>` (and an Admin → Apps "Add" entry), which registers a remote MCP server as an `apps` row of tier `provider`, kind `remote_mcp`, with the operator's credential as a Gateway setting, snapshots its `tools/list` into `app_tools` behind an allowlist, names them `<id>_<tool>`, maps annotations to risk and forwards each call with the credential; and only then adapters for upstreams that are not MCP (OpenAPI, GraphQL), which is where Dynamic Workers (`worker_loaders`, open beta, Workers Paid) come in: a per-app adapter Worker that holds no secret, with `globalOutbound` limited to the upstream's host and a service binding back to the ToolBus, the same runner shape the code-mode design describes for `everyapp_execute` (C1). Model- or operator-written adapter code never runs in the Gateway's isolate. The five-line Executor summary and the sketch: [docs/design/2026-10-mcp-apps-ui.md](docs/design/2026-10-mcp-apps-ui.md) sections 7 and 8.
- [ ] **Curated DataForSEO tools stay; its own MCP is not passed through** (Ben, 2026-10-24: "why are we offering curated skills? the whole benefit is you can just use their MCP"; verified and written up in [docs/design/2026-10-mcp-apps-ui.md](docs/design/2026-10-mcp-apps-ui.md) section 6). No skills are curated: seventeen tools, and `setup/dataforseo.md` is the operator's key setup. DataForSEO's hosted MCP (`https://mcp.dataforseo.com/v3/mcp`; Basic with the API login and password, or an OAuth bearer; a 2025-era stateless Express server) is four tools, three of them docs and one `api_request` catch-all (any `/v3` path, `method` parameter, `readOnlyHint: false`, `.ai` mode by default, which drops the `cost` the caps read, sandbox unreachable); the older `/mcp` and `/http` paths are the deprecated v2 server with about 90 tools, which would push a read & write member of the four launch apps past the 256 at which the listing goes compact (#567). Passing it through is wirable (Basic in `requestInit.headers`, `noAiMode: true`) and worse: one write-risk catch-all that sidesteps the per-tool policy, argument check and audit column, costs hidden, no sandbox for the live check, ChatGPT confirming every call. Pass-through is right for providers whose MCP is a real tool list (Treg, Monid once its MCP takes a key): that is PR 3 below, not built this weekend because its first upstream has no account (Ben's "not now"), it changes B4 and B5 (a reviewed register change) and the reframe was the ask. If DataForSEO is ever proxied, it is the v2 module list behind an allowlist, never the v3 catch-all.
- [x] **The remote-MCP proxy kind (PR 3)** for Treg (`https://treg.to/mcp/`, bearer Agent key, 10 tools, `X-Treg-Meta` attribution) and Monid (OAuth-only MCP today; REST with a `monid_live_…` key first, pending Monid's written yes to server-side resale: its ToS §8 and its integration docs disagree): the in-process app's tools are the upstream's `tools/list` behind an operator allowlist, calls forwarded over `@modelcontextprotocol/client` 2.x with the operator's key only (never the member's token), legacy era pinned, no elicitation/sampling/tasks declared, 25 of the 30 s budget. Built against an in-process fake until Ben opens accounts. Done 2026-10-25 as a registrable kind instead ("MCP apps by URL (2026-10-25)", #627 and the lane PRs): any server is added by URL at run time, so Treg and Monid are not static entries; a server is reached over the ToolBus's own dependency-free client rather than `@modelcontextprotocol/client`, the 2026-07-28 era first with a 2025 fallback; the per-tool `--off` is the allowlist after the fact. Monid stays OAuth-only, which is not supported yet.
- [ ] **Tool count**: a provider app lists all its tools in full with their hints, no `<app>_search` + `<app>_call` wrapper (it would sidestep the per-tool policy, argument check, identity claim and audit column) and no `alwaysLoad`, as the code-mode thread ruled ("Code mode and skills (2026-10-24)", [docs/design/2026-10-code-mode-and-skills.md](docs/design/2026-10-code-mode-and-skills.md)). Ben's ruling of 2026-10-24 ("why wouldn't we add the search? why would we want to enforce these arbitrary caps") lands on that thread's branch `t3/compact-surface` (PR #567, merged 2026-10-25 as d1719b57): `everyapp_search_tools`, `everyapp_call_read`/`everyapp_call_write`, a `compact` listing mode (by header, query or automatically past 256 entries or 1 MiB) and the 100-per-app cap removed. Provider apps change nothing under those thresholds; their snapshots are in `app_tools`, so the search covers them. The numbers: claude.ai and Cowork drop a connector's tools past 256 silently; the four launch apps list 187 for a read & write member, 207 with DataForSEO on.
- [x] **The remote-MCP proxy kind's spec duties** (from the code-mode thread, folded into the design doc's PR 3 section): follow `nextCursor` when snapshotting; honour `ttlMs`; per-call initialize or a stored `Mcp-Session-Id` for 2025-era upstreams; re-sync on the hourly cron and on drift rather than holding the upstream's `subscriptions/listen`; normalize names with a reverse map; map annotations to risk explicitly (unhinted tools are write, not destructive); strip `x-mcp-header`; no sampling, elicitation, roots or tasks in the first slice; an operator allowlist per upstream (the per-app cap goes with `t3/compact-surface`, PR #567). Done 2026-10-25 in #627 ("MCP apps by URL (2026-10-25)"), each of them, with the same difference: the operator's allowlist is the per-tool override (`mcp tool <id> <tool> --off`), and a mode where new tools start off is a follow-up there.
- [ ] **Per-organization keys** (a hosted-tier need) and **spend caps per provider app**: the teams swath's `monthly_call_cap` applies to provider rows as to apps: counted the same way, and enforced in `providerCall` (`checkMonthlyCap` before every call, a tool error naming the cap and Admin → Usage; the AppGateway entrypoint enforces it for apps). A per-provider USD cap would read `cost_micros`.
- [ ] **The live check**: Ben puts `DATAFORSEO_API_KEY` and `DATAFORSEO_SANDBOX=true` in the gateway's `.dev.vars`, runs the gateway locally, `pnpm everyapp provider on dataforseo` against it (the CLI needs a Gateway; for a local one the row can be seeded by hand: the seed script #462 is the place), and connects Claude Code to `http://localhost:<port>/mcp` with a personal token.
- [ ] **Setup skill**: `setup/dataforseo.md` now mentions `provider on`; a `setup/provider-apps.md` (turn on, grant, connect, verify with one `dataforseo_keyword_overview` call on the sandbox) would let an agent do it end to end.

### Deploying state (2026-10-25)

Ben's first deploy of mission-control to everyapp.team (Access in front) left the launcher saying "Deploying" for 47 minutes, until he typed the app's URL. Cause: the Gateway makes a `binding` app active on the first request that reaches it, and behind Access the CLI's own probe never does (`ACCESS_IN_FRONT`), so nobody made that request; the launcher's card wasn't a link, so the one thing that would have finished the deploy was hidden. Defaults taken on the fix PR:

- The launcher's and Admin → Apps' list reads probe each `binding` app over its service binding and mark it active (`activateBindingApps`), at most five per read. Walk back: drop the call from `AppAccessService.getAppsForUser` and `AppService.getAllWithAccessCounts`; the first visit to the app host still activates it.
- The launcher reads its list again every 5 s while an app is deploying (`DEPLOYING_POLL_MS`). Walk back: remove the effect in `useAppLibrary`; a reload or a window focus refetches anyway.
- A card `binding` for 10 minutes or more reads "Deploy stalled" (`DEPLOY_STALL_MS`, the CLI's live-wait limit) and says to run `status`, then deploy again. Walk back: delete `AppStatusLabel.Stalled`; the threshold and copy are in `appStatus.ts` and `AppListItem.tsx`.
- The deploying card says what is happening in place of its description and stays no link: a direct visit to a truly unreachable app is a bare 503 JSON. Alternative: make the card a link anyway.
- The `ACCESS_IN_FRONT` message now names the app URL and the Gateway's home page as the visit that makes it live; docs updated to match. _Superseded by the cron PR below: the warning is now only the 10-minute stall._
- [x] **Decided, Ben 2026-10-25: `/health` stays as it is.** It was the idea for the CLI's own wait under Access (the public, Access-bypassed route probing binding apps); it would widen what an unauthenticated request does. The Gateway's public surface is unchanged; the per-minute cron does the probing instead.

The cron PR (2026-10-25) finishes the deploy without any visit, and the CLI's wait sees it. Defaults taken; confirm or change each:

- [ ] **The per-minute cron probes every `binding` app of the install and makes it active** (`activateDeployingApps`, `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-activation.ts`; at most 50 rows a run, oldest change first, every organization's; the hourly cron does it too, for an install whose per-minute trigger was never published). Nothing thrown: a sweep that fails is logged `app.activate_failed` with trigger `scheduled` and tried next minute. Walk back: drop the call from `scheduled` in `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.ts`; the list reads and the first visit still activate.
- [ ] **One `app.live` event per transition** (`{ app, version?, trigger: "request" | "list" | "scheduled" }`, gateway actor, "crm is live" in Admin → Activity), from whichever trigger made the row `active`; an isolate that finds another got there first says nothing (`AppRepository.activate` now reports the transition and the row's name). Alternative: a log line only (`app.activated` is still logged), and no new event type.
- [ ] **The CLI's live wait stops requesting the app host once Cloudflare Access answers, and only reads the row** until it is `active` or the 10 minutes run out (`progress` "Cloudflare Access answers before the Gateway; waiting for the Gateway to make <app> live (it looks every minute)", `data.access: true`). A deploy behind Access now ends live, with no warning, in about a minute. Walk back: in `waitForLive.ts`, emit the old warning at the Access answer and return `first_visit`.
- [ ] **`ACCESS_IN_FRONT` from `deploy` is now the stall alone**: after 10 minutes behind Access still `binding`, a warning (not an error, since this run can't tell the Gateway's reasons apart from behind Access) that says the deploy stalled, keeps the app uploaded and bound, carries `data.rerun` `pnpm everyapp status` "then deploy again", and names the visit through Access as the other way to finish it. The same 10 minutes as the launcher's "Deploy stalled" and `APP_NOT_LIVE`. Alternative: make it the `APP_NOT_LIVE` error, retryable, as without Access.
- [ ] **`--no-wait`'s help says "the Gateway makes it live within a minute, or on its first visit"**; `status`'s `○ live on first visit` and `APP_BINDING` ("goes live on its first visit to …") keep their wording, which stays true (a visit still works) but no longer says the whole of it. Alternative: "live within a minute" there too (status.md, status.mdx, json.md's problem table and the status tests follow).

### MCP apps by URL (2026-10-25)

Ben, 2026-10-24: "assume we want all of this and build the best, most robust thing we're going to want long term, so that when we launch on Monday people can just run with it and they're not waiting on us for PRs." Any remote MCP server becomes an MCP app of the Gateway's: `pnpm everyapp mcp add <url>`, Admin → Apps → Add app → Add an MCP server, and later an agent's workspace tool. Its tools reach members' AI clients through the one `/mcp` as `<id>_<tool>`, with the Gateway holding the server's credential, every call attributed, audited, counted and capped like an app's, read and write told apart, and the operator able to turn one tool or the whole app off. The design is [docs/design/2026-10-mcp-by-url.md](docs/design/2026-10-mcp-by-url.md); the agent reference is landing-page/content/agent/mcp.md, "MCP apps". The pull requests: #623 (the foundation: the `remote` block, the `_MCP_TOKEN` family, the URL and risk rules, the registry rules, migration 0011; merged), #627 (the gateway serving it in-process, the ToolBus's remote client, the credential pin, the hourly re-list), #629 (Admin → Apps: Add an MCP server, the MCP app dialog, Turn off and Remove; merged), #630 (the `claude -p` harness against a fake and a live server), #631 (the CLI: `mcp add|remove|show|refresh|tool`, `on`/`off` as serve and stop, `secret set` pins a credential; merged), #635 (these docs, the threat model's nine `remote-mcp.*` invariants, this section), and the agent's workspace tools to come on #614's seam. Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **`on` and `off` serve and stop serving, for both kinds; `remove` is the tombstone.** `off` sets `status` `disabled` and keeps access, tokens, the snapshot and the overrides, so `on` is instant; `remove` is what `off` did from #599 (access, tokens, snapshot and AI connections go; the key or credential stays). Admin → Apps says "Off" for an app that is off, and its row menu has Turn off and Remove apart.
- [ ] **The credential family `<ID>_MCP_TOKEN`, and no reuse of a provider key.** A remote server's credential is a Gateway setting named in the family (`--secret` picks another name in it, nothing else), so `secret set` routes it without the registry, an app secret can't take one, and an admin adding a server can't point `OPENAI_API_KEY` at their own URL. A credential is pinned to one origin (`remote_mcp_credentials`); `secret set` pins it, the Gateway pins one set elsewhere on first use (known gap 33).
- [ ] **A tool with no hint is `write`.** Only an explicit `readOnlyHint: true` is `read`, an explicit `destructiveHint: true` is `destructive` (hidden), everything else `write`, hidden from read-only connections. The spec's missing-means-true would hide every unhinted tool. `--risk read` (and Everything read in the form) makes every tool of an app `read`, for a docs or search server that never annotates.
- [ ] **Who registers: the operator, owners and admins; members never; only owners remove.** Like deleting an app. The planned workspace tools keep the same roles. _Superseded 2026-10-25 by Ben's ruling (owners and admins control the Gateway): remove is owners' and admins' too._
- [ ] **Upstream OAuth: not supported yet.** `--auth oauth` adds the row and serves nothing; it reads "Not supported yet" in Admin → Apps and `OAuth: not supported yet` in `mcp list`. The probe at `add` says when a server wants OAuth.
- [ ] **The probe at `mcp add` warns and never refuses.** One look at the URL from the operator's machine, no credential, no redirect followed, 10 s: `REMOTE_MCP_WANTS_OAUTH` or `REMOTE_MCP_PROBE_FAILED`. The Gateway, not the laptop, is what fetches the server.
- [ ] **`mcp add` writes the row before it probes, so `--auth` defaults to `none`** (`bearer` with `--secret`, `header` with `--header`). Alternative: probe first and choose `bearer` when the server answers 401.
- [ ] **`mcp remove` asks no confirmation**, as the old `off` didn't. Alternative: confirm unless `--yes`, as `delete` does.
- [ ] **Usage `target` is `mcp:<id>`** (`kind` `capability`, the row as the app, the member as the user; a server error or an `isError` result counts as a failure), so Admin → Usage and the caps read a remote app like a provider app.
- [ ] **The server's tool list is cached 10 minutes per isolate, or for the server's own 2026-07-28 `ttlMs`, at most an hour**; the hourly cron re-lists every active remote app whose snapshot is missing or older than an hour, at most 50 a run; a call for a name the cache doesn't know lists once more.
- [ ] **`remote_mcp_tools` is replaced at every listing** (one row per server tool: its name, its ToolBus name, what its annotations say, whether it is listed and why not), and goes with the app row. It is what `mcp show`, the dialog and `mcp tool` read; nothing keeps older listings.
- [ ] **The "Use it from your agent" dialog's example for a remote app is generic**: `List what <name> offers, then use one of its tools.` A built-in keeps its own (`PROVIDER_APPS[id].example`).
- [ ] **`mcp list --json` answers `data.apps`** (it was `data.providers`), each with `kind`; `mcp on`'s `was` can be `off`. No alias kept: publishing is paused and nothing outside this checkout reads it.
- [ ] **A cross-organization credential name is refused in the form without saying where** (the Gateway has one set of secrets; on the hosted tier one organization's server must not learn another's). The CLI acts for the install organization only.

Follow-ups:

- [ ] **An OAuth client for upstream MCP servers.** Sketch: protected-resource discovery from the 401's `resource_metadata`, then authorization-server discovery; dynamic client registration or a Client ID Metadata Document published by the Gateway; an authorize round trip through the operator's browser with PKCE and a callback at `/mcp-apps/<id>/oauth/callback`; access and refresh tokens encrypted at rest and refreshed before use; the token bound to the row's origin like a credential. A new security boundary: its own design and threat-model rows first. Since 2026-10-25 the token store exists (`provider_connections`, `secrets/sealed.ts`, "OAuth providers (2026-10-25)"): what remains is discovery, DCR and the callback.
- [ ] **The 2024-11-05 HTTP+SSE transport** (a `GET` stream plus a `POST` endpoint) is not spoken; such a server's listing fails. Add it if a server people want still serves only that.
- [ ] **Per-organization credentials on the hosted tier.** Today a credential is one Gateway setting for the whole install (the provider registry's "per-organization keys" again).
- [ ] **An allowlist mode where a server's new tools start off**, so a server that adds a write tool doesn't reach members until the operator turns it on. Today the per-tool `--off` is the operator's allowlist after the fact, and `mcp.remote.tools_changed` logs what came.
- [ ] **The agent's workspace tools** (`mcp_add`, `mcp_remove`, `mcp_on`, `mcp_off`, `mcp_list`, `mcp_show`, `mcp_refresh`, `mcp_tool` under `everyapp_`) on #614's seam: owners and admins, `remove` owners only, hidden below, never a credential. Then the parity matrix and the agent page name them. _Superseded 2026-10-25 by Ben's ruling: `remove` is owners' and admins' too._
- [ ] **The live harness run** (#630's live case: DeepWiki or the Cloudflare docs server) on a deployed Gateway, and one credentialed server end to end (Treg once Ben opens an account).
- [ ] **`secret list <id>` and `configure <id>` on an MCP app** answer the deployed-app `APP_NOT_FOUND`; they could point to `mcp show <id>` and `secret set <NAME>`.
- [ ] **The manifest validator's refusal of an app secret ending in `_MCP_TOKEN` has no test of its own** (`remote-mcp.secret-name-family` is partial for it).
- [ ] **`everyapp provider` is still the `mcp` alias**, so `provider add <url>` adds an MCP server, while the provider registry's PR 2b plans `everyapp provider add` for API providers ("Provider registry (2026-10-25)"). Settle it when 2b lands: drop the alias for `add` first.
- [ ] **Once #627 merges**, the threat model's "pinned (PR 2)" marks become plain pins, and the remote-mcp rows' enforcing code can name its lines (#629's are plain already).

### UI states (2026-10-24)

The overnight pass over every gateway page and state (empty, loading, error, toast, confirmation, long names, zero and many apps; light and dark; desktop and phone) against a seeded local D1. One rule: a page says only what the UI does not already show, and one pattern each for page frames, empty states, errors and confirmations, implemented once and reused. Merged: #450 (`Page` frame, headers without descriptions, admin tabs scroll on phones, no build stamp over phone content), #452 (`ErrorState` with inline, card and page variants; `useQueryFailed`, so a failed collection load reads as an error, never as empty or as the onboarding banner; theme follows the OS, toasts follow the theme), #457 (one `EmptyState` inside `DataTable`, the usual state as plain text instead of a badge, a clear-search button), #459 (the activity table fits its card, failures in one colour), #460 (dialogs and confirmations say it once, invite and reset links share one dialog, the onboarding banner is one card). Merged too: #461 (10993ec9), the sign-in, sign-up, password, verification, invitation and consent pages, with two bugs fixed inside it (`/verify-email?error=1` crashed the route; `/reset-password` rewrote the URL on a bad token). Dev tooling: #462, `scripts/seed-local.mjs`. Before/after screenshots for every PR are on the `ui-screenshots-2026-10-24` branch under `ui-states-2026-10-24/`. The secrets-model thread could not be reached (`thread_not_found` on 0dbfe738); #457 touches `AppsTable.tsx` and `admin/apps.tsx` in hunks #395 does not, so #395 rebases cleanly but should be re-read against the new `EmptyState` before merging. Judged and left, for the design review:

- [ ] **Consent defaults to Read & write** even when the client asked for a read scope. The form should preselect what was requested; that is a consent-logic change, so it was not touched. Same page: a missing `client_id` shows the authorize endpoint's raw "client_id is required" text, which should become a `StatusScreen` with "Can't connect this app".
- [ ] **Forgot password shows "Check your email" when no email sender is configured.** The server cannot send and says nothing. Either say so on the page, or hide the link when sending is off.
- [ ] **Verify email's "send failed" maps to "Link not valid".** The route has one error state for every failure; a resend that fails should say so instead.

Security-relevant, so this is a HELD PR of its own; not written tonight.

Original: (users, apps, tokens, activity). The fix is a card-per-row layout under `md`, not more truncation. Also at 1280 px the activity table now caps the Who/App/Tool cells to fit; `research_keywords` reads "research_key…" with the full name on hover.

Delete one. The home page's wrapper and empty copy also change once #395 lands ("Needs setup"), so the home page was left alone.

Each is a small fix in a sibling thread's component; noted, not taken.

Each PR was reviewed by a per-PR reviewer and a per-finding refuter (a Workflow); the confirmed findings went in as follow-up commits (a seed group name over 60 characters, `usage_monthly` rows so the seeded cap is reached in the gateway's check too, the member's intro and command heading both saying "admin", a secret's label in monospace, the Activity hover in the page's "AI app" voice). ### Secrets model (2026-10-24)

"What does this app need, and have I set it?" Each provider, capability and secret in a manifest carries a need; the gateway and the CLI evaluate it against what is set; an app that isn't ready shows "Needs setup" and doesn't open; an optional setting degrades inside the app. Decided 2026-10-23 after a three-lens design review, built 2026-10-24 as #383 (the model: the manifest fields, `packages/sdk/src/internal/appSettings.ts`, `apps.secret_names`, the gateway's `app-setup.ts`) with the lane pull requests stacked on it, `t3/launch-secrets-cli`, `t3/launch-secrets-gateway-ui`, `t3/launch-secrets-gate-mcp` (#396, the perimeter gate and `/mcp`; merged 2026-10-24) and `t3/launch-secrets-docs`, plus one manifest pull request per app in its own repository (openseo, crm, analytics, agentic-inbox; none deployed). The reference is [docs/manifest-settings.md](docs/manifest-settings.md).

**Decide** (each shipped with a default; confirm it or change it)

- [ ] **Required by default.** A bare `providers`, `capabilities` or `secrets` entry is required: the app doesn't open until it is set. The author's rule: required = the app is useless without it; a setting only one feature needs is `optional: true`, and the feature says what is missing where it is used. Alternative: optional by default, with the app opening into features that fail one by one (design review, 2026-10-23).
- [ ] **`anyOf: "<group>"` names a group, and promises the app picks among the set members at run time.** The group is met once any member is set; while none is, every member blocks and the command names the first with the others in its note. The gateway refuses a call to a provider whose key isn't set whichever others are, so an app that chooses its provider at build time (agentic-inbox's `AI_PROVIDER`) declares that one required and the other optional instead. An entry can't be both optional and in a group; a group needs two or more members, counted across providers, capabilities and secrets. Alternative: `oneOf` with the gateway picking the provider (needs the provider status route below).
- [ ] **Each setting names its setup guide, `setup`: the registries' for a Gateway setting, the entry's for an app's own secret.** A provider's or a capability's guide is a registry default beside `docs` (`DEV_AI_PROVIDERS[id].setup`, a capability's `setup`: openai, openrouter, workers-ai, dataforseo, context, email, google-workspace, microsoft-365 and slack; the AI routes share `setup/openai.md`; `members` and the 16 social clients have none), written by the setup-skills thread under `packages/cli/skills/everyapp/setup` and copied into every app's `.claude/skills/everyapp/`; the strict schema refuses `setup` on a provider or capability entry and takes it on a secret entry as `setup/<name>.md`. Alternative: a manifest override for every kind; `docs` pointing at the guide instead of a page.
- [ ] **The model names `EMAIL_FROM` for email; the sender still needs a display name.** The registry's `settings` for `email` is `["EMAIL_FROM"]` with `EMAIL_FROM_NAME` under `optionalSettings`, and the gateway answers the capability by its own rule rather than by the names (`isEmailConfigured()`: a sender that parses, which needs a display name from `EMAIL_FROM_NAME` or `EMAIL_FROM="Name <address>"`, and a provider that is selectable). Open: the names only approximate the rule, so where values can't be read (the CLI's `secret list` and `status`) a bare address with no name shows `✓ set` while the gateway refuses to send (`EMAIL_SENDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`). Alternative: both names required, which would call the `Name <address>` form unset.
- [ ] **App secrets are recorded by the CLI (`apps.secret_names`, migration 0004), and unknown never blocks.** The gateway can't list an app Worker's secrets, so the CLI writes the names it sees (`deploy` after the upload in the same `markDeployed` update under the lease, `register`, `secret set|unset` after the targets are patched, `status` for every deployed app) with everyapp's reserved names left out; null (never recorded) reads `! not recorded` and never closes an app, so a pre-column row or an out-of-band change is a warning until the next `status`. The gateway reads the column like the email columns, with no PRAGMA guard: the schema is this checkout's, and `setup` migrates before it uploads. Alternative: the app Worker self-reporting (follow-up below).
- [ ] **`status` records names.** Every `pnpm everyapp status` re-reads each deployed app's Worker and writes `secret_names`, so it is also the repair ("Already set it another way? `pnpm everyapp status` re-reads the Worker."). A failed `secrets list` is one `SETTINGS_UNKNOWN` warning with the rows unknown; `pending` apps and missing Workers are skipped. Alternative: a read-only `status`.
- [ ] **One command per app: `pnpm everyapp configure <app>`.** It asks for each unmet setting (hidden input, with why, where to get it and the guide beside it), which member of an any-of group, then offers the optional ones (one keystroke skips all); run again, it shows what is set and lets you change one; agents drive it with NAME=value lines on stdin or --from-env; a value never goes on the command line (`configure NAME=value` is refused). The same string everywhere (setupCommand in the SDK): the gateway's dialog, status's APP_NEEDS_SETUP, deploy's and secret list's next, the MCP instructions; the command's any-of note is gone. `secret set NAME [--app <id>]` stays for one name and for scripts; a provider app's command stays `secret set <KEY>`. Alternatives for the verb: `setup <app>` (overloads the Gateway install verb), `app setup <app>` (a group for one command), `secret setup <app>`; alternative for agents: KEY=value arguments (refused: shell history and the process list).
- [ ] **A shared app seen from another organization gets no command.** Its `setup` says what is unmet with `command: null`; the platform operator fixes it. Admins of the app's own organization get the command; other members get the same names with "Ask an admin to run:".
- [ ] **Dispatch-tier apps aren't gated at run time.** Their providers and capabilities ride the platform's settings, which the platform checks at deploy (follow-up: the hosted deploy API refusing a manifest whose required platform settings are unset); `setup` is null for them.
- [ ] **Public routes close with the app.** The gate runs for every path after the status and activation checks, public routes included (a tracker's `/collect` goes dark with its dashboards), so authors weigh `required` against the public surface. Alternative: public routes stay open and the app's code checks its own secrets.
- [ ] **A closed app: 302 to the launcher for a browser, a nameless 503 for everything else.** A private HTML GET navigation goes to `${GATEWAY_URL}/?setup=<appId>`, which opens the dialog; a public route, an API call or a non-GET answers `503 { "error": "app_needs_setup", "app" }` with no `retry-after` and never a setting name. The gateway's evaluation is cached per row, version and recorded names (500 entries), with freshness bounded by the 30 s hostname cache. The gateway's `/mcp` leaves the app's tools out and says why in the instructions; `everyapp_list_apps` carries `needsSetup`.

- [ ] **The gate runs before authentication.** One hook point, no names in the body, app ids are hostnames, Access in front hides it from strangers; the cost is that a visitor to a private route of an unready app gets the nameless 503 with the app id where they used to get the sign-in redirect. Alternative from the threat model (docs/threat-model.md B1): run the hook after authentication for private routes and before it for public ones.

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] An `/__everyapp/health` self-report of the app Worker's present secret names, so `apps.secret_names` heals without the CLI.
- [ ] A provider status route (`GET /api/ai/<provider>`) so an app can pick among the set members of an any-of group at run time; agentic-inbox then moves from required-plus-optional to `anyOf: "AI model"`.
- [ ] The hosted deploy API refusing a dispatch-tier manifest whose required platform settings are unset, since the gate doesn't run for the dispatch tier.

A second ordering constraint on crm's redeploy.

### MCP surface (2026-10-24)

The code-mode research's concrete fixes to the gateway's `/mcp` ("Code mode and skills (2026-10-24)" above, design doc section 6), built overnight 2026-10-24/25 under the overnight rule as #499 (the listen hold), #501 (the instructions head), #503 (alwaysLoad), #504 (the drift test), #505 (client_info) and #516 (polish from the reviews). Each bullet is the default taken; yes keeps it.

- [ ] **The listen hold is per surface: 30 minutes on the gateway's connector, 10 on an app host** (#499) (`GATEWAY_LISTEN_MAX_MS`, `APP_LISTEN_MAX_MS` in `packages/toolbus/src/http.ts`; `listenMaxMs` on `ToolBusDeps` overrides both). Why 30 and not the design's 15: Claude Code 2.1.309 (read from its binary) keeps the time of every reopen in a trailing one-hour window and parks re-listening for about six hours once five are in it, whatever ended the stream; a 10-minute hold parks it after fifty minutes (six closes an hour), 15 leaves room for one unscheduled close an hour, 30 for two (a deploy, an evicted isolate, a token expiry) with two scheduled. Proven with `claude -p` against a local ToolBus (`packages/toolbus/claude-code/listen-reopen.claude.ts`, docs/TESTING.md): with the hold scaled to 60 s Claude Code reopens five times and goes deaf at the sixth close; with 30 minutes it is still reopening and hearing changes past ninety minutes. The app host keeps 10 because it rechecks nothing between requests (a longer hold would let a stream outlive a revoked token longer). Alternatives: 15 minutes as designed; one hour (one scheduled close, bounded by the token anyway); have the app-host surface recheck its token each poll and hold 30 too.
- [ ] **A held stream never outlives its credential** (#499)**.** The gateway passes the OAuth access token's `expiresAt` (`ctx.auth` from the resource server) or the Access assertion's `exp` as `listenUntil` on the view; a personal token needs none, since each poll resolves it again, expiry included. Before, a stream could run up to ten minutes past an expired token.
- [ ] **A failed poll keeps the stream** (#499)**.** A re-read that throws anything but `ViewRefusedError` (the gateway's recheck throws that for a revoked, removed, deactivated or suspended caller) counts as a transient failure: the stream stays open and pushes nothing, and ends after three in a row (`LISTEN_REFRESH_FAILURES_BEFORE_END`). Before, any failure ended it, which spent a reopen, and three failed reopens in a row (Claude Code's `uc` loop: 1 s, 2 s, 4 s) stop its re-listening until the person reconnects, so a ten-second D1 blip could silence a session. Alternative: fail closed as before.
- [ ] **The gateway instructions' head stays inside Claude Code's 4,096-character read** (#501): header, app list and the "not available" lines first, tested on the five launch apps (openseo, crm, analytics, agentic-inbox, dataforseo); if that test ever fails, shorten those lines, not the apps' guidance. Codex reads 512 characters, so an app's own guidance may fall past a client's cut. Alternative: cap the head in code by dropping app names from the list when it would overflow.
- [ ] **`_meta["anthropic/alwaysLoad"]: true` on both built-ins** (#503), so Claude Code (2.1.285 and later) keeps `everyapp_list_apps` and `everyapp_whoami` loaded ahead of its tool search; other clients ignore it; a person's server-level `alwaysLoad: false` wins. Alternative: leave them deferred.
- [ ] **`mcp_tool_calls.client_info`** (#505, migration 0007): the client's self-description from the MCP handshake, `<name> <version>` cut to 200 characters, from the 2026-07-28 per-request `clientInfo` or, for 2025-era clients, from `initialize` carried in the session id's middle part (`<uuid>.<base64url client>.<hash>`); shown on Admin → Activity beside the credential. Self-asserted, never verified; it is what makes "which clients call us" answerable for Access and token callers (the trigger for the compact surface). Alternatives: record nothing for 2025-era clients (no session-id carrier); a filter on Activity.
- [ ] **The docs drift test derives the built-in names from `listBuiltinTools()`** (#504, tightened in #516) (the agent page, the skill's `mcp.md` and the invariant `toolbus.builtins-readonly-reserved` must name every built-in), and the toolbus checks that the gateway instructions only name built-ins that exist.
- [ ] Follow-ups: the agent page's listen identifiers (`GATEWAY_LISTEN_MAX_MS`, `APP_LISTEN_MAX_MS`, `listenUntil`, `ViewRefusedError`) added to the drift test's list once #499 and #516 are both on main; the head of the instructions is not clipped in code (#501's review: 20 apps needing setup put it past 4,096, 40 past 8 KiB), decide whether to cap it; the live check against everyapp.team after the deploy (connect Claude Code, wait an hour, redeploy an app, see the refetch); a counter of held streams per isolate in the maintenance log; the app-host surface's recheck.

### Code mode and skills (2026-10-24)

Should the gateway's `/mcp` offer a code-mode surface (`search_tools` plus `execute(code)` in a sandbox over the member's tools) or only tool search and lazy loading, and should skills live on the gateway per organization? Researched 2026-10-23/24 in [docs/design/2026-10-code-mode-and-skills.md](docs/design/2026-10-code-mode-and-skills.md): the landscape verified against current docs and source, the recommendation adversarially reviewed; the provider apps themselves are the "MCP apps (2026-10-24)" thread's (#485, #489). Ben ruled 2026-10-24 (the design's section 11): the search goes in, no arbitrary per-app cap, and the compact surface is built now and hides nothing; later that day, code mode stays on the roadmap (its runtime answered below) and the every-app plugin is parked here in plain words. Built 2026-10-24/25 on `t3/compact-surface` and merged 2026-10-25 as #567 (d1719b57) on #559 (e85fad11), not held: Ben asked for no hold ("what do you need my review of?"), and nothing in it was his to decide; the defaults taken are in the follow-ups bullet. Ticked is what Ben decided; open is what he hasn't.

- [x] **Search: in** ("why wouldn't we add the search?"). Was: full listing stays the default and no gateway-wide search tool this week, since the deferring clients don't need one and the loading clients wouldn't load less for it. Now: `everyapp_search_tools` (`{ query?, app?, limit? }`, read-only, over the caller's policy-filtered view, deterministic ranking by name, title and description, each hit with its name, risk and input schema, the result capped at `SEARCH_RESULT_MAX_BYTES` 256 KiB) is listed in both listing modes; the first built-in with arguments, so every built-in now passes the shape checks and its own schema and is audited with an `args_digest`.
- [x] **No per-app cap** ("why would we want to enforce these arbitrary caps on the amount of tools that sub-apps can have"). `CATALOG_LIMITS.toolsPerApp` (100) is removed; what bounds a catalog is its per-tool limits (the 64-character namespaced name, 2048-character description, 100-character title, 20 KiB per schema), the 5 MiB catalog response with 2048 characters of instructions, and the one atomic D1 write of the snapshot (`AppToolRepository`, `TOOL_ROWS_PER_INSERT` rows per statement in one `db.batch`), which holds the largest catalog that passes (its comment checks Cloudflare's documented D1 limits: 100 bound parameters per statement, 2 MB per row, no cap on a batch's statements), so the 5 MiB response is the bound; the warnings list keeps its own cap of 100. Codex fails outright past 2,048 catalog items: the compact listing is now what keeps a large organization usable there, not a cap.
- [x] **The compact surface: built now, automatic and by choice, never hiding.** Was: opt-in per connection, never the default, after a `claude -p` measurement. Now: `everyapp_call_read` and `everyapp_call_write` (`{ name, arguments? }`, exactly one inner call each through `executeToolCall`'s app-tool path: policy, shape checks, snapshot validation, one identity, one invocation, drift, and one audit row naming the inner tool with `via` set to the wrapper and the inner arguments' digest; the read wrapper resolves read tools only and mints `mcp:read` alone, the write wrapper is hidden from read-only callers like any write tool); the listing mode on the view (`listing`), compact once a caller's full listing would pass `FULL_LISTING_MAX_TOOLS` 256 or `FULL_LISTING_MAX_BYTES` 1 MiB, or chosen by `X-EveryApp-Tools: full|compact` or `?tools=` on `/mcp` for any caller kind and carried into the view's `refresh` so a held listen stream keeps it; the session hash computed from the listed tools, so a mode change rotates a 2025-era session; the instructions head naming the mode. Compact lists the built-ins alone and leaves every allowed app tool callable by name and through the wrappers; a policy-hidden tool stays `Unknown tool` through every path. The register rows `toolbus.hidden-equals-unknown` and `toolbus.builtins-readonly-reserved` are amended, `via` added to `audit.one-row-per-call-no-arguments`. Not built: a choice on the consent page.
- [x] **Two half-day PRs**, built 2026-10-24/25 under the overnight rule as #499, #501, #503, #504, #505 and #516 (the defaults under "MCP surface (2026-10-24)"; the hold went to 30 minutes, not 15). As planned, PR 1 (the design's option A): a test that the gateway instructions' header, app list and "not available" lines stay inside Claude Code's default 4,096-character read (the 8 KiB total and the 1,024 per-app clip stay; `mcp.md` says an app's own guidance may fall past that cut and past Codex's 512); `_meta: { "anthropic/alwaysLoad": true }` on the two built-ins; the `subscriptions/listen` hold on the gateway surface from 10 to 15 minutes after a `claude -p` reopen count confirms the backoff, the app-host surface unchanged, and the gateway recheck ending a stream whose OAuth token has expired; the drift test deriving the built-in names from `listBuiltinTools()`; `mcp.md` and the skill updated. PR 2: a nullable `client_info` column on `mcp_tool_calls` from the per-request `clientInfo`, so Admin → Activity can say which clients members use (today only OAuth connections carry a client name; everyapp.team's Access callers carry none). Alternative: leave all of it as it is.
- [ ] **Code mode: on the roadmap, not built** (Ben, 2026-10-24: "if you don't think we should do any of this code mode stuff either, then you can add that to the roadmap"; the design's recommendation stands). What it is: one more built-in, `everyapp_execute({ code })`, that runs a short program the model writes against the member's tools, so a ten-call workflow is one round trip and the model sees only what the program returns. The runtime ("do we use Dynamic Workers or something?"): nothing today, since nothing runs model code; the gateway's own isolate never can (Workers forbid `eval` and `new Function`, and an escape there reaches `JWT_PRIVATE_KEY`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN` and D1). If built, two runtimes qualify, both gateway-side and read-only first (the design's section 4.3, C): C1, a Dynamic Worker (Cloudflare's `worker_loaders` binding, one line of config; open beta since 2026-03-24, Workers Paid only, 1,000 unique Workers a month included then $0.002 per Worker-day, about $98 plus CPU for fifty thousand runs a month; what Cloudflare's own MCP server and its portals run), loaded per execution by a thin runner Worker that holds no secret and no database, only a service binding to a ToolBus entrypoint, with `globalOutbound: null`, no `nodejs_compat` and explicit `limits: { cpuMs, subRequests }`; or C2, a restricted interpreter inside the gateway isolate (the shape OpenCode, Executor v2 and Goose ship: a JavaScript-like language with no network, filesystem, imports or host globals by construction, bounded in time, tool calls and output bytes), about half C1's effort with no new Cloudflare primitive and no isolate-escape class to carry, at the price of a language that is not quite JavaScript and CPU that counts against the `/mcp` request. Either way each inner call is an ordinary `executeToolCall` with its own audit row and an execution id, nine new register invariants, a threat-model section, and about two weeks including live tests against four clients. If it is ever built, C2 first: its failure class is a bug in code we own, not a V8 boundary we do not. What reopens it: Admin → Activity showing repeated multi-call sequences the compact surface does not collapse, or results the clients' caps truncate, and a measurement on our own tools showing code mode recovering what the compact surface loses.
- [ ] **Skills: no store on the gateway and no usage-mined suggestions.** Answered in the thread on 2026-10-24 as the design puts it; Ben's decision is pending. The direction is apps shipping markdown-only skills beside their tools (`defineTools(..., { skills })`, snapshotted with the catalog, policy-filtered, capped per app, bodies naming tools as `<app>_<tool>` with a drift test, served through a built-in, a per-organization marketplace endpoint behind its own `skills:read` credential, and SEP-2640 now that the ToolBus is on `@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.4.0), started when an app needs it, OpenSEO's twelve first. Alternative: a PostHog-style per-organization store with write tools (five to eight days; reconsidered if mixed-harness organizations ask for a shared catalog git does not give them).
- [ ] **An every-app plugin: parked here, in plain words** (Ben, 2026-10-24: "I don't think I understand this plugin, so maybe you just add it to the TODO"). It is packaging, not a feature: a small public git repository Claude Code installs in two commands (`/plugin marketplace add`, then `/plugin install`), holding an `.mcp.json` that adds the Every App connector (`https://<their gateway>/mcp`; the gateway URL is asked once at install, as `user_config.gateway_url`) and one `SKILL.md` that tells the agent how to use it (connect, find a tool with `everyapp_search_tools`, read before write, what the compact listing is). The result is the same connector a person adds today with `claude mcp add`, plus a how-to the agent reads, in the shape Claude Code's marketplace installs and `npx skills add` installs for Codex and Cursor. About a day; worth doing as launch messaging ("Every App in Claude Code in two commands") or as a docs item after launch; nothing on the gateway changes for it.
- [x] **Found on the way: the ten-minute `subscriptions/listen` cap spends Claude Code's reopen budget** (fixed 2026-10-24: "MCP surface (2026-10-24)" below) ("after five reopens in an hour, Claude Code waits about six hours before the next one"), so by that rule a connected Claude Code stops hearing about app changes after about fifty minutes of a session; deploys and dropped connections spend the same budget. Fixed by PR 1. Alternative: keep ten minutes and document it.
- [x] **`@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.4.0 on the ToolBus** (#559, 2026-10-24): the Skills extension under `ext/skills` and gateway-aware `ServerEvent` forwarding; the SDK now rejects a number-typed `x-mcp-header` (the gateway strips the keyword anyway) and no longer wraps 2025-11-25 structured results.
- [ ] **Follow-ups.** Measure live, with the `claude -p` harness and a claude.ai account, what the everyapp.team connector loads in each listing mode and whether the 256 cap bites (the reports have no Anthropic answer; a throwaway app of 80 tools crosses it); tune the auto-compact thresholds (`FULL_LISTING_MAX_TOOLS`, `FULL_LISTING_MAX_BYTES`) once `client_info` shows which clients call and which get the compact list; decide whether claude.ai-class clients get the explicit choice at all (they can set no header, and `?tools=` fails their RFC 9728 resource check since the metadata names the bare `/mcp`; the alternative is registering `/mcp?tools=full` and `/mcp?tools=compact` as resources of their own, which amends `mcp.one-protected-resource` and is a held auth change) or stay on the automatic rule, the default taken; the every-app plugin above; a "repeated sequences and repeated identical calls" view on Admin → Activity as the instrument for the code-mode and skills triggers; the 2025-11-25 tasks extension for calls over 30 seconds when an app needs it.

### App first-run (2026-10-24)

The first five minutes inside the four launch apps were walked overnight on fresh local installs (`everyapp dev` with the local state wiped, the seeded owner, every key absent from `.dev.vars`, then each missing-setting state in turn), on desktop and phone, light and dark, with the MCP catalog probed once per app. Each app got one small PR of copy, empty states, error surfaces and first-run guidance, reviewed and rebased on the merged manifest PRs, then squash-merged under the launch-week rule for UI copy: OpenCRM #6, OpenAnalytics #8, Agentic Inbox #4 and OpenSEO #12 (OpenSEO's GitHub CI is red on every branch, see below; its checks were run in a checkout linked to main instead). What each app still owes is in its `TODO.md` under "First run (2026-10-24)"; what belongs to the platform, or to Ben, is here.

- [x] **`secret set --app <id>` is printed by three apps; it exists since #397 (b6865bc2, 2026-10-24), and the docs sweep re-checked it against `secret set --help`.** Was: The merged manifest PRs (crm #4, openseo #10, analytics #7) and tonight's PRs tell an admin to run `pnpm everyapp secret set TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY --app crm` (and `--app openseo`, `--app analytics`); main's `secret set` has no `--app` flag, an app secret is set from the app's directory. The flag arrives with the secrets CLI lane (#397). Merge #397 before Monday, or the apps name a flag the CLI refuses.
- [ ] **`EMAIL_FROM` alone is not a sender.** `parseEmailSender` needs a display name too, so a sentence that says "set EMAIL_FROM" sends an admin to a refusal. Analytics now says `pnpm everyapp secret set EMAIL_FROM EMAIL_FROM_NAME`; the inbox's composer and home banner say the same. Check the Gateway's own "Needs setup" copy and the setup skill say both names everywhere email is required.
- [ ] **An AI-provider readiness call, like `integrationStatus()` for capabilities.** Three apps asked for it independently: OpenSEO opens SAM's chat and the first message fails, the CRM learns the model is unset only at the first refusal, the inbox's agent says so only after the first prompt. A `GET /api/ai/<provider>` status (set or not, never the key) lets each app say up front "AI isn't set up on this gateway" where the feature lives. Until then the apps infer it from the first `ai_provider_not_configured` refusal.
- [ ] **Refusal bodies in member voice.** The AI proxy's `Provider is not configured: the gateway needs OPENROUTER_API_KEY for openrouter.` and DataForSEO's `everyapp secret set … --for gateway` sentence are relayed verbatim by the CRM and OpenSEO on some surfaces. Say `pnpm everyapp secret set NAME` and "an owner or admin" in the Gateway's refusal messages; the apps that quote them then read right without a fix each.
- [ ] **The email status should report the app's inbound addresses.** After creating a mailbox the inbox cannot say whether mail to it will arrive: routing is `pnpm everyapp email set --inbound`, which `GET /api/integrations/email` doesn't expose. Add `inbound` to the status so the app can say "mail to hello@example.com arrives once an owner routes the domain".
- [ ] **`everyapp dev` passes no `GATEWAY_URL`.** "Back to Every App" links to `/` (analytics: a redirect loop) or `https://gateway.dev.localhost` (CRM, OpenSEO: a dead host) and the analytics "Invite members" step 404s locally. Either the gateway-lite serves a launcher page and hands its URL to the app, or the SDK's dev branch sets it empty and the apps hide the links.
- [ ] **The SDK's dev members directory answers nobody for background work.** `listInDev` returns `{ members: [] }` unless the call is for a request, so every OpenAnalytics notification rule run under `everyapp dev` creates 0 messages and a developer cannot see a report locally. Answer the seeded dev user for background calls too.
- [ ] **`integrationStatus()` turns a failed status read into "not set up".** A non-OK answer from `GET /api/integrations/<id>` becomes `{ requested: false, configured: false }` instead of an error, so an app tells an admin to set a name that is already set during a Gateway blip. Throw (or return `null`) so apps can show nothing rather than a wrong instruction; analytics already treats `null` as unknown.
- [ ] **`EMAIL_FROM` without a name reads "configured" in dev and "not set up" in production.** `devEmailStatus` reports `configured: true` from `EMAIL_FROM` alone while the Gateway's `isEmailConfigured()` needs the name, so a developer sees a green Sender row with no address. Make the dev stand-in use `parseEmailSender` too.
- [ ] **OpenSEO's CI is red on every branch, including the docs PR #11.** `knip` in `ci:check` runs where `../private-every-app` doesn't exist, so the `link:` dependencies `everyapp` and `@every-app/sdk` read as unused and `everyapp` as an unlisted binary, and the job stops before tsc. Until publishing resumes (or CI clones the platform beside the app), "CI green" means `pnpm ci:check` and `pnpm test` in a checkout linked to main; the first-run PR #12 was checked that way: 1726 tests pass, and `tsc` shows the same three errors as `main` (`provider-access.ts` and `vite-plugin-every-app.ts` no longer fit the SDK's capability ids and the Vite plugin's types after the OAuth-scopes PRs), none in files the PR touches. Those three need an OpenSEO commit of their own.
- [ ] **The ports' linked platform checkout is stale, and the manifest PRs made that visible.** The ports link `~/workspaces/private-every-app`, which is at bc3fe476 (#378); its SDK reads `manifest.secrets` as strings, and since the manifest PRs merged every app's `cf workers types`, `cf build` and a fresh `everyapp dev` fail with `secrets: "[object Object]" isn't a valid binding name`. Tonight's PRs were verified in throwaway worktrees linked to main (`pnpm run check` and the CRM's `ci:check` green there). Pull and reinstall that checkout before touching the ports again; the running dev servers only survived because they booted before the merges.
- [x] **Set `CONTEXT_API_KEY` on everyapp.team before Monday, or ship OpenSEO's unrendered first scan.** (Set: `pnpm everyapp secret list` on 2026-10-24 22:10Z lists `CONTEXT_API_KEY` among the gateway's settings, and `status` shows openseo ready.) Saving the first domain always starts a scan with JavaScript rendering, and without the Context key the toast says "The first scan could not start. JavaScript rendering is not configured on this deployment" (now app-voiced with the command, still a failed first scan). OpenSEO's TODO has the fallback (openseo-04); setting the optional key is the shorter path for launch.
- [ ] **OpenAnalytics treats every member as an admin.** `viewer.ts` hard-codes `isAdmin: true`, so a plain member gets Add site, Create rule, retention and Delete site, and the member copy ("Ask an admin to add one") can never render. Honour the Gateway role, or delete the dead copy and say every member manages sites. Touches auth, so it stayed out of the PR.
- [ ] **The inbox's draft verifier fails closed with "Draft verification failed… Please try again."** When the model call fails (AI unset, or any refusal) upstream's verifier returns an empty draft and the send/draft tools refuse with that sentence, which an agent will retry forever. Say the real reason once ("AI isn't set up on this gateway, so drafts can't be verified") or let an unverified draft through as a draft only. Upstream's security check, so Ben's call. Related: a skipped auto-draft is logged as a fake user bubble "[Auto-triggered] New email from …" (upstream's convention).
- [ ] **OpenSEO's `/ai` setup page copies the hosted installer prompt.** On Every App the copyable prompt tells an agent to add `http://openseo.localhost:8788/...` as an MCP server with a personal token; the Gateway's `/mcp` connector is only in a footer alert. The CRM, the inbox and analytics now lead with the Gateway address and a `claude mcp add` line; OpenSEO's page should too (openseo-08, medium, in its TODO).
- [ ] **Two held items in the app TODOs touch identity.** CRM: the `AUTH_CONFIG_MISSING` sentence ("Deploy it with `everyapp deploy`") is built in the identity middleware and reaches a member on a misdeploy. OpenSEO: role-aware setup copy needs `orgRole` on `getEveryAppAccount()`, and better-auth's `useSession` still runs in Every App mode (a 404 on every load). Both are small; both are yours.
- [ ] **Decisions parked in the app TODOs.** CRM: `/settings/tracking` is a full page no nav item links to (delete or link), and the sidebar has no Help link. Analytics: the `MAIN_SECRET` setup page's "Deployment guide" link points at the private repository until the public one exists. Inbox: PR #6 (OpenRouter as the build default, held) decides whether the AI-off surfaces ever show on everyapp.team, since with the default build the Gateway keeps the inbox closed until `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is set.
- [ ] **Not fixed, by size (in the app TODOs).** OpenSEO: pre-check DataForSEO before creating a rank tracker or starting AI-visibility research (both end in a failed state today), the dashboard's "Too many requests" card for five minutes after a refusal, MCP tools answering "Internal error in openseo (ref …)" for an OpenRouter refusal, `/api/health` reporting capabilities. Inbox: no agent panel at all under 1024px, dark mode never applied, the reading pane at 265px with the agent panel open. CRM: MCP tools that never say why research is held or a connection is unavailable. Analytics: admin tables clipped at 1280px, SSR date hydration errors, run logs that lead with event codes.
- [ ] **Tooling note for overnight threads.** T3's `preview_*` tools fail for workflow subagents with `parent_not_active` whenever the parent thread is between turns; the walks fell back to Playwright from a sibling checkout's `node_modules`. Keep the parent in a foreground wait while subagents drive the browser, or give them Playwright from the start.
