### Public copy (2026-10-24)

The public repository gets the launch tree as one fresh commit (launch doc A1, B9): `pnpm public-copy` builds it, [docs/runbooks/public-copy.md](docs/runbooks/public-copy.md) is the runbook, `scripts/public-copy/policy.mjs` the policy. Built and verified overnight on 2026-10-24: the copy installs from nothing with the frozen lockfile, type-checks, passes the CLI tests, the docs drift tests and the gateway's cf dry run. PRs: #522 (no private names in the docs), #523 (LICENSE, SECURITY.md), the script PR (this section, the runbook, a CI dry run on every PR).

**Needs Ben**

- [x] **The license holder.** Ben (2026-10-24): "Every App, Inc." Applied. LICENSE says "Copyright (c) 2026 Every App contributors". Recommendation: keep it (MIT needs no legal entity, and the line can change when there is one); or put a name or a company there now.
- [ ] **Does TODO.md travel?** Default: yes, scrubbed (private repository names become "the maintainers' private repository" or the app's name, personal addresses "an e-mail address", a teammate's name "a teammate"; the script prints every rewrite). `pnpm public-copy --todo replace` ships `scripts/public-copy/TODO.public.md` instead, a one-page public TODO. Recommendation: scrubbed; B9 said candid is fine. Know that it still describes everyapp.team's internals and the committed-token incident (#488, #496).
- [ ] **Do docs/design and docs/threat-model travel?** Default: the five designs do, the research synthesis (`docs/design/2026-10-mcp-provider-apps-research.md`) does not (`--design designs`); the threat model, its invariants and the security model do. Recommendation: yes to the threat model (the README and SECURITY.md point at it), no to research. `--design all` or `--design none` change it; `none` breaks the docs drift test in the copy, since public documents cite the designs.
- [ ] **The launch and simplify logs** (`docs/launch-2026-10-27.md`, `docs/simplify-2026-10-24.md`) stay home by default (`--logs drop`): the hygiene sweep found they publish the maintainers' account's Access team name and two-factor posture, the old Workers still answering on the account's workers.dev, and the Sunday checklist against everyapp.team. A citation of either in another document becomes plain text in the copy, so the drift test there still passes. `--logs keep` ships them, scrubbed, if "moving fast is fine to show" (B9) should cover them too.
- [ ] **Roll the test account's token** (#488's `1k`): gitleaks and trufflehog both find it in three commits (6ed7fa47, 1696bda9, 424f92dd), with the test account's id and zone. Trufflehog could not verify it, which proves nothing (account-owned tokens don't verify at the user endpoint it tries). Sign in as the test user, Manage Account → Account API Tokens → Roll. The copy has no `1k` and refuses one. Nothing else in the history verified or looked real: the rest is the deleted research folder's pasted changelogs and the tests' fakes.
- [ ] **The old public history.** `every-app/every-app` holds the 2026-08-01 copy and its `gateway-v0.1.x` tags. `--push` force-replaces `main`; the tags stay until deleted (`git push <url> --delete <tag>`). Delete them, or they advertise releases that never worked.
- [ ] **After the push**: default branch `main`, flip public, Settings → Code security → private vulnerability reporting (SECURITY.md and the threat model promise it), Actions → "Require approval for all external contributors". Then the README's clone line works and the CLI's `IDENTITY_KEY_UNKNOWN` fix link resolves.
- [ ] **The hard-coded account id.** The maintainers' Cloudflare account id is in the public code as the "never touch this account" guard (`FORBIDDEN_ACCOUNT_IDS` in `packages/cli/src/lib/teardown/guards.ts` and `packages/onboarding-loop/src/cf.ts`, and in `docs/runbooks/onboarding-loop-test-accounts.md`). An account id is not a secret (it is in every dashboard URL), but it names the account. Keep (recommended: the guard is worth more than the obscurity), or move it to an environment variable. Other identifiers that travel on purpose, none secret: the landing page's D1 id in `landing-page/wrangler.jsonc`, the names of everyapp.team's apps and migrations in test comments, the dev keypair, and the gateway logo's signed content credentials (made with ChatGPT; a C2PA manifest inside `apps/every-app-gateway/public/transparent-logo.png`).

**Decided by default, confirm**

- [ ] The copy's commit is dated as the source commit and authored as `Every App <noreply@everyapp.com>` (never the source author's address), with only the source commit's id in its message, so the same tree gives the same commit hash; `--write` replaces an earlier copy and refuses a directory the script didn't write; `--push` builds and writes again, accepts only the public repository's URL unless `--any-remote`, needs a typed `push` with no flag to skip it, and refuses a remote whose name says private.
- [ ] `.worktreeinclude` and `*.local` files don't travel. A file named `1k`, `accounts.json`, `providers.env`, `.dev.vars*`, `.env*` (not `.example`), `*.pem`, `*.key`, `.npmrc` fails the build, and so does a secret shape in any text file (Cloudflare, Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter, GitHub, Slack, AWS keys, a private key block other than the dev keypair, a `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` value, a setup link, a signed token outside the tests), a private repository link, or the maintainer's home directory.
- [ ] The scrub applies to `TODO.md`, `docs/**/*.md` and `adr/**/*.md` only, case-insensitively. Code and the public docs must be clean at the source, or the build fails; that is how a teammate's name was found in `landing-page/content/agent/access.md` and a personal address in `landing-page/content/agent/email.md` (both fixed by #527 and the script PR). The hygiene sweep's other finds are rules now: the maintainers' `workers.dev` hostnames, the old Workers' inventory, three Access ids, the old dogfood domain.
- [ ] A new CI job, "Public copy dry run", runs the script on every pull request. The private-name list (`scripts/public-copy/private.txt`) is left out of the copy, so the public repository's own CI runs the generic checks only.
- [ ] gitleaks is not a dependency: `--scan` uses a `gitleaks` on `PATH`, and the history scan is a runbook command. Neither is in CI; `--self-test` (the deny list and every secret shape against values no allowlist excuses) is.

### Starter (2026-10-24)

The overnight pass over `starter/`, what `pnpm everyapp create` copies: run the way a new person does (root install, `create`, `dev`, every file an agent reads, the checks a fresh app ships), judged against Ben's four claims (fast to build, secure behind the Gateway, the Gateway gives the app only what it declares, agent-friendly), then rewritten as the smallest set of patterns an agent copies. Six review lenses, a merge, three competing designs and two judges (workflow `judge-starter`), before/after screenshots in the PRs. Merged on green: #541 (a fresh app's `format:check` and knip), #544 (the rewrite: one server-rendered screen that shows who the Gateway signed in and what `everyapp.config.ts` allowed, a public `/health` route, one todos module with server-assigned ids and queries scoped by `user.id`, `defineTools()` on that module, one 0.3 s test against an in-memory SQLite with the app's migrations, AGENTS.md in the order feature → table → capability → secret → deploy, a README for the person; the users table, react-query and its localStorage persister, the History route, sidebar, tab bar, modal, daisyUI, sonner, lucide and the devtools pill are gone: 33 source files to 15, 9 dependencies fewer). #543 (the manifest schema fix the rewrite ran into) merged under Ben's overnight rule.

- [x] **#543 (merged overnight under Ben's rule): the built CLI refused every manifest with a capability that takes no scope sets.** `capabilityEntrySchema()` gave `members`, `email`, `dataforseo`, `context` and the social OAuth clients `scopes: z.undefined()` in a strict object; the perimeter and the gateway run it under zod 3 where a missing key passes, but the CLI's tsup bundle inlines the perimeter and resolves `zod` to the CLI's zod 4, where that key is required: `everyapp create|dev|deploy|status` answered `Invalid everyapp.config: capabilities.members.scopes: Invalid input: expected nonoptional, received undefined` for the crm, analytics, inbox and openseo manifests, and the CLI's tests never saw it (vitest resolves the perimeter from source, zod 3). #543 is the one-line `.optional()`; a sibling thread adds a CI job that runs the built CLI against a fresh app; the remaining follow-up is one zod major across the perimeter, the gateway, the SDK and the CLI.
- [x] **`capabilities.members` is on the starter's screen** (#544, after #543 merged): the manifest declares it, `src/serverFunctions/home.ts` calls `listMembers({ env, for: request })`, and the Allowed card says how many people can open the app: the one Gateway call an agent copies.
- [ ] **KV left the starter's manifest.** Nothing read `env.KV`, and the browser deployer refuses KV for third-party apps (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/metadata.ts`), so a starter that asks for it taught the wrong lesson; `cfDryRun.test.ts` now expects `env.DB` alone. If a KV pattern is wanted in the starter, it needs a real read (a per-user preference) and the test's `env.KV` line back.
- [ ] **`everyapp dev` prints the perimeter's JSON proxy line for every request**, assets and 304s included, with no path, under the ready block (`packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts` `logProxy`, written for Workers Logs). In the gateway-lite, drop it or print `GET /path 200 3ms` for documents and non-2xx only. The create output and the ready block themselves read right.
- [ ] **A fresh app has no CI for `test` and `types:check`** and cannot until the packages publish: `create` rewrites `workspace:*` to `link:` paths into this machine's checkout. When `everyapp` and `@every-app/sdk` publish, give the starter a `ci.yml` (`pnpm install`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm types:check`); today the starter ships no workflow at all.
- [ ] **The onboarding loop's verifier can now read the signed-in page's body.** `/` server-renders the email and the Allowed card (nothing is behind `ClientOnly` any more), so row A3 could assert on text as well as the `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` meta it keeps; `packages/onboarding-loop/README.md` and this file no longer say the marker is `Todos`.
- [ ] **The skill's opening says to run everyapp from the checkout**; an app's `package.json` links the CLI, so `pnpm everyapp …` works from the app's directory too (every example in the skill runs there). Its manifest sentence omits `providers` and `capabilities`, the two fields behind "the Gateway gives the app only what you allow". Small wording, docs lane.
- [ ] **Editing a server-function file and its route in the same instant can leave `everyapp dev` answering 500** (`Cannot use 'in' operator … TSS_SERVER_FUNCTION_FACTORY`, TanStack Start's dev registry mid-reload) until a restart; a single-file edit hot-reloads fine. Seen once; worth a line in the troubleshooting page if it recurs.
- [ ] **`everyapp dev` reads `public` and `resources` once, at start.** Vite restarts the app when `everyapp.config.ts` changes (the manifest define and the title follow), but the gateway-lite keeps the public routes it registered: removing `/health` from `public` left anonymous `GET /health` at 200 until a restart. AGENTS.md says to restart; the CLI could watch the file and re-register the app instead.
- [ ] **Not changed, by choice:** `pnpm build` prints two Rollup warnings about zod's own comments and one hidden-sourcemap warning (upstream; a filter would be a comment an agent doesn't need); the starter ships no workflow (the security review went local on 2026-10-24); `@tanstack/react-start` stays pinned at 1.168.61 (1.169 needs Vite 8); the identity card shows email, role and organization id, not `channel`, `scopes` or `jti` (the person's facts, not the token's).

### Built CLI in CI (2026-10-24)

CI now runs the CLI people actually run. A new workflow, "Built CLI" (`.github/workflows/built-cli.yml`, every pull request and push to main, 20-minute limit), builds the SDK and the CLI and runs `packages/cli/src/bin/builtCli.test.ts`: `bin/everyapp.js` over `dist/` as a child process, with the Node running vitest, against a fresh app in a temp directory outside the checkout and no Cloudflare account. In order, sharing one app: help for every route `commandPaths()` lists plus no arguments, `--help` and `--version`, and an unknown command under `--json` (exit 251, one error event); `create` (no error or warning events, the app links the checkout's packages by path); `db migrate`; `dev` up to the first 200 through the gateway-lite, by Host, then SIGINT and exit 130; a manifest with `members`, `email`, `dataforseo`, `context` and a scoped `slack` client through `db migrate` (the #543 regression) and one with `scopes` on `members` refused with `INVALID_MANIFEST`; `release --json --out` (a gzip tarball whose SHA-256 matches the `done` event); and `status --json` against the fake Cloudflare account (`src/test/fakeCloudflare.ts`, a Gateway installed and one app live) through a new seam, `EVERYAPP_CF_BINARY` in `packages/cli/src/lib/cf/bin.ts`, which makes the file it names cf for every call; the test's stand-in posts each call to a loopback bridge and the fake answers. Every `--json` line must be a protocol event with the exit code the protocol gives (a line that isn't one fails the run at once, naming it), and no run may rebuild `dist/` (the launcher says "Updated everyapp CLI" when it does, so the artifact under test is the one CI built). A run gets none of vitest's variables: with the worker's `NODE_ENV=test` inherited, `release` built a development bundle a quarter larger (1,317,955 bytes to 1,026,850) that cf's `config.json` still called `production`, so the release test now also asserts React's production chunks (`Minified React error` present, `jsxDEV` absent). A `dev` still up when its test fails is stopped as a person would, SIGINT, SIGINT, then SIGKILL, so vite and workerd go with it. `pnpm test:built-cli` from the checkout's root builds both and runs it; `pnpm -C packages/cli test:built` runs it on a `dist/` already built; `pnpm test` skips it (`EVERYAPP_BUILT_CLI=1` gates it). Local timings on 2026-10-24: help 0.8 s (44 runs, four at a time), create 16 s (its `pnpm install`), db migrate 2.5 s, dev 7 s to the first 200, manifests 2.7 s, release 7 s, status 0.7 s; 45 s in all, the job under three minutes with the install and the builds.

Why: at 13:20Z on 2026-10-24 the CLI as built from main refused every manifest with a capability that has no scope sets (`capabilities.members.scopes: expected nonoptional`); #543 fixed the schema with `.optional()`, and CI never saw it because every test resolves the packages from source. The root cause is a zod split in the bundle: `packages/cli/package.json`'s tsup config has `noExternal: [@every-app/perimeter, @every-app/sdk, @every-app/toolbus]`, so the perimeter's source is inlined into `dist/`, but every other import stays external, the perimeter's `import { z } from "zod"` included (dist has seven `from"zod"` and two `from"zod/v4"`). At runtime Node resolves `zod` from `packages/cli/node_modules/zod`, which is zod 4.6.5 (the CLI declares `zod ^4.2.0`), while `packages/perimeter` declares `zod ^3.25.76` and resolves 3.25.76 from source, and so do the perimeter's vitest, the CLI's vitest and the gateway (`apps/every-app-gateway`, `zod ^3.25.76`). The SDK and the toolbus devDepend on `zod ^4.2.0` (resolving 4.6.7); `@modelcontextprotocol/server` (a toolbus dependency) depends on `zod ^4.2.0`; the starter and the onboarding loop declare `^4.2.0`; the landing page `^4.6.5`. So: CLI dist 4.6.5, perimeter source 3.25.76, gateway 3.25.76, SDK dist 4.6.7, toolbus 4.6.7. The only perimeter file importing zod is `packages/perimeter/src/manifest/manifest.ts`; the SDK's and the toolbus's sources import zod only in tests; the CLI's own zod code (`src/lib/cf/schemas.ts`, `runner.ts`, `access.ts`, `plan.ts`, `emailRouting.ts`, `app/release.ts`, `gateway/release.ts`) already runs under zod 4 in production.

**Needs Ben**

- [ ] **Merge #547, the perimeter on zod 4** (https://github.com/every-app/private-every-app/pull/547; held because it touches the perimeter package and the lockfile). It moves `packages/perimeter` to `zod ^4.2.0`, the zod the bundle runs it under, so the schema the CLI's tests validate is the schema the built CLI runs. It changes `packages/perimeter/package.json`, one lockfile line (the perimeter's zod, 3.25.76 → 4.6.7), `manifest.ts` (zod 4 idioms and the issue formatter), five test regexes, the CLI's docs walker (`packages/cli/src/docs/manifestFields.ts` reads zod 4's `_zod.def`), and the two docs that quote zod's messages. Verified there: the perimeter's 408, the CLI's 1109, the SDK's 731, toolbus's 113 and the gateway's 1225 tests, every type-check, the gateway release build, and the built CLI loading a manifest that lists members, email, dataforseo, context and a scoped google-workspace entry. Until it merges, the built-CLI job is what catches the next split.

**Decided by default, confirm**

- [ ] **The seam is an environment variable, named only in code and here.** `EVERYAPP_CF_BINARY` makes any file cf for every call, project plane included (the runner spawns `node <file> …args` with the call's cwd and env). It is not in the agent docs (`landing-page/content/agent`), since nobody but this test should set it; the docs drift tests don't ask for it. Anyone who can set the CLI's environment already decides what it runs, so it adds no reach; say so if you'd rather it were a build-time flag the published CLI can't read.
- [ ] **`status` against a fake, not a deploy dry run.** `everyapp deploy` has no `--dry-run` and needs a login, so the closest the job gets to a deploy is `release` (the build, the policy check, the schema check, the tarball) plus `status` over the fake account; a deploy to a real account stays the onboarding loop's.
- [ ] **A separate workflow, on every pull request.** Its own job rather than a step of `pr-checks.yml`, so a slow `pnpm install` in the new app never holds the unit tests; 20 minutes is the ceiling, three is the expectation.
- [ ] **The test needs the network once**, for the new app's `pnpm install` (the starter's dependencies come from the registry; the store the runner caches has them). Nothing else leaves the machine: the fake account is in process, the stand-in cf talks to 127.0.0.1.
- [ ] **Messages that were zod 3's are now zod 4's** (#547): `Unrecognized key: "senders"` (was `Unrecognized key(s) in object: 'senders'`), `Invalid option: expected one of "read"|"write"` (was `Invalid enum value. Expected 'read' | 'write', received 'x'`), `Invalid input: expected string, received undefined` (was `Required`), `Too big: expected string to have <=200 characters` (was `String must contain at most 200 character(s)`), `Invalid URL` (was `Invalid url`). The perimeter's own messages (`Members offers read access`, `takes no scopes`, `reserved scope id`, `senders moved out of the manifest`, …) are unchanged. The agent docs' manifest page and `docs/manifest-settings.md` quote the new text.
- [ ] **Record keys keep their own messages.** A key that fails its schema (`capabilities.OpenAI`, `scopes.*`) still reads as the key schema's own message (`provider must start with a lowercase letter…`, `invalid scope id`), not zod 4's `Invalid key in record`; the keys are checked inside the refinement rather than by the record's key schema, so a malformed capability id no longer hides its siblings' problems, and after the fix round every problem is reported in one pass, not one per run.
- [ ] **Deprecated-but-working zod 4 idioms stay** (`.passthrough()`, `.strict()`, `.extend().strict()`, `z.string().url()`, `z.ZodIssueCode.custom`, `z.ZodTypeAny`); behavior is the same, and rewriting them (`.loose()`, `z.strictObject`, `z.url()`) is a separate pass.
- [ ] **zod 4's url check trims its input**, so a whitespace-padded `docs` URL is now accepted and stored trimmed where zod 3 refused it.
- [ ] **The gateway's Worker bundle already carried three zods** before #547: 3.25.76 (the gateway's, better-auth's and the MCP SDK's), 4.6.5 (`@cloudflare/config`, `better-auth`) and 4.6.7 (toolbus's `@modelcontextprotocol/server`, `@better-auth/core`, TanStack's router generator); the perimeter's manifest module moved from the first to the third. Server chunks grew 927 bytes (3,544,192 → 3,545,119), the release tarball 3,736 bytes (6,769,094 → 6,772,830).

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **Fold the job into `pr-checks.yml`?** One fewer workflow to read, against the unit tests waiting on an install; decide once the job's time on the runner is known.
- [ ] **If `create` is slow on the runner**, cache the pnpm store for the new app's install (setup-node's `cache: pnpm` covers the workspace's store; the app in `/tmp` uses the same store, so it should already hit).
- [ ] **`dev` under the test prints the perimeter's JSON proxy line per request** (the Starter section above); the test tolerates it, a quieter gateway-lite would make its failure output readable.

### Cold read (2026-10-24)

Two outsiders against `main` at 65fcac70 on the Saturday before launch: a skeptical first user (five guises: technical founder, non-technical founder, the founder at a logged-out terminal, their coding agent on the README prompt, the founder opening the created app) and an outside security researcher (154 read-only, unauthenticated probes against everyapp.team and its four app hosts, a code read, the public copy). [docs/design/2026-10-cold-read.md](docs/design/2026-10-cold-read.md) has the journey with a verdict per moment, the findings ranked with evidence, and the five fixes that would most change Monday. The perimeter held: no 5xx, every control-plane route behind Access, the bypassed prefixes answer as the threat model says; the confirmed security findings are low or informational. The first user's problems are in the words and the packaging. PRs: #549 (threat-model lines), #550 (README and user docs), #551 (starter and skill), #552 (public copy), #553 (CLI and agent pages), all merged 2026-10-24; #554 is HELD for Ben.

**Needs Ben**

- [ ] **The four app repositories (fix 1, the biggest Monday risk).** `github.com/every-app/open-crm`, `open-analytics` and `agentic-inbox` do not exist (checked 2026-10-24 with `gh repo view`); `every-app/open-seo` is public but has no Every App port and no everyapp release; `every-app/every-app` is still private. The README's "Four apps on day one" links are the skeptical reader's first click, and the port checkouts pin `link:../private-every-app`, so a created repository fails on the second click until they are unlinked. Create the repositories and unlink, or change the README links; publish a release per app or keep `install <url>` conditional (the README now says none publishes one yet). Launch-morning check: `for r in every-app open-seo open-crm open-analytics agentic-inbox; do curl -s -o /dev/null -w "$r %{http_code}\n" https://github.com/every-app/$r; done`, then a clone of each into an empty directory with `pnpm install` succeeding.
- [ ] **Where a created app lives (fix 2).** `create` inside the checkout leaves the app untracked in `apps/<name>` and rewrites `pnpm-lock.yaml`; the documented `git pull` then aborts on the next upstream lockfile change (five since 2026-10-17). The docs now say so and give the recovery (`git checkout -- pnpm-lock.yaml`). The product decision stays: fork, create outside the checkout by default, or `create` stops rewriting the root lockfile (see "Onboarding loop" finding 4).
- [ ] **HELD: security PR #554** for the two gateway changes an outside reader would file: server-function error bodies carry the `PublicError`'s `stack` string (behind Access on everyapp.team, anonymous on a self-host without it), and case variants of the Access bypass prefixes (`/MCP`, `/OAUTH/TOKEN`, `/.WELL-KNOWN/x`) pass Access and fall to the control-plane 404 shell because the gateway matches case-sensitively; the PR answers a JSON 404 for every spelling of a bypassed prefix the gateway does not serve. Neither reaches a data route today.
- [ ] **The `.dev`/`.app` first-year price.** README, `/docs`, `/docs/domain` say $1; the skill's `setup/gateway.md` says about $8 at cost. Neither figure came from Cloudflare. Verify at the registrar, then one number everywhere, or state only "at cost" and the renewal prices.
- [ ] **The headline setup number.** "About 15 minutes" (README, `/docs/gateway`), "20 minutes" (the prompt), "5 to 20" (`setup --help`), "about 30" for the whole Get started. The docs PR labels what each number covers; pick the headline.
- [ ] **The home page** (yours by the preflight rule): three taglines (`index.tsx` H2 "Make every app open source", the title, README line 3), which a non-technical reader took as "my CRM becomes public"; "Get started" lands on `/docs` with no command; the unlabeled OpenSEO star badge wraps away from its link on a phone (an `aria-label` and a visible "OpenSEO" span are ready); no screenshot of the Gateway anywhere; refresh `openSeoStarsFallback` before the deploy.
- [ ] **A README line on the hosted tier.** `/llms.txt` and the agent nav show "Hosted Every App" and `app.everyapp.com`; README and home never mention it, and a lock-in reader asks whether self-host is the bait. One sentence ("a hosted version is planned for after launch; the self-hosted Gateway stays, from the same code") or nothing, your call.
- [ ] **Who "we" is.** The README's only "who makes this" was an X handle (now "Made by Ben Senescu, who also makes openseo.so, and contributors"); no About or Terms; three domains in front of the reader (everyapp.com, `support@everyapp.dev`, `analytics.everyapp.team`); no response expectation (the docs now quote the threat model's "a very small team"). An About line, Terms, and whether `support@everyapp.com` gets a mailbox.
- [ ] **`npm deprecate everyapp@"<0.3.0"` and the SDK** (launch item A5): the README's "Never `npx everyapp`" now says why, but the deprecation is the real fix.
- [ ] **The Access team name.** `/docs/access`, the CLI prompt (`access/index.ts`), `--team` help and `agent/setup.md` say it cannot be changed later; the skill's `setup/access.md:60` says Cloudflare lets it be changed. One of them is wrong; unverifiable offline.
- [ ] **The device login's breadth.** `cf auth login` asks for cf's full default scope set across every account the person belongs to (DNS edit and registrar included); everyapp uses Workers, D1, KV, the zone list and DNS. The docs now say so and offer the five-permission API token instead. Asking cf for fewer scopes is a product change (the pinned cf has no scope flag).
- [ ] **Landing page hygiene** (you deploy it): no `public/_headers` (HSTS, nosniff, frame denial, a CSP that allows `analytics.everyapp.team`), `workers_dev` and `preview_urls` on in `landing-page/wrangler.jsonc`, the waitlist server function accepts unbounded anonymous inserts (a limiter or Turnstile, or drop the form).
- [ ] **OpenAnalytics `POST /collect`** reflects any Origin with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`. Harmless today (the gateway strips cookies and bearers before proxying, CSRF downgrades to anonymous), but a beacon needs no credentials: drop the header in the app repository (`src/lib/edge/collector/collect.ts`, its tests pin it). Optionally drop `bindings` and `now` from `/healthz`.
- [ ] **Does this document travel?** `docs/design/2026-10-cold-read.md` goes into the public copy by default (designs travel). It is written without private names or scratch paths and the dry run passes. If a candid outside read of the dogfood gateway should stay home, add it to the `--logs drop` pattern in `scripts/public-copy.mjs`.

- [ ] The README prompt's exit-2 rule says run `data.rerun` (or a `data.choices[].rerun`), hand a `needsTerminal` command to the person without `--json`; the same text in `landing-page/content/docs/setup-prompt.txt`.

- [ ] The public copy: the Worker-prefix rule matches the literal `*`, two rules for the agents subdomain and the scratch paths, a test fixture address rewritten, the seven mangled TODO lines fixed; `--self-test` proves the prefix shape.
- [ ] `/docs/update` has "Remove everything" (no command exists; setup finds the Gateway by its D1 database, so a fresh start works); `/docs/cloudflare` lists what Every App puts in the account; `/docs/gateway` step 3 says what the login grants.

### UI review (2026-10-25)

The Saturday design review of the gateway before launch, presented as a designer's walk: [docs/design/2026-10-25-ui-review/index.html](docs/design/2026-10-25-ui-review/index.html) (open it in a browser; GitHub renders only the README beside it). 177 real screenshots from a seeded local gateway on main (1b424eac) as owner, admin, member, signed out and behind the mock Cloudflare Access ring, light and dark, 1440 and 390 wide, plus the terminal (real CLI help and logged-out states, the real status and deploy renderers over the CLI's fake Cloudflare account, the setup-skills fake for setup). Each flow has a caption and a three-part critique per screenshot (clear / extra / off); 101 findings faced two refuters each and 66 survived; the ranked fixes below are those, merged. No code changed in this pass. Items marked "already noted above" repeat an open line of the UI states section with the review's evidence behind it.

A rename in Manage workspace is the better medium-sized follow-up. - [x] **Multiple owners** (Ben, 2026-10-24: "Can we allow multiple owners?"). Done in #604: an owner makes a member or admin an owner from the Users menu, and changes the role of, deactivates or deletes another owner; `everyapp workspace role <email> <owner|admin|member>` does it from a terminal. The invariant, enforced by the write itself on the gateway (one conditional UPDATE or DELETE) and in the CLI's SQL: an organization always keeps at least one owner who can act (`LAST_OWNER` refuses a demotion, deactivation or deletion that would leave no other live owner, so two owners acting on each other at once can't both succeed; a deactivated owner does not count); owners are peers; nobody changes their own role; admins never manage owners; invitations stay member or admin; a password reset for an owner stays refused (a reset link hands the account to whoever holds it). The threat-model row `roles.owner-immutable-no-self-admin-needs-owner` and gap 17 ("no ownership transfer") say so; a sole owner who is locked out still needs the operator (or `everyapp workspace role`).

**Decisions taken 2026-10-24, the defaults Ben can flip** (each merged under the merge-green rule; none blocks launch):

- The invitation card shows the invited address masked (`i•••@example.com`), so a leaked link reveals no address before sign-in; the full address is a one-line change in `peekInvitation` (#601).
- `GET /api/auth/sign-in-methods` says whether the gateway can send email (`emailConfigured`), so the forgot-password page can say "Ask an admin for a password reset link" instead of promising mail (#603). It discloses one boolean to anyone who can reach the sign-in page.
- Admins, not only owners, can rename the workspace (#605): the name is the organization's, and admins already manage its people and apps.
- Both email names are required settings (#615, TODO 641's alternative): the CLI reads names, not values, so the combined `EMAIL_FROM="Name <address>"` form reads as missing EMAIL_FROM_NAME in `secret list` and `status` while the gateway still sends.
- The "N more below" cue sits inside the list box's bottom fade rather than under the box (#600).

### Gateway shell (2026-10-24)

Ben's screen walkthrough of everyapp.team on 2026-10-24, the sidebar and the account menu: the Pinned tab went (#571), AI connections, Documentation and the theme moved into the account menu with a plain Sign out (#576), the Tokens page says what tokens are for (#577). Screenshots on the branch `ui-screenshots-2026-10-24` under `gateway-shell-2026-10-24/`.

- [ ] **Tokens for members, or an honest doc line.** A personal access token acts as whoever created it, and the Tokens page sits under Manage workspace, which refuses members, so a member cannot make one. [landing-page/content/docs/connect-ai.mdx](landing-page/content/docs/connect-ai.mdx) says "An owner or admin makes one for you", which hands the member a token with the admin's access. Either open Tokens to members (the `TODO` in `apps/every-app-gateway/src/routes/_app/admin/tokens.tsx`; the natural home is a per-user Settings page with AI connections beside it, reached from the account menu) or make the doc say a token acts as its creator and a member asks to be made an admin. Pointers: `apps/every-app-gateway/src/routes/_app/admin.tsx` (the owner/admin gate), `src/serverFunctions/userTokens.ts` (`userId: context.user.id`), `packages/cli/skills/everyapp/mcp.md` and `landing-page/content/agent/mcp.md` say the same as the user doc.
- [ ] **A persisted apps list never refetches on reload.** Seen while taking the screenshots: with `every-app-gateway-react-query-cache` in localStorage, reloading the launcher issues no `getMyApps` request, so the library shows whatever the cache held (a new owner who deploys their first app and refreshes keeps seeing "Deploy your first app", whose line "Refresh this page and it appears here" is then untrue); with the key removed the apps load. The TanStack DB collection subscribes its own `QueryObserver` once at init, so `refetchOnMount: "always"` in `queryClient.ts` never applies after the persister restores. Pointers: `apps/every-app-gateway/src/routes/__root.tsx` (`PersistQueryClientProvider`: refetch the collections in `onSuccess`, the way `refetchCollectionsAfterAuth` in `src/client/tanstack-db/cache-utils.ts` does after sign-in), or a `staleTime` on `userAppsCollection`; the UI review's "Clear cached data on sign-out and when switching organizations" is the same cache.
- [ ] **The pinned-first order is the one order.** Pinned apps lead All apps and the Name A–Z / Pinned first menu went with the tab; a pin is the only way to reorder. If a sort control is wanted back, it goes with the search as a toolbar item, not as a preference.
### App open latency (2026-10-24)

Ben, recording on everyapp.team: "Why is it so slow to navigate to each of these apps?" Measured from Workers Logs (48 h) and the perimeter's proxy lines joined to invocations, his own CRM click included: Cloudflare Access costs nothing per navigation (one login per app host in 36 h, the cookie lives 720 h, 3-22 ms at the edge); the gateway Worker adds 35-70 ms per request warm and 170-370 ms (60-140 ms CPU) on a fresh isolate; the CRM Worker's own cold start is 400-1200 ms on a trivial request; a page is a document, ~6 script chunks and two server functions in a row, each through the gateway. The gateway's entry chunk was 1,982,528 bytes and carried the whole control plane (React DOM server, the route tree) into every app-host isolate. D1 itself answers in under a millisecond (p50 0.25 ms); the cost is round trips in a row. The agent docs' identity page now has a "What a request costs" section with the table. Shipped: #572 (the launcher prefetches an app's document on hover in Chrome via speculation rules, and opens the connection in every browser), #573 (the perimeter: one round trip fewer with expired caches, the Access assertion verified once a minute per isolate and started beside the session's reads, fingerprinted assets kept by the browser for a year, `Server-Timing` on every proxied response, `gatewayMs` in the proxy log), and #574 (the cold-start diet: the TanStack Start server entry, the ToolBus intercept, the AppGateway entrypoint and the third-party handoff loaded on first use, so an app-host isolate never evaluates the control plane; the entry chunk 1,982,528 → 1,594,881 bytes). Each PR had two adversarial reviews (security, correctness); #573's correctness review found a rejected verification kept for a minute and an in-flight promise shared across requests, both fixed before merge. The app-owned costs (CRM's client-only shell, its two sequential server functions, its cold start) went to the "Four apps clarity pass" thread with numbers.

**Needs Ben**

- [ ] **Deploy everyapp.team** (`pnpm everyapp update` from the checkout) to get any of this: nothing was deployed from the swath. Then the proxy log's `gatewayMs` and the `Server-Timing` header give the after numbers; the before are in the identity page.
- [ ] **Take Better Auth off the app-host path?** Warm, a request still waits on D1 three times in a row (Better Auth reads the session, then the user; then the access row), and a fresh isolate pays Better Auth's initialisation. A direct reader (the signed cookie checked, one `batch()` for the session with its user, the membership, the organization's status and the access row, the Access vouch beside it) would be one round trip and far less code on a cold isolate, and would have to reproduce `session.db-read-every-request-no-cookie-cache`, the ban check, `access.outer-ring.session-counts-only-when-vouched` and the daily `updateAge` refresh. A smaller step is Better Auth's `advanced.database.joins` (one query for session+user), which needs drizzle relations declared for every model Better Auth joins (session→user, and the organization plugin's) and a check that every control-plane query still answers. Both are security-boundary rewrites of the hot path: yours to call, not done in launch week.
- [ ] **A per-isolate verdict cache for subresources?** The six script chunks that follow a document could reuse the document's session verdict for a few seconds instead of reading the session and the access row again each. With #573 the chunks stop being requested at all on repeat visits, so the gain is the first visit after a deploy only; it would relax `perimeter.cache-bounded-revocation` (live reads) by that window. Parked unless the first-visit numbers still hurt.

**Decided by default, confirm**

- [ ] **Prefetch on hover, not on load.** The speculation rules use `eagerness: "moderate"` (Chrome prefetches after ~200 ms of hover or on pointerdown), for every app that opens; nothing is fetched for apps the person never points at, and Chrome keeps at most two such prefetches. `immediate` for pinned apps would warm them on every launcher visit at the cost of one document render per pinned app per visit.
- [ ] **A verified Access assertion is remembered for 60 s per isolate**, never past the token's `exp`, keyed by the exact token and configuration; failures are never kept. Revocation at Access stops a request at the edge before the gateway sees it, and the JWKS is already cached 10 min, so no revocation path got slower. 0 s (verify every time) is a one-line change if you'd rather.
- [ ] **Fingerprinted assets are `private, max-age=31536000, immutable`** only when the app left the asset server's default (`public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`) and only for `/assets/<name>-<8 char hash>.<ext>`; `private` keeps shared caches out of content served behind the session. An app that wants no caching sets its own `Cache-Control` (a `_headers` file in its assets directory).
- [ ] **`Server-Timing` on every proxied response**, private hosts included: it exposes the app's and the gateway's processing time to the page's own scripts (same-origin) and the network panel. Harmless, and the only way a person sees the split without Workers Logs.

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **The gateway's fresh-isolate CPU (60-140 ms)** is the first-time execution of the auth path (Better Auth's router, drizzle, jose) rather than its own initialisation, which runs in under 2 ms on a laptop; only a smaller hot path (above) shrinks it.
- [ ] **Nothing pins the cold-start diet** (#574's correctness review): a later static import of the TanStack server entry, the ToolBus intercept, the host-session handoff or the capability proxy anywhere in server.ts's graph folds them back into the entry chunk and no test fails. A build-level check (the entry chunk's sourcemap must not list those modules) would hold it; `server/toolbus/catalog-sync` is still in the entry through `app-activation.ts`, a second diet if wanted.
- [ ] **The starter should ship a `_headers` file** (`/assets/* Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`), as crm and openseo already do: the app knows its naming; the perimeter's rule (#573) stays the fallback for apps that don't.
- [ ] **CRM's cold start is its bundle** (the clarity thread measured a production build: the Worker entry 5.8 MB plus a 2.1 MB router chunk it imports statically, ~3.5 MB of agent and AI stack evaluated on every cold isolate because the CrmAgent Durable Object class is exported from the entry); the fix shape (a thin exported DO class that `await import()`s the agent runtime; lazy MCP client, Think and AI SDK in cron and dispatch) is in open-crm's TODO.md under "Cold open (2026-10-24)" (crm-40..42), not done in launch week. OpenSEO likely has the same shape (SamChatAgent).
- [ ] **CRM's first paint** (the sibling thread): a client-only shell, ~6 chunks, then `getWorkspace` (480 ms cold: `ensureUserRecord` + `ensureEveryAppMembership` write per call) then the dashboard summary, each a gateway hop; and a 400-1200 ms cold start on `/health`.
- [ ] **Measure once deployed**: a Workers Logs query over `evt = proxy` grouped by `app` with p50 of `gatewayMs` and `ms`, before and after, is the table to keep.

### First screen (2026-10-24)

Ben, over the briefing: the empty home should show the four apps as cards that give the install command when clicked, plus a "want to build your own app?" card with the prompt for the person's agent, and stop telling a new user to deploy the starter todo first. Shipped as #583 (gateway only): an owner or admin with no apps meets OpenSEO, OpenCRM, OpenAnalytics and Agentic Inbox as cards and a fifth, Build your own; an app card opens a dialog with `pnpm everyapp install <repository>`, what to have ready and the repository; Build your own opens a dialog with a copyable prompt that sends the agent to /llms.txt, the `build-every-app` skill (/docs/agent/build) and the patterns index (/docs/agent/patterns), has it interview the person one question at a time, then create, build and deploy on their say-so. Members see one quiet empty state. Once apps exist the cards go; Manage workspace → Apps gets an Add app button (and the empty table's action) opening the same chooser. The four repositories, lines and needs live in `apps/every-app-gateway/src/client/lib/featuredApps.ts`; every other string in `firstScreen.ts`; the icons are the apps' own launcher icons. `everyapp create` is unchanged, just not on the home. Decide:

- [ ] **The command the cards show.** `pnpm everyapp install https://github.com/every-app/<repo>`, the README's story, held in `featuredApps.ts`. Until a repository publishes an `everyapp-release.tar.gz` the command answers `NO_RELEASE`, whose fix line names the clone path (`clone it, then run pnpm install && pnpm everyapp deploy in it`). If the four will launch without releases, flip the cards to the clone path (one function in that file) or publish a release per app (the "Releases for the four apps" item above).
- [ ] **Members see no cards.** A member of a workspace with no apps gets "No apps yet / An owner or admin adds apps. Ask them." The cards would show them what the Gateway is for, but with no command there is nothing for them to do; say if the gallery should show for everyone with the command replaced by that line (the dialog already has the member variant).
- [ ] **The CLI still points a new owner at the todo.** `everyapp setup`'s printed next steps (`landing-page/content/docs/gateway.mdx` line 108: `pnpm everyapp create my-app  start your first app`) and the README's "Your first app" predate this screen. Either point them at the Gateway's home ("open your Gateway: it offers four apps and a prompt to build your own") or keep the todo as the CLI's path and the four as the Gateway's. CLI and README lanes.
- [ ] **Card order.** README order (OpenSEO, OpenCRM, OpenAnalytics, OpenMail); Ben's spoken order was Inbox, OpenSEO, OpenCRM, OpenAnalytics. One array.
- [ ] **Browser installs.** When the browser deployer is on (hosted, or a configured self-hosted gateway), the app dialog could offer "Install in browser" → `/install?source=<repository>` beside the command; left out while browser-install authority is Ben's open decision (threat model).
- [ ] **OpenSEO opens to owners and admins only after install** (its manifest sets no `access: "everyone"`; the other three do). The card and dialog do not say so; the Apps table's "Available to" column does. Say if the dialog should carry one line for it, or OpenSEO's port should set `access: "everyone"` like the others.
- [ ] **The four lines are now printed in three places** (the cards, the README, the docs home; the launch-copy thread copied the cards' lines verbatim). `featuredApps.test.ts` holds the cards to the README's repository URLs only; once the README settles, hold the lines too or generate one from the other.

### Events out (2026-10-25)

Ben's mandate for the batch: "assume we want all of this and build the best, most robust thing we're going to want long term, so that when we launch on Monday people can just run with it and they're not waiting on us for PRs." Built as an event bus inside the gateway and signed outbound webhooks, so people wire Slack, Zapier, n8n, Linear or their own agent without us building each integration: the design is [docs/design/2026-10-events-out.md](docs/design/2026-10-events-out.md) (the catalogue, the envelope, delivery, the CLI, the admin MCP tools, the Events tab, the SDK's `emit`, the threat-model rows). Landed: the gateway core #628 (`2083a662`; migration `0012_events.sql`, renumbered from the contract's 0009 after `0011_remote_mcp.sql` took the slot), the SDK's `emit` #643, the CLI's `events` group with its outbox inserts #644, the docs pages for the person and the agent #652, the threat model's B9 with its register rows #647; open at the time of writing: the MCP-app emit points #645, the `everyapp_workspace_events*` twins #649 and the Events tab #651. The defaults it shipped with, each with its walk-back in the design's section 14; yes keeps it:

- [ ] **The retry scheduler is D1 rows plus a per-minute cron trigger**, not a Durable Object or a Queue: the gateway had neither, both are new surface for every self-hosted install two days before launch, and the delivery log has to be in D1 anyway. Retry resolution is one minute; the first attempt is immediate. Walk-back: a per-subscription Durable Object with alarms, same tables.
- [ ] **A webhook's signing secret is stored as written in D1.** The CLI writes subscriptions straight into D1 (ADR-0003) and holds no key to wrap it with. What it protects is a forged webhook to the subscriber's endpoint, not access to the gateway. Walk-back: wrap with a gateway-held key once the gateway is the only writer.
- [ ] **Cap alerts are on by default**: setting an organization's first cap creates a built-in email subscription "Cap alerts" (`cap.warning` at 80%, `cap.reached`) to owners and admins, which the Events tab or `everyapp events disable` turns off (a built-in row is disabled, never deleted, so it is never recreated). Replaced the Caps branch's `usage_cap_notices` path, which #621 dropped before it merged.
- [ ] **Limits**: 30-day retention of events and deliveries, 25 subscriptions per organization, 600 app emits per app per hour, 8 KiB per app event (16 KiB for the gateway's own), seven attempts over about 35 hours (1 min, 5 min, 30 min, 2 h, 8 h, 24 h) then dead, a 10-second timeout, 1 KiB of a failed answer read and 200 characters kept.
- [ ] **Apps declare nothing in the manifest to emit** (`app.<id>.<name>` through `emit()`; subscribers pick `app.crm.*`). Walk-back: an `events` list in `everyapp.config.ts` the UI shows.
- [ ] **`everyapp events` is a top-level group** (like `email`, `mcp`, `access`), not `workspace events`.
- [ ] **Targets are `https:` to a public hostname**; `http:` and private names need the Gateway setting `EVENTS_ALLOW_PRIVATE_TARGETS=true` (self-host only; a private address is not routable from Cloudflare's edge in any case, so the setting buys `http:` and internal names that resolve publicly); the gateway's own and its apps' hosts are never a target.
- [ ] **The envelope carries a top-level `summary`** (one human line per event) beside `type` and `data`, so Slack's Workflow Builder webhook trigger and Zapier post it without parsing.
- [ ] **No `member.signed_in` event**: the chattiest one and nothing people wire today.
- [ ] **The admin MCP tools stack on #614** (`everyapp_workspace_events*`); `everyapp_workspace_events_add` returns a freshly generated secret once, the one credential a workspace tool returns, because it opens nothing on the gateway.
- [ ] **The tools show a webhook's host and path, never its full URL** (a query often carries the receiver's own token, and a Slack or Zapier hook's path is itself a capability the tools still show, as the CLI's `events list` does); the Events tab shows the whole URL to the person. Walk-back: return `url` to owners and admins over MCP too.
- [ ] **`everyapp_workspace_event_deliveries` shows an event's type and summary, never its `data`**, to keep an agent's answer small (a page of 100 events could be 1.6 MB); the Events tab's Show payload has it. Walk-back: a `payload: true` flag on a one-subscription page.
- [ ] **Removing a subscription over MCP is an admin's** (with `confirm: true`), as in Admin → Events, unlike `workspace_remove` and `workspace_app_delete`, which are owner-only. _Superseded 2026-10-25 by Ben's ruling (owners and admins control the Gateway): `workspace_remove` and `workspace_app_delete` are owners' and admins' too; an admin still never removes an owner._

Settled while building, where the code differs from the contract (the code wins; the design and threat model B9 say the same):

- `app.installed` is emitted on the first recorded upload of a pending row (the CLI's `markDeployed`, the gateway's deploy pipeline), not at registration, so a registration whose first deploy fails announces nothing; a later upload with a new version is `app.updated`.
- `token.created` and `token.revoked` carry the token's row flat (`{ id, name, scopes, appId?, expiresAt?, user }`), not under a `token` key: the credential-key rule refuses any key named like one, `token` included, and the gateway's own events keep the rule an app's must.
- A `ping` reaches a paused subscription, so a paused hook can be tested; every other event skips disabled rows.
- `redeliver` resets the schedule (attempts back to 0, due now, then an immediate attempt) for a failed or dead delivery and leaves a delivered one alone.
- The private-name list a target is refused under lives in `@every-app/sdk/internal` (`EVENT_PRIVATE_NAME_SUFFIXES`, `isPrivateEventTargetName`, #644), which the CLI reads; the gateway's `target.ts` still carries its own copy of the same ten names (a follow-up below).
- The envelope's `summary` is computed at delivery from the stored type, data and actor, not stored, so an event the CLI queued straight into D1 reads the same as one the gateway emitted.
- `member.password_reset` is emitted when a reset completes (Better Auth 1.7.7 has `onPasswordReset`); there is no `member.reset_link_created`.
- Event ids are `evt_` and a 26-character uppercase Crockford base32 ULID the gateway makes itself; subscriptions and deliveries are `sub_` and `dlv_` with 24 hex characters.

Follow-ups:

- [ ] A Durable Object per subscription for sub-minute retries and strict ordering, when someone needs it.
- [ ] Pause a subscription automatically after N days of dead letters and tell the owners (today it stays enabled and the tab shows the failures).
- [ ] `EVENTS_RETENTION_DAYS` as a Gateway setting (a constant now).
- [x] The Caps branch rebase: done in #621 (migration `0014_caps_defaults`; its notice mail, repository, table and migration deleted; `cap.raised` from `AppCapService.setMonthlyCap` and `setDefaultMonthlyCap`, the latter with `app: null` and `source: "default"`).
- [ ] Pace deliveries to a receiver's own limit (Slack's Workflow Builder webhook trigger takes about one request a second per workflow): today a refused delivery is retried on the backoff schedule and nothing reads the receiver's `Retry-After`; a per-subscription Durable Object is where a rate would live (threat model B9's gaps).
- [ ] The CLI's target check can't read `THIRD_PARTY_DOMAIN` (a Worker secret; hosted only): a URL under it passes `events add` and is refused by the Gateway at the first attempt, retried, never fetched. Fine for self-host; a deploy-key route or a server function would close it on hosted.
- [ ] Point the gateway's `target.ts` at the SDK's `EVENT_PRIVATE_NAME_SUFFIXES`, so the list exists once (the CLI already reads it).
- [ ] The email sink's mailed markers are best effort and kept three days (`events:mailed:<delivery>:<recipient hash>` in `rate_limit_counters`, `email-sink.ts`): a marker write that fails costs that recipient one duplicate on the retry, and a redeliver of a partial send more than three days later mails every owner and admin again (threat model B9's gaps; pinned by `events.sqlite.test.ts` :: a redeliver more than three days after a partial send mails every recipient again). Keep the marker as long as the delivery (30 days) or on the delivery row itself, and let a failed marker write fail that recipient's attempt.
- [ ] Wrap the signing secret at rest once the Gateway is the only writer of subscriptions (the second default above; threat model B9's first gap and ranked gap 42).

### Admins + audit log (2026-10-25)

Ben, 2026-10-25: "We definitely want owners and admins being able to control the gateway. Maybe there's just an audit log so you can see what actions each person takes." Built as three lanes: the gates #676 (`7369dac7`: deleting a user, an app or an MCP app is an owner's or admin's, not owners only), the audit log #685 (`f304c10e`: Admin → Activity → **Audit** (`?view=audit`), `everyapp workspace audit`, `everyapp_workspace_audit`, over the `events` table, migration `0015_audit_actor`), and the event coverage #690 (every admin, CLI and MCP-tool mutation emits one event with its actor; eighteen new types). The wrap-up is #693 (this section and the last owner-only sentences in the docs; the end-to-end read-back test is in #690). Defaults taken; confirm or change each:

- [ ] **An admin is an owner except for the owner role itself.** An admin invites, removes a member or deletes their account, deletes an app, removes an MCP app, sets caps, manages groups and access, events and sign-in. What stays an owner's is inside `MembershipService`, not a middleware: making or demoting an owner, removing or deactivating an owner or another admin (`OWNER_REQUIRED`), and the last owner never goes (`LAST_OWNER`). Deleting the organization is offered nowhere. `organizationOwnerMiddleware` and `McpAppService.assertOwner` are gone; the UI shows Delete and Remove to admins by `isAdmin`, Make owner and demote by `isOwner`.
- [ ] **Events are kept 90 days, deliveries 30** (`AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS`, `EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS`; both constants, not settings). Walk-back: one number again, or a Gateway setting.
- [ ] **The audit log is a toggle on Activity, not its own tab**; the tool calls (**Agent activity**) stay the default view, `?view=audit` opens the trail. Walk-back: an Audit tab.
- [ ] **`ping` and `tool.*` stay out of the default view** (the tool-calls view lists every call); `--type tool.*` or `*` brings them in. Each row: when, who, the type in plain words, the summary line, the subject; never the event's `data`, a secret, a tool's arguments or a mail body.
- [ ] **A page is 1 to 200 rows, default 50** (`AUDIT_PAGE_MAX`), the activity page's numbers; filters are actor (a member's email or any id), type (exact, `prefix.*`, `*`) and since; paged by `(time, id)` newest first.
- [ ] **`actor_kind` and `actor_id` are columns on `events`, backfilled by migration 0015** from the actor JSON (an `UPDATE … json_extract` appended to the drizzle-generated file); the readers fall back to the JSON for a row an older CLI writes without them. An older CLI keeps working; `workspace audit` on a Gateway from before 0015 is `GATEWAY_OUTDATED`.
- [ ] **A member actor shows by their current name and email** (joined from `users`), a gone one as "Former member" with the id; the JSON's name is the fallback.
- [ ] **The actor is who acted, on every surface**: `user` from Admin, `agent` (`{ id: <credential id>, name: <client name>, email: <the member it acts for> }`) through the Gateway's MCP tools (`OrgContext.actor`, `actorFor(ctx)`), `operator` (the Cloudflare login) from the CLI, `app` for an app's own event, `gateway` for cron and internal work. Consequence: filtering the trail by a member's email finds what they did in Admin, not what their AI client did for them (that is under the credential's id; the email is in the row). Walk-back: resolve a member's email to their credentials too, or store the member as a second column.
- [ ] **The CLI prints the type's label and the subject, not the summary line**: the catalogue's `summarize` lives in the gateway, and the CLI reads D1 directly. Walk-back: move `summarize` into the SDK.
- [ ] **Eighteen event types added** (three new groups `organization`, `group`, `subscription`; labels derive from `EVENT_TYPES`, so the Audit view and the CLI name them by themselves; the shapes are in [/docs/agent/events](landing-page/content/agent/events.md)): `member.invitation_cancelled` `{ invitation: { id, email, role } }`, never the link; `organization.renamed` `{ name, previousName }`; `app.renamed` `{ app, previousName?, description? }`; `app.settings_changed` `{ app | null, settings: string[], action: set|unset, target: gateway|app }`, names only, `app` null for a Gateway setting set outside any app; `app.access_changed` `{ app, granted: user[], revoked: user[], grantedCount, revokedCount, everyone? }`, the first 50 each way, counts exact; `app.email_changed` `{ app, senders: string[], inbound: string[] }`; `cap.changed` with `cap.raised`'s fields; `group.created|updated|removed` `{ group: { id, name }, previousName?, description? }`; `group.members_changed` `{ group, added, removed, addedCount, removedCount }`; `group.apps_changed` `{ group, granted, revoked, grantedCount, revokedCount }`; `mcp_app.updated` `{ app, kind, changed: (risk|name|description|tools)[], risk? }`; `mcp_app.tool_changed` `{ app, kind, tool, risk?, enabled?, cleared? }`; `subscription.created` `{ subscription: { id, name, sink }, events, host?, recipients? }`, the host only, never the secret or the URL; `subscription.updated` `{ subscription, changed: (enabled|events|name|description)[], enabled?, events? }`; `subscription.removed` `{ subscription, disabled }`; `subscription.redelivered` `{ subscription, delivery: { id, eventId, eventType } }`. `provider.updated` was dropped (no update path exists). The builders' credential-key unit test covers them.
- [ ] **A catch-all `*` subscription does not receive `subscription.*` or `ping`**, so a new hook's first delivery is the event it was made for; name `subscription.*` to get the bus's own housekeeping (`eventPatternMatches`; `events list --types` says so).
- [ ] **`cap.changed` beside `cap.raised`**: a raise or a removal is still `cap.raised` (the Cap alerts mail); a cap set under the default, lowered, set at the default's value, or `unlimited` taken back under no default is `cap.changed` (the binding cap may stay; the row changed).
- [ ] **`access on|off` queues `access.changed` alone** (`announce: false` on its settings write); every other `secret set|unset`, `configure` and `signin add|remove` write also queues `app.settings_changed` with the names, so a rotation is in the trail.
- [ ] **The CLI's `member.removed` carries `accountDeleted`** from the policy (`deleteAccounts && otherOrganizations === 0`), queued in the removal's own batch; `connection.revoked` from `workspace connections revoke` says reason `operator` (the row's `revoked_reason` stays `user`, the Gateway's enum); `token.revoked` from `workspace tokens revoke` names the operator, the owner under `user`.
- [ ] **The names**: UI Admin → Activity → Audit; CLI `everyapp workspace audit [--actor] [--type] [--since] [--limit] [--before]`; MCP `everyapp_workspace_audit` (owners and admins; a read tool); the parity row is in docs/design/2026-10-agent-parity.md.
- [ ] **The pinned deploy snapshots moved** (`deployApp.pinned.test.ts`): the release tarball bundles the migrations, so any migration PR moves that sha.

Follow-ups:

- [ ] A summary line in the CLI's `workspace audit` (needs `summarize` in the SDK).
- [ ] CSV export of the trail, and a per-row detail dialog in the Audit view.
- [ ] Admin has no "revoke someone's token or connection" action, so no admin-actor event there; only the CLI's `workspace tokens|connections revoke` have one.
- [ ] `everyapp update` (the Gateway redeploy), `workspace invite-link` and `reset-link` emit nothing (not workspace mutations; `member.password_reset` fires when a reset completes).
- [ ] Filtering the trail by a member finds their Admin actions, not their agents' (the default above).
- [ ] `pnpm knip` fails on main (`packages/cli/bin/everyapp.js` unused file), not from these lanes.
- [ ] Remove the lanes' local worktrees under `/tmp/ea-audit` once the swath is done.

### Sign-in providers (2026-10-25)

Ben's mandate for the swath: "build the best thing we'll want long term, fully done, merged on green." Shipped: "Continue with Google, Microsoft, GitHub, Apple, Cloudflare or SSO" beside passwords, on Better Auth's providers under the invitation flow's rules, with the settings as Worker secrets in the `<ID>_SIGNIN_<FIELD>` family. The gateway core and threat model B10 #666 (`b42b1c3d`), the CLI's `everyapp signin add|list|show|remove`, the six setup guides and the Apple bypass and the `signin_provider.added`/`.removed` events #674 (`889a371f`), Manage workspace → Sign-in, the sign-in page's buttons, the account menu's Connect and the read-only MCP twins #677 (`81b4b313`), the docs (`/docs/sign-in`, `/docs/agent/signin`, the Access runbook's pointer, this section) #671 (`9d4cbbe1`); the parity rows unmarked #683 (`fc008714`); an off provider is missing nothing and the oidc entry sends Entra ID to the microsoft provider #678. The operator's runbook is `docs/runbooks/sign-in-providers.md`. The defaults it shipped with, each with its walk-back; yes keeps it:

- [ ] **No auto-join by email domain for sign-in providers.** A provider signs in members and invited addresses only (Access keeps its own `CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_AUTO_JOIN_DOMAINS`). Recommended no for launch: a Google account at a domain is easy to come by, and an invitation is one command. Walk-back: a `SIGNIN_AUTO_JOIN_DOMAINS` setting, applied in `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/sign-in-providers/membership.ts` as Access's is in its own `membership.ts`.
- [ ] **`signin add` and `signin remove` have no MCP twin**; `everyapp_signin_list` and `everyapp_signin_show` are read-only. The settings are Worker secrets, which only the Cloudflare login writes, so a stolen agent token can't add a way in (threat model gap 45). Walk-back: a Gateway-held Cloudflare API token that can write the Gateway Worker's secrets, and write twins for owners.
- [ ] **Cloudflare's address is trusted on the strength of a private OAuth client.** Cloudflare's `/user` reports no verified flag; a private client is authorized only by the account's members, who joined by an invitation to that address. A public client would let any Cloudflare user present any address, and the Gateway can't tell the two apart; the setup guide says to keep it private. Walk-back: drop the `cloudflare` entry from the catalog, or require Access's "Sign in with Cloudflare" identity provider instead.
- [ ] **Apple shipped though untested live**, against mocked endpoints only, with a client secret JWT that expires within six months and has to be generated again by hand. Walk-back: remove `apple` from the catalog until a live run.
- [ ] **Microsoft defaults to `common`**, any Microsoft account, with `xms_edov` (or `email_verified`, a matching `verified_primary_email`, a personal account) required for the address to count, rather than refusing to start without a tenant. Without the optional claims every work or school sign-in is refused as unverified, which is safe and confusing. Walk-back: require `MICROSOFT_SIGNIN_TENANT`.
- [ ] **Providers register in both deployment modes**, and on the hosted Gateway's open sign-up a provider sign-in with a verified email creates an account and a personal organization, as the sign-up form does. Walk-back: self-host only.
- [ ] **One generic OIDC entry** (`oidc`), not several: one company identity provider per Gateway. Walk-back: `OIDC2_…` entries (a second family member per issuer).
- [ ] **A provider sign-in verifies the account's email** on its first use after a signed-in Connect (the provider vouched for the same address the password account claimed), so later links and Access treat the account as verified.

Settled while building:

- Accounts are keyed by the provider's stable subject (`sub`, GitHub's id, Entra's `oid`, Cloudflare's user id), not the email, unlike Access, which keys by the verified email because Access changes `sub` when someone is removed and added back.
- RS256 only for the generic OIDC issuer's ID tokens (every major issuer's default), through the Access verifier's fail-closed JWKS cache.
- The OIDC discovery document is fetched when a sign-in starts (cached an hour, 5-second timeout), never at startup, so an issuer that is down costs one sign-in (`SIGNIN_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`), not the Gateway.
- `member.joined` gained `via: "sign_in"` (the event catalogue, `events.md`, the events design).
- The CLI queues `signin_provider.added` and `signin_provider.removed` (`{ provider, label }`) when a provider goes on or off, not on every rerun, so a rotated secret announces nothing.

Follow-ups:

- [ ] A live run with a real Google OAuth app, once one exists in the loop's account notes (`docs/runbooks/onboarding-loop-test-accounts.md`); then Microsoft, GitHub and Cloudflare (threat model gap 43).
- [ ] Apple live: a Services ID, a key and a generated secret on a test team; then confirm or walk back the Apple default above.
- [ ] A `status` line for sign-in providers (which are on, which need setup), from the Worker's secret names as the Access line is.
- [ ] `everyapp signin test <id>`: a dry run that fetches the OIDC discovery document, checks the redirect URL against the Gateway's and, where the provider allows it, the client credentials.
- [ ] The providers' brand marks on the sign-in page's buttons (#677 ships neutral icons: lucide has no Google, Microsoft or Apple mark, and the repo draws none).

### Agent-forward layer (2026-10-24)

Ben's three asks of 2026-10-24 (how people learn what a Gateway offers, apps against MCP apps and the compact MCP surface, and whether it should suggest what to do next from what is installed and used; a skill for building an app on the example apps' patterns, with a starter first screen for a person who does not read code; which MCP servers to recommend), built as lanes from one thread and merged on green: #585 (f3b1372f, the MCP servers worth recommending: the setup skill's section and connect-ai, nothing installed by `setup` or `create`), #590 (9ac3f421, the `build-every-app` skill and its patterns index in `packages/cli/skills/build-every-app/`, `everyapp create` copies every skill the CLI ships, a claude -p harness in `packages/cli/test/build-skill` on runner pieces shared with the setup-skills harness in `packages/cli/test/shared`), #633 (77b89fdd, `everyapp create <name or path>`: `pnpm everyapp create ../my-app` makes an app beside the checkout as its own repository, the documented default; #636 900c42f4 took the gateway's Build-your-own prompt there, #637 1a3e5b07 the README and the home page), #588 (b905c114, [docs/design/2026-10-proactive-next-steps.md](docs/design/2026-10-proactive-next-steps.md) and `everyapp status` `Next:` and `data.next`), #620 (b61f6210, the setup skill's step 11 installs the four apps from their releases unless the person skips one), #584 (4c6f0d7e, the starter's first screen). #613 (d10510ac, the agent pages `build` and `patterns` generated from the skill by `pnpm -C packages/cli docs:update`, `gateway` ("Everything a Gateway offers": apps, MCP apps and the compact surface, people, Access, the CLI by job, what to suggest next, which MCP servers to recommend), the one rule in `index.md` for what stays human-only under `landing-page/content/docs` and what is agent-only under `landing-page/content/agent`, llms.txt's header naming the two skills, and drift tests that hold `gateway.md` to every CLI route, built-in tool and MCP app, the listing modes and their header, both manifest `access` values, every Admin tab and every MCP server address the setup skill names, and the gateway's Build-your-own prompt to agent pages that exist). Held for Ben: #619. The `status` slice's own follow-ups and Ben's calls on the rest of that design are under "Proactive next steps (2026-10-25)"; the rest is here.

- [ ] **Setup and create do not offer to install MCP servers; the setup skill and connect-ai name them.** Decided for the launch: `pnpm everyapp setup` and `pnpm everyapp create` print nothing about other MCP servers and write no client config. The two worth adding (the Gateway's own connector for every member; Cloudflare's `https://mcp.cloudflare.com/mcp` for the owner, to read the account the Gateway runs in, never to deploy) and the one that needs nothing (GitHub: `gh` in a shell, the built-in connector in claude.ai; `pnpm everyapp install` is anonymous) are named in `packages/cli/skills/everyapp/setup/gateway.md` ("Optional: MCP servers worth having beside Every App"), on [landing-page/content/docs/connect-ai.mdx](landing-page/content/docs/connect-ai.mdx) ("Also worth connecting") and on the agent page `gateway` ("Other MCP servers to recommend"), with the rule that an agent offers one in a line, never adds it unasked, and never to a config the repository shares. Ben: confirm that setup stays silent, or name the one line it should print after a first install.
- [ ] **Later, if Ben wants it: `everyapp mcp recommend`, or a closing line of `setup`.** Prints the Cloudflare server's add command for the person's client with the read-before-write warning, once the owner link is claimed; never writes a client config. Not for the launch.
- [x] **Main's Test job was red on #613's own drift tests, from d10510ac to 907b4307.** #640 (bd10d9e5) added the eight `everyapp_mcp_*` workspace tools (list, show, add, remove, on, off, refresh, tool) to the Gateway connector's built-ins before #613 landed, and #644 (bb644c05) the `events` routes right after it, neither on `gateway.md`, so `agentDocs.test.ts` "names every built-in tool and every provider app" and "names every CLI route" failed on main and on every PR in between; #642 (907b4307) named them all and main is green again. The nag works: a sibling that adds a CLI route or a built-in edits `gateway.md` in the same PR.
- [ ] **#619, the `/mcp` head line (held).** One line in the Gateway's MCP instructions, to the install organization's owners and admins only, when an MCP app is off while its key is set (`dataforseo (DataForSEO) is off and its key is set: an admin runs pnpm everyapp mcp on dataforseo`), and `offered: true` on that app's `everyapp_list_apps` entry; members and other organizations see neither. Every check green, no review, and now `CONFLICTING` with main: whoever merges rebases first. Ben: should an AI client's instructions carry any suggestion, even one about state? Merge #619, or close it and `status` alone is the slice (the first item under "Proactive next steps (2026-10-25)" is the same question; one answer). What would read usage (rule 6, a usage-ranked app order), the members' nudge, the home card and the digest email are his calls, listed once under "Proactive next steps (2026-10-25)" (the design's sections 7 and 11), not here.

Same item as "Apps shipping skills" under "Ben's briefing answers"; when one of the four apps ships a skill, the patterns index gets a row.

Still the bare form: setup's done screen and `setup --help` (`nextAfterInstall` in `packages/cli/src/commands/setup/index.ts` prints `create my-app`, mirrored in the agent pages `setup.md`, `json.md` and `cli.md` and in the setup skill's `gateway.md`), the setup prompt's step 3 (`create hello --json`, then from `apps/hello`; the same text in `README.md`, `landing-page/content/docs/setup-prompt.txt` and `packages/onboarding-loop/prompts/setup.md`; the Launch copy thread's), and `landing-page/content/docs/troubleshooting.mdx` under "No everyapp.config.ts" (`cd apps/my-app`). "The CLI still points a new owner at the todo" under "First screen (2026-10-24)" is the same seam. Alternative: keep the bare form for the first app (the simplest first step, nothing to say about `../`) and `../` for every app after it.

Next: a capability-and-secret scenario (an app that lists an MCP app and a secret), a public-route scenario (a webhook), one opus run per scenario (`--model opus`; none yet), and a Codex run through the same harness once `codex exec` is wired (`packages/onboarding-loop/src/harnesses/codex.ts` has the pattern). Not in CI: it spends tokens, about $0.15 a run.

Trim by pointing the job table at `cli.md`, or accept it: an agent fetches it once.
- [ ] **Two things the drift tests cannot see.** The four app-citation tests in `packages/cli/src/lib/app/buildSkill.test.ts` run only where the app checkouts exist (`EVERYAPP_APP_CHECKOUTS`; `it.skipIf` with the reason elsewhere, CI included), so a file renamed in OpenCRM, OpenSEO, OpenAnalytics or Agentic Inbox leaves `patterns.md` stale until someone runs them locally; and `build.md` and `patterns.md` are only as current as the last `pnpm -C packages/cli docs:update` (the test fails CI when they drift, the intended nag). Alternative: a weekly job that runs the four citation tests against fresh clones of the public repositories once they exist.
