OpenSEO, OpenCRM and Agentic Inbox stop once on `R2_NOT_ENABLED` (OpenAnalytics binds no bucket); the step handles both codes inline, so the "If it fails" table does not list them. Add the two rows if an agent is seen landing in the table first.

### Agent parity (2026-10-24)

Ben: "You should totally be able to manage the workspace in the CLI. The CLI should include everything you can do in the UI, and more. The UI should be simpler than the CLI, because we want agents to be able to do basically everything." The matrix and the decisions are in [docs/design/2026-10-agent-parity.md](docs/design/2026-10-agent-parity.md); `packages/cli/src/docs/parity.test.ts` fails CI when a server function of the UI gets no CLI or MCP twin and no reason. Built as `everyapp workspace …` (the UI review thread's `rename` and `role` included) and the `everyapp_workspace_*` tools on `/mcp`; the agent page is [landing-page/content/agent/workspace.md](landing-page/content/agent/workspace.md).

- [ ] **The CLI group is `workspace`**, matching the sidebar's "Manage workspace" and Ben's words; `admin` was the alternative and is also a role name. Renaming is one route map and one page.
- [ ] **The CLI writes D1 under the Cloudflare login** (ADR-0003), not through the Gateway: personal tokens are HMAC-hashed with `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, a Worker secret the CLI never holds, so it cannot mint itself a credential, and the self-host model says the login is root and the Gateway may be down. The cost: each action exists twice (the service for the UI and MCP, SQL for the CLI), tested against the real migrations; nothing the CLI does shows in Admin → Activity. A "workspace changes" log that both write is the follow-up if agents' CLI actions need to be visible there.
- [ ] **No MCP tool returns a credential, and none creates a personal access token.** `everyapp_workspace_invite` says whether the Gateway mailed the invitation and never returns the link; the password-reset link and token creation stay in the UI and the CLI. The reasoning: a revocable connection should not mint itself something that outlives it, and an AI client acting for an admin should not be handed another person's way in. Flipping any of these is a small change per tool.
- [ ] **`remove` and `app_delete` are owner-only writes that take `confirm: true`**, not `destructive` tools (those stay hidden until approvals exist). Decide whether an approvals flow should gate them instead. _The owner-only part is superseded 2026-10-25 by Ben's ruling: they are owners' and admins' writes; the approvals question stands._
- [ ] **Workspace tools are listed in the full listing only**; in the compact listing a client finds them with `everyapp_search_tools` and runs them through the wrappers, like app tools, so an admin's compact connection does not grow by thirty definitions. Listing them in both modes is one flag per tool.
- [ ] **`workspace invite` needs the operator to be a member** (an invitation records its inviter). An API-token operator with no account is told to claim the Gateway or get invited. The alternative, attributing the invitation to the first owner, misattributes.
- [ ] **Org switching has no MCP twin** (a connection is made in one organization); `--org` is the CLI's. Cloudflare Access, secrets, email senders and provider apps are Gateway settings or registry changes: CLI only, never writable from inside the Gateway, by design.
- [ ] **Follow-ups.** `onboarding.getOnboardingStatus` and `client/tanstack-db/onboardingCollection.ts` are dead since the four-app home (#583): nothing reads them; delete both and their matrix row. A `workspace list` of the operator's organizations (today `workspace show` per `--org`). `workspace token create` once a credential the CLI can mint exists (a setup-link-style sha256 row the Gateway resolves, or `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` readable by the operator); the Caps thread's default cap got `workspace cap default`, `workspace app cap --unlimited` and `everyapp_workspace_cap_default` (#659); the MCP apps thread's modal (provider on/off from the UI) gets `everyapp_workspace_provider` if it lands.

### OAuth providers (2026-10-25)

Ben, the night of 2026-10-25: "Could you please actually support OAuth providers? They'll need to just upload a client ID and a client secret, and then we'll need logic to handle OAuth flows. Implement that. GitHub in particular is something people may want. I want people to be able to add whatever they want to the gateway." Then: prove the general pattern with Google first (OpenSEO on the port checkout), GitHub second as the upload-only proof. Design [docs/design/2026-10-oauth-providers.md](docs/design/2026-10-oauth-providers.md) (#672); the agent page [providers](landing-page/content/agent/providers.md) ("OAuth providers"); the person's page [Connect GitHub](landing-page/content/docs/connect-oauth.mdx). PRs: #672 design (merged), #687 the SDK's `oauth2-user` kind, presets, `flow`, held-token calls (merged, `d23e266f`), #680 sealed tokens, the `oauth_clients` / `provider_connections` / `oauth_states` tables (migration 0016), provider events, the fake provider (merged, `64729c22`), #696 the `/connect` flow with PKCE and state, sealed connections, refresh and revoke, the held-token proxy branch (merged, `e87b3f1f`), #694 Admin → Providers and Connected accounts, #697 `provider add --oauth | presets | connections | disconnect` and the MCP twins, #698 the Google and GitHub proofs ([docs/design/2026-10-oauth-providers-proof.md](docs/design/2026-10-oauth-providers-proof.md): the fake-provider harness, the local walk to GitHub's and Google's consent screens, and open-seo-everyapp #16, OpenSEO's Google through the Gateway), and these docs. Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **A client pasted in the Gateway lives in D1, the secret sealed** (AES-256-GCM, a key derived by HKDF from `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, no new setting), and a Worker secret of the same name set from a terminal wins. The CLI never writes the sealed copy (the key is the Worker's). Alternatives: Worker secrets only (the dialog would need a Cloudflare token that can write the Gateway's secrets); a dedicated `OAUTH_SEALING_KEY` install-owned setting (rotation without touching sessions).
- [ ] **A client is gateway-wide; connections are per organization and per person** (`account: user`), plus one organization account for background work (`account: organization`, connected by an owner or admin). Alternative: per-organization clients on a hosted Gateway (parked with hosted multi-tenancy).
- [ ] **The callback is `/connect/<id>/callback` on the Gateway host**, one per provider, beside the Gateway's own OAuth server under `/oauth/*`. Alternative: one callback for every provider with the provider in the state.
- [ ] **Google is never revoked at Google on disconnect** (`oauth.google-never-revoked`): its revocation is per person per client and would also kill an app-driven app's tokens under the shared client; the Gateway's copy dies at expiry. GitHub, GitLab, Dropbox and Zoom revoke. Alternative: revoke once no app-driven app lists `google-workspace`.
- [ ] **Presets become rows when added** (editable, visible in `provider show`); the catalog's own OAuth entries (`google-workspace`, `microsoft-365`, `slack`, `discord`) stay entries and take a client only. Preset ids carry `-oauth` where the catalog already holds an API-key entry of the name (`github-oauth`, `notion-oauth`, `linear-oauth`, `gitlab-oauth`, `hubspot-oauth`; `x-oauth` because bare `x` is too short for the id rule), and their settings follow (`GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`). Alternative: one id per provider with the kind chosen at add time.
- [ ] **Adding, replacing a client, removing a provider, listing every connection and disconnecting others: owners and admins**; connecting one's own account: any member; the organization's account: owners and admins. The Admins swath's widening rule (owner-or-admin) applied to every new gate.
- [ ] **A token answer with no `scope` is taken as granted-as-requested** for the `space` and `comma` dialects (RFC 6749 §5.1); `google`, `microsoft` and `slack` keep their parsers and the scopes design's `integration_scope_unverified`. Alternative: refuse every scopeless answer.
- [ ] **The CLI's `provider disconnect` deletes without revoking** (it holds no token) and says so; the Gateway's own disconnect revokes where the spec has a `revokeUrl`. Alternative: a privileged Gateway route the CLI calls.
- [ ] **`pkce: false` on the Slack `flow` and the Notion, Linear, HubSpot and Atlassian presets** (providers that refuse or ignore `code_challenge`); every other flow carries S256. The spec field exists so a custom provider can say either.
- [ ] **`access: "read"` is enough to list an entry with a `flow`** (held-token calls); the app-driven token-endpoint call still needs `access: "write"`, as before.
- [ ] **Local development has no flow**: `integrationFetch` puts the developer's own token from `.dev.vars` on the call (`<PREFIX>ACCESS_TOKEN`: `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_OAUTH_ACCESS_TOKEN`; `devAccessTokenName()`), and `integrationStatus` reports a connection while it is set. No dev-only name rule covers them (a pattern would collide with the catalog's `MAPBOX_ACCESS_TOKEN`). Alternative: the dev twin runs the flow against the real provider with a localhost redirect.
- [ ] **The `EVERYAPP_RELEASE` deploy pins hash the SDK bundle**, so every PR that touches SDK source refreshes the five `deployApp.pinned.test.ts` snapshots (#680 did twice). Worth a pin on the SDK's public surface instead of its bytes; the provisioners lane hits it too.
- [ ] **Migration 0016** (the audit log took 0015 while #680 was open), regenerated against the merged schema; the design said 0015.

Follow-ups:

- [ ] Ben's real client ids: the local proof runs the whole flow against a fake provider and walks the browser to Google's and GitHub's consent screens with the right redirect URI and scopes; the real token exchange needs a Google client with `https://<gateway>/connect/google-workspace/callback` registered and a GitHub OAuth App, then one connect each on everyapp.team.
- [ ] OpenSEO's own-client Google path (its `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` app secret, its own callbacks) retires once the Gateway-owned path is live for it; the port's `googleAccess.ts` keeps both until then.
- [ ] Per-organization clients on a hosted Gateway (today one client per Gateway, every organization's connections on it).
- [ ] Upstream MCP OAuth ("An OAuth client for upstream MCP servers" under MCP apps by URL) now has its token store: `provider_connections` and `sealed.ts`; the remaining work is discovery, DCR and the callback.
- [ ] Rotation of the sealing key: a `v2` prefix and a re-seal pass when `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` changes (today a rotated secret reads every pasted client and connection as lost, and people connect again).
- [ ] The social networks' entries get a `flow` (LinkedIn, Facebook, …) once Postiz or another app wants the Gateway-owned path; each needs its dialect and identity endpoint checked live.
- [ ] `oauth2-client-credentials` (a machine client, no person) stays the Provider registry section's own follow-up.

### Provider registry (2026-10-25)

Ben, the evening of 2026-10-24: "Right now we hard-code support for each provider in the gateway. Change it to a registry pattern instead of hard-coded: here's what the secret needs to be, here's the API docs, here's the endpoint, here's how you authenticate to it. Make it general so people can add whatever they want to the gateway super easily. Then we can support hundreds and hundreds of API providers." The design is [docs/design/2026-10-provider-registry.md](docs/design/2026-10-provider-registry.md), reviewed by a four-lens Workflow with refuters before it opened (40 of 42 findings folded in): one declarative `ProviderSpec` (id, name, docs, hosts or a host setting, `auth` of kind bearer, header, query or basic with its placement said outright, secret names with hints, access and methods, account-wide paths, whose data the key reaches, a hint sentence, a free smoke request, cost and rate hints, an example sentence), a built-in catalog in the SDK plus per-gateway rows in a D1 table `providers` (migration 0010), `everyapp provider add|list|show|remove`, later the MCP tools `everyapp_provider_add|list|remove`, and the existing providers (DataForSEO, Context.dev, OpenAI, OpenRouter, Workers AI, the OAuth clients) as catalog entries with no behaviour change, pinned byte for byte. The pull requests: this note (PR 1), the registry core (PR 2a), rows and the commands (PR 2b), the MCP tools (PR 2c), fifty catalog entries (PR 3). Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **The name.** "API providers" wherever a person reads it (the dialog, `provider list`, the docs); the manifest key stays `capabilities` and the CLI group is `everyapp provider`. "MCP apps" stays for the tier-`provider` apps (`everyapp mcp on`), which are built on an API provider's entry. Alternatives: "integrations" (the research era's word), "connections" (collides with AI connections).
- [ ] **`everyapp provider` is the registry's group; the `mcp` alias is withdrawn for `list`.** `provider on|off` stay as hidden routes that run `mcp on|off` and say so through launch week; `provider list` lists API providers and ends with one line pointing at `mcp list`. PR 2b rewrites the places that promised the whole alias: landing-page/content/agent/mcp.md, docs/design/2026-10-mcp-apps-ui.md section 5, packages/cli/skills/everyapp/SKILL.md, docs/capabilities.md, packages/sdk/src/internal/appSettings.ts, and the `everyapp provider on` citations in docs/threat-model.md, docs/threat-model-invariants.md and docs/security-model.md become `mcp on`.
- [ ] **A row is gateway-wide, not per organization**, as every Gateway setting is: one key per provider, shared by every app that lists it. A provider whose `data` is `account` (Stripe, Airtable, Linear: every list is the whole account) is refused on a hosted gateway (`integration_not_available_hosted`) until per-organization keys exist; self-hosted gateways are one organization and are untouched.
- [ ] **`provider add` from MCP: owners and admins on self-host, nobody on hosted**, the people who grant apps and set caps; the CLI is the operator. The MCP tools (PR 2c, built) take the spec object only (never a URL), never replace a row, and refuse to remove a row whose key is bound (`PROVIDER_BOUND`) or that a deployed app lists (`PROVIDER_IN_USE`, no force through MCP). `everyapp_provider_list` is every member's, since an agent writing a manifest needs the ids and hints; whether a setting is set, and a row's binding, show to owners and admins only, and the apps listing a provider are the caller's organization's alone. Alternatives: `add` owners only; the list owners and admins only. Ben's call.
- [ ] **Rows never shadow the catalog, and a row's value is bound to its hosts.** A row's setting names start with its own id (`STRIPE_`) and are nobody else's (never an install-owned, dev-only, reserved, settable, catalog, other row's, or registered app's own secret name), checked at `add` and again by the gateway on every read; and `everyapp secret set` records a digest of the row's hosts and auth, which the gateway requires to match before it reads the value, so a replaced or re-added row gets nothing until the operator sets the key again (`providers.value-bound-to-hosts`, the review's blocker). Alternative declined: letting whoever edits rows move a key the operator set.
- [ ] **`provider remove` refuses while an active app lists the provider** (naming the apps) unless `--force`; the Gateway setting stays until `secret unset`, and no row can read it again without a new `secret set`.
- [ ] **The catalog ships in the SDK**: every gateway offers the same fifty. Alternative: a community list fetched by URL (`provider add <url>` already takes one, and the same parser and path serve both), with only the six today's apps use built in. Ben's call.
- [ ] **Inference APIs are `ai` entries, never capabilities** (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google AI, Mistral, Groq, Cohere: their hosts also serve files, batches and fine-tunes, which the AI proxy's inference allowlist keeps closed); the capability proxy gets a per-call API whose reads are POSTs (search, scrape, speech) with its POST write paths under `accountWidePaths`, and a sender or a GraphQL API as write-only.
- [ ] **`access: "write"` unlocks the spec's write methods** for an entry that asks for it on a provider that offers write; an agent identity needs `mcp:write` (`integration_read_only`, email's rule). Decided in PR 2a rather than left open: without it senders and GraphQL APIs do nothing.
- [ ] **`basic` says where the key goes** (`user: "<literal>"`, `user: { secret }`, `user: "key"` for a key-as-username provider, or `pair: true` for DataForSEO's login:password-or-base64 rule) instead of guessing from the value, so a bare key never goes out as if it were base64.
- [ ] **`query` auth ships with its redirect rule and a body scrub** (the scrub since PR 3, from its security review: Mapbox's TileJSON writes the token into `tiles` URLs, so a query provider's text answer is read whole, 4 MiB at most, and every copy of the value replaced; binary answers stream through): the app's own copy of the parameter is dropped, a 3xx answer is never relayed (`integration_upstream_redirected`), `location`-type headers are deleted, failures log the error's name only; the kind is for providers verified not to echo the request URL in error bodies. Alternative: drop `query` and use `header` where the provider accepts one.
- [ ] **The cost tap reads the body only where a spec names `cost.responseField`** (DataForSEO's `cost`): the same 2 KiB head regex as today, no longer run for every provider. The caps thread reads `cost.usd` and `rate` as hints; a per-provider dollar cap is Ben's call.
- [ ] **`format` (a value regex per secret) was dropped**: a row-supplied regular expression the CLI would run on a typed value. `hint` alone reaches the dialog, `status`, `secret list` and `everyapp configure` (agreed with the CLI app-setup thread).
- [ ] **Local development reads `.everyapp/providers/*.json`** into `EVERYAPP_DEV_PROVIDERS` for a registered row (`provider show <id> --save`, or `provider add` run in an app directory); the catalog needs no file.
- [ ] **`everyapp dev` warns about a capability id outside the catalog (`CAPABILITY_NOT_IN_CATALOG`); `deploy` and every connected command validate against the gateway's registry and refuse one it doesn't have** (`CAPABILITY_UNKNOWN`, naming `provider add`).
- [ ] **A smoke check command** (`everyapp provider check <id>`: the spec's free request with the Gateway's key, through the proxy) is not built: the CLI never holds a value, so the check would have to run on the Gateway and be a privileged route. The catalog page carries the request for the person's terminal instead.
- [ ] **One generated catalog page instead of fifty setup stubs**: `setup/providers.md` (regenerated by `pnpm -C packages/cli docs:update`, a drift test holds it) is what a catalog entry without a hand-written guide points at; a row has `Guide: pnpm everyapp provider show <id>`. Alternative: a generated `setup/<id>.md` per provider (fifty files copied into every app's skill, each needing the nine headings the skill contract requires).
- [ ] **`provider add --replace` keeps `added_by` and `added_at`** (the first registrant's); the row's `updated_at` moves. `secret set` says "registered by <first person>" even after someone else replaced the hosts. Alternative: replace rewrites `added_by`, or the line shows when the row last changed.
- [ ] **`secret unset NAME` reaches the Gateway for a name no row holds but the Worker does** (and that isn't one of the Gateway's own names), so the "`secret unset NAME` removes it" line after `provider remove` stays true. Only names a row could hold (provider-name shape, not reserved, not a near-miss of a catalog name) make `secret` read the rows before routing; every other refusal still happens before connecting.
- [ ] **`Guide: pnpm everyapp provider show <id>`** is shown for every provider without a skill guide, the catalog's OAuth clients included (LinkedIn and the others have none). Alternative: point those at `setup/providers.md#<id>`.
- [ ] **The catalog's fifty (PR 3)**, each written from the provider's own docs on 2026-10-25 and checked by a second agent; the calls the checkers raised:
  - **Resend is left out.** Its natural setting `RESEND_API_KEY` is already the Gateway's own email transport key (`GATEWAY_OWN_SETTINGS`), so a `resend` provider would either share the transport key (an app with `capabilities: { resend: { access: "write" } }` could then send outside the email service's sender rule and daily budgets) or need another name (`RESEND_PROVIDER_API_KEY`). Ben's call; the entry the research wrote is `docs/design/2026-10-provider-registry-resend.json`, ready for `catalog/comms.ts` or `everyapp provider add` once its setting has a name of its own.
  - **`cloudflare` reads `CLOUDFLARE_PROVIDER_API_TOKEN`**, not `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, which is the variable the CLI reads for its own deploy login (`packages/cli/src/lib/cf/account.ts`): with the shared name, `--from-env CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` would hand apps the deploy token, which can edit Workers, D1 and KV on the Gateway's account. The hint says a scoped token, never a Global API Key. Alternative: the shorter name, accepting the footgun.
  - **`shopify` ships although Shopify no longer lets a store create a legacy admin custom app**, and Dev Dashboard client-credential tokens expire daily, which the Gateway can't refresh; the hint says so. Alternative: drop it until an OAuth-client adapter exists.
  - **`anthropic` uses `Authorization: Bearer`** (the documented header today; `x-api-key` is the legacy fallback). The AI proxy strips `x-api-key` and, since the security scan PR (#713, 2026-10-25), `anthropic-workspace-id`, so the key alone decides the workspace.
  - **`huggingface`: `X-HF-Bill-To` is stripped** since #713 (2026-10-25), so a call bills the token's own account; GET on the router's provider-prefixed paths (`/replicate/…`) reaches what HF routes there, undocumented.
  - **`gemini`, not `google-ai`**: the id and `GEMINI_API_KEY` follow Google's own naming; the design note said "Google AI". The OpenAI-compatible paths under `/v1beta/openai` are the entry's routes.
  - **`plaid` and `serper` are `verified: false`**: Plaid wants a client id and a secret on every call (body or two headers) and this kind sends one header, so the app adds `PLAID-CLIENT-ID` itself; Serper publishes no API reference (its request shape is read from the playground).
  - **`assemblyai` is `data: "account"`** though speech is per-call: `GET /v2/transcript` lists every transcript on the account and shares its segment with `/v2/transcript/<id>`, so no path rule separates them.
  - **`twilio` takes an API key (SID as the Basic username, its secret as the password), never the Auth Token**: with the Auth Token a write grant could close the account (`POST /2010-04-01/Accounts/{Sid}.json` with `Status=closed`).
  - **Echo endpoints**: Jina's `r.jina.ai/echo` reflects the request, key included, and is refused as an account-wide path; no general guard stops an upstream echo endpoint from handing the key back. A sweep of the other forty-nine for one is a follow-up, as is a proxy rule that strips the request's own `Authorization` from any body it relays.
- [ ] **`oauth2-client-credentials` waits for its own PR**, with the OAuth path's guards (an https token URL that passes the host rules, `redirect: "manual"`, a capped answer, name-only logs) and a token cache keyed by the row's digest. The user flow, `oauth2-user` rows ("refused until a later note"), landed 2026-10-25: see "OAuth providers (2026-10-25)" above.

### Model providers (2026-10-25)

Ben, launch week: "assume we want all of this and build the best, most robust thing we're going to want long term, so that when we launch on Monday people can just run with it." The design is [docs/design/2026-10-model-providers.md](docs/design/2026-10-model-providers.md); the agent page is /docs/agent/ai, the person's page /docs/ai, the setups `setup/ai.md` and `setup/ai-gateway.md`. Built: the AI proxy's per-route rules as fields of the `ai` entries (`shape`, `chat`, `embeddings`, `aig`, `authOptional`, `kind: "proxy"`), the `ai-gateway` entry, the default route `/api/ai/default/…` behind the manifest's `ai: { chat: true }` and the SDK's `ai()`, the resolution order, `org_settings.ai_default` and `usage_models` (migration 0017), `everyapp ai show|list|default|gateway on|off`, `everyapp_ai_show|ai_default`, the Usage page's AI default card and By model table, the event `ai.default_changed`, the byte pins for all eight AI entries. The answer to "why not AI Gateway as the default everywhere": it is a proxy with no models of its own and needs an upstream key; the right default is OpenRouter through AI Gateway when both are set, else OpenRouter, else Workers AI (design note, section 1). Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **The shipped order: `openrouter`, `workers-ai`, `openai`, `anthropic`, `groq`, `mistral`, `gemini`**, the first whose key is set; OpenRouter first because one key reaches many models and the price comes back in every answer, Workers AI second because it needs no provider account (weaker models). Cohere is pinnable but never a default (its own chat shape; its OpenAI-compatible endpoint is on another host). Alternative: Workers AI first (zero accounts) at the cost of the inbox agent's quality.
- [ ] **The per-provider default models**: OpenRouter `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`, Workers AI `@cf/meta/llama-4-scout-17b-16e-instruct` (the inbox's proven function-calling model; the research found `@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash` cheaper), OpenAI `gpt-6-luna` (tool calling on chat completions needs `reasoning_effort: "none"` on the GPT-6 family), Anthropic `claude-haiku-5-5` (its OpenAI-compatible endpoint, which Anthropic calls not long-term), Groq `openai/gpt-oss-20b`, Mistral `mistral-small-latest`, Gemini `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`; embeddings OpenRouter `openai/text-embedding-3-small`, Workers AI `@cf/baai/bge-m3`, OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small`, Mistral `mistral-embed`, Gemini `gemini-embedding-001`. `everyapp ai default <provider>/<model>` overrides per Gateway. Ben's call on each.
- [ ] **AI Gateway is one operator switch** (`AI_GATEWAY_ID`): every call to an entry with an `aig` block goes through it, direct routes and the default alike; the provider's key stays on the call; BYOK stored keys, Unified Billing, the `compat` endpoint and per-request `cf-aig-*` options are not used. Alternatives: per-app opt-in (a manifest field), per-provider. **Not on by default on everyapp.team**: Ben's call whether to run `pnpm everyapp ai gateway on` there before Monday.
- [ ] **The AI Gateway paths per provider were read from Cloudflare's docs, not probed live**: OpenRouter's page is ambiguous (`/openrouter/chat/completions` in the text, `/openrouter/v1/chat/completions` in its curl; the entry records the latter, `trim: "/api"`); Workers AI uses its own URL plus `cf-aig-gateway-id` (the form Cloudflare documents now); Gemini and Cohere have no documented AI Gateway form for their OpenAI-shaped paths and go direct. One live call per fronted provider on loop-1 before relying on it is a Sunday check.
- [ ] **The operator's choice wins over the order even while its key is unset** (apps get `ai_default_not_configured` naming the key, setup asks for it). Alternative: fall through to the order silently, which would hide a typo.
- [ ] **`ai: { chat: true | { why, optional, anyOf, docs } }` is the manifest shape**, not a `default` entry under `providers`; the default route serves `/v1/chat/completions` and `/v1/embeddings` only; the `ai.models` allowlist doesn't apply to it (the operator chose the model).
- [ ] **`usage_models` is its own monthly table** (organization, app, provider, model, month) rather than a `model` column in `usage_daily`'s primary key, so the migration is additive; the Usage page shows it as By model; `workspace usage --json` as `byModel`. Alternative: rebuild `usage_daily` with the model in its key.
- [ ] **A registered row still never carries `mode: "ai"`**: an entry's URL template and AI Gateway slug are routing a stranger's row shouldn't write until the key-binding rule covers them. The parser's refusal is unchanged.
- [ ] **`everyapp_ai_default` is owners and admins** (the people who cap apps); `everyapp_ai_show` is every member with `configured`/`settings` hidden below admin. Alternative: owners only for the write.
- [x] **The app pull requests are merged on `0.3.0-rc.3`** (agentic-inbox-everyapp #12, squash 085291df; open-crm #12, squash 67198288; both pinned with `pnpm pack-for-app <app> --npm 0.3.0-rc.3`): each manifest says `ai: { chat }` and the chat model goes through `/api/ai/default`; `rc.1` and `rc.2` were cut before #675 and refused the field. The everyapp.team redeploy of both apps is pending Rollout 3; with the default route each needs one key, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, which is already set there.
- [ ] **Caps in dollars: built** (the follow-up pull request stacked on this one, branch `t3/caps-dollars-2026-10-25`; migration `0018_cost_caps`, to be regenerated against main at merge time). `org_app_settings.monthly_cost_cap_micros` and `org_settings.default_monthly_cost_cap_micros` beside the call caps; the effective dollar cap resolves like the call cap (the app's own, else `unlimited` means none, else the default), each kind on its own; the app is over when calls reach the call cap or the month's `usage_monthly.cost_micros` reaches the dollar cap, and the refusal says which (`… its monthly cap of $20.00 of provider-reported cost …`); `cap.warning`/`cap.reached`/`app.paused_cap` carry `kind` (`calls` or `cost`, `costCap`/`cost` in dollars for the latter) with the kind in the dedupe key; `cap.raised` carries `costCap`/`previousCostCap` when the dollar cap changed. The two dialogs take "or dollars per month", By app shows the dollar cap beside the call cap with its own bar; `workspace app cap <app> [<n|off>] [--usd <dollars|off>] [--unlimited]`, `workspace cap default [<n|off>] [--usd <dollars|off>]`, `GATEWAY_PREDATES_COST_CAPS`; the MCP tools take `costUsd`. Defaults taken; confirm or change each:
  - [ ] **A dollar cap is $0.01 to $1,000,000, in whole cents** (stored as micro-dollars; a fraction of a cent is refused, not rounded).
  - [ ] **The dollar cap counts only where the provider reports a price** (OpenRouter's `usage.cost`, DataForSEO's `cost`); OpenAI, Workers AI and the rest move only the call cap, and the dialogs say so ("Counted only where the provider reports a price: OpenRouter, DataForSEO. Other calls count toward the call cap alone."). No per-model price table, no forced `stream_options.include_usage` (the Caps and budgets item's reasons).
  - [ ] **"Which cap paused" is said as "Paused by calls" / "Paused by dollars"** on the Usage row (the launcher card keeps its one "Paused" chip); `pausedBy` in the usage JSON is `calls` when both are reached, as the refusal names the call cap first.
  - [ ] **A kind left out is kept**: `--usd 20` alone (and `costUsd` alone, and the dialog's empty field under a default) keeps the app's call cap; `unlimited` clears both and is refused beside either cap. A cap of either kind on an `--unlimited` app ends the exemption, and the kind not given then follows its default (rather than refusing or keeping "no call cap at all").
  - [ ] **The cap event keys now include the kind** (`cap.reached:<org>:<app row>:<month>:calls:<cap>`), so an app already announced at its call cap this month on everyapp.team is announced once more after the upgrade.
  - [ ] **The stop is as soft as the call cap's, in cents**: the check reads the cost already reported this month, so the call that crosses the dollar cap is answered and the next one is refused.
- [ ] **Anthropic's `/v1/chat/completions`** is in its inference routes so Anthropic can be a default; Anthropic documents the endpoint as "not a long-term or production-ready solution" and ignores `strict`. Alternative: drop it and make Anthropic pin-only.
- [ ] **`AI_GATEWAY_ID` is a Gateway setting written by `ai gateway on`**, and `ai show` reports `aiGateway: { on }` without the id (a setting isn't read back). The provisioners thread's per-app AI Gateways (`resources.aiGateway`) are separate and never touch it.

### MCP apps by URL: the agent's tools (2026-10-25)

The design's section 4, "The agent" ([docs/design/2026-10-mcp-by-url.md](docs/design/2026-10-mcp-by-url.md)): `everyapp_mcp_list|show|add|remove|on|off|refresh|tool`, the twins of `everyapp mcp …` and of Admin → Apps' MCP apps, are workspace tools on `/mcp` (definitions `packages/toolbus/src/mcpApps.ts`, handlers `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/mcp-app-tools.ts` over `McpAppService`). They supersede the `everyapp_workspace_provider` follow-up under "Agent parity" and the "MCP way to turn a provider on" line under "MCP apps". Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **Owners and admins, `remove` the owners' alone with `confirm: true`**, listed in the full listing only like the other workspace tools; a read-only connection sees `list` and `show`. `remove` is not a `destructive` tool (those stay hidden until approvals exist), as for `workspace_remove`. _Superseded 2026-10-25 by Ben's ruling: `remove` is owners' and admins' too._
- [ ] **A built-in that was never added has no row, and only the operator adds it** (`pnpm everyapp mcp on dataforseo`): `everyapp_mcp_on` answers `APP_NOT_FOUND` naming that command, and `everyapp_mcp_list` lists the rows alone (the CLI's `mcp list` also shows "not added" built-ins). Letting the tool add the first row is a call to `planProviderAppOn` beside the service.
- [ ] **`everyapp_mcp_tool` changes one tool's risk and on/off**, as `mcp tool` does; the risk default, the name and the description change in the UI alone after `mcp add`.
- [ ] **An admin's AI client can add a server** whose tool descriptions then reach every member given access: the same power as Admin → Apps → Add an MCP server, audited under `everyapp`, with the URL rules on top. Owners only is the alternative.
- [ ] **The tools never say where a credential is bound.** `McpAppService.get` (the MCP app dialog) shows `credential.boundTo`, the origin from `remote_mcp_credentials`, which is per name across the Gateway: on a hosted Gateway it can be another organization's former server (the add rule refuses a name only while another organization's app using it is live). Show the origin only when it is the app's own, or say "another server".

### Public surface (2026-10-24)

Ben's launch-week asks on what is reachable without a sign-in (2026-10-24: "should we just do that [Access] by default, or make it a highly recommended additional command?"; "make sure Cloudflare Access gets set up with sign in with Cloudflare if possible … but support both"; "unless there's a super good reason an endpoint needs to be directly public, we should err on not public"; "we'll just have our own skills for doing that [the security review] locally"), built as #612 (the perimeter: the manifest field `mcp: { by, why }`, the Gateway's public `/health`, the starter's `/health` private), #622 (the CLI: `access on` with both sign-in methods and the new bypass plan, the setup offer, `status` and `deploy` saying `/mcp`), #606 (`pnpm security-review` and the skills instead of the CI workflow), the app manifests (OpenCRM #8 drops `/health` from `public`, OpenAnalytics #10 drops `/healthz`; agentic-inbox and openseo had nothing public) and the threat-model PR this section came with. Nothing was deployed: Ben's order is `pnpm everyapp update`, then `pnpm everyapp access on`, then the crm and analytics redeploys from their updated checkouts (`access show` says "paths differ" on everyapp.team until that `access on`: expected, not breakage). Each bullet is the default taken; yes keeps it.

- [ ] **Per-app `/mcp` is off unless the manifest opts in** with `mcp: { by: "gateway" | "app", why }` (`why` plain text, 1 to 200 characters, shown by `deploy` and `status`; the old string form is refused with the new shape; a `public` route covering `/mcp` is refused at author time). `https://<gateway>/mcp` is the one connector, and the four launch apps' UIs already hand it out. On a host without the field `/mcp` is an ordinary private route (the app answers, usually 404) and an MCP-only token opens nothing there, `/mcp` included; any client still on `https://<app>.everyapp.team/mcp` must move to the Gateway's. `everyapp dev` keeps its local `http://<app-id>.localhost:8787/mcp` whatever the manifest says (`createDevGatewayHandler` fills in `by: "gateway"`). Alternative: `by: "gateway"` on by default as before (every app host an MCP surface and, behind the outer ring, an Access bypass); a Gateway setting instead of a manifest field.
- [ ] **An app's health route is private; the Gateway's `/health` is the public liveness check.** `GET`/`HEAD https://<gateway>/health` answers 200 `{"status":"ok"}` after a `SELECT 1` bounded to 2 seconds, else 503 `{"status":"error","database":"down"}`; no session read, no version, `no-store`; one probe at a time per isolate, its answer reused for 5 seconds; other methods 405; Access lets it through, under Managed OAuth too, and `/HEALTH` is the JSON 404. crm's `/health` and analytics' `/healthz` stay as handlers, reachable by a member or a personal access token; the starter's manifest has no `public` entry (its comment shows a webhook, `POST /webhooks/github`, as the public example). Alternative: `public: [{ path: "/health" }]` on the one app you monitor.
- [ ] **Setup offers Access, Yes by default, never unattended.** Interactive `setup` (and a `deploy` that installs a Gateway) asks "Put Cloudflare Access in front of your Gateway?" right after the owner claim; `--yes`, `--json`, CI and `update` never turn it on (a Zero Trust team name is permanent), `next` keeps naming `access on`, and a failure is the warning `ACCESS_SETUP_SKIPPED` with `data.rerun`, never a failed setup. Measured: `access on` needs the Cloudflare login and, with no team yet, a name; the API path creates the team on the free plan with no plan pick or payment details. Alternative: on with no question; a recommended command only (the state before).
- [ ] **Both sign-in methods.** Sign in with Cloudflare (type `cloudflare`, created with Restrict to account members on when the team has none) for members of the operator's Cloudflare account, the one-time PIN for everyone else; `access on` allows only those two on both applications, adds them to applications made before (everyapp.team's gain the Cloudflare provider on Ben's next run, and Access then shows its chooser before the PIN), never takes a provider off, and warns when the Cloudflare login's address is no member (`ACCESS_LOGIN_NOT_MEMBER`: Ben's must equal his Gateway owner email or the Gateway answers `ACCESS_EMAIL_MISMATCH`; the PIN keeps working). A `cloudflare` provider with Restrict to account members off is left off the applications and named (`ACCESS_IDP_ANY_CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT`); no restricted one is made beside it, so that team has the PIN alone until the operator turns the restriction on and runs `access on` again (an application that allowed it earlier keeps it: nothing is taken off). The detail and the `--no-pin` follow-up are under "Cloudflare Access from the CLI", "Sign-in methods". Alternative: the PIN alone.
- [ ] **crm's `POST /internal/erase-organization` stays public**, with the reason beside it in the manifest: an operator's GDPR erasure across organizations, answering 404 unless `ERASURE_SECRET` is set and the request is signed with it within five minutes; no member identity could reach it, since the operator is not a member of the organization being erased. crm's `GET /t/*` and `POST /t/e`, analytics' `POST /collect`, `GET /script.js` and its share routes with their assets stay public by design (tracking scripts and shared dashboards run on other sites). OAuth callbacks and inbound webhooks must be public when an app has them; none of the four does today. Alternative: an `everyapp` command that calls the erasure over the service binding, and the route private.
- [ ] **Uptime monitoring: a personal access token, plus a service token behind the ring.** The Gateway's `/health` needs nothing. One app's health route takes `Authorization: Bearer epat_…`: a Full-access token (an `mcp:` token is `403 token_scope` there) of a dedicated monitoring member who can open that one app, bound to it, expiring in 90 days by default; behind the outer ring the request also needs an Access service token (`CF-Access-Client-Id`/`-Secret`) from a **reusable** Service Auth policy on the apps application (an inline one makes `access on` skip the application, `ACCESS_INLINE_POLICIES`). The service token alone opens nothing but removes the outer ring for its holder on every app host; the path is unverified live and the docs say so (`docs/runbooks/cloudflare-access.md`, the agent docs' access page). Any monitor on crm's `/health` or analytics' `/healthz` needs this after their redeploys, or should move to `https://everyapp.team/health`. Alternative: that app's health route public in its manifest (verified, no credential).
- [ ] **The CI security review is gone** (`.github/workflows/security-review.yml`, the starter's copy and the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` requirement; #606): `pnpm security-review` (`scripts/security-review.mjs`: Claude Code `claude -p` in restricted mode over the branch's diff, with the upstream audit prompt pinned at its commit, this repository's scan instructions and false-positive rules), the `security-review` project skill (`.claude/skills/security-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/`) and the everyapp skill's `security-review.md` (the app boundaries checklist) for new apps. Advisory, not hardened against prompt injection: a stranger's branch gets a person's read first. The detail is under "Threat model and security scanning". Alternative: the workflow back, behind "Require approval for all external contributors".
- [ ] **The threat model and its register follow** (the PR this section came with): rows `perimeter.app-host-mcp-opt-in`, `gateway.health-liveness-only` and `access.sign-in-methods-both` added (328 rows on merge: B1 35, B2 47, B3 39, B4 42, B5 47, B6 38, B7 40, B8 40; the threat model's B7 count, 39 against a register of 40, corrected too), `perimeter.mcp-intercept-after-authz`, `perimeter.mcp-only-token-scope`, `apphost.mcp-session-or-pat-with-csrf`, `pat.mcp-only-tokens-stay-at-mcp` and `access.bypass-list.machine-endpoints-only` restated for the opt-in and the smaller bypass list (the wildcard bypasses nothing), gap 6 (the `by: "app"` hatch needs an opt-in with a reason; the perimeter still forwards `mcp:read` scopes to an app that serves its own MCP), 33 (the old Workers, deleted) and 37 (`access show` compares the shape; the gateway still verifies none of it) rewritten, the diagram's bypass box corrected. Line numbers for those rows are as of `c2d04d85` (`manifest.ts` as of `88b4117c`, where #626 moved it).
  - [x] **The 17 old Workers, listed and deleted** (Ben's Q11d, "delete them this weekend"; 2026-10-24 22:54Z). The record (what was checked before deletion, the per-Worker table with the storage left behind, the verification after) is `docs/overnight/2026-10-24-old-workers.md`: it names the account's inventory, and `docs/overnight/` stays out of the public copy (`scripts/public-copy/policy.mjs`), where TODO.md travels scrubbed and `scripts/public-copy/private.txt` rewrites only the parenthesized lists, the `workers.dev` host and three names.
- [ ] Follow-ups: `status` could warn when a stored manifest still carries the string `mcp` form (the row no longer validates, so the Gateway answers 404 `unknown_app_host` on every request of that host: `packages/perimeter/src/registry/apps.ts:161`, `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.ts:399-420`; everyapp.team's four are clean); agentic-inbox's `workers/everyapp/tools.ts` comment still says the Gateway serves its tools on the app host's `/mcp` (one line, with the next inbox PR); the old Workers' D1, KV, R2, Hyperdrive and Secrets Store entries after launch; the `/__everyapp/health` probe the Gateway runs over the service binding when it activates an app (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-activation.ts`) is the in-platform health check and stays private.

### Old-era resources (2026-10-24)

The 17 old Workers on workers.dev are gone (2026-10-24 22:54Z, the "Public surface" section above; the record with every name is `docs/overnight/2026-10-24-old-workers.md`, kept out of the public copy). Deleting a Worker leaves its storage, which still exists in the Cloudflare account and costs storage until removed: 13 D1 databases, 13 KV namespaces, 6 R2 buckets, one Hyperdrive config and one Secrets Store entry, each listed by id against its Worker in that record's "Storage left behind" section, none read by any live Worker (the scan of the remaining Workers found no binding to them). The Durable Object storage of the four Workers that had namespaces went with them. **Ben decides; nothing is deleted without his word**, by id from that list, never by prefix. Alternative: leave it all; it costs storage, not safety.

- [ ] **D1 and KV (13 each).** `cf d1 delete <id>` and the KV namespace delete, one by one from the record; two of the deleted Workers shared one database and one namespace.
- [ ] **R2 (6 buckets).** Each may hold files (uploads, audit artifacts); look before deleting.
- [ ] **One Hyperdrive config** (it points at a Postgres database elsewhere, which this list does not cover) and **one Secrets Store entry** (the token of the December 2025 CLI's Alchemy state Worker, now deleted).
- [ ] **Five more old Workers without workers.dev**, not in Ben's 17 and still present, named in the record: they answer nothing publicly (no workers.dev, no Custom Domain, no route). Delete them too, or keep; same rule, by name.

Open is what the design's section 11 gives him.

If not, close #619; `status` alone is the slice.

### Alchemy (2026-10-24)

Ben asked, over ratstack.sh, what converting the Gateway and the CLI's provisioning to Alchemy would entail and whether to do it now. [docs/design/2026-10-alchemy-research.md](docs/design/2026-10-alchemy-research.md) has the answer with the evidence: not now; keep cf plus the in-Worker REST deployer plus the Gateway's D1 as the operator path. The feasible conversion is Alchemy as the uploader behind the CLI with a D1-backed state layer, 40 to 70 engineer-days, and it meets one hard blocker in Alchemy 2.0.0-beta.84 (every upload replaces the whole binding set, so secrets set outside the stack and the Gateway's JWT keypair are wiped on the next deploy) plus three traps (service-binding prop keys snake-cased on the wire, `appId` arriving as `app_id`; secrets persisted in plaintext in a D1-backed state store; D1 migrations taken over one way). The typed-infrastructure property RatStack sells already exists here through `cf workers types && tsc`. The document stays out of the public copy by name (`--design designs` drops `*research*`). Nothing in the code changed. The hosted-gateway Alchemy stage noted under Done ("OpenSEO's first deploy is the Alchemy stage") is deferred with it.

**Needs Ben**

- [ ] **Confirm the deferral and the probe dates.** Re-probe on 2026-12-01 and 2027-02-01, or when Alchemy 2.0.0 reaches GA, against the document's section 8; the gating trigger is a keep-or-inherit bindings mode. In a loop test account with the cleanup step, never the main account; results go in the document's probe log.
- [ ] **A plan before mutation** (`gateway.plan` NDJSON step, then `setup|update --dry-run`): the one Alchemy benefit the product lacks, built on the Gateway's D1 as the state with no dependency (document section 7, items 4 and 5; two to four plus one to two days). After the first week of real self-hoster `update` runs, and only if you want it.
- [ ] **Delete the dead `everyapp` Alchemy stage in the OpenSEO port repository** (it never ran against a real account; about 730 lines; closes the second half of "OpenSEO onto `everyapp deploy`" under Build). Port-repository venue rules apply.

**Follow-ups, no decision needed, after launch**

- [ ] **Pin the gateway test fixture to the real `cf build`** inside `packages/cli/src/lib/cfDryRun.test.ts` (CI only): `gateway-worker.config.json` is hand-copied, last touched 2026-10-13, and 42 apply tests run on it.
- [ ] **A gateway binding fence test**: every name `patchBuildOutput` writes is declared with the same type in `cloudflare.config.ts`, no `KV` or `APP__*` there, then delete the legacy `delete env["KV"]`.

### Provisioners (2026-10-24)

Ben's mandate, 2026-10-24: "support as much of Cloudflare's developer-platform surface area as possible" so people aren't waiting on PRs after Monday; later that evening: "it doesn't need to be generic", one small PR per kind in the order Queues, Workers AI, Vectorize, AI Gateway, Hyperdrive, Browser Rendering, Email sending, Images, Analytics Engine, Secrets Store. The contract is [docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md) (section 2 per kind; the scope change at its top; the Alchemy decision in section 6, one paragraph). Landed 2026-10-25, each squash-merged on green: Workers AI #669, Hyperdrive and the Postgres answer #679, Browser Rendering #682, Images #689, Vectorize #670, AI Gateway #673, Queues #684, Email sending #681, Secrets Store #686, Analytics Engine #691; the docs repair #695 (two merges had duplicated paragraphs) and #705 (the `everyAppAuxiliaryWorker` typing regression against the Vite plugin, from #323's typed Durable Object namespaces, found by the OAuth providers thread). Each kind's own section follows this one ("Provisioners: <kind>"), with its defaults and follow-ups. Shared across the kinds: the byte-for-byte pin of the six older kinds (#665, `pnpm -C packages/cli test -- deployApp.pinned`), the live harness on the loop test account (#667, `EVERYAPP_LIVE_PROVISIONERS=1`), the people's page [landing-page/content/docs/resources.mdx](landing-page/content/docs/resources.mdx), and the runbook's live facts (docs/runbooks/onboarding-loop-test-accounts.md, "loop-1 after the provisioners probes").

**Defaults to confirm**

- [ ] **`cf`, not Alchemy and not a REST client of the CLI's own.** Every kind goes through the CLI's `CfRunner` (#657's blocker stands: Alchemy's upload replaces the whole binding set). Re-probe with the assessment on 2026-12-01; the extra flip is three kinds without a working `cf` command (today one: `secrets-store stores create`), which would earn a thin REST client behind the same seam, still not Alchemy.
- [ ] **Ledger, not adopt, for the kinds that hold data or credentials** (queues, Vectorize, AI Gateways, store secrets): a resource of the app's name that no deploy recorded is `RESOURCE_NAME_TAKEN`, as for D1. R2 keeps #611's named model. Walk-back: an `adopt: true` per entry.
- [ ] **Binding-only kinds must be declared** (`ai`, `browser`, `images`, `analyticsEngine`, `sendEmail`): the policy refuses the binding type unless `everyapp.config.ts` names it, so a `cloudflare.config.ts` can't add account-wide capabilities on its own. Walk-back: allow by type alone.
- [ ] **`cfDeploy` passes `--no-provision`** (the Queues PR): cf's deploy otherwise creates a missing queue, KV, D1 or R2 bucket on its own, unrecorded. The pinned cf args changed by that one flag.
- [ ] **The hosted deployer refuses every new kind** (its bundle allowlist already did; the manifest sentences are per-kind follow-ups). Teaching it is after launch.
- [ ] **No `everyapp status` RESOURCES cell yet**: each kind's section lists it as a follow-up; one small PR adds the cell from the stored manifest for all kinds at once.

**Needs Ben**

- [ ] **A Secrets Store on loop-1** (dashboard: Secrets Store → Create store), so the Secrets Store live test can run its create, upload, read-back and delete path; today it stops at `SECRETS_STORE_MISSING` by design.
- [ ] **Roll loop-1's token to the runbook's template** after launch week: the one in `accounts.json` carries nearly every permission group plus Account API Tokens Write and Billing Write (runbook, live facts), and add the rows the new kinds need (Queues, Vectorize, AI Gateway, Secrets Store Edit).

**Follow-ups**
