### Provisioners: Workers AI (2026-10-25)

`resources.ai: ["AI"]` binds Workers AI (`env.AI.run()`) on every Worker of an app: nothing created, recorded or purged, billed to the account ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md), section 2's `ai` row; `landing-page/content/agent/manifest.md`, "Workers AI").

**Defaults to confirm**

- [ ] **Declared or refused.** The deploy's policy allows an `ai` binding only under a name `resources.ai` declares, so `cloudflare.config.ts` can't add account-wide inference on its own. Walk-back: allow `ai` by type alone.
- [ ] **Remote in development, one switch.** The SDK marks the dev binding `dev: { remote: true }`; the starter keeps `remoteBindings: false`, so every call throws `Binding AI needs to be run remotely` until the app sets `remoteBindings: true` in `vite.config.ts` and runs `cf auth login`. Alternatives: the starter on `remoteBindings: true` (every new app's dev then needs a login), or no dev binding at all.
- [ ] **Refused on the dispatch tier,** with a pointer to the `workers-ai` provider: the binding would bill the platform's account.
- [ ] **No Gateway budget on the binding.** Its calls aren't counted, capped or attributed by the Gateway; the docs send apps that want caps to the `workers-ai` provider. A metered wrapper the SDK hands out is a later choice.

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **`everyapp dev`'s ready block** says nothing about the remote binding yet (the design's section 4 `Remote bindings:` line); Workers AI is the first remote kind.
- [ ] **The hosted deployer's manifest check** (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/metadata.ts`, `manifestProblems()`) doesn't name `resources.ai`; its bundle check refuses the binding type (`AI is a ai binding, which third-party apps can't have`).
- [ ] **`everyapp status`'s RESOURCES cell** gains `AI` once the first lane's cell is on main.

### Provisioners: Email sending (2026-10-25)

`resources.sendEmail: [{ name, allowedSenderAddresses, destinationAddress?, allowedDestinationAddresses? }]` binds Cloudflare's `send_email` binding on every Worker of an app, beside the Gateway's `email` capability (which stays the recommended path): nothing created, recorded or purged ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md), section 2's `sendEmail` row; `landing-page/content/agent/manifest.md`, "Email sending").

**Defaults to confirm**

- [ ] **Every entry names its senders.** The strict validator requires a non-empty `allowedSenderAddresses` on each `resources.sendEmail` entry, so a manifest (a third-party release's too) can't declare an unrestricted binding that sends as any address on the account's Email Sending domains, the Gateway's own invitation sender included; the policy holds the built binding to exactly the declared lists. Walk-back: allow an entry without a sender list.
- [ ] **The listed senders are accepted as written.** They need not be on a domain among the installation's senders (`apps.email_senders`, `everyapp email set --sender`); Cloudflare refuses a `from` whose domain isn't onboarded to Email Sending in the account at send time. Walk-back: require each sender's domain among the installation's senders (the deploy reads the row under its lease already).
- [ ] **Lists compared as sets.** Order and repeats don't matter; a list is never a single address; absent is absent.
- [ ] **Addresses, not patterns.** `*@domain` is refused in the binding's lists (Cloudflare's docs don't say it takes wildcards); an empty destination list is refused (ambiguous between "none" and "any").
- [ ] **Refused on the dispatch tier and by the hosted deployer's manifest check**: the binding would send from the platform's account, past the Gateway's budgets and sender list.

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **The design's rationale for the binding** (section 2: "for `message.reply()` from an inbound handler") is wrong: `reply()` needs no binding. The docs give the real reasons (code written against `env.EMAIL.send()`, mail sent beyond what `reply()` can); fix the design row.
- [ ] **`everyapp status`'s RESOURCES cell** gains the binding once the first lane's cell is on main.

### Provisioners: Hyperdrive and the Postgres answer (2026-10-25)

The Hyperdrive lane of the provisioners swath ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md), section 2's `hyperdrive` row). Hyperdrive's origin is now the binding's required setting where a person meets it: the deploy's `HYPERDRIVE_ORIGIN_MISSING` names the variable for this app (`POSTGRES_DATABASE_URL (or HYPERDRIVE_ORIGIN_URL)` for one config, the binding's own for several) and the file, with `data.binding`, `data.variables` and `data.file`; `everyapp status` repeats it under a `pending` app's `APP_NOT_UPLOADED` until the config is recorded. The Postgres answer is written once, `## Postgres` on the manifest page (Neon, Supabase, PlanetScale Postgres, Xata, Crunchy Bridge, a server of your own; the three steps; the code; development; the direct-URL note; Turso), and linked from the Hyperdrive, database, secrets, dev, errors and troubleshooting pages and both skills. A deploy with its origin present is byte for byte what it was (the pinned snapshots are unchanged).

**Defaults taken, each to confirm**

- [ ] **`everyapp configure` doesn't write the origin**; the deploy's error and `status` are the gate, and the person writes `.everyapp/secrets.env`. Wiring it was more than a small change (a third holder beside the Gateway and the app's Worker in the plan, the prompts, the piped-name check, the finish). Walk-back: in the app's directory `configure` asks for each missing origin with a hidden prompt (or takes it from stdin and `--from-env`), checks it with `parseOriginUrl`, and writes the file `0600`, gitignored, never to a Worker.
- [ ] **`status` names a missing origin only for a `pending` app.** It can't read the deploying machine's file, and a deployed app's config needs no origin; a recorded config deleted out of band shows nothing until the next deploy recreates it (which stops with the same error when the origin is gone). Walk-back: a `data.apps[].hyperdrive` field for scripts.
- [ ] **One direct URL for both** in the docs: `POSTGRES_DATABASE_URL` serves the migrations and the app's only config; `HYPERDRIVE_ORIGIN_URL` splits them when wanted.
- [ ] **Turso is an app secret pair** (`TURSO_DATABASE_URL`, `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`, `@libsql/client/web`), not a kind, with no deploy migrations. Walk-back: a `turso` migrations dialect.
- [ ] **MySQL is documented as Hyperdrive with no `database` or `migrations`**: the deploy accepts a `mysql://` origin and migrates nothing. Walk-back: `database.provider: "mysql"` and a migrations dialect.
- [ ] **No Hyperdrive live test**: loop-1 has no Postgres and `hyperdrive create` needs a reachable origin. Walk-back: a Neon free database for the loop account, its URL in `providers.env`.

### Provisioners: Browser Rendering (2026-10-25)

`resources.browser: ["BROWSER"]` binds Browser Rendering on every Worker of an app: nothing created, recorded or purged; browser time is billed to the account, which needs Workers Paid ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md), section 2's `browser` row; `landing-page/content/agent/manifest.md`, "Browser Rendering").

**Defaults to confirm**

- [ ] **Declared or refused.** The deploy's policy allows a `browser` binding only under a name `resources.browser` declares, so `cloudflare.config.ts` can't add account-wide browser time on its own. Walk-back: allow `browser` by type alone.
- [ ] **Local in development, not remote.** The design's row said `dev: { remote: true }`; Miniflare serves a local stand-in and the first session downloads Chrome `126.0.6478.182` into the global `.wrangler` cache, so the binding is `{ type: "browser" }` in both modes and dev needs no login. Walk-back: mark it remote (dev sessions then bill the account).
- [ ] **Refused on the dispatch tier:** the browser time would bill the platform's account.
- [ ] **No Gateway budget on browser time.** Sessions aren't counted, capped or attributed by the Gateway.

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **The hosted deployer's manifest check** (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/metadata.ts`, `manifestProblems()`) doesn't name `resources.browser`; its bundle check refuses the binding type (`BROWSER is a browser binding, which third-party apps can't have`).
- [ ] **`everyapp status`'s RESOURCES cell** gains `Browser` once the first lane's cell is on main.
### Provisioners: AI Gateway (2026-10-25)

`resources.aiGateway: [{ name, collectLogs?, cacheTtl? }]` is a manifest resource kind ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md) section 2): an AI Gateway of the app's own, `<worker>-<binding>`, bound on every Worker as a json binding `{ id, accountId, url }` (empty `accountId` and `url` in development), provisioned through the resource ledger, purged and torn down; `packages/cli/src/lib/cf/aiGateway.ts` is also the Model providers thread's library. Probed live on loop-1.

**Needs Ben (defaults taken)**

- [ ] **One hyphen in the id**, `every-acme-notes-ai-gateway`: Cloudflare refuses `--` in a gateway id, so the id alone doesn't say whose it is (app `notes-ai`'s `GATEWAY` collides with `notes`'s `AI_GATEWAY`); the ledger refuses the second app with `RESOURCE_NAME_TAKEN`. Walk-back: a hash suffix, or an id unrelated to the Worker name recorded in the ledger.
- [ ] **Ledger, never adopt**: an existing unrecorded gateway of the id is refused (it holds logs of prompts and answers). Walk-back: `adopt: true` per entry.
- [ ] **Logs on, cache off, no rate limit, `authentication: false` by default.** Unauthenticated because the app sends its own provider key; an authenticated gateway would need a Cloudflare token in the app. Walk-back: manifest fields for each.
- [ ] **The strict validator counts the longest org key (12)** so a manifest that validates deploys on any Gateway; it refuses ids that would fit with a shorter key.
- [ ] **A json binding of `{ id, accountId, url }`**, not a text URL (design section 9).

**Follow-ups, no decision needed**

- [ ] `RESOURCES_KEPT` on `delete --purge` doesn't look for an unrecorded gateway of the app's id (D1 and KV only, as Hyperdrive).
- [ ] `RESOURCE_REPLACED` can't fire for a gateway: its id is its name, so a recorded one deleted and recreated by someone else reads as the app's.
- [ ] The update is a PUT that sends back every setting it reads; guardrails, OpenTelemetry and spend limits aren't read back, so a deploy that changes `collectLogs` or `cacheTtl` may reset ones set in the dashboard (unverified).
- [ ] `everyapp status`'s RESOURCES cell, once the first lane adds it.

### Provisioners: Images (2026-10-25)

`resources.images: ["IMAGES"]` binds Cloudflare Images on every Worker of an app: nothing created, recorded or purged; transformations are billed to the account ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md), section 2's `images` row; `landing-page/content/agent/manifest.md`, "Images").

**Defaults to confirm**

- [ ] **Declared or refused.** The deploy's policy allows an `images` binding only under a name `resources.images` declares, so `cloudflare.config.ts` can't add account-wide transformations on its own. Walk-back: allow `images` by type alone.
- [ ] **Refused on the dispatch tier:** the transformations would bill the platform's account.
- [ ] **No Gateway budget on transformations.** They aren't counted, capped or attributed by the Gateway.

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **The hosted deployer's manifest check** (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/metadata.ts`, `manifestProblems()`) doesn't name `resources.images`; its bundle check refuses the binding type (`IMAGES is a images binding, which third-party apps can't have`).
- [ ] **`everyapp status`'s RESOURCES cell** gains `Images` once the first lane's cell is on main.

### Provisioners: Secrets Store (2026-10-25)

`resources.secretsStore: [{ name: "STRIPE_KEY" }]` binds a Cloudflare Secrets Store secret, `<worker>--<binding>` in the account's store, on every Worker of an app; the deploy creates it from `STRIPE_KEY` in `.everyapp/secrets.env` through the ledger and `delete --purge` deletes it ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md), section 2's `secretsStore` row; `landing-page/content/agent/manifest.md`, "Secrets Store").

**Defaults to confirm**

- [ ] **The store is the person's to make once** (dashboard: Secrets Store → Create store): cf 1.0.0-beta.14 has no `stores create`, though the API does (`POST /accounts/{id}/secrets_store/stores`). Without one the deploy stops with `SECRETS_STORE_MISSING` before anything is created. Walk-back: one REST call for that endpoint (design section 6).
- [ ] **Which store:** the account's only one; with several, the one named `everyapp`, else the first listed; an app keeps the store it recorded a secret in.
- [ ] **Ledger, not adopt:** a secret of the app's name that no deploy recorded is `RESOURCE_NAME_TAKEN` (it may hold someone's credential).
- [ ] **The deploy never rewrites a value:** a recorded secret is bound as it is; a changed `STRIPE_KEY` in `.everyapp/secrets.env` does nothing once the secret exists. Rotation is the dashboard's for now.
- [ ] **Secrets go first:** the store and every value a missing secret needs are checked, and the secrets created, before D1, KV and Hyperdrive, so a missing store or value stops the deploy with nothing created.
- [ ] **Scope `workers` only** on the secrets the deploy creates (not `ai_gateway`).
- [ ] **Refused on the dispatch tier** (the store is the platform account's).

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **`everyapp dev` seeds the local store** from `<BINDING>` in `.dev.vars` before the app starts: Miniflare's admin API through the CLI's own `cf` dependency works (prototype 2026-10-25: ~5 s per start; the seed script is in `landing-page/content/agent/dev.md`); skip when unchanged (a hash under `.cloudflare/state`). Until then the ready block says the binding is empty.
- [ ] **`everyapp secret set <BINDING>`** routes a `resources.secretsStore` name to the store (`secrets-store secrets edit`, value in a body file), so rotation needs no dashboard.
- [ ] **`RESOURCES_KEPT` on purge** for a secret of the app's name that no deploy recorded (today only the recorded ones are looked at).
- [ ] **A live run with a store:** loop-1 has none; once Ben makes one (Secrets Store → Create store on loop-1), run `EVERYAPP_LIVE_PROVISIONERS=1 pnpm -C packages/cli exec vitest run test/provisioners/secrets-store.live.test.ts`; it confirms the secret name rule, the duplicate-name error text and the token permission a deploy needs (Cloudflare documents **Account Secrets Store Edit** to bind a secret).
- [ ] **The hosted deployer's manifest check** doesn't name `resources.secretsStore`; its bundle check refuses the binding type.

### Provisioners: Analytics Engine (2026-10-25)

`resources.analyticsEngine: [{ name: "EVENTS", dataset? }]` binds an Analytics Engine dataset on every Worker of an app, named `<worker>--<binding>` unless the entry names one: nothing created, recorded or purged; Cloudflare has no delete (data expires after 90 days) ([docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md](docs/design/2026-10-provisioners.md), section 2's `analyticsEngine` row; `landing-page/content/agent/manifest.md`, "Analytics Engine").

**Defaults to confirm**

- [ ] **A named `dataset` is used as written,** any name Cloudflare accepts, except one named like another app's: the policy refuses a named dataset that starts with `every-` unless it is `<worker>--<name>`, so two apps share a dataset only under a name of their own choosing. Walk-back: any name Cloudflare accepts.
- [ ] **Declared or refused, and only to the declared dataset.** The policy allows an `analytics-engine-dataset` binding only under a declared binding name and only with the declared (or default) dataset. Walk-back: by type alone.
- [ ] **Binding names under `analyticsEngine` are capped at 63 characters,** so the default `<worker>--<binding>` (Worker names are at most 63) fits Cloudflare's 128. Walk-back: check the installed name instead.
- [ ] **Purge and teardown leave datasets,** the app's default ones included: Cloudflare has no delete. The data expires after 90 days.
- [ ] **Refused on the dispatch tier:** names are account-wide and the writes would bill the platform's account.

**Follow-ups**

- [ ] **The hosted deployer's manifest check** (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/metadata.ts`, `manifestProblems()`) doesn't name `resources.analyticsEngine`; its bundle check refuses the binding type (`EVENTS is a analytics-engine-dataset binding, which third-party apps can't have`).
- [ ] **A Gateway reader for an app's datasets** (the SQL API needs an account token the app doesn't hold): an `analytics` capability, or a page in Admin → Usage. None today.
- [ ] **`everyapp status`'s RESOURCES cell** gains `Analytics Engine` once the first lane's cell is on main.

### Provider docs (2026-10-26)

`/docs/providers`: one page per company, generated from the catalog, the OAuth presets and the sign-in providers by `packages/cli/src/docs/providerPages.ts` (steps in `providerGuides.ts`; drift test `providerPages.test.ts`), plus the hand-written `/docs/providers/custom` and the issue form `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/provider-request.yml`. Defaults the orchestrator took under Ben's standing default; walk any back:

- [ ] **One page per company**, a section for each way to connect (API key, connect accounts, sign in): GitHub's token, `github-oauth` and Sign in with GitHub share `/docs/providers/github`. The other way was one page per catalog entry.
- [ ] **Hand-written steps for the top ~20** (every provider with a setup skill, the four featured apps' needs, the AI providers, Stripe, GitHub, Linear, PostHog, Postmark, SendGrid, Cloudflare). Every other provider gets generic three-step text (sign in, create a key, copy it) under the catalog's docs URL, its hint, the exact commands, and "Steps for this provider are brief; open an issue to request a full guide".
- [ ] **Minor providers get full pages** (Kick, Tumblr, Dribbble, NewsAPI, OpenWeather, the social clients): a thin page over a 404, free from the generator.
- [ ] **The issue form** asks for the provider's name, its API docs, how it authenticates and what you want to do with it, with the label `provider-request` (create the label on the public repo, or the form files issues unlabelled).
- [ ] **Full guides link to GitHub** (`packages/cli/skills/everyapp/setup/<file>.md` on every-app/every-app main): the setup skills aren't mirrored on the site. Mirroring them under /docs/agent/repo is the alternative.
- [ ] **Unverified entries** (`verified: false`: Plaid, Serper) say so on their page. Mastodon's console is instance-relative (`/settings/applications` on the host in `MASTODON_URL`); its page says so.
- [ ] **The social OAuth clients** show as "OAuth client for apps" (the app runs the flow) while they are catalog entries; when lane 5 of Platform shortcuts moves them to presets, `docs:update` turns them into "Connect accounts" sections with no change here.

## Build

- [x] **CLI redesign**, decided 2026-10-15 and built 2026-10-16 as #284 to #296 (`docs/cli-redesign.md` (deleted 2026-10-24; git history at 5ef30882) §8 has the implementation notes). Originally: calm output and pure `--json`; the registry written through D1 with no deploy API, deploy tokens or `login` (ADR-0003 first); the Gateway built from the checkout and found through the account; dev without files; the flat command tree; one routing mode and one naming scheme; the 3-minute lease and the schema-drift check. First, and in an hour: squash the Gateway's and the apps' migration histories to one generated file each, regenerated rather than appended while nothing live exists. No backwards compatibility: thecomeup.cc is reinstalled, not adopted. **Ended 2026-10-19:** everyapp.team runs on the `0000_lyrical_wild_child` baseline, so Gateway migrations now append; `release-migrations.test.ts` freezes the shipped ones (SHIPPED_MIGRATIONS).
- [x] **Deploy from a GitHub release or a local checkout** (designed with Ben 2026-10-19, built the same day as #340 to #344). Two sources, two targets, one artifact: an app or the Gateway deploys from a release tarball its own repository's GitHub Action publishes, or from a local checkout that builds the same tarball; both end in `cf deploy --prebuilt`. What landed, and what each PR left for Ben to confirm or walk back:
  - [x] **Builds are tenant-neutral** (#341): `everyAppWorker()` in production reads no file and builds with the placeholders in `@every-app/sdk/cloudflare/sentinel` (`BUILD_SENTINEL`, `sentinelDeployTarget(manifest)`; perimeter's `HOSTED_DEPLOY_SENTINEL` is gone); `.everyapp/deploy.json` is gone; `everyapp deploy` runs build → `installAppBuildOutput` (`packages/cli/src/lib/app/installBuildOutput.ts`, one pure rewrite of every `worker.config.json`) → the policy check with the real inputs; the hosted deployer keeps its own rewrite (`metadata.ts`). A production build needs `EVERYAPP_BUILD=1` (the CLI sets it), so a bare `cf build` still fails with the "use everyapp deploy" advice. Decide: keep that guard? Keep refusing an app whose own text bindings contain a placeholder string? cf's `--provision` adopts a D1 database by name when a binding has no id (checked in cf 1.0.0-beta.14) and `@cloudflare/config` 0.24.1 makes the id optional, so the rewrite could stop writing ids; the registry owns provisioning, so it still does.
  - [x] **The app release** (#344; format in `packages/cli/src/lib/app/release.ts`): `everyapp-release.tar.gz` = `release.json` (schema 1, `kind: "app"`, `appId`, `version` from the app's package.json, `gitSha`, `builtAt`, `cfVersion`, `cliVersion`, `sdkVersion`, `sha256`, `migrations`, `minCli`, `minSdk`, the evaluated `manifest` with `migrations.dir = "migrations"`) + `.cloudflare/output/v0` + `migrations/`; packed with `COPYFILE_DISABLE=1`, no `.DS_Store`/`._*`, no local state, no Prisma migrations (refused at packing). The Gateway's release keeps its name and `drizzle/`; `release.json` gained optional `kind` and `committedAt`.
  - [x] **`everyapp deploy [<github url> | <tarball>]`** and **`--bundle-only [--out]`** (#344; since stage 4 on 2026-10-24: `everyapp install <source>` and `everyapp release [--out]`): a GitHub repo URL fetches `releases/latest/download/everyapp-release.tar.gz` (or `/releases/tag/<tag>`), anonymously, following redirects (the first redirect names the tag); no release → `NO_RELEASE` with "Clone it, then run: pnpm install && pnpm everyapp deploy" (never builds from a URL); `CLI_TOO_OLD` below `minCli`. A release deploy skips install, build and `checkSchemaDrift`, migrates D1 from `migrations/`, refuses Postgres migrations (`RELEASE_MIGRATIONS_UNSUPPORTED`) and reads `.everyapp/secrets.env` from the current directory. Works through the hosted deploy API too (`packageHostedBundle` takes a Build Output root). Decide: should `--bundle-only` refuse self-host-only flags (ignored today)? Is refusing Postgres releases for self-host fine for now? Follow-up: the registry's `apps` row has no column for a release's `version`/`gitSha` (the step event carries them).
  - [x] **The release workflow** (#344): `starter/.github/workflows/release.yml` (on `v*` tags or by hand: install, `pnpm everyapp deploy --bundle-only` (now `pnpm everyapp release`), `gh release create`/`upload --clobber`), copied by `everyapp create`. It cannot run for an app created outside this checkout until `everyapp` is on npm (the template links the CLI by path); the workflow's comment says so. (template workflow removed 2026-10-24 until the CLI is on npm; see docs/simplify-2026-10-24.md)
  - [x] **The Gateway from GitHub** (#340): `everyapp setup --release <url|file>`; without it, a checkout builds as before, and a CLI with no checkout takes the latest release of `GATEWAY_RELEASE_REPO` (`https://github.com/every-app/every-app`, `packages/cli/src/lib/gateway/release.ts`) for setup and every rebind (`packages/cli/src/lib/gateway/source.ts`), with the downgrade guard ordered by `release.json` and a `minCli` check that says to update everyapp. `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds and publishes the Gateway release on `gateway-v*` tags; this private repository's releases can't be downloaded anonymously, so the default only works once the public repository is synced (`.github/RELEASE.md`). Decide: is it fine that a no-checkout CLI always rebinds with the latest release, so it refuses when the live Gateway is newer? GitHub's `latest` is one release per repository: app releases must live in their own repositories, or the Gateway's would be hidden. No signing: HTTPS plus the tarball's SHA-256 only proves the files match each other. `create` still needs a checkout for its template. (CLI surface removed 2026-10-24; see docs/hosted-vision.md)
  - [x] **Hosted install** (#342): Admin → Apps → "Install from GitHub" (`POST /api/admin/apps/install`, `GET /api/admin/deploys/:id`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/{release,tar,install,run}.ts`): the gateway downloads the release (100 MB cap, gzip bomb refused), reads the tar with its own reader (no links, no `..`), checks `release.json` and its hash, refuses helper Workers, then registers and deploys through the existing pipeline, policy check before the row is written. Behind the same gate as the deploy API (hosted + `THIRD_PARTY_DEPLOYS=on`); the admin's role is read live; `deployed_by_actor` is the admin's email; new `deploy_jobs.source` column (`0001_deploy_job_source.sql`; #342 first regenerated the baseline, which broke `setup` on everyapp.team, so the fix restored it and appended). Not yet opened in a browser. Decide: public releases only? keep the gate? the column? should installing an app id the organization already has redeploy it from the release?
  - [ ] **Unpause npm publishing** (the item under "Paused while we work from the checkout"): the template's release workflow and the no-checkout Gateway install both wait on it. Not done without Ben's go: publishing is outward-facing.
- [ ] **Teams roadmap**, ranked 2026-10-23 (`docs/design/2026-10-teams.md` section 5; the slice's defaults are under "Teams" in Decide; agent-days of what remains). It folds the earlier lines "Approvals by browser link, then show destructive tools again", "An "Agent activity" page over the `mcp_tool_calls` audit table", "Access levels for personal access tokens" and "Owner recovery link and ownership transfer".
  - [ ] Finish the usage lane: OpenRouter `usage.cost`, `include_usage` (with its extra final chunk; see Counting rules in Decide), the user id on the usage line, a `cancel()` handler on the tap (0.5).
  - [ ] Launch docs sentence on provider spend; the leaver checklist, the deploy role, the log reader's Cloudflare role and "SSO means self-host" if a teams page is written (0.25).
  - [x] An "Agent activity" page over the `mcp_tool_calls` audit table at `/admin/activity` (#434, merged 2026-10-24 as `4451b350`; defaults under "Admin → Activity" in Decide).
  - [ ] Access levels for personal access tokens: a member-reachable Tokens page with MCP-only and full presets, a 30-day default, `token.created` and `token.revoked` logged (1, held).
  - [ ] Admin action log lines with the actor: `access.granted`, `access.revoked`, `app.default_changed`, the group events (0.5).

## Check on the first live deploy

Everything below needs a real Cloudflare account. The rest was checked locally: fake-cf end-to-end tests, `cf dev` smoke tests, and offline `cf deploy --prebuilt --dry-run`.

- [ ] `cf auth whoami` with several accounts, and the CI path with `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`.
- [ ] The real error text when an account has no workers.dev subdomain, even with workers.dev off. The CLI matches on that text.
- [x] A brand-new Worker accepts a secrets file holding the PEM keys, and its first version has the required secrets. Checked 2026-10-23: `every-app-gateway` was created by `setup` on 2026-10-16 with `JWT_PRIVATE_KEY`/`JWT_PUBLIC_KEY` among its secrets and the public key in the install row; whether the very first version already had every required secret isn't on record (deployments list reaches back to 2026-10-19).
- [ ] Remote `cf d1 migrations apply` doesn't prompt and prints the expected output. Check `cf d1 time-travel get-bookmark` output, and that the printed restore command works. (Two of three clauses proven by 2026-10-23: four Gateway migrations applied unattended by setup and upgrade runs between 2026-10-16 and 2026-10-20. The bookmark output and a live `db restore` (#360), the safety net this item is about, have no record.)
- [ ] Several SQL statements in one `cf d1 query --body @file` call run atomically. The lease, setup link and install record rely on it.
- [ ] The shape and order of `cf workers deployments list`, and who a `cf deploy` is attributed to. A cf deploy must not trigger the "edited in the dashboard" abort on the next deploy; a real dashboard edit must. (Three of four clauses proven by 2026-10-23: `deployments[]` newest first with `source: cf_cli`, `author_email` and `annotations.workers/triggered_by`; six consecutive deploys and secret versions on `every-app-gateway` raised no strict-mode abort. Whether a real dashboard edit triggers it has no record.)
- [ ] Source map size limits and Issues symbolication. Logs and traces show up in the dashboard.
- [ ] Rate limit namespace ids 4180 and 4181 don't collide with others in the account, and the limits behave in production.
- [ ] `cf workers delete <name>`, and the D1 and KV deletes behind `--purge`.
- [ ] Activation latency: from the Gateway deploy that binds a new app to the first request that makes it `active` in D1, against the live wait's 10 minutes, including a new hostname's certificate; and that a Cloudflare Access redirect is told from the Gateway's own answers. (Latency proven by 2026-10-23: all six apps went `active` with certificate packs issued about 25 seconds after upload. The Access-redirect branch of the wait has never run live: no app deploy since Access went in front on 2026-10-23; the first one will exercise it.)

### Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/6 extensibility: bundled specs

`everyapp deploy` and `install` register the provider specs an app bundles (`.everyapp/providers/<id>.json`; a release carries them in `release.json` as `providers`) after one explicit confirmation, so a third-party app brings its own provider. Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **One confirmation per deploy, `--yes` for agents, refused under `--json` without it** (`CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`, the existing takeover shape; `data.providers` lists what would be registered). The same `--yes` also installs a Gateway when there is none. Alternative: a flag of its own (`--register-providers`).
- [ ] **A row with a different spec stops the deploy (`PROVIDER_SPEC_DIFFERS`) before anything is registered**; a deploy never replaces a row (replacing unbinds the key). The person runs `provider add <file> --replace` or deletes the file. Alternative: `--yes` also replaces.
- [ ] **Specs compare canonically** (keys sorted at every level): a row that says the same thing however it was written is left alone, silently noted on `app.providers`.
- [ ] **A catalog id in a bundled file is a warning and the file is ignored** (`PROVIDER_BUNDLED_BUILTIN`), never a refusal: an app written against a provider that later joined the catalog keeps deploying.
- [ ] **Specs ride in `release.json` (`providers`, 50 at most), never as a `.everyapp/` directory in the tarball**, so neither release reader needs a new entry and the browser installer (the hosted deploy API) ignores them for now: browser installs register nothing (a follow-up once it has a confirmation step).
- [ ] **Registration happens before the manifest check**, so an app whose manifest lists the bundled provider deploys in one go; the rows are the login's (`added_by`), exactly as `provider add` writes them, and the keys are set next (the setup warning names them).
### Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/6 extensibility: the ai spec kind

A new OpenAI-compatible model vendor is a registered spec (`mode: "ai"`), not a catalog release: `baseUrl`, the key's placement, `models`, `defaultModel`, `embeddingsModel`, `routes`, `cost.responseField`. Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **The row names its `models` and the proxy enforces them** (`model_not_allowed` for any other, the default route included); a catalog entry has no list. Alternative: the list as documentation only.
- [ ] **Default routes are chat (and embeddings with an embeddings model) alone**; a row lists more (`/v1/completions`, `/v1/audio/speech`) explicitly. Files, batches and fine-tunes stay closed: a row never gets `extraRoutes`.
- [ ] **A registered `ai` row may be the Gateway's default model provider** (`everyapp ai default <id>`, Admin → Usage, the tools), and the automatic order considers it after every catalog entry, only once its key is set and bound. Alternative: catalog entries only.
- [ ] **AI Gateway never fronts a row** (no `aig` slug): calls go direct, with the row's key.
- [ ] **The key is bound to the base URL and auth**, as an API-key row's is to its hosts: a changed `baseUrl` unbinds it (`ai_provider_not_configured` until `secret set` again).
- [ ] **`deploy` refuses an id under `providers` the Gateway doesn't offer as a model provider** (`CAPABILITY_UNKNOWN`, naming what it offers); until now an unknown `providers` id passed the deploy and failed at the first call.
- [ ] **The `models` field was added to `AiProviderCapability`** (a type in `capabilities.ts`, lane 5's file; additive, no catalog entry sets it) and the SDK's dev fallback resolves an `ai` row from `.everyapp/providers/<id>.json` (`devAiProvider(name, env)`, one line in lane 3's `gatewayFetch.ts`).
### Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/6 extensibility

Lane 6 of the platform shortcuts swath (the brief in the coordinator thread): three things an app needed a platform release for, now registrable. HELD: egress and OAuth; Ben reviews the PRs. Defaults taken; confirm or change each.

- [ ] **Scope-set overlays extend the three built-in clients with scope sets** (`google-workspace`, `microsoft-365`, `slack`) and nothing else: a registered OAuth provider's sets are in its spec (`provider add --replace`), the social-network clients have no sets (the whole client is the grant). Alternative: overlays on registered rows too.
- [ ] **An overlay adds, never replaces or widens a built-in set.** A name the client has is `SCOPE_SET_BUILTIN`; changing a built-in set stays a reviewed catalog change. Alternative: let an overlay shadow a built-in set.
- [ ] **An overlay must name `routes` (on the client's `api` hosts alone) and say `access` outright**; a set with no routes unlocks nothing on the held-token path, so it is refused rather than accepted empty. A write set is never inferred from its scope strings.
- [ ] **The client's authorize scopes grow by the overlay's**, so the operator's OAuth client at the console must be allowed them and every person connects again to grant them; the CLI, the dialog and the tool say so. The binding key excludes the sets, so an uploaded client stays live. Alternative: a per-set consent (`sets=` on `/connect`) only, leaving `authorize.scopes` as the catalog has it.
- [ ] **Local development doesn't know an overlay set**: `everyapp dev` reaches a built-in client from the catalog alone, so an app listing `gmail.send` gets `integration_scopes_undeclared` locally until the SDK's dev fallback reads overlays (a follow-up: `provider show <client> --save` writing the effective client, and the dev path preferring the file). The local validator accepts a well-formed name (`unknownCapabilities: "accept"`); `deploy` checks it against the Gateway.
- [ ] **Removal is refused while a deployed app lists the set** unless `--force` (`SCOPE_SET_IN_USE`, the apps named); the UI and the tool never force. Connections keep their tokens; calls under the set answer `integration_scopes_undeclared`.
- [ ] **The hosted deploy API checks scope sets against the Gateway's registry** (overlays included) rather than the catalog alone (`manifestProblems` takes a lookup; `parseManifest`, `bundleProblems` and the pipeline's `DeployTarget.lookup` thread it).
- [ ] **Events:** an add or remove emits `provider.updated` with `change: "scope_sets"` and `scopeSet: { name, action }` rather than a type of its own.
- [ ] **Migration 0019 (`provider_scope_sets`)** was generated with drizzle-kit, which named it 0017 because the journal's indices lag the file names (earlier migrations were renumbered by hand); renamed to 0019 with its journal tag and snapshot. Regenerate against main at merge time.

## RepoLens port (2026-10-26)

Found while putting RepoLens on everyapp.team with its old Wrangler D1 history (every-app/private-repolens #3).

- [x] The setup gate also blocks `public` routes: with a required secret unset, RepoLens's badge route answers 503 `app_needs_setup`, so a badge embedded in a README breaks. Decided 2026-10-26 (platform shortcuts L7, PR 2): public routes bypass the gate.
  Default taken (#754, HELD): A route the manifest declares public is served while required settings are unset, and the app's own code answers for missing config (the SDK's `integrationStatus`/refusals say what isn't set). The alternative, keeping the 503, broke webhooks and badges and protected nothing: a public route takes no credential, and the gate's dialog redirect helps nobody there. The hook's `publicRoute` input is gone.
- [x] A new app's first deploy rebinds the Gateway from the checkout it runs in, which silently upgrades the Gateway to that checkout. Done 2026-10-26 (setup-from-release stack, PR 3): the rebind redeploys the live Gateway's own release (its GitHub tag, URL, file, or this checkout's build cache for the live tree); when it can't, it says so and `gateway.upgrade` names the change, even for the same version. A held rollout no longer needs a detached worktree once the live tree is in the cache; `update` is the verb that moves the Gateway.
- [x] The published `everyapp` CLI (0.3.0-rc.3) cannot do a first deploy: the rebind fails with `CHECKOUT_MISSING`. Done 2026-10-26 (setup-from-release stack, PRs 2 and 4): the Gateway comes from the published release for the CLI's version, and `create` works from the npm package too.
- `cf` has no `d1 export`/`import`; the thread used the D1 REST API with cf's stored token. Worth a `everyapp db export|import --app` so history moves without raw API calls.
- No way to adopt an existing D1 into the ledger (a claimed row would be purged by `delete --purge`); export + import is the documented path.

An owner or admin turns off **Available to everyone** in Admin → Apps (and removes member grants). Members can see MRR and Stripe customer emails until then.

The dashboard still shows the buttons to members, who get "Only owners and admins can add, remove or capture repositories" when they click. Hide them (a `canManage` flag on `listRepos`) if that reads badly.

A repository that turns private keeps a cached badge for up to an hour (`max-age=3600`), and loses it at the next capture.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 2 sdk-surface

New in the SDK: `gatewayUrl()` and `EveryAppEnv` (`/server`), `defineEveryApp` and `EveryAppManifest` (the package root), `env.EVERYAPP_MANIFEST`, `@every-app/sdk/testing`, `@every-app/sdk/tanstack-start`. The starter, `patterns.md` and the build skill use them (#778). The npm `create` path needs the next rc, which Ben publishes after the starter PRs. The apps drop their copies in phase 2, on the next rc. Defaults taken for Ben:

- [ ] **`gatewayUrl(env, path?)`, not `gatewayUrl(env, request?)`.** `everyapp dev` now sets `GATEWAY_URL` to its launcher, so no request is needed to guess the dev host. The second argument is a path (`gatewayUrl(env, "/admin/users")` for inviting people). Without `everyapp dev` (a bare `cf dev`), it is `http://localhost:8787`.
- [ ] **The manifest reaches the Worker as a JSON binding, `EVERYAPP_MANIFEST`, in dev and deployed.** `cf workers types` types it from the file. This replaces the starter's Vite `define`. The four apps' manifests are 1.0–2.3 KB as JSON, under Cloudflare's 5 KB per-variable limit. A manifest past it would fail the upload; nothing checks for that before.
- [ ] **The SDK's `EveryAppManifest` is written by hand.** The zod schema stays in `@every-app/perimeter`. Moving it into the SDK would have collided with lanes 5 and 8. A type test (`packages/perimeter/src/manifest/sdkManifest.test.ts`) fails when a field is added to one and not the other. The perimeter's own `defineEveryApp` is deleted.
- [ ] **`GATEWAY_ERROR_HEADER` is public in `/server`; `IDENTITY_HEADER` is public only in `/testing`.** Production code that reads the identity header goes through lane 1's `currentUser()` instead.
- [ ] **`@tanstack/react-start` is an optional peer dependency of the SDK.** That is what makes TanStack Start compile `ensureUserMiddleware` like the app's own code, so its server half stays out of the browser bundle (checked on the starter's build).
- [ ] **`callTool()` defaults to every MCP scope** (`mcp:read`, `mcp:write`, `mcp:destructive`), so any tool can be called. A test passes fewer scopes to check a refusal.

## Setup from a published release (2026-10-26)

Ben, launch morning: "currently this requires cloning every-app in the setup prompt. Should we just have them install the everyapp CLI and have that deploy the release?" Built as a stack (PRs #743 Gateway Release workflow, #745 CLI resolution order + install-row origin + status, #746 rebind from the live release, #747 the starter inside the npm package, this TODO/threat-model PR, then the setup-prompt flip, held). Defaults taken, to confirm or change:

- [ ] **A Gateway release per CLI version, on a tag, not on every push to main.** `gateway-v<version>` is pushed when `everyapp@<version>` is published (`.github/RELEASE.md`: npm first, then the tag on the public repository, then `npx everyapp@<version> status` from a no-checkout directory). The app repositories release on every push because an app release has no version coupling; the Gateway has (`minCli`/`minSdk`, the schema, the SDK contract). A rolling `gateway-main` prerelease for people who want main is a workflow_dispatch away if wanted.
- [ ] **Prerelease tags are GitHub prereleases**, so `/releases/latest` is the newest stable one. A CLI at an rc with no exact tag falls back to the latest stable with `GATEWAY_RELEASE_LATEST`; with no stable release at all it stops (`RELEASE_NOT_FOUND`, fix: `--release` or a checkout). So the first tag Ben pushes should be the one matching whatever is `latest` on npm.
- [ ] **release.json's `version` is the tag's** (`build-release.ts --version`), and `minCli`/`minSdk` are the workspace's CLI and SDK versions at that commit, not constants. The Gateway package is 0.3.0 now; keep it on the CLI's version, prerelease suffix aside.
- [ ] **The rebind never upgrades the Gateway silently.** It redeploys the live release when it can be had again; otherwise what `setup` would deploy, announced. This changes one contributor habit: deploying an app from a newer checkout no longer upgrades the Gateway when the live tree is still in the build cache (three builds are kept); `pnpm everyapp update` does.
- [ ] **A replaced release asset** (`GATEWAY_RELEASE_DIFFERS`) was a warning in #746; agreed with lane 4 (cli-rebind) on 2026-10-26 that its follow-up PR makes it a refusal, like every case where the live release can't be had again (`GATEWAY_RELEASE_UNAVAILABLE`, fix: `everyapp update`). Consequence: a Gateway installed before #745 has no recorded origin, so one `update` must run before its next app deploy (a step of the next everyapp.team rollout).
- [ ] **`create` from the npm package pins `@every-app/sdk` and `everyapp` at the CLI's own version** (the two publish together). An app made from a checkout still links by path. The template is packed into the package at `build` (`packages/cli/scripts/bundle-starter.mjs`), so `pnpm pack-for-app --npm` and a publish carry it.
- [ ] **`CHECKOUT_MISSING` is gone.** Nothing needs a checkout any more; the agent docs and the setup skill drop the row.
- [ ] **Trust in a downloaded release** rests on TLS to github.com and push access to every-app/every-app (threat model B7, gap added). Wanted later: a detached checksum or a Sigstore signature on the Release that the CLI verifies before opening, and the publishing commit pinned in it.
- [ ] **Ben, before the flip:** publish `@every-app/sdk` at the same version as `everyapp` (today npm has `everyapp@0.3.0-rc.4` but the SDK stops at rc.3), move `latest` to it, flip every-app/every-app public with the Gateway Release workflow on it, push `gateway-v<that version>` there. Then the loop-1 run from a fresh temp dir (`npx everyapp@<version> setup`, create, deploy, install, update, status) and the flip PR.
- [ ] Follow-ups: `everyapp update` could say the tag it is about to take before downloading (it says it after: "Using Gateway 0.3.0-rc.4 from every-app/every-app release gateway-v0.3.0-rc.4"); a `--release` on `deploy` for a held rollout that wants a specific Gateway; the launch doc's clone-based checks (section D) become `npx`-based once the flip lands.

Any other machine that can reach a live account runs `echo <account id> >> ~/.config/everyapp/forbidden-accounts` once; the loop's preflight warns on its `account` line while the list is empty.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 3 fromMcpTools

Defaults taken in `fromMcpTools()` (SDK, `packages/sdk/src/server/fromMcpTools.ts`):

- [ ] **Risk from annotations:** `readOnlyHint: true` is `read`, `destructiveHint: true` is `destructive`, anything else `write`. MCP's own default would make an unannotated tool destructive (hidden from AI clients). Apps override with `risk(tool)`.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/9 create-release (release workflow, pack-for-app)

- **release workflow** (`everyapp release --publish`; the app repositories' `release.yml` is now ~30 lines, one step). Defaults taken for Ben:
  - [ ] The publish logic lives in the CLI, not a reusable workflow or composite action: this repository's Actions access level is `none`, and third-party developers could never call one from a private repository anyway. The repository setting was left alone.
  - [ ] It talks to the GitHub REST API with `fetch` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` (or `GH_TOKEN`), not `gh`. The tag check is an API lookup, so the checkout no longer needs `fetch-depth: 0`.
  - [ ] A failed asset upload deletes the release it just created, so a rerun starts clean.
  - [ ] The default branch comes from the event payload; `main` when it can't be read (only for a hand-run `--dry-run`).
  - [ ] `--dry-run` implies `--publish`; it still does the read-only tag lookup.
  - [ ] Release notes say `everyapp install https://github.com/<repo>` (no `pnpm`, no "from your EveryApp checkout").
  - [ ] Two new codes: `GITHUB_RUN_MISSING` (not in Actions, or no token) and `RELEASE_PUBLISH_FAILED` (retryable on 5xx/429).
  - [ ] `--publish` ships in the next rc. The four app repositories pinned to `0.3.0-rc.3` keep their 118-line workflow until their pin moves; then `pnpm pack-for-app <app> --npm <rc> --workflow` swaps it in.
  - [ ] `everyapp create` writes `.github/workflows/release.yml` into every app made outside the checkout (`starter/.github/workflows/release.yml` is now the one template, `pack-for-app --workflow` copies it). An app created inside the checkout (`apps/<name>`, `workspace:*`) gets none: it's not a repository of its own. Every other app, on npm ranges with a committed lockfile (#768), gets it.
- **pack-for-app** (npm only). Defaults taken for Ben:
  - [ ] The vendor mode is gone: `--npm <version>` is required, `--no-build` went with it. Nothing on main used it; Mission Control, its last user, moved to rc.3 (lane 10). An app that needs something unpublished waits for an rc.
  - [ ] `--npm` still deletes `vendor/everyapp-*.tgz`, `vendor/every-app-sdk-*.tgz` and `vendor/README.md` (and an emptied `vendor/`), so apps migrate off them.
  - [ ] The historical records (launch doc, the "App releases (2026-10-24)" summary) still describe the tarballs as they were; only live guidance changed.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 3 binding shims

`gatewayAiBinding(env)` and `gatewayEmailBinding(env)` in `@every-app/sdk/server`. Defaults taken for Ben:

- [ ] **`gatewayAiBinding` goes to `workers-ai` unless told otherwise**, so `@cf/…` ids work as they are. `provider: "default"` sends an `@cf/…` id as `default` (the operator's model). It runs over the OpenAI-compatible routes, because the Gateway keeps Workers AI's native `/run/<model>` closed: text generation, streaming and embeddings only; images, audio, batches and the binding's other methods throw.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 5 catalog

Default models (PR "Default AI models become a Gateway setting"). Defaults taken for Ben:

- [ ] **Shape: an `org_settings.ai_models` column (migration 0019), not Gateway env settings.** JSON `{ "<provider>": { chat?, embeddings? } }` beside `ai_default`, cached with it, written by `everyapp ai model`, `everyapp_ai_model` and the model in `ai default <provider>/<model>`. Env settings (`<PROVIDER>_CHAT_MODEL`) would need a secret write per change and the generated settings lists.
- [ ] **`ai_default` now holds the provider alone.** A stored `provider/model` from before reads as the provider; its model is dropped (the seed serves). Re-run `ai default <provider>/<model>` on everyapp.team if one was set.

- [ ] **`ai default off` keeps the model settings;** `ai default <provider>` without a model keeps that provider's model setting.

- [ ] **No UI for embeddings or other providers' models yet.** The AI default dialog still sets the chosen provider's chat model only. Add a per-provider models editor if Ben wants it on the page.
- [ ] **`ai.model_changed` is a new event** (v1); `ai default <provider>/<model>` fires `ai.default_changed` only.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/9 create-release

- **dev token** (`everyapp dev token`, replaces the inbox's `scripts/mint-dev-identity.mjs`). Defaults taken for Ben:
  - [ ] `--role` defaults to `owner`. Without `--user`, an owner token is the seeded dev user (`dev-user`). An admin or member token is a separate person (`dev-admin`, `admin@everyapp.localhost`), as in the inbox script, so the seeded owner keeps its role in apps that mirror users. With `--user`, the sub is `dev-<email>`.
  - [ ] `--ttl` defaults to 1 hour, and 24 hours is the most it allows. The gateway-lite's own tokens last 120 s, and the SDK puts no cap on lifetime.
  - [ ] The token is a full web session: `chan: "web"`, `scopes: ["*"]`. It opens no tools, because `/__everyapp/tools/call` only takes the ToolBus's `tc` token. Tools stay on `<app>.localhost:8787/mcp`.
  - [ ] Scripts send it straight to the app's internal port, which the ready block now prints as `App port`. The gateway-lite keeps replacing any inbound identity header, so the perimeter is unchanged.
  - [ ] `dev` became a route group whose default command runs the app (stricli `defaultCommand`, a hidden `run` route). `everyapp dev --help` and the CLI reference still show it as `everyapp dev`. `everyapp dev run` also works.
  - [ ] The patterns.md row pointing scripts at `dev token` waits for the flip, in its own PR.
- **skills update** (`pnpm everyapp skills update`, `packages/cli/src/commands/skills`). Defaults taken for Ben:
  - [ ] Only the skills the CLI ships (`everyapp`, `build-every-app`) are touched; any other directory under `.agents/skills` or `.claude/skills` is left alone, so a skill the CLI stops shipping stays in old apps until someone deletes it (no retired-names list yet).
  - [ ] A copy that differs is replaced whole (local edits to a shipped skill are lost; the app's own skills go under other names, as the build-every-app skill now says). An identical copy is not rewritten. A `.claude/skills/<name>` symlink becomes a plain copy, as `create` makes.
  - [ ] It also adds AGENTS.md lines for skills AGENTS.md doesn't mention (the same code as `create`).
  - [ ] No `--check`/dry-run flag, and neither `status` nor `update` mentions it (they work on the Gateway, not the app). Add a `--check` that exits non-zero on drift if an app repo wants it in CI.
  - [ ] The app repos (Mission Control's ~7k stale lines, OpenSEO, CRM, analytics, inbox, RepoLens) still need `skills update` run and committed: phase 2.
- gateway zod 4: `apps/every-app-gateway` is on `zod ^4.2.0`, and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` overrides `zod: ^4.6.7`, so the lockfile carries one zod 4 (4.6.7; the 4.6.5 and knip's 4.1.12 copies are gone). Defaults taken for Ben:
  - [ ] **One zod 3 copy remains, on purpose:** `"@shadcn/registry>zod": ^3.25.76`. The shadcn component CLI (a gateway devDependency, run by hand, never bundled) asks for `zod ^3.24.1`; forcing it to 4 was untested. Drop the scoped line when shadcn moves to zod 4, or if nobody runs `shadcn add` from here.
  - [ ] **Code changes were small:** `z.nativeEnum` became `z.enum` (one use), and the MCP-app form's `optionalText` moved `.optional()` after its `.transform()` (zod 4 made the transformed key required in the inferred type). The other deprecated-but-working idioms (`z.string().email()`/`.uuid()`, `.merge()`, `z.ZodIssueCode.custom`, `.strict()`) stay, as in #547. Every `.default()` value is already a valid output, so zod 4's no-parse defaults change nothing. Server-function validation goes through Standard Schema, so the wire error is unchanged.
- create deps (#768):
  - [ ] **#768 merges last, right before the rc.5 publish.** Until rc.5 is on npm, `create` outside the checkout fails its install. Built CLI on main stays green across that window: it creates its app with `EVERYAPP_CREATE_LINK=1` and never installs `^0.3.0-rc.5` from npm. After the publish, run one `create` from the published `npx everyapp@0.3.0-rc.5` as a smoke test.
  - [ ] **rc.6 bump merges right before the rc.6 publish** (2026-10-26): `PUBLISHED_VERSION` → 0.3.0-rc.6 so new apps pin the CLI that carries `release --publish` (#780), the starter release.yml (#792) and the new starter (#756/#778). After the publish, smoke `npx everyapp@0.3.0-rc.6 create`.
  - [ ] **Ranges, not exact pins.** An app made outside the checkout, by a checkout's everyapp or one installed from npm, gets `"@every-app/sdk": "^0.3.0-rc.5"` and `"everyapp": "^0.3.0-rc.5"`, and its first commit holds the `pnpm-lock.yaml` its install wrote (the starter's `.gitignore` no longer ignores it). The lockfile pins what it got; the caret lets `pnpm update` take the next rc or 0.3.0, never 0.4.0.
  - [ ] **One constant, set before publishing.** Both packages now publish together at one version, `PUBLISHED_VERSION` in `packages/cli/src/lib/app/publishedVersions.ts`, not read from the CLI's package.json (which says 0.3.0 while the rcs go out under `next`). `.github/RELEASE.md` step 1 says to set it to the version about to be published; a test fails when the checkout moves to a new base version (0.4.0) without it. Until rc.5 is on npm, a fresh `create` outside the checkout fails its install.
  - [ ] **No `--link` flag; one test variable.** Platform work on unreleased SDK or CLI changes makes the app in the checkout's `apps/<name>` (still `workspace:*`). `EVERYAPP_CREATE_LINK=1`, from a checkout, links the checkout's packages by path into an app made outside it; the built-CLI test sets it, so CI never installs the range from npm (it would be red between merging a bump and publishing). The cost: an app made beside the checkout runs on the last published rc, so a starter change that needs an unreleased SDK export breaks new apps until that rc is published.
  - [ ] **No lockfile in `starter/` here.** The starter is a member of this repository's pnpm workspace, so its install goes to the root lockfile; a `starter/pnpm-lock.yaml` would never be written by pnpm and would drift. Each created app writes and commits its own.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 3 optional and base URLs

Defaults taken for `{ optional: true }`, `aiBaseUrl()` and `providerBaseUrl()` (`packages/sdk/src/server/ai.ts`, `gatewayFetch.ts`, `integrationFetch.ts`):

- [ ] **`optional: true` only covers "unavailable"** (no binding, and in dev no settings in `.dev.vars`). A binding that isn't one, a wrong provider id and an attribution mistake still throw: those are bugs, not setup.
- [ ] **`aiBaseUrl(provider)` comes from the catalog's chat route**, minus `/chat/completions`. A provider without an OpenAI-compatible chat route (Cohere), or one registered only on a Gateway, throws, and the error points at `providerBaseUrl(id)`.
- [ ] **`GatewayFetchEnv` is no longer exported.** `env` is typed `object` on every factory, so apps drop their `env as GatewayFetchEnv` casts in the phase-2 slim-downs.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 3 setup info

Defaults taken for setup info on statuses and refusals, and for `aiStatus()` (`apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/setup-info.ts`, `packages/sdk/src/server/refusal.ts`):

- [ ] **`settings` names every setting the capability needs, not only the unset ones.** The Gateway can't always tell which of a pair is missing (an OAuth client resolved from D1, email's sender plus transport). `configure` asks only for what is unset.
- [ ] **`command` is null for a shared app and on a hosted Gateway.** The sentence then says the Gateway's operator sets it. Elsewhere it is `pnpm everyapp configure <app>` (`SETUP_CLI`, until publishing resumes).
- [ ] **Email names `EMAIL_FROM` and `EMAIL_FROM_NAME` only.** The sentence no longer lists the transports or the runbook path; `configure` asks for a transport.
- [ ] **`GET /api/ai/<provider>` (the bare path) is now the AI status.** Before, it was proxied to the provider's root. It is never counted or capped. The default resolves on the organization's choice when `for` says whose; it falls back to the order alone when attribution fails.
- [ ] **A status for something the app doesn't ask for carries no setup info**, as `configured` already said nothing.
- [ ] **`refusalFromError()` reads local dev's missing-key `ConfigurationError` as a 503 refusal**, `ai_provider_not_configured` or `ai_default_not_configured`, with the `.dev.vars` names and a null command.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 8 manifest settings

Per-install plain settings (`settings` in the manifest). Defaults taken; confirm or change:

- [ ] **Held as Worker secrets.** A setting's value is a `secret_text` binding on the app's Workers, not a `plain_text` var. It takes the secrets' path (`configure`, `secret set`, leases, `apps.secret_names`, kept by deploys) with no new storage or migration. The catch: the values aren't readable back, so `status` and Admin show set/unset and what the setting takes, never the value.
- [ ] **`why` is required** on a setting (it's optional on a secret): whoever installs the app gets asked for it.
- [ ] **Four types:** `text`, `url` (http or https), `number`, `boolean` (`true`/`false`), plus `values` for a text enum. No regex patterns, no min/max.
- [ ] **No `anyOf` or `setup` on settings.** Add them if an app asks.
- [ ] **`secret set NAME` sets a declared setting too,** checked against its rule. `configure` is still the documented path.
- [ ] **Provider name clashes.** The `PROVIDER_SECRET_TAKEN` checks (`provider add`, the gateway's provider routes; lane 6's files) still read `secrets` only. A provider registered later could take a setting's name. Follow-up: switch them to `appWorkerNames()`.
- [ ] **Defaults are written by the deploy** (the stacked after-flip PR). Each deploy sends the `default` of every setting the app's Worker doesn't hold yet. A value someone set is never replaced, and there's no "reset to default" verb: set it again. In dev, `everyAppWorker()` binds an unset setting with a default as text.
- [ ] **`install` asks `Set up <app> now?` (Yes by default)** at a terminal when the app needs setup, then runs `configure <app>`.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/L7 gateway-runtime

Defaults taken for Ben in the gateway-runtime lane (identity token claims, the setup gate, the scope rule on app routes, WebSocket lifetime). HELD PRs: Ben merges.

- [ ] **`org_name` is optional in the identity token** (PR 1). `EveryAppUser.orgName` is `string | undefined`: the claim is minted when the authenticator read the name (every production path does) and dropped when malformed, so an app shows `user.orgName ?? "your organization"`. Required would have made every test token and every third-party minter carry a name for a display-only field. The dev gateway's seeded organization is called `Dev organization`. A rename reaches apps within the 30 s membership cache, like a role change.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 8 public assets

Manifest `publicAssets`: static files of the asset bundle, forwarded with no identity and no marker. HELD for Ben (public routes). Defaults taken:

- [ ] **A member gets no identity on a `publicAssets` path.** The Gateway reads no session there, so a page under such a glob renders signed out. The docs say to keep pages and APIs out of these globs. The alternative (auth-optional, like `public`) would hand a member's identity to Worker code on an "assets" path.
- [ ] **A `public` route wins over a `publicAssets` pattern** on the same path (auth-optional, marker for anonymous visitors).
- [ ] **No `Authorization` is forwarded on an asset path**, not even an app's own bearer, and an `epat_` token there is dropped unchecked rather than failing closed (the response is open to anyone anyway).
- [ ] **The setup gate treats a public asset like a public route:** since lane 7 (main), public routes and public assets stay up while the app needs setup.
- [ ] **At most 25 patterns**, the size of an app's Access destination (shared with `public` routes and `/mcp`).
- [ ] **Patterns may use `:name`, `*` and `**` like `public`**, so `/*.png` and `/:id/og.png` are allowed. Only the catch-alls and patterns covering `/mcp` (such as `/:page`) are refused; a broad pattern like `/**/*.png` is allowed.
- [ ] Follow-up: `deploy` warns when a `publicAssets` pattern matches no file in the built asset directory. Not built here.
- [ ] Follow-up (phase 2): open-analytics moves its eight icon/asset `public` routes to `publicAssets` and drops the re-encoding in `server.ts`.

## Platform shortcuts (2026-10-26)/lane 5 catalog: social clients to presets

HELD for Ben (OAuth clients, redirect URIs, reserved setting names). Defaults taken:

- [ ] **Eleven social networks are presets now**, tagged `social`: linkedin, facebook, instagram, threads, youtube, reddit, pinterest, discord, kick, twitch, tumblr. A gateway reserves their setting names only once an operator adds one (`everyapp provider add --oauth linkedin`). They run the Gateway-owned flow (`/connect/<id>/callback`), not Postiz's app-driven one. None has been tried against a live connect; their hints say so.
- [ ] **Four networks were dropped**, because a preset can't express them: TikTok (`client_key` instead of `client_id`), TikTok Business (a JSON token body), Mastodon (a per-instance host, `MASTODON_URL`) and Dribbble (the client in the query string). Bring them back as presets if lane 6's spec kinds grow those features.
- [ ] **The machinery only they used is deleted**: query and JSON client injection, renamed client fields, RegExp token paths, an OAuth client's `hostSetting`/`defaultOrigin`, `origin` on the integration status, and the Meta token-exchange grant types. Token requests are form-encoded, the client in the form or in HTTP Basic.
- [ ] **Scope sets chosen for the presets** (identity, posts.write, pages.write, …) are my cut of Postiz's scope lists; `r_basicprofile` (LinkedIn) and `youtubepartner` (YouTube) were left out. PKCE is off where the provider's web flow doesn't take it (LinkedIn, Meta's, Reddit, Pinterest, Twitch, Tumblr).
- [ ] **Short-lived tokens:** Facebook, Instagram and Threads issue no refresh token and the Gateway doesn't do their long-lived exchange, so a person reconnects every hour or two. Fine while nothing uses them.
- [ ] **Redirect paths come from each app's manifest** (`capabilities.<id>.redirectPaths`), no longer from the catalog. `everyapp provider show <id>` prints them on each app's host. Apps that run Google, Microsoft or Slack sign-in themselves (OpenSEO `/integrations/google/callback`, OpenCRM `/integrations/google/callback`, `/integrations/microsoft/callback`, `/integrations/slack/callback`) should add the field in phase 2; nothing breaks before then, the field is only the operator's checklist.
- [ ] **Egress:** a dispatched app is still refused every preset's token endpoint, added or not (`outbound.ts`), as it was for the catalog clients.
- [ ] **An API-key provider's host setting stays hidden** (`SHOPIFY_HOST`, `SUPABASE_HOST`, …): `everyapp secret` treats it like a credential, never shown back. Only the OAuth clients' host settings were plain, and with Mastodon gone there are none (#767 added `OAUTH_CLIENT_HOST_SETTINGS`; this PR deletes it once #767 is in).
- [ ] **After the flip (low priority, from the provider-add thread, #761):** relabel `google-workspace` as "Google Workspace" and `microsoft-365` as "Microsoft 365", moving "(Gmail, Calendar, Search Console and Analytics)" and "(Outlook mail and calendar)" into their descriptions; drop the parenthetical from scope-set labels ("Google account (who you are)"), since other surfaces wrap labels in parentheses.
